- A separate "needs a service" layer in the engine model, driven by its condition (build roughness plus wear) rather than its roughness at the moment, so cold, rich and lean running behave as before. Above `erraticFrom` 0.8 it grows steeply (smoothstep times linear) to 1:
  - stumbles: runs of 2 to 6 misfires, at up to `stumbleRate` 0.45 a second (per second rather than per firing, so the two-strokes, which fire every revolution, aren't twice as bad), with only 30% of its power while it stumbles, so it hesitates under throttle too;
  - catching again with a bang (28% of stumbles: a 2.6-strength firing pulse, so a thump in the sound, a kick in the shake and a puff of smoke) or, at worst, not at all (`stumbleStallChance` 1.5%);
  - surges: the idle flaring up by up to half its idle and dying away over a second or so, at up to 0.25 a second;
  - a fast, irregular hunt on top of the slow rough hunt.
- Wear now goes all the way to 1 (it stopped at 0.7, so the smoother quads could never get there): roughly half an hour of hard riding.
- Measured at warm idle, 2 minutes x 3 seeds per quad: below 0.8 nothing changes; at 0.9 about 7 to 10 stumbles a minute and no stalls; at 1, 20 to 29 stumbles a minute, 5 to 9 bangs, the idle swinging over roughly 0.4 to 1.6 times itself, and a stall every one to three minutes. The orange, whose build roughness goes up to 0.9, can stumble now and then from new.
- Tests: no stalls at idle up to the edge of erratic (hard stops up to halfway there), and fully worn, an idle spread of over 0.9 times the idle with under 9 stalls in 6 minutes. The wear test now expects it to wear past 0.85 (it stops wearing once it stalls).
- The girls remark on a quad that's this bad when they're on it or beside it.

## Sitting on the back alone, and being offered a ride (2026-10-05)

- Request: sit pillion with nobody else there; the girls jump on and drive you about, and get off when bored without waiting to be told.
- `B` while looking at a parked, upright quad with a pillion seat and nobody on it puts you on the back (`canSitOnBack`). E still takes the front. The prompt shows `B sit on the back` when it's possible.
- `RideOffer` (`src/npc/rideOffer.js`, pure, tested) times it: you must have sat on a parked quad with nobody at the front for `offerRideWaitMin..Max` (6 to 25 s; any interruption restarts the count). Then `pickOfferer` chooses among the girls who are standing idle with nothing else on the go, weighted to the nearer (1 / (1 + distance / 10)), and she goes through the existing `giveRide` path (walk over, get on the front, start it, bigger loops). If nobody is free it tries again after the rest period.
- Why it ends by itself: a ride you ask for keeps going until you say stop, because `HerDrive.carrying` counted a passenger as a reason not to tire. For her own offer, `tires: true` turns that off, so the drive lasts `offerRideForMin..Max` (60 to 150 s, then the usual pull up, bored or scared, and get off). Rides you ask for with "Take me for a ride" are unchanged. If you get off before she's on, she gives up after `offerRideGiveUp` (25 s).
- After an offered ride, `offerRideRestMin..Max` (45 to 120 s) must pass before anyone offers again, even if you're still sat there; the rest runs while she climbs down, so it isn't reset by that. A first version reset it and Amy offered again at once.
- New line `offerRide` for each girl, said on the way over.
- Checked once headless (offer shortened to 3 to 4 s, ride to 15 s): Amy walked over, got on, drove, pulled up and got off with you still on the back, and didn't offer again. Not checked: Jess offering, both girls busy, real-length rides, or the new lines recorded.
- Follow-up: on the back with nobody else on the quad, E did nothing (it only opened the ride wheel when a girl was on it). It now gets you off, and the prompt says so ("Get off", with C look back). The interaction prompt is now a main line (what E does) over a row of key chips (K Ignition, R Start, Q Z Choke, F Fuel tap, B Sit on the back, X Winch cable) instead of one long line of text; prompts that are plain text are unchanged. Checked once headless with a screenshot.

- Follow-up: the rest after an offered ride comes from the girl who gave it. Jess, cheekier and bored sooner, rests 25 to 70 s (Amy 45 to 120) and drives you for 45 to 110 s (Amy 60 to 150). The wait before a first offer is still shared (Amy's numbers).

## Two girls and you on one quad: the front rack (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: the second girl sits on the front rack (not squeezed onto the pillion, not standing on the back); only quads with a front rack (red, blue, green) allow it; only the girls take it, so it happens with you on the back and one girl driving (you driving with two girls aboard is left for later); the handling effect is small; it starts the same ways as the pillion swap (from her wheel, or a bored girl hopping on as the driver sets off, never onto a moving quad by choice).

- A third seat, `rack`, beside `rider` and `pillion`. The model gets a `rackAnchor` on the front rack (`quad.hasRack`, `quad.rackRider`), so which quads have one follows the body style rather than a flag in the spec. `Companion.seatKind` ('pillion' or 'rack') sets which anchor she walks to, sits on and claims, and the seat claims already used by the two-girl sharing keep her out of the others' way.
- Rule in `src/npc/rackSeat.js` (pure, tested): a rack, upright, free, her friend at the controls and not following you or waiting to give you a ride, and the back already taken (otherwise she'd take the back). The driver treats her like a friend on the back: pulls up while she's coming and sets off once she's on. With you already aboard the loops aren't scaled up a second time.
- She sits facing forward, hips 0.66 m up the deck, legs 30% of the pillion spread (`rackLegSpread`), no hold-on pose (the sitting clip's hands on her lap). `rackSeatRaise` and `rackSeatForward` tune her position. She gets off when her friend has stopped driving (the same rule as the back), landing beside the front wing. She can't be asked to drive from the rack: she'd have to get off first.
- Handling: with a girl on the rack the steering lock is `rackSteerLoss` (0.08) smaller. Nothing else changes: weight isn't modelled for riders anywhere, so there's no nose dip or extra load on the front springs.
- Checked once in headless Chrome: Amy gave me a ride on the green, Jess was asked to sit on the front, waited for the quad to stop, climbed on and sat upright on the rack with Amy driving on. Not checked: getting off, a full ride with her aboard, the other two racked quads by eye, and the swap ("Let me drive") with her on the rack. She says the usual "get on" acknowledgement; no new lines.

- When: `loveYou` on a smooth ride with you (fun or gentle for 15 s, 3% a second, which is the slow check) or after 15 s stood together on foot (5%); `careForYou` instead of it when it's raining, on foot or riding with you after 40 s. Both go through the Chatter, so they wait their turn and never talk over the other girl.

## The polo collar, third go

- Feedback: the collar sat lower than the top of the blue shirt and looked separate; it should start where the neck skin stops. A real folded collar wanted, and her chest was hiding part of the placket.
- Cause: the band was placed from the neck bone (y 1.20 m), but the shirt's neckline is about 4 cm higher up the middle of the neck. `shirtVertices` in `src/npc/outfit.js` now reads which body vertices are shirt from the recoloured texture, and the neckline is the top of those up the middle of the neck. The band is the shirt's own top 3.5 cm pushed out a few millimetres, so it joins the shirt exactly.
- The folded collar is a ring round the neckline with a profile (`collarSection`): up a 2 cm stand, over a crease, down as a flap resting on the shirt. The flap runs longer near the front opening, so it ends in points. Its radius is found by rays at each height, so it follows the shoulders and chest, and it is carried by the upper spine.
- The old flat flaps are gone. The placket is a ribbon laid along the chest surface (ten samples, lifted 7 mm along the surface normal) rather than a straight strip, so the bust no longer swallows it. Buttons sit on the ribbon, facing out along the normal.
- Colours: the collar and placket use the shirt's exact colour (the texture holds the linear value as if sRGB, so the material colour is converted the same way), then are lightened by 1.6 and 1.9 so the folds and placket read against the dark navy and burgundy.
- Checked once headless on Amy, close-up from the front and side. Not checked: Jess's burgundy by eye, and the collar while she rides or sits.
- Placket, follow-up (it still clipped her neck): the neck rises almost vertically from the neckline, so a ribbon laid by raycast from the neckline ran up the neck itself. It now starts 3 cm below the front neckline and is 12 cm long. Checked headless from the front and the side on Amy.
- Collar, fourth go (it read as a mandarin collar, and the front points were small): the stand is lower (1.2 cm, was 2 cm), the flap wider (3.2 cm, was 1.4 cm) with a clear crease, and the flap is lighter than the stand (vertex colours, `COLLAR_SHADE`) so the fold shows. The front opening is narrower and the flap runs 3.6 cm longer at its ends, so the points hang down either side of the placket. Checked headless on Amy, front and side; Jess not looked at.

## Who you favour (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: the score counts rides, talking, her favourite quad, washing it, and being ignored, and fades back to nothing; the player never sees a number, but the tuning panel shows it; the consequences are voice lines, ride willingness, banter and a neglected girl taking a quad off alone; a neglected girl sometimes refuses; nothing persists between games.

- `src/npc/favour.js` (pure, tested). One score per girl. Events: she drives you about (+0.05 a second), you open her wheel (+1), you get on her favourite quad (+1.5; the quad is the `favourite` each girl already picks at random for her "I like the red" remarks), you hose it (+0.1 a second), you talk to her friend with her stood there (-1), she's on foot while her friend drives you (-0.02 a second). Scores fade by 0.01 a second (a score of 10 loses about 6 a minute) and stop at plus or minus 20.
- What matters is her standing against her friend, `(her score - friend's) / 6`, clamped to -1..1. Beyond plus or minus 0.35 she's favoured or neglected. With two girls it is symmetric, so treating both the same keeps both even. Everything else reads that one number.
- Favoured: kind words (`loveYou`, `careForYou`) come up to twice as often and she has her own `favouredWarm` lines when stood near you; she's weighted up to 1.7 times in who offers you a ride. Neglected: the kind words fade to nothing, `neglectedSulk` lines instead, and ride offers weight down to 0.3.
- Refusing: only a neglected girl, up to 35% at the worst, and only for requests about a quad (ride, follow, drive, start, get on). It only happens when her friend is standing about and could do the same thing, so you're never left without: the friend does it two seconds later, gloating 70% of the time (otherwise her usual okay). If the friend can't take it on, she just does it.
- Bored and neglected, her chance of choosing to drive is multiplied up to 2.5 times, and when she does she shouts that she's off on her own (`sulkOff`).
- The rivalry: a new banter kind `favoured` (four exchanges each way, one every 90 s at the soonest) between the two when they're stood about near you and one is clearly favoured.
- The tuning panel (backtick) has a "Favour (live)" folder with each girl's score, standing and mood, plus buttons to push either one by 5 or reset, and a "Favour" folder with every number above.
- Checked once headless (banter timing shortened, refusals made certain): with Amy favoured the three-line exchange played, and asking the neglected Jess for a ride got "Make me.", then Amy walked over, gloated and drove. The ten new situations are recorded for both girls. Not checked: how quickly the standing builds up in an ordinary play session (the numbers are a first guess), Jess being the favoured one in the running game, or a neglected girl's solo drive by eye.


## Pranks (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: only while you're away; you find out by the quad misbehaving or the thing missing, no hint; hidden things go to fixed spots and come back by themselves; Jess does engines, Amy hides things; favour changes who does it; nothing persists between games.

- `src/npc/pranks.js` (pure, tested). The first prank can't happen until 4 minutes in, then at most one every 10 minutes. Every 5 s there is a 12% chance of a look for something fair game: a quad that is parked, switched off, not being got on and more than 30 m from you (Jess: tap not already off, or choke off on an engine warmer than half), or the jerry can or toolbox not in your hands and more than 30 m from you (Amy). You must not be riding for the item pranks.
- Favour: a favoured girl never pranks; a neglected one is twice as likely to be the one.
- The girl has to be stood about. She walks over (`sneakTo` in `companion.js`, a `pranking` state), pauses 1.4 s, then does it, and only if it is still fair game then (nobody on the quad, not running, item not picked up). Otherwise it lapses; so does one she hasn't reached in 90 s.
- Hiding: the item moves to one of the farm's existing can spots (`jerryCanSpots`, so known good ground) between 8 and 40 m from where it was and not within 12 m of you. She puts it back herself after 10 minutes. Picking it up ends the prank.
- Finding out: sat on a pranked quad for 3 s, or putting it right yourself, or walking within 6 m of where a hidden item should be. The culprit then says a `prankGiggle` line (not a "tell"; Amy's poker face is poor, Jess blames you). Talk to her afterwards and she says `prankConfess` once. Both are new situations for both girls, recorded.
- Tap off gives the existing 10 to 30 s warm idle before it dies; choke on a warm engine runs rich and may stall. No new engine behaviour.
- The tuning panel (backtick) has a "Pranks" folder with every number above.
- Checked once headless, with the delays and distances squeezed: Jess and Amy both walked over, paused and did it (choke on the orange, toolbox hidden with its home remembered), no errors. Not checked: how it feels at the real pace, the culprit's lines in the running game, a hidden item being found and put back by eye.

## Teaching them (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: coaching is a "Coach her" slice on the wheel you get on the back while she drives, listing only what fits the moment; four skills (starting, choke, cornering, mud) replace the random flaws; right-moment advice teaches, practice teaches far more slowly, wrong-moment or repeated advice doesn't; her lines show her level; only from the back for now; nothing persists between games.

- `src/npc/skills.js` (pure, tested). A level 0 to 1 for each skill per girl. 0.5 behaves exactly as she did before; `mistake(level)` is 2 at 0, 1 at 0.5 and 0 at 1 and scales her chance of each error. Amy starts at starting 0.5, choke 0.3, cornering 0.6, mud 0.35; Jess at 0.45, 0.3, 0.4, 0.65.
- What each skill changes in `HerDrive`: choke is the chance she forgets it (`driveForgetChokeChance` is now the chance at 0.5, so a start of 0.3 forgets about 0.63 of the time); starting scales how often she checks the tap and puts the choke on and how soon she remembers after a failed go; cornering scales how often she yanks the bars and the cornering speed (0.8 to 1.2 times `driveCornerAccel`); mud gives a better-judged boost when stuck and one more go before giving up from 0.8. The levels are a live object, so coaching changes them under her mid-drive.
- The right moment (`HerDrive.isMoment`): choke is running, warm (over half) with the choke still on; starting is cranking, just stalled or a stall in the last 5 s; cornering is a yank in the last 5 s or the bars well over in a turn; mud is a rollback or being stuck in the last 5 s, stuck over a second or sinking. The wheel only shows advice that fits when it opens, and the check is made again when you pick it.
- Learning: advice at the right moment gives +0.12 and +0.5 favour; advice on the same skill within 25 s is nagging (nothing, -0.5 favour, she's short with you); the wrong moment gives nothing. Practice gives +0.01, at most once every 30 s per skill, for a clean corner, the choke taken off when warm, a start that catches or getting out of a bog. A good piece of advice also makes her act at once (choke off, a boost in the mud). Opening her wheel already counts as talking to her for favour.
- Her voice: for each skill, `skill_<skill>_novice` (she's just made the mistake and is under 0.35, said in place of the usual yelp), `_learning` and `_learned` (on a clean success, half the time), `taught_<skill>` after good advice, and `coachWrongTime` and `coachNagged`. Lines are in `src/npc/skillLines.js`, Jess's with her own wording. 18 new situations each.
- Checked once headless with Jess driving a ride: the Coach options appeared (choke off, corners, mud), advice raised her choke from 0.3 to 0.42 and she said the taught line, the same advice at once got "I heard you the first time!", and she had said her novice choke line earlier. Not checked: how it feels over a long session (the 0.12 step means about five good pieces of advice from novice to learned, which is a first guess), Amy, the other three skills in the running game, or the wheel by eye.

## Favours (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: three simple favours (wash her favourite quad, take her to the bales, fetch the jerry can; a race against Jess is left for its own item); she asks only while you're on foot near her and she's idle; nothing shows on screen and she reminds you if you talk to her; doing it is a big favour score, ignoring it lapses into a grudge; nothing persists between games.

- `src/npc/requests.js` (pure, tested). First ask not before 180 s, then each girl no more than every 240 s, one open request per girl. Every 5 s a 15% chance that someone asks, among the girls who are idle, within 14 m of you, with you on foot and a favour that makes sense: wash needs her favourite quad (the one she already likes for her remarks) over 30% muddy; bales needs a quad with a free pillion seat; the can needs it more than 6 m from her.
- Done: wash when that quad is under 10% muddy; bales when she's on the back of the quad you're riding, it's under 0.8 m/s and within 7 m of a straw bale (the spots are now `strawBaleSpots` in `layout.js`, filtered the same way `props.js` filters them, so nothing points at a bale that wasn't built); the can when it's within 3 m of her, with you also within 3 m if you're still holding it. Done gives +4 favour and her thanks; a favoured girl with her friend within 12 m gloats 4 s later 60% of the time.

A grudge adds a 15% chance of saying no to quad requests (on top of the neglected-girl refusals, using the larger of the two; the friend takes it on as before) and she says a grudge line the first time you open her wheel. Doing any favour for her clears it. While one's open, opening her wheel gets a reminder line instead.
- Lines: `ask`, `thanks`, `remind` and `grudge` for each of Wash, Bales and Can, plus `gloatFavour`, in `src/npc/requestLines.js` (Jess's with her own wording). 15 new situations each.
- Bales follow-up (TODO 5): the girl who asked didn't do anything when you got on a quad, so the favour mostly lapsed. Now `Requests.joiner`: 1.5 s after you get on a quad with a free pillion seat (`joinDelay`, so you see her coming), if she's still waiting, free and within 30 m (`joinRange`), she walks over and gets on the back (`requestMount`) and says her reminder line. Once per quad; get on another and she tries again. Not checked in the running game: how it looks when you drive off before she's aboard (she chases the quad's mount point), or the timing by feel.
- Checked once headless (delays squeezed): both girls asked, the can was put by Amy, she thanked and her favour went up. Not checked: a bale favour or a wash by eye, the grudge and reminder lines in the running game, a lapse at the real six-minute pace, or how the 4-minute pace feels over a session.

A girl on a quad or doing something else is left alone. Spots are `restSpots` in `layout.js` (four fence seats, three barn door spots) plus one for each straw bale not too near the track, which the game adds. Spots taken, or promised to a girl who's still getting up, aren't offered to her friend.

## Batteries and starting (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: a battery per quad with no gauge (you hear and see it); pull-start on the two-strokes only, with a hold-and-yank knack; jump leads as a new item; the girls notice and say so but don't fix it, except Jess pulling the cord; bump-starting left out for now (the engine already gets turned over by the wheels with the throttle shut, so it would be a small addition later).

- Battery lives in `EngineModel` (`battery`, 0..1, in `engineParams.js` so it's in the tuning panel). Ignition on and engine not running loses 0.0021 a second (flat in 8 minutes), the starter 0.0125 a second, running gains 0.0017 a second (10 minutes from flat). Under 0.6 the starter's speed falls to 40% at 0.25; under 0.25 `starterClick` is raised once per press and nothing turns; a slow crank also lowers the chance each cycle of catching, in proportion to its speed. `hasPower` (ignition on and over 0.1) is what the dash shows, so it goes dark when it's nearly flat. The recorded engine sound gets a new `starterClick` (two solenoid clicks).
- Pull-start: `pullStart: true` on the yellow and orange. `engine.pull(held)`: hold 0.45 to 1 s is strength 1; shorter ramps up from 0.25, longer falls to 0.4 by 1.6 s. One pull turns it over for 0.9 s at the strength times the starter's speed (it uses the same cranking state and catch chance, so cold, choke, flooding and no-fuel all still matter), and takes nothing from the battery. `P` is held in `quad.js` for you, someone beside the quad, and a girl (`helperPull`).
- Her starting (`StartingAttempt`): says `slow` once if the battery's under 0.6; on a flat one says `flat`, then with `pullWhenFlat` (Jess 1, Amy 0, a companion param `startPullWhenFlat`) on a quad with a pull-start holds the cord for 0.3 to 1.4 s a go and yanks up to five times, otherwise gives up and switches the key off. Lines `startSlow`, `startFlat`, `startPull` (Jess only), and for you struggling near her `crankStruggleBattery` and `crankFlat` (`batteryLines.js`).
- Jump leads (`src/items/jumpLeads.js`, the hose and toolbox pattern): clamp one end on a quad, then the other; whichever is running charges the other at 0.06 a second (the higher battery donates if both run). They reach 4.5 m between quads and 5 m from a clamped quad to your hand, and come off past that. In the tuning panel as "Jump leads".
- Checked once headless: a flat yellow only clicked and stayed at 0 rpm; the first yank on the cord caught it; the leads (with the reach loosened, as the quads were too far apart) took a flat battery from 5% to 34% in 5 s; Jess faced with a flat battery got a two-stroke going. Not checked: how the weak crank sounds and looks by eye, the pull-start's feel by hand (the 0.45 to 1 s window is a first guess), the real time it takes to flatten a battery, the dash going dark, a girl's slow-crank line in the running game, or the leads' look and carrying.


## Two-stroke oil (2026-10-05)

Decided with you ("go" on my recommendations): a yellow pre-mix can and an oil bottle by the tank; pre-mix in a four-stroke is wrong too; a seize is recoverable at the toolbox but costs wear; the girls react but don't pour; no mix gauge, only the can's prompt text.

- Pure logic in `src/engine/lubrication.js` (`mixOil`, `ratioText`, `lubeFactor`, `oilExcess`, `stepLube`, `nipping`); `EngineModel.updateLube` applies it. Tunables are in `engineParams.js` ("Oil in the petrol"). A `FuelSystem` and a `JerryCan` each carry `oil`, the oil fraction of what's in them; pouring blends it (`mixOil`). The two-strokes start at 0.02 (50:1) via `startOil` in their spec; the four-strokes at 0.
- Two-stroke: `starved = 1 - oil/0.02`. Extra heat (`overheat`, 0..1, eases in over 12 s) is starved times how hard it's working, and goes onto the dash gauge past the warm mark, up into the red (`tempGaugeFraction` has a third argument). Scuffing (`scuff`) builds at starved squared times work over 70 s, so flat out on straight petrol seizes in about 70 s at full work and idling (work 0.15) takes about 8 minutes; half-oiled is a quarter as fast. It never heals by itself. From 0.55 it nips up: roughness up by 0.45, power down by 0.6 and it goes erratic. At 1 it seizes: stalls with `seized`, `starterClick` on each press, no pull-start.
- Strip down (toolbox panel, `F`, or its button): clears the scuffing and the heat; if it had seized it adds 0.3 wear, which a service clears.
- Too much oil: above 0.04 on a two-stroke, or above 0.004 on a four-stroke, `oilRich` rises to 1 at 0.1 and 0.02. It adds smoke (density and a darker grey), up to 0.2 roughness and plug fouling at 0.15 a second, so it fouls at idle and a good handful of throttle clears it.
- Items: red petrol can as before; yellow pre-mix can (12 L at 50:1) and an oil bottle at `layout.oilBottle`/`premixCan`. Filling at the tank adds petrol, which thins any mix. Oil goes in at 0.04 L a second, so 0.4 L (ten seconds) takes 20 L to 50:1. `game.can` is whichever can you're holding (else the red one), so the other code is unchanged; the favour and prank code keeps to the red one.
- The girls: lines in `oilLines.js`. Amy warns if you pour the wrong can into a quad near her (`oilStraight`, `oilPremixFour`), and says what's wrong when a two-stroke runs hot, nips up, seizes or smokes. Jess is cheerful about the same things and blames the quad when it seizes. They don't refuel quads themselves, so a girl never ruins an engine on her own.
- Checked: unit tests (`tests/oil.test.js`) and once headless that the game loads with no errors, the pre-mix can holds 12 L at 0.02, and straight petrol poured into the yellow's tank thins it as expected. Not checked: how it feels and sounds to be nipping up, how the cans and bottle look and sit by the tank, the ten second oil pour, or any of the girls' new lines in the running game. Run times are first guesses.

## Faults that develop (2026-10-05)

The TODO item settled the symptoms; I chose the rest without asking: the four faults all go in, fixed by four instant jobs in the toolbox panel (a hands-on version belongs to the workshop item), and the girls notice but never fix.

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/faults.js` (`Faults`, one per quad, tests in `tests/faults.test.js`; tunables under `<quad>/Faults` in the tuning panel). A fault starts with a random chance per second of the right kind of use, at level 0.15, and rises to 1 until fixed: puncture while moving (average 2400 s, takes 120 s to go flat, and goes on parked), belt while moving with the throttle over 0.6 (1800 s, 300 s of hard riding to its worst), cable and carb while the engine runs (2700 s and 2400 s, taking 600 s and 900 s). One of each at a time. Over ten minutes of riding a quad averages somewhere between 0.3 and 2 faults (a test bounds it); they start only on a quad that is running, so a parked one stays well.
- Puncture: the physics steer gets `steerPull` added (0.09 rad at worst towards the flat tyre, half for a rear one; the visible bars don't move, only the quad pulls) and the drive loses up to 12%. Belt: drive loses up to 80%, and in `EngineModel` the `slip` control cancels the load that holds the revs down and adds up to 1800 rpm at full throttle (`beltFlareRpm`), so the revs flare and the quad hardly goes. Cable: `stickThrottle` holds the throttle where you left it and eases it back over up to 5 s (a fully sticking cable); below level 0.2 it's only stiff and doesn't hang; pressing the throttle is never held back and switching off frees it. Carb: the `dirt` control adds up to 0.3 roughness, takes up to 25% of the power and drives `erratic` (so the existing idle hunting and stumbling get worse and worse).
- Fixes: toolbox panel keys `T` tyres, `R` belt, `L` cable, `C` carb (buttons too). Each does its job if there's a fault and otherwise says it was fine, so pressing them doesn't diagnose. All instant.
- The girls (`faultLines.js`, `watchFaults` in `companionVoice.js`): she mentions a fault once it passes her `faultNoticeFrom` (Amy 0.3, Jess 0.6), at most twice per fault per quad, only when it's showing (the tyre and belt while it's moving, the cable and carb while the engine's running), and more readily on the quad she's on. Amy names the fault; Jess is cheerful and blames the quad.
- Left out: the hedge as the cause of a puncture (it can happen anywhere), a sagging tyre you can see, and a sound for the belt (the revs do it). Checked: unit tests, and once headless that the game loads with no errors, that forcing all four faults pulls the steering, cuts the drive and roughens the engine, and that the four toolbox jobs clear them. Not checked: how any of it feels or sounds to drive, whether the pull or the cable's hang read right, the real frequency in play, the panel's wrapped buttons, or the girls' new lines in the running game. All the rates and strengths are first guesses.

## Heat (2026-10-05)

The TODO item settled the symptoms; I chose the rest without asking: no new controls, the girls comment but never act, and the water-cooled orange is the only one with a radiator.

- Pure logic in `src/engine/heat.js` (tests in `tests/heat.test.js`; tunables under "Heat" in `engineParams.js`, so they're in the tuning panel). `EngineModel` has `heat` (0..1, running hot, on top of the warm-up `temperature`) and `soak` (0..1, heat held in the metal). It steps once a frame rather than per substep (the per-substep version pushed the long idle sweep in `quadCharacter.test.js` over its time limit, which now has a 30 s allowance anyway).
- Heat heads for the sun (`heatSun` 0.75 times the day's warmth) plus work (`heatWork` 0.5), less airflow (`heatAirCool` 1.2, full at 8 m/s ground speed) and, on the orange, the fan (0.7). It rises with a 240 s time constant and falls with 90 s. Idling warm in full sun the target is about 0.83, so pinking (from 0.45) starts at about four minutes; flat out at 8 m/s it sits near nothing. Crawling at 1 m/s in mud with the throttle open reaches pinking in a few minutes even on a fair day. Overcast and wet days never get there. The day's warmth is `Weather.warmth`: the kind's sun intensity over 2.6, less rain (sunny 1, sunny intervals 0.73, overcast 0.48, rain about 0.1).
- Pinking (`pink`, 0..1 from heat 0.45 to 1, air-cooled only): up to 0.3 roughness and 25% of the power, and rattles at up to 14 a second under load (fewer on a closed throttle), each a 2 to 4 ping burst in the engine worklet. The gauge's red zone starts at half of `overheat`, so the needle enters the red just as the pinking starts. The gauge shows the larger of the oil `overheat` and this heat (`EngineModel.gaugeHeat`), so the dashes needed no change.
- The orange (`waterCooled: true`): it feels 60% of the load, the fan cuts in above heat 0.28 and out below 0.12 (so it cycles, about once a minute or two idling in full sun), its gauge shows only a quarter of the heat, and it never pinks. The fan is a soft rush of air with a faint hum that eases in and out (`fanVolume`), only while the engine runs. The other two-stroke, the yellow, is air-cooled.
- Soak follows work (a quarter at idle, up to 1 flat out, or the heat if that's higher) with a 60 s rise, and sheds with a 240 s time constant running or off. Switched off, a soaked engine ticks (`coolTick`, one small high ring) at up to 1.1 a second, in proportion to how far it's over 0.3, so it slows and stops after about ten minutes; an engine that only idled doesn't tick. A hot restart: while it isn't running, soak over 0.5 makes the mixture richer by up to 0.6 (fuel boiling in the float bowl), and holding the throttle open takes 70% of that away, using the existing flooding and clearing while cranking. Soak that's gone after about ten minutes starts normally.
- The girls (`heatLines.js`, `watchHeat` in `companionVoice.js`): `heatPink` when it's pinking near her or under her, `heatTicking` stood by a ticking quad. Amy says what's wrong; Jess is cheerful. Both banks are re-recorded.
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless on a sunny seed with the heat time constants shortened to seconds: the game loads with no errors, the four air-cooled quads reached heat 0.69 with pinking events and the gauge value in the red zone. Not checked: how pinking, the cooling tick and the fan sound by ear (all first guesses at the volume), the orange's fan cycling in the running game (that quad had stalled in my check, so it didn't show the fan; the unit test covers the cycle), ticking in the running game (it needs minutes of real heat soak), the real times in play, and the girls' new lines. All the rates and strengths are first guesses.

## Popping on the overrun and running on (2026-10-05)

Done together after Heat, as the same hot, worn or lean engine drives both; I chose the details without asking. Item 31 (warming their hands) was left out: it needs a new two-handed pose, a spot for her to stand and a reason to go there, which is a feature of its own.

- Pure logic in `src/engine/runOn.js` (tests in `tests/runOn.test.js`; tunables under "Popping on the overrun" and "Run-on" in `engineParams.js`).
- Pops: the engine model decides, so sound, flash and the girls can share it. With the throttle under 0.05 and revs over 3200, each firing pops with a chance of `popBase` (0.12) times a propensity (`popProne` 0.03 for a healthy four-stroke, 0.7 on the two-strokes via their specs, plus 1.0 per unit of leanness, 0.8 for full wear and 0.4 for running hot) times (0.3 plus how far up the revs). The `overrunPop` event plays a crack with a thump in the worklet. The old audio-only random pop on every firing is gone (it only ever sounded on the synth, not the recorded engine). Rough numbers from tests, over a 3 s run-down from 5000 rpm over idle: the yellow about 15 pops, a healthy four-stroke none, a four-stroke at 0.8 wear about 2.
- Flash: a small additive orange sprite at the exhaust tip (`ExhaustSmoke`, `flashBrightness`), fading in about 0.03 s, scaled by `darkness`: 1 minus 1.3 times the day's warmth, so none on a sunny day and plenty under heavy cloud or rain.
- Run-on: switching off a running engine has a chance of `runOnHot` 0.55 times how heat-soaked it is (over half), plus `runOnWear` 0.6 times wear and `runOnLean` 0.5 times leanness, capped at 0.9; a healthy cool engine never does. It then keeps firing for 3 to 15 beats (more the worse it is), each losing revs and misfiring 45% of the time, then dies with the usual shutdown run-down. While it runs on `ignitionOn` is false (the dash goes dark) but `isRunning` is true. Switching the key back on carries on. The 'shutdown' event is held back until the end.
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless on an overcast seed with a forced overrun: the yellow popped, the flash showed, and a hot worn engine ran on and then shut down once. That first run popped on nearly every firing (63 in 2 s at 11000 rpm), so I turned `popBase` down from 0.4 to 0.12 afterwards and didn't look again. Not checked: how the pops sound or the flash looks by eye, whether the crackle on a two-stroke is the right amount in real riding (in the game the wheels bring the revs down over a second or so), how often run-on shows in play, and a girl's reaction (none added).


## Inside the barn (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: build the barn procedurally rather than cut into the model; the big doors are always open (no door to work); same footprint; quads under cover stay clean and warmer; in the rain the girls sit on bales inside; engines boom and echo and rain drums on the tin; only the toolbox moves, to a workbench inside (the workshop item builds on it).

- `barn.glb` is gone (and its ASSETS.md line). `src/world/barnBuild.js` draws a hollow shed from boxes: board walls (canvas texture), gable ends, a tin roof on timber ties, the doors slid back along the outside of the east wall, a concrete slab, a workbench with a pegboard and a bare bulb. Footprint is the old barn's (about 10.4 by 9.2 m outside, door 4.6 m wide and 3.2 m high, eaves 4.2 m, ridge 6.3 m), door facing the yard on the +x side. The numbers are in `LAYOUT.barn`; `barnBaleSpots`, `bench` and `toolbox` (now on the bench, `lift` 0.92) are beside it.
- Pure logic in `src/world/barn.js` (tests in `tests/barn.test.js`; tunables under "Barn" in the tuning panel): `insideBarn`, `underRoof` (the roof overhangs 0.3 m), `indoorLevel` (0 in the yard, fading in over `doorFade` 1.5 m past the threshold, so rain and light change smoothly as you walk in), `wallBoxes` (five colliders; the east wall in two halves leaves the doorway), `floorBlend` (the terrain is levelled under the barn and eases off over 2.5 m, so the floor is flat; the slab is 1 cm proud of it and not a collider).
- Rain: no streaks fall under the roof (each drop is tested against the roof's footprint, so you see the rain stop at the door). The sound: `rainHeard` makes it louder under the roof (`rainTin` 3, up to about 1.7 times as loud) and a second looped layer of sparse short rings on tin fades in with `indoor`. Light: the hemisphere light drops by 55% indoors and a warm bulb comes up (30 cd, stronger on gloomy days); the sun's shadow from the roof does the rest.
- Echo and boom: the hub has a convolver with a generated 1.6 s impulse; every quad's `SoundPath` (so engine and ride sounds) has a low shelf and a send into it, set by whether you and the sound's source are inside (`reverbWet` 0.55 both in, 0.2 one in; bass up 6 dB for an engine inside heard from inside, 40% of that for you inside and it outside).
- Under cover: `EngineModel` takes `sheltered` (the quad is under the roof). Switched off, it cools 2.5 times slower (`barnCoolFactor`) and only down to 0.25 (`barnWarmFloor`); it never warms up on its own, so a quad that spawns cold in the barn stays cold, but one you've run and put away starts much better after a long wait (900 s time constant outside: after an hour outside 0.02, in the barn 0.31). It stays clean because the floor isn't mud, so none builds; I added nothing to dry it.
- The girls: the doorway spots are gone. Two `shelter` spots with a seat sit on the bales at the back, facing the doors; `restStandPoint` now treats a shelter spot with a seat like a bale (she climbs up from behind it), and a shelter spot without one still just stands. Two bales means two girls; a third would have no place and stay out. Bales in `barnBaleSpots` are built by `buildBales`.
- Checked once headless in rain: the game loads with no errors, a quad driven into the barn counts as sheltered, both girls ran in and sat on the bales, no streaks inside, and screenshots from outside, the doorway and the back looked right (doors, roof, ties, bench with the toolbox, light). Not checked: how the echo, boom and tin drumming sound (first guesses, all tunable), the bulb's look in the dark (the seed was rain at midday), driving a quad through the door and the bales' girls getting down in the running game, the real cooling times in play, whether a girl on the bale clips the quad parked beside it, or any fine-weather use of the barn.

## The workshop bench (2026-10-05)

You answered "all as recommended" to five questions, so this is what was proposed.

- Where: a quad parked in a yellow-painted bay on the barn floor in front of the bench (`layout.bay`, drawn in `barnBuild.js`; a quad counts as in it when its middle is 0.35 m inside the lines). The bench needs the toolbox within 2.5 m of it, so Amy hiding the toolbox stops bench work until you find it.
- Jobs (`src/workshop/benchJobs.js`, pure, tests in `tests/bench.test.js`): plug, carb, tyre and, on two-strokes, oil. Each is four or five steps, a press (tap `E`) or a hold. A hold with a window (tightening, pumping, pouring oil) is botched by letting go early or holding on past the limit. Holds without a window just restart if you let go. Tunables under "Workshop bench" in the tuning panel (`holdScale` and what each botch leaves).
- Results: a clean plug job clears the fouling and 0.15 of wear; botched it leaves 0.3 fouling. A clean carb or tyre job fixes the fault completely; botched, the fault comes back at 0.2. Oil sets the mix by the pour: short 0.012 (hot), right 0.02 (50:1), over 0.05 (smokes). The inspect step on the plug tells you its old state, which the toolbox panel never did.
- The yard fixes (`T R L C` in the toolbox panel) are now rough: `Faults.fix(kind, { rough: true })` leaves the fault at 0.12 and caps it at 0.28, just under what Amy notices (0.3), so it never gets much worse but never goes away until the bench. A second rough fix does nothing.
- Girls: `benchLines.js`, `onBench` in `companionVoice.js`. Start, step, slip, done and botched, from anywhere within her voice range. Amy helps and corrects; Jess heckles. Both banks re-recorded.
- Left out: hands-on animation of the quad (it doesn't lift or lose a wheel), the girls physically helping (handing tools), a plug choice, and a four-stroke oil change. Checked: unit tests, and once headless that the game loads with no errors and a plug job runs through all its steps with a faked quad in the bay. Not checked: that you can really drive a quad into the bay and open the bench by looking at it, how the painted bay looks, how the holds feel to time, and the new lines in the running game. The hold times are first guesses.

## The hayloft (2026-10-05)

You answered "all as recommended" to six questions, so this is what was proposed.

- Layout: a plank floor over the west end of the barn, 2.6 m above the barn floor and out to x -64.6 (4.4 m deep, the full width), clear of the bay and the bench. The eye height on it is 4.2 m, just over the tie beams (their tops are at 4.16 m), so the camera doesn't pass through them. The two bales downstairs have 2.1 m of headroom under it, enough for a girl sat on one. Numbers are in `LAYOUT.loft`; the shape and rules are in `src/world/loft.js` (pure, tests in `tests/loft.test.js`; tunables under "Hayloft" in the tuning panel).
- Climbing: look at the ladder and press `E`; you're carried up (or down) over `climbSeconds` 2.6 with no ladder physics, and can't be stuck on it. You can't climb while your hands are full. The ladder foot is just west of the bay and its top lands at the gap in the rail.
- Edge: a rail (1 m, with a collider) along the open edge in two pieces with a 0.7 m gap at the ladder. The gap is the only drop, so rather than a separate hatch hole the ladder just comes up through the gap in the rail. A step off there is the ordinary landing; there's no damage in the game.
- View: a hay door cut in the east end wall above the big doors and in the gable above it (sill 3.5 m, 1.6 m wide, with a white frame and a hoist beam). It's a gap in the header and a notch in the gable, with no glass or leaves.
- Hiding: look at the hay stack while up there and press `E`. You move to a nook behind it and your eyes drop 1 m; any of `E`, `G`, `Esc`, movement keys or `Space` gets you out (you're put back where you were). While hidden, ride offers and favour requests are paused. After `hideCallAfter` 30 s a girl calls for you ("Where have you gone?"), again every 25 s, and after `hideFoundAfter` 110 s one finds you and you come out. A girl sat up in the loft finds you after 5 s. They don't walk to look for you: whoever calls or finds just says it, from wherever she is.
- Girls: `loft0` is a rest spot of its own kind (`loft`) at a bale at the edge. On a fine day, when she decides to sit out and the loft is free and within 45 m, she picks it `girlLoftChance` 0.3 of the time (else the usual bale or fence). She walks to the ladder foot, climbs along a path through the ladder top and across the planks (about 4 s) and sits, and climbs down the same way; it's the same rest as the bales, so talking to her or rain sends her down (rain then sends her to the barn bales). `restSpots` can have `stand` and `via` points for a spot like this.
- Straw: if Jess is sat in the loft and you stand below within 2.6 m of her, after 1.5 s each second has a 0.25 chance of her tipping a handful on you, then a 90 s cooldown. About three dozen stalks fall and stay on the floor (`src/effects/strawFall.js`, cosmetic). Jess is delighted, Amy tells her off. It doesn't happen while you're hidden.
- Lines: `loftLines.js`: `loftGo`, `loftSat`, `loftCall`, `loftFound` and `loftDrop` for each girl, both banks re-recorded.
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless in rain that the game loads with no errors, the climb takes you up onto the planks, the hay door shows the yard through its frame, and a girl sent to the spot climbs up and sits. Not checked: how the climb or the girl's ladder walk look in motion, hiding and being found, the straw fall, the loft in daylight, dropping off the edge at the gap, or whether the camera clips the tie beams when you look up. The girl's walk up is a straight line along the ladder with the walking animation, which will look stiff.

## Small changes before the next run (2026-10-06)

- **Tuning panel**: the live engine folder now also shows speed, fuel (litres and tap), oil, wear, erratic, heat, pinking, fan, soak, scuff, nip and seized, and a new "Faults (live)" folder shows each fault's level and lets you drag it to start, set or clear one (and shows which wheel has the puncture). Mud was already in "Mud on it".
- **Sticking throttle**: now progressive. Below level 0.2 nothing; from there the throttle takes `cableHangMin` 0.4 s to close, lengthening faster than linearly to `cableHang` 5 s just before `cableStuckAt` 0.9; at that level it never closes: it holds where you last opened it (a ratchet: opening more raises it), until you switch off or fix it. The fault grows 1 per 600 s of running from 0.15, so it takes about 450 s of running to stick for good. A girl driving a stuck quad switches it off (she then treats it as a stall).
- **Girls' lines**: audited every spoken line for both against the recorded banks. All of Jess's 668 and Amy's 1,074 are recorded, apart from four of Amy's one-word barks that never matched when recorded ("Oof!" and friends; they fall back to browser speech). Jess has her own lines for every situation in the barn (bench, loft, doorway shelter, rain) and shares Amy's only for five driving ones. I found nothing missing; if Jess went quiet somewhere specific, I need to know where.
- **Front rack on request**: a girl can now be asked to "Sit on the front" of a red, blue or green quad that is parked with nobody on it or driving it (`parked` in `rackSeat.js`), whoever else could take the back. You can then get on and ride with her on the front; she stays until asked to get off. Checked headless: Amy walked to the red, sat on the rack, and stayed there at 14 m/s with me driving. Not checked: Jess, the blue and green, her getting off, or whether the steering feels different.
- **Kids' quad**: the pink is faded, 0xcf6f98 (was 0xdc3f8f).

## Each quad has a knack (2026-10-06)

Picked item 21 as the first with nothing done (item 20 still has the mini moto). Scope settled by the item: two knacks, hidden from the player, known to the girls.

- **Green: the double blip.** `src/engine/knack.js` (pure, tested) counts rising edges of the throttle past `knackBlipAt` 0.5 while the engine isn't running; blips go stale after `knackWindow` 8 s and a running engine forgets them. Cold (under `knackColdBelow` 0.6) with fewer than `knackBlips` done, the catch chance is multiplied by `knackMissFactor` 0.04, so it cranks and sputters but almost never fires. Everything is an engine param with a range, `knackBlips` 0 on every quad but the green (2). Over 20 seeded cold starts with full choke and 6 s on the starter, two blips first caught many more than none (test asserts a gap of 8 or more).
- **Blue: choke off sooner.** `chokeEnrichment` 1.5 (default 1.0), so full choke over-enriches as soon as it warms: it runs rich, hunts and fouls unless the choke comes off early. Its cold need (1.1) is still met, so it still starts.
- **The girls.** `StartingAttempt` has `knackChance` 0.6: at the start she may already know the trick and blips the throttle twice (`helperThrottle` on the quad, `blipHold` 0.2 s, `blipGap` 0.3 s) before cranking, saying `startKnack`. If she doesn't, a failed go can make her remember (the existing `rememberChance`). Near you cranking the cold green without the trick, `crankStruggleKnack` is the hint; near a running blue left on choke with the mixture rich, `chokeOffBlue`. Lines are in `knackLines.js`. No nicknames yet, so they say "the green" (item 35 can change that).
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless that the game loads without errors. Not checked: how it feels to blip and crank by hand, whether 0.04 is too punishing on the green in play, how the blue's rich running sounds, or the girls' lines in the running game.

## Drowning it (2026-10-06)

Item 23, chosen as the first with nothing done. The farm has puddles but no fords, so it's puddles only; I settled the rest from the item.

- Pure logic in `src/engine/water.js` (tests in `tests/water.test.js`; tunables under "Drowning it" in `engineParams.js`, all with ranges). The quad hands the engine a `gulp` (water going up the intake per second): the deepest puddle under any wheel (`puddleAt`, 0..1) past `waterDepthFrom` 0.35, times the square of how far its speed is between `waterSafeSpeed` 3 m/s and `waterFullSpeed` 10 m/s, times `waterGulpRate` 6. Squared so a steady pace through is a splutter and flat out is a drowning. The engine keeps `water` 0..1.
- Effects: roughness (0.9) and misfire chance (0.6) and power loss (0.7) all scale with `water`; past `waterStallAt` 0.65 each firing has a 0.25 chance of stalling it (reason `water`). Cranking: the catch chance fades to nothing at `waterNoStart` 0.15, so it cranks and won't fire until it has drained.
- Draining: 0.022 a second off (about 45 s from full), 0.03 running plus 0.05 with the throttle wide open, so keeping it running and revving it clears it fastest.
- Steam: the exhaust smoke gets denser and whiter with `water` (`waterDensity` 0.8).
- A puddle's high-water zone is only about 2 m across, so crossing it at 10 m/s takes about a quarter of a second. I first set the rate at 3.5 and a headless run through a puddle at 6 m/s took on only 0.13, so I raised it to 6 by arithmetic, not by trying it again.
- Checked: unit tests, and the game loads without errors headless. Not checked: how it feels and sounds crossing a puddle at different speeds in play, the steam by eye, or whether 5 to 6 m/s is the right point to start spluttering. The girls say nothing about it yet, and the AI drivers don't slow for puddles.

## Silencer off (2026-10-06)

Item 24. The item settled the effects; I chose the rest without asking: one on/off toggle per quad in the toolbox panel rather than a hands-on bench job, and no downside beyond the noise and the girls' opinions.

- `X` in the toolbox panel (or its button) sets `engine.silencerOff`. "Back to stock" refits it. It lasts for the game and works on any quad, switched off, like the other panel jobs.
- Engine (`engineParams.js`, all with ranges): `silencerPower` 0.08 multiplies the power by 1.08 (the drivetrain treats power as a fraction, so this nudges the pull slightly past a healthy engine's; I didn't raise the rev limit, since that's clamped by the spec), and `silencerPop` 0.5 is added to the overrun propensity, so a healthy four-stroke that never pops now does, and a two-stroke does a lot more.
- Sound (`audioParams.js`): `silencerLoud` 2.5 raises the exhaust level by 250% (it started at 0.9, but the output's tanh squashed that into barely louder, so I raised it a lot) and `silencerBright` 0.8 opens the low-pass by 80%. Done for both the recorded and synthesised engines through a `loud` flag in the state sent to the worklet. These are first guesses by arithmetic.
- The girls (`silencerLines.js`): `silencerOff` and `silencerOn` when you do it near her, and `silencerLoud` now and then when a quad with it off is running near her or under her. Amy covers her ears; Jess loves it. Both banks are re-recorded.
- Checked: unit tests (`tests/silencer.test.js`). Not checked: how loud or bright it sounds against the standard engine, whether 8% more power can be felt, or the toolbox panel's layout with the extra button.

## Diagnosing by ear (2026-10-06)

Item 25. The item settled the behaviour; I chose the rest without asking: four conditions plus "fine", no new controls, and the correction as a banter exchange rather than a new system.

- Pure logic in `src/npc/diagnosis.js` (tests in `tests/diagnosis.test.js`). `engineCondition` scores cold (temperature under 0.35), rich (mixture over 0.2), fouled (plug over 0.35) and worn (wear over 0.2) from 0 at the threshold to 1, and returns the highest, or `fine` if none is over. `guessCondition` returns that with probability `diagnoseAccuracy` (Amy 0.9, Jess 0.5), else one of the other four (which can be `fine`, so a girl can say "sounds fine" about a sick engine or find a fault in a healthy one). All the thresholds, the accuracy and `diagnoseChance` (0.03 a second) are companion params with ranges in the tuning panel.
- When: she's on foot (idle or resting), within 6 m of a running quad, and the chatter cooldowns allow it. `watchDiagnosis` in `companionVoice.js` says it at most once per quad every 120 s, so a girl stood by one quad guesses roughly every minute or two.
- Correction: a wrong guess from Jess is left in `voice.lastDiagnosis`; `updateBanter` in `game.js` turns it into a `corrected` edge trigger if Amy is within 10 m and not driving or starting a quad. `DIAGNOSIS_BANTER` (in `diagnosisLines.js`, appended to `BANTER`) has one three-line exchange for each wrong pairing of truth and guess (20): Amy says what it really is, Jess insists on her own guess, Amy has the last word. Banter roles gained `guess` and `truth`, which are condition names rather than girls, so the test that roles name girls skips those two. Amy's own wrong guesses are never corrected, and Jess being right gets no remark.
- Left out: Amy correcting herself, Jess crowing when she's right, and a girl guessing about a quad you're riding. Not checked: how often it comes up in play (the rates are first guesses), whether the thresholds fit what you actually hear, and the new lines in the running game. Checked once headless that the game loads without errors and that both girls produce a guess from a forced engine state.

## Engine sounds for the kids' quad and the tractor (2026-10-06)

Shopped for on Freesound (CC0 only) and picked by ear by Jamie; not wired in yet (TODO 27 and 51). The kids' quad borrowed the yellow's recordings and didn't sound like a 50cc; the loan quad sharing the farm quads' sound is fine. The candidates, spectrograms and search scripts are in `.scratch/shop/` (untracked; `SHORTLIST.md` there). Use the HQ MP3 previews (`https://cdn.freesound.org/previews/<first 3 digits>/<id>_<user id>-hq.mp3`, linked from each sound's page) as for the other banks.

- **Kids' quad: Gilera Runner SP50** by lovretta, https://freesound.org/s/140384/ (61 s). A 50cc two-stroke scooter, derestricted, mic about an inch above the exhaust, so close and clean. Cold idle on its automatic choke from about 3 to 10 s, rev blips from 10 to 35 s, a long held high rev from about 40 to 53 s, then back to a warm idle to 58 s. At idle it fires every other turn (harmonics spaced about 18.6 Hz), which is the two-stroke ring-ding. Possibly also a Simson 50cc moped failing to start, https://freesound.org/s/378350/ (5 s), for cranking that doesn't catch; and its idle, https://freesound.org/s/378347/ (9.5 s), as a spare.
- **Tractor: kyles's "old throaty rattly" tractor**, the same recordist as the quarry ATV and the motocross idle. Chosen over a smoother, cleaner tractor in the same series (637880 to 637884, 637893) because it sounds old: broadband and rattly, with an uneven tickover where one beat in each cycle is stronger (harmonics spaced about 8.1 to 8.3 Hz, so one four-stroke cycle at about 980 rpm). The cost is less steady material to cut from.
  - https://freesound.org/s/637890/ (79 s), recorded close beside it like onboard: start about 12 to 14 s, running 14 to 31 s, driving 32 to 61 s, shut-off about 61 to 63 s. The main source.
  - https://freesound.org/s/637891/ (43 s): start about 2 to 4 s, revs 4 to 10 s, an almost-stall, shut-off about 37 to 40 s.
  - https://freesound.org/s/637892/ (84 s): start about 5 s, steadier running 27 to 37 s; the rest are passes with Doppler.
  - If the steady stretches turn out too short to slice well, look for a long close idle of a similar old diesel rather than mixing in the smoother tractor.

## The kids' quad's own sound (2026-10-06)

Item 27. Built from the Gilera Runner SP50 recording picked above; it's `engine-bank-50` and only the kids' quad uses it (`recBank` in its spec; the loan quad keeps the farm quads' sound).

- Cut by `tools/slice_engine_audio.py engine-bank-50 gil=140384.wav quad=379915.wav`: idle from the cold auto-choke idle (3.2 to 9.6 s) and the warm idle at the end (53.6 to 57.6 s), revs from the blips (10 to 35 s) and the long held rev (40.5 to 52.5 s). Starter and shutdown borrow the quad recording, as the yellow's bank does. 126 idle grains (about 1,000 to 3,700 rpm by the bank's pulse-rate measure, which is where the game's 1,100 to 1,300 rpm idle lands), 825 accel and 117 decel.
- The scooter fires every other turn at idle, so its beats alternate strong and weak and the old "next thump in tail" and "odd length" checks threw almost all of them out (4 idle grains). The slicer now takes `idleTail` and `idleOdd` per bank, as it already did for revs; this bank uses 1.2 and 0.4. A cut with zero length (two cuts at one point in a noisy stretch) no longer divides by zero.
- Rev stretches are noisy (many grains are rejected, and the tracker's periods run to double the real rate in places), so the revs lean on the existing "ok" filtering and on picking by nearest pulse rate. First guesses, by arithmetic only.
- Left out: the Simson 50cc "failing to start" clip for cranking that doesn't catch (cranking is the shared starter loop, so a separate sound would need a new case in the engine audio), and the yellow's short-pipe settings, which I kept (`pipeLength` 0.55, `recToneClosed` 9500).
- Fix, the same day: the first cut wrote one zero-length grain whose loudness came out as NaN, which isn't valid JSON, so the browser refused the whole bank and the kids' quad played the synth instead (that's what sounded very wrong). The slicer now skips zero-length cuts and refuses to write NaN, the bank is rebuilt (identical apart from that grain), and `tests/engineBanks.test.js` parses every bank strictly.
- Checked: unit tests (`tests/engineBanks.test.js`: every quad's bank is listed and on disk) and that the game loads headless without errors. Not checked: how it sounds in play (idle, revs, the blend point at 3,200 to 4,200 rpm, loudness against the other quads), or whether the pipe settings now suit a recording that's already close and clean.

## The dashes, planned (2026-10-06)

Brief for TODO 26 in `docs/briefs/dashes.md`, with SVG mockups in `docs/briefs/dashes/`. Settled with Jamie:

- Each quad's own dash goes close to the real machine it's based on: the red a Honda Foreman's speedo with hour meter and odometer, R / N / oil lamps and the key; the blue a Grizzly 660's grey-green LCD; the loan a newer Kodiak-style LCD; the green a Bayou's two lamps and key on its nacelle; the yellow a Blaster's single lamp on its headlamp; the orange bare race bars with a kill switch; the kids' quad a key. Most lose the rev counter, fuel and temperature gauges they have now.
- Jamie's idea to make up for it: two accessory pods, one older analogue, one new digital, each showing everything for whichever quad it's fitted to. Only one of each, swapped with the toolbox, and each starts every game in the toolbox or on a random quad. Not realistic (a meter wouldn't carry readings between quads), but more fun than bolting extra gauges onto every quad, which was the first plan for the red and dropped.
- Reference photos (eBay listings, not committed) were used for layouts only; the mockups are drawn from scratch.

## The dashes, built (2026-10-06)

Built to the brief above. What differs or wasn't said:

- Drawing: every dash and pod is one canvas face drawn in the mockups' own millimetre coordinates (`dashParts.js` scales the context so the code reads against the SVG), over a moulded housing. Faces redraw only when something they show changes, with the needle and bar values quantised for the gate. Pure parts are `dashState.js` (neutral, lamps, counters, usage, clock) and `pods.js` (where each pod is), both with tests.
- Hours and odometer: `hoursRange` and `odoRange` on each spec (the brief's suggested numbers; odometer about 2.5 times hours for the farm quads, 4 times for the sport ones). Hours run only while the engine does, at real speed. They use their own seeded stream (`seed ^ 0xd45b`), as do the clock (`0xc10c`) and the pod placement (`0x90d5`), so a seed keeps its old layout.
- Pod placement: the analogue is placed first among the toolbox and every quad, then the digital among the toolbox and the quads with no pod, so each lands anywhere equally likely.
- Odometer drums have four digits and tenths on the stock red and the pod, as in the mockups (the brief's text said five on the pod).
- Neutral is "ignition on, not in reverse, stopped, rpm below the clutch bite", so it lights while idling at a standstill, as the brief said.
- The pods sit on the dash's top (red, blue, loan, green), the crossbar pad (yellow, orange) or the bar clamp (kids), leaning back about 20 degrees whatever the dash's own tilt, and the kids' quad cancels its scale so the pod is full size. They stand above the dash, so they don't cover it, but they do cover a slice of the view ahead; I didn't try to lower them.
- Blaster: the real headlamp stays in the nose. Only the shell's back (lamp and key) is on the bars, so the yellow has two lamps' worth of shell; moving the lamp up was more modelling than the dash needed.
- The fuel tap's position (ON, RES, OFF) was on the old dashes and isn't on any now, which is as the brief wanted.
- No sound for fitting a pod (silent was allowed) and the girls don't remark on it yet.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless run (`.scratch/dashes/`) of every stock dash with the ignition on and each pod on the red, yellow and kids' quad, with no page errors. Looked at the close-ups and seat views. Not checked: the faces while moving (needle sweeps, lamp changes, the LCD line cycling), a pod being fitted through the toolbox panel by keyboard, or the dashes with the battery flat.

## Looking closely at the dash (2026-10-06)

- Hold `V` while riding at the controls to lean in: over 0.3 s your eyes move to 0.44 m in front of the middle of the dash (and the pod, if one's fitted, so both fit in view) and the field of view narrows from 75 to 42 degrees. Let go and it eases back. Turning to look back (`C`) wins over it, it doesn't work from the back seat, and the view is locked on the dash while it's held. `dashZoomFov`, `dashZoomDistance` and `dashZoomTime` are in the player params.
- On the kids' quad the distance isn't scaled down when a pod is fitted (the pod is full size) but is when it isn't (its dash is 0.65 scale).
- Checked once headless: red with the analogue pod (both dash and pod legible) and the kids' quad with its key switch. Not checked: the other quads, or the ride back out of the zoom at speed.

## Mostly sunny (2026-10-06)

- Weather weights are now sunny 0.70, sunny intervals 0.15, overcast 0.07, drizzle 0.03, rain 0.03, fog 0.02 (they were 0.2, 0.2, 0.25, 0.13, 0.12, 0.1). Showers within a game are unchanged; `?weather=` still forces a kind.
- The yellow's lamp shell was floating above the bars; it now sits down on the bar clamp (`DASH_PLACE.blaster`, checked once headless).


## Sound cutting out for good (2026-10-06)

Jamie: the sound sometimes stops altogether, perhaps only in Firefox, with nothing in the console.

- Cause: the worklet turns each grain's and clip's time into a sample index at the audio context's rate, usually 48 kHz on a Mac, while the banks are 44.1 kHz WAVs resampled on decoding. The engine-off clip ends exactly at the end of `engine-bank` and `engine-bank-2t`, and at 48 kHz its last read lands one past the decoded data when the browser rounds the length down (Chrome and Firefox round differently, hence "maybe Firefox"). That read is `undefined`, the sample becomes NaN, the NaN sticks in the worklet's tone filter, and once it reaches the master every sound in the game is silent for good. No exception, so nothing in the console.
- Fix: every grain and clip read is clamped inside the decoded data. And as a backstop, a worklet that ever outputs NaN resets its filters, pipe and voices, outputs silence for that sample and warns once in the console ("Engine sound went NaN"), so if anything else ever does it, the sound recovers and there's a clue.
- Tests in `tests/engineWorkletNaN.test.js` load the real worklet in a sandbox at 48 kHz: a bank a sample short plays its last clip to the end without a NaN, and a NaN forced into a filter is cleared. Both fail on the old worklet.
- Not checked in Firefox itself: I couldn't reproduce it there, so this is the most likely cause found by reading, not a confirmed one.


## Mechanical sympathy (TODO 1)

- Cold thrash: wear per minute gets `wearColdBoost` (6) times how cold it is (0 at `wearColdBelow` 0.5 of warm, 1 stone cold) times how far up the revs it is, on top of the existing work boost. Held cold and flat out in a test, it wears about 3.5 times as fast as the same revs warm. Warm running is unchanged.
- Who wore it: `src/engine/wearLedger.js` (pure) takes each quad's wear every frame and the rider (`you`, or a girl's id when she's driving, else nobody) and adds the increase to that rider's share. Wear a quad comes with, and idling with nobody on it, belongs to nobody. A fall in wear (a service or strip down) shrinks every share in proportion. A culprit needs `minBlamed` 0.04 of pinned wear and `blameShare` 0.5 of it.
- Speech: Amy tells off a girl thrashing a cold engine and tells you to go easy; Jess cheers you on (she doesn't tell herself off). Both need to be near or on the quad, with a `thrashChance` of 0.15 a second and the line cooldowns. Stood by a quad past `blameWearFrom` 0.15 wear, a girl names the culprit at `blameChance` 0.02 a second, once per quad every three minutes. Amy owns up when it's her; Jess owns up cheerfully. Lines in `thrashLines.js`.
- Not checked in the running game: how often Jess actually thrashes a cold engine on her own, or the lines in play. Tests cover the ledger, the wear rate and the lines being present.

## Rev-offs (TODO 2)

- `src/npc/revOff.js` (pure, tested). A blip is your throttle rising past `blipAt` 0.5 while you are on a running quad going under `maxYourSpeed` 2 m/s. Any girl within `hearRange` 16 m who is sat on a running quad with nobody at its controls may answer: Jess always (`jessReplyChance` 1), Amy `amyReplyChance` 0.2. She waits 0.3 to 0.8 s, then holds her quad's `helperThrottle` (the one the starting knack uses) for `blipHold` 0.35 s.
- Escalation: blipping again within `roundWindow` 7 s of her answer ending is another round. Round n is n blips (at most 4), each held `holdStep` 0.12 s longer (at most 1.1 s). Amy gives up after `amyMaxRound` 1 and says sorry. A blip while she is still answering is ignored; a quiet spell resets the rounds.
- Complaints: from `complainRound` 3, each answer has a `complainChance` 0.6 of being the last, at most one a minute. Amy tells you both off if she is within 40 m and not the one revving; otherwise the engine complains: stalls if under 0.5 warm or over 0.4 worn (Jess says so), else nothing more than the noise. The exchange then starts again.
- Hard revving is the existing engine model, so a cold or worn quad she keeps revving wears, and shows up in the wear tally from TODO 1 as hers.
- Lines in `revOffLines.js` (situations `revAnswer`, `revAnswerMore`, `revComplain`, `revStall`); tunables in the "Rev-offs" tuning folder.

## Parking brake and torque rock (TODO 18)

- Agreed with you: Space toggles; with it on, W revs the engine and the wheels don't move; the girls set it themselves; blips rock the quad only when it is standing.

A girl taking the controls releases it. Getting off the front seat she pulls it on with `parkBrakeChance` (Amy 1, Jess 0.5). A toast says so when you mount a quad with it on. A dash light was not added; the toast is the only sign.
- `src/vibration/torqueRock.js` (pure, tested) spots a blip: the throttle rising by `torqueMinRise` 0.4 within `torqueWindow` 0.15 s, once until it falls back. The kick (`torqueGain` 1.2 rad/s, always the one way) goes to a new slow oscillator in the vibration model (3.2 Hz, damping 0.25) that adds to the body's roll (about 3 degrees at full) and to your head's (`torqueCamera` 0.4). It only fires with the engine running, the quad slower than 0.5 m/s, and the brake on or nobody at the controls, so pulling away never rocks it. A girl sat on it moves with the body, so she wobbles too.
- Jess's prank: a free girl with `rockPrankChance` (Jess 0.02 a second, Amy 0) within `rockPrankRange` 3.5 m of a quad her friend is sat on, running, holds its throttle for 0.4 s (so the same rock fires), at most every two minutes. Jess says `rockPrank`, Amy `rockPrankAmy`. No new state.

## Nicknames, whose quad, and hearing one (TODO 9, 10, 11)

- Recorded lines can't take a name at runtime, so every line that names a quad is written out once for the colour and once for each possible nickname (`expandQuadLines` in `quadNames.js`), keyed `kind:spec:nickname`, and the game says the one for this game's names. Two nicknames per quad (seven quads), picked per game from a seeded stream. That is a lot of lines to record (about 400 across both voices) but they are cached and the unused ones cost nothing in play.

Amy answers `nameAmy` ("I am not calling it that"). `QuadNames.refer` says what a girl calls a quad when she mentions it: Jess uses the name once it's been said; Amy keeps to the colour for `amyHolds` 4 mentions that she actually said, then the next one slips (`voice.slipped`) and Jess says `nameCaved` a couple of seconds later. After that Amy uses names too. A mention that wasn't said (cooldown, chance) doesn't count.

Objecting: someone else has been at the controls of her quad for `upsetDelay` 1.5 s and she is within `noticeRange` 35 m, at most every `upsetCooldown` 80 s; lines differ for you, Jess and Amy (`ownerUpset_you` / `_jess` / `_amy`). Only the driver counts, not somebody on the back.

Its sound is `rough` if it is cold (under 0.45) or worn (over 0.4), else `hard` over 7 m/s or 0.8 throttle, else `steady`. Out of sight is judged by distance only, not by what is in the way. She doesn't place you riding: you would not be near enough to hear her say it.

## Warming their hands (TODO 5)

- A cold day is weather warmth under `coldBelow` 0.5 (overcast 0.48, fog 0.23, drizzle 0.19, rain 0.05 on the existing scale; sunny intervals 0.73 and sunny 1 aren't) or rain over `wetAbove` 0.25. After `startAfter` 20 s, every 2 s a free girl with a quad that's running, unattended and not moving within `warmRange` 16 m goes to it with `warmChance` 0.2, or 0.7 if her friend is already there. At most two at one engine, one each side. She stays `warmForMin` 25 to `warmForMax` 70 s, then doesn't think of it again for a minute.

Boredom doesn't build up while she's at it. A rig poser puts both hands over the engine with the same arm IK as the grips.

One at a time, at least `gap` 40 s after the last. Amy does it `amyMax` 1 time a game; Jess has no limit.

If she gets off, or you stop riding, it ends at once. It is added to the quad as `quad.handThrottle` and taken as the larger of that and your own throttle, so your W is still there if you want more; it's ignored while you're braking or reversing.
