## Smoke and smell (TODO 7)

- "Orange or yellow" is any two-stroke (`engine.params.strokes === 2`), which is just those two. How much a two-stroke smokes (`smokeLevel`, 0..1): 0.35 running, plus 0.5 of the throttle, plus up to 0.3 when cold; she reacts at 0.3 or more, so any running two-stroke counts.
- The plume (`inPlume`): from the pipe, 1.1 m behind the quad's middle, downwind for `plumeLength` 9 m and `plumeWidth` 1.3 m across at the pipe, widening 0.22 m per metre. Wind is the smoke's own (`windX`, `windZ`, which the weather already scales); under `calmWind` 0.35 m/s the smoke hangs straight behind the quad instead.
- A free girl (stood about) in it for `reactAfter` 1.2 s reacts: Amy coughs, waves her right hand across her face for 2.6 s (a rig poser, `companionWaving.js`) and says `smokeCough`, at most every 30 s. Jess says `smokeLove`, at most every 50 s. A girl on a quad or walking doesn't react.
- Following you (`followSmokeGap`, a multiple of `followGap` 8 m): Amy 1.8, so about 14 m behind a two-stroke; Jess 0.7, about 5.6 m. Each says `followSmoke` once per drive when she first notices she is behind one. It also shifts the point at which she pulls up when you stop.
- Not checked in the running game: how the wave looks, whether a plume 9 m long matches what you see in the smoke particles, or whether Amy's 14 m gap loses you on a fast ride. Unit tests cover the plume shape, the cough and the gap.

## Clogged air filter (TODO 8)

- `AirFilter` (`src/vehicle/airFilter.js`, one per quad, pure) has `clog` 0..1. While the engine runs and the quad moves faster than `minSpeed` 1.5 m/s it grows by `mudRate` 0.0024 a second times the worst mud under any wheel plus `puddleRate` 0.0033 times the deepest puddle. That is about seven minutes from clean to choked in full mud all the time, which is not what a typical ride is: ordinary riding on this farm clogs it by a few per cent in a ride. The numbers are a guess at "slowly"; they are in the tuning panel.
- The engine takes `clog` as a control. It adds `clogRich` 0.3 to the mixture (the existing path, so everything that already follows from a rich mixture follows: fouling, hunting, power loss, and darker smoke, which the smoke reads from the mixture), and takes up to `clogPowerLoss` 0.3 of power, scaled by the square of how far up the revs it is, so idle is almost untouched. Stock richTolerance is 0.25, so a fully choked filter is just past it on a stock-jetted quad: it smokes and slowly fouls but is not undriveable. A lean-jetted quad is hurt less by the richness and a rich-jetted one more.
- Cleaning (the workshop panel): hold `I` (or the button) for `cleanSeconds` 3; let go and the hold starts again. It only takes the time if the filter has something in it. A service doesn't clean it, on purpose: it is a separate job.
- Not done: nothing the girls say about the filter, and nothing on the dash. The toolbox panel is the only place it shows, apart from how the quad runs.
- Not checked in the running game: how quickly it clogs in a real ride, how the hold feels with the mouse button, or how much darker the smoke is. Unit tests cover the clogging rates, the cleaning, and the rich and power effects.

## Racing (TODO 3)

- Agreed with you: use the mud track, with non-collision cones; feed the rivalry and track wins and losses.
- `src/npc/race.js` (pure, tested) runs the race; `HerDrive` has a race mode. After `startAfter` 120 s, with both girls free for `freeFor` 20 s, `chance` 0.015 a second (at most one race per `cooldown` 300 s): Jess dares Amy 0.7 of the time. Each girl's quad is her claimed one if it's free (`pickQuads`), else any she may ride that is parked and unattended. Neither can be the one you're on.
- Each girl is sent to drive her quad with a race: `route` (the grid point, then one lap round the track and back to the grid point, which is the finish), a cruise speed (`cruiseBase` 9 + `cruiseConfidence` 4 times her driving confidence, plus or minus 1.5, so Jess is usually quicker; the quad's power does the rest), full throttle allowed and no fright speed or pauses. She starts it as usual, drives to the grid and stops; when both are there it counts down `countdown` 3 s (`raceCount`), then she goes. First over the line wins; the other has `raceLose` after a pause. Out of it (a quad that stalls out, a bog that holds it, you get on) is a loss; `raceTimeout` 150 s after the start ends it for anyone still going; `gatherTimeout` 150 s without both on the grid cancels it (they stop where they are).
- The route (`planLap`): a waypoint every `routeSpacing` 6 m round the track, each girl 0.9 m to her side of the middle so they don't share a line. Every bog and puddle is avoided sideways, on whichever side she's on, to its radius plus `hazardMargin` 1.4 m (puddles count as 1.5 m wider than they are). That is needed: a puddle at 12 m/s drowns the engine (checked: in the first headless runs both girls stalled at the puddle by the yard, each with water in the engine, and neither could restart in time). The waypoint arrival radius is also much tighter in a race (0.2 of her speed in metres, not 0.5), or she cuts the corner round a puddle.
- Score: wins and losses by girl are kept in `race.tally` for the game. `race.rivalry()` is the one ahead by `rivalLead` 2 wins or more. After a race there is a banter exchange for the winner and loser (`raced`, six, three for each result), and while one is ahead and both are stood about close together, a `rivals` exchange now and then (four). Not tied into the favour system.
- The cones are four, either side of the track at the point nearest the yard (`src/world/startLine.js`), no collider.
- Checked headless (five short runs, forced to start at once): they gather and grid up, the count-down runs, both leave the line together and Jess ran a whole lap in about 75 s and pulled up over the line. The puddle problem above was found and fixed that way. Not seen: Amy finishing (the run was cut at 150 s), the result and win lines in play, the banter after, the cones, or a race with the green or another stubborn starter. A girl who can't start the quad she picked cancels the race; that is common with a cold green and with novices (two of the five runs), which I haven't tried to cure.

## Racing them (TODO 1)

- Agreed with you: three ways in (Jess dares you, `Enter` at the line, or they invite you when they start one), the girls stage so they face the right way, you are judged on checkpoints, the tally gets a third name, no tie to the favour system.
- You are a racer called `you` in `Race` (`src/npc/race.js`, pure, tested). `update` takes `you: { riding, near, still }`. Near is within `nearLine` 45 m of the line; a dare needs you sat still, engine running, for `dareStill` 4 s, then `dareChance` 0.05 a second when the girls are free and the usual timing is up; Jess is the one who dares. An ordinary girls' race invites you if you are riding and near. Your quad is `picks.you`; the girls are never given it.
- Grid: you in the middle, the girls `gridSpacing` 3 m either side (1.5 as before when you are not in). You count as lined up (`youOnGrid`) within `youGridRadius` 5 m of your grid point, stopped and within `youGridYaw` 0.7 rad of the track's direction; once lined up you may creep up to 2 m/s on a slope. If you haven't lined up in `youWait` 30 s, get off, or pull off the line in the count-down, you are dropped (`youDropped`) and they race without you rather than calling it off.
- `YouRun` judges your lap: the lap is `youCheckpoints` 4 bands in order, so the only way to finish is to go round (cutting across or going backwards gets you nowhere), and the finish is within `youFinishRadius` 8 m of the line after the last band. Off the quad or stalled for `youOutAfter` 15 s and you are out. The wins and losses use the same tally; `rivalry()` ignores `you`, so the score they go on about is still Amy against Jess.
- Facing the right way: the girls' route now starts with a staging point 14 m behind their grid point on the track (`RACE_STAGE_BACK`), so they come at the grid along the track. If one still arrives more than `raceGridYaw` 0.6 rad off the track's direction (in the Companion folder) she goes back to the staging point and tries again, twice at most, then accepts it.
- Lines: `raceInviteYou` and `raceJoinYou` (the inviter, then the other), `raceBeatYou` (the winner, when you were in), `raceLoseToYou` (a girl who finishes behind you). When you win, the girls' own post-race banter is skipped.
- Checked: unit tests, and a headless load with a forced girls' race (they pick quads and the route builds, no errors). Not checked in play: a race with you on the grid, the toasts, whether the staging point sits clear of everything, or the new lines in the girls' voices (recorded in the background).

## Debug panel for the girls, and fewer stuck cables (after TODO 3 to 11)

- Jamie: the throttle cable was sticking too often. `cableEvery` (average running seconds per cable fault) goes from 2700 to 7200. The other faults are unchanged.
- A "Girls (live)" folder in the tuning panel: each girl's claimed quad, each quad's owner and nickname (and whether Jess has said it yet), the race phase and score, and the throttle-grab state. Buttons: start a race now (it begins as soon as both girls are stood about and two quads are free), make Jess or Amy grab the throttle on your next ride (you must be moving with her on the back), and have Jess name the quads now.
- Jamie hadn't seen the hand on the throttle: it needed 25 s of riding with a girl on the back, then 3% a second for Jess (0.75% for Amy), and only up to 11 m/s. The speed limit goes to 16 m/s; the rest is unchanged.
- Jamie: she should hold the throttle a lot longer. The hold goes from 1.2 to 2.4 s up to `holdMin` 5 to `holdMax` 10 s (ranges widened to 20 and 30 s). The rest of the grab is unchanged, so with `maxSpeed` 16 a long hold can take you well past your usual pace; it ends early if she gets off or you stop riding.

## Faults differ per quad

- Jamie: they all need to be random. `Faults.roll(random)` deals each quad, once a game, a multiplier for each of the four faults on how often it starts and another on how fast it worsens, each log-uniform between 1/`proneSpread` and `proneSpread` (2.5, so 0.4 to 2.5 times the default rates). That is eight independent rolls per quad. The stream is seeded from the layout seed and the quad's place in the fleet, so the same `?seed=` deals the same luck; the fault dice themselves use that stream too, but what happens still depends on how you ride, so a seed does not repeat the faults.
- Not tied to the spec (an old quad isn't more prone than a new one): that belongs with TODO 38 and 39. Faults that depend on riding (belt needs hard riding, puncture needs moving) still need it, so a quad nobody rides stays fault-free however prone it is.
- The averages across the fleet move a little: a log-uniform from 0.4 to 2.5 has a mean of about 1.27, so faults are somewhat more common overall than before, which partly undoes the longer cable interval. Not checked in play.

## A girl driving that keeps reversing

- Jamie: sometimes one of them driving gets stuck going backwards all the time. Not reproduced, so this is the loop found by reading `herDriving.js`, not a confirmed cause: she meets something in front of her (or the `stuck` check finds it within 3.5 m), reverses for 1.5 to 2.5 s, pauses, drives, meets the same thing again, and so on for ever. Each reversal also skipped a waypoint, so even a new loop was planned from the same blocked spot facing it.
- Fix: `backOff` counts backing off in the same spot (within `backOffRadius` 6 m and `backOffWithin` 25 s of the last time). The second time she throws away her waypoints, so the next plan tries other headings and picks one that is clear; the fifth (`backOffsToGiveUp`) she says there's no room (`noRoom`, the existing line), stops and gets off. Moving elsewhere or waiting longer starts the count again. In a race the route is kept and only the give-up applies; following you, the point to aim at is recalculated anyway. All three are in the Companion folder.
- If it still happens, the next suspect is the quad's reverse flag, which only flips when it's moving slower than 0.3 m/s, so a quad rolling back on a slope can stay in reverse while she gives it forward throttle. I didn't change that.

## Drag race (TODO 2)

- Agreed with you: a flat gravel strip for now (tarmac comes with the bigger field); you against one girl; `Enter` at the start line to ask; Jess's challenge after you have sat still on a quad for 10 s; the lap race's behaviour left as it was; Jess and Amy both have lines. Then you asked for it rougher: ragged edges and ends that thin into the grass, and only cones at the start and finish (no banner, no painted lines).
- The strip (`layout.dragStrip`, `world/dragStrip.js`): 56 m from start to finish line, 8 m of run-off, 10 m wide, heading a little south of east. That is as long as the room allows: the track, the bog at the north-east, the bales near (12, 14) and the jump's run-up and landing leave about 56 m clear in the field. A test checks it is clear of the track (by more than the dish's reach), bogs, puddles, bales, the ramp and the hedges, and level. The ground under it is levelled (`terrainHeight.js`) to the average of the ground along it, with a 6 m shoulder. Not done: gravel grip or squeal (the surface is a mesh over the grass), which belongs with the tarmac in the bigger field.
- `src/npc/drag.js` (pure, tested) runs it, separate from `Race` so the lap race is untouched. It shares `pickQuads`, and the girl's drive is the lap race's: HerDrive's race mode with a three-point route (staging point 10 m behind the line, her grid point, a point past the finish), plus two small additions: `blip()` on the race (she holds the brake and opens the throttle on the spot) and `reroute(race)` (Jess is already on her quad beside you, and is sent to the strip without getting off).
- The challenge: you sit still on a quad with the engine running for `blipStill` 10 s (after `startAfter` 90 s, `cooldown` 240 s, both girls free, no lap race on); then `blipChance` 0.03 a second Jess drives to 3.2 m beside you (`approachTimeout` 40 s, forgotten if you get off or ride off). She waits `blipPause` 1.2 s, then blips twice. Your answer is your throttle going from under 0.15 to over 0.6 while still; a throttle already open when she arrived doesn't count. You have `blipWait` 8 s after her last blip, then she calls you chicken and it's off.
- The race: she takes the lane you are not nearer to (2 m either side of the middle); you are lined up within `gridRadius` 2.5 m of your point, stopped and within `gridYaw` 0.5 rad of the strip. Count-down 3 s. Rolling more than `falseStartAlong` 1 m over the line is a false start: she says so, and you line up again (`falseStarts` 2 forgiven, the next calls it off). Pulling off the grid some other way calls it off. Finish is along the strip reaching its length, for both of you (not her arrival at a waypoint, so it's fair). A stalled or abandoned quad is out for `youOutAfter` 8 s; `raceTimeout` 45 s. Wins and losses go in `drag.tally`, not the lap race's score.
- The gravel mesh sits 4 to 7 cm above the ground everywhere (its edges used to dip below the visual terrain, which is coarser than `heightAt`, so they shimmered as you moved), and no grass tufts are placed within 3 m of the surface (`props.js`). The ragged edges are fixed at build time from a seeded generator.
- Jamie: the girls still struggled to reach the line and face along it. Both races now ease a girl who has arrived and is holding on the grid onto her exact point and round to face along the track or strip, at 2.5 times a second's worth of the gap (`Game.helpOnGrid`), instead of sending her back to the staging point to come at it again (the `gridYaw` retry is not used any more; the races pass `assistYaw`). Only within 5 m of the point and below 1.5 m/s, and only until the start. Not checked in play: how the nudge looks, or whether a quad nosing a hedge gets pulled through it.
- Jamie: they needn't get off when it's over. Each girl, in both the lap race and the drag race, rolls `keepRidingChance` 0.5 (Racing folder) when her race is set up. If she keeps riding, crossing the finish drops her back to her ordinary driving (`HerDrive.carryOn`): her usual throttle limit, nerves and pauses, a drive of the usual length from then, and the usual dismount at the end. Otherwise she pulls up and gets off as before. Not checked in play.
- Carrying on after the other things, too (Jamie, after the above): the same idea for doughnuts, the ramp, showing off (unless there was no room) and the end of follow the leader. When one finishes (a jump that ended in a bail still means she gets off, scared) each girl rolls `keepRidingAfter` 0.3 (Companion folder), lower than the races' 0.5 because carrying on straight after a doughnut is less natural; if she wins the roll she goes back to ordinary driving (`HerDrive.endActivity`, `carryOn`). `carryOn` restores the throttle limit, nerves, pause chance, drive length and loop size she had before the activity changed them, and drops the doughnut, ramp, show-off and follow state. A convoy that is broken up (someone got on a quad, one stopped doing anything) still just stops. Not checked in play.
- She drives flat out with the quad's power deciding the result, so the quad you pick matters. Not tied to the favour system.
- Checked: unit tests, and a headless load with no errors. Not checked in play: the challenge and the race end to end, how the strip looks, whether her blip revs the engine on the brake with every quad, whether the staging point sits clear of things, or the new lines in the girls' voices (recorded in the background).

## Race hardening, and calling the girls back

- Jamie: the race gather stage was slow and they mostly drove round the track looking for the start line. Cause found by reading: backing off something (the other racer on the grid 1.8 m away, a hedge on the way) skipped the waypoint she was heading for, and the start line was waypoint 0, so she carried on round the whole lap without ever lining up until the race was called off. Now in a race she has another go at the same waypoint after backing off (and never counts herself past the grid before the start); the grid point counts from 3.5 m (`raceGridRadius`, was 2.2); the two quads sit 3 m apart (+/-1.5 m, not 1.8); a racer who has stopped doing anything about it for 4 s is out, which calls the race off; and the gather timeout is 100 s, not 150. Tried first, and dropped: stacking the second quad 6 m behind the first, which put it right in the first one's way, and ignoring the other racer in the obstacle probe, which had Amy pushing into Jess.
- Checked headless: the line-up and the call-back below. Not seen again: a whole race from the new grid. That run ended with both on the grid in time but I did not run it on to the finish.
- Call the girls back (N, or "Call the girls back" in the tuning panel's Girls folder): any race is called off, and each girl is told to stop what she's doing and stand in front of the barn doors, one either side of the middle (`recallSpot`). Driving, she pulls up and gets off first; sat on a quad or the bales, she gets down first; mid-change she's nudged again every 0.7 s until she is there (within 2.5 m and stood), or two minutes have gone. Races don't start while it is going on. They say `recalled`. Once they're there they carry on as usual (wander, get bored, and so on); this is not a hold. Checked headless: both were at the doors in about 20 s.

## Reading the dash (TODO 12)

- Amy reads in good time: fuel the moment the lamp comes on, hot the moment the hot lamp does, cold when the engine is under `coldBelow` 0.4 and you have been faster than `coldPace` 5 m/s or on more than 0.6 throttle for 2 s. Jess is late: fuel only at `fuelLateLevel` 0.04 of a tank, hot after the lamp has been on `hotLateFor` 6 s, and cold only once the engine has warmed past 0.65 after you had gone hard while cold for 6 s ("you thrashed it").
- Each reading is once per spell (it can be said again after the lamp goes off, or the engine warms) and per girl, with `gap` 45 s between readings by the same girl. Only the girl on the pillion seat, with you at the controls and the engine running. The dash lamps come from `quad.lamps`, kept up to date with the dash itself.

## Follow the leader (TODO 14)

- `src/npc/convoy.js` (pure, tested). Gating like the race: after `startAfter` 150 s, both free for `freeFor` 20 s, `chance` 0.012 a second, `cooldown` 420 s, and not while a race is on or they've been called back. Jess leads 0.6 of the time. Quads picked as in the race (`pickQuads`).
- The leader drives her usual loops, bigger (`rideLoopScale`) and without tiring (`driveFor` 1e6). The follower is an ordinary follower (the same HerDrive follow as for you: gap, smoke gap, catch-up) whose target is the leader's quad (`followWho`), so she waits for the leader to set off. When both are going it is "running".
- After `swapMin` 60 to `swapMax` 120 s the follower is told to overtake (the pass from TODO 13, `startPass`); when she is ahead and waiting, the roles flip: the old leader's follow target becomes the new leader's quad and the new leader drops her follow and plans her own loops. A pass that doesn't get there within 45 s is given up and they carry on in the same order. After `swaps` 2 swaps and a `lastLeg` 60 s they stop (`drive.stop('bored')`) and get off.
- If either drive ends, someone gets on one of the quads, or one stands about doing nothing for 4 s, the whole convoy ends and the other stops. Calling them back (N) ends it too. A convoy that hasn't got both going within `formTimeout` 120 s is called off.
- The swap is a banter exchange (`swapped`, four, two for each order), and each girl has an offer and an answer line.
- Checked headless (one run, with the timers shortened): forming, both driving, the follower overtaking, the swap, and the stop at the end all happened; Jess backed off obstacles several times in the field. Not seen: the banter, or a full-length convoy.

## The green's knack off, and a starter sound that never stopped

- Jamie: remove the green's need for the throttle to be blipped; it stopped the girls starting it. `knackBlips` is 0 on the green. The mechanism (`knack.js`, the girls knowing the trick, the struggle line) is left in, ready to put back as a different quad's quirk (TODO 39).
- Jamie: when the girls give up starting a quad it keeps making a slow start sound for ever. I couldn't reproduce it headless (a weak-battery start just clicked and switched off). The sound is the starter loop in the engine worklet, which only stopped on a `starterOff` event; if that event is missed the loop winds down to its slowest rate and carries on. It now also fades out once the engine has not been cranking for a quarter of a second, whatever events arrive. This is the likely cause, not a confirmed one.

## Red diesel (TODO 1)

- Agreed with you: a third jerry can (dark green) filled at the bowser and poured like the others; the prompts say what's in it, so the mistake is avoidable if you look; a quad that has had it runs worse, smokes grey, stalls and won't restart until drained at the toolbox; Jess sometimes makes the mistake and Amy tells her off, and Amy never does; the bowser stands next to the petrol tank.
- Diesel is a fraction of the tank and the float bowl, mixed by volume like the oil (`FuelSystem.diesel`, `bowlDiesel`; a can has `diesel` too, and the bowser fills at 1). The engine takes it up as `dieselSoak` (`src/engine/diesel.js`, tested): it climbs toward `diesel * dieselPotency` 2.5 at `dieselSoakRate` 0.04 a second while running only (not cranking or standing: it used to climb while cranking, which looked like it getting worse with the engine off), and never falls by itself. That is how a petrol top-up doesn't cure it, and why it gets worse over time rather than all at once. The numbers: 10% diesel soaks to 0.25 (rough), 20% or more to 0.5 and up (stalls). Full soak: roughness +0.8, power -60%, misfire chance +0.5 a firing. It catches less from 0.15 and not at all from `dieselNoStart` 0.45; it may stall from `dieselStallAt` 0.5 (10% a firing), which is past the no-start point on purpose, so a stalled one never restarts. The smoke gets thicker (`dieselDensity` 0.9) and goes mid grey (`dieselGray` 0.5).
- The drain: `P` in the toolbox panel, instant, for a quad that has any diesel in the tank, bowl or engine. It empties the tank and bowl (the fuel is lost, so you have to refuel it) and clears the soak. Instant rather than a hold like the filter, since it isn't fiddly in real life.
- Jess's mistake (`src/npc/dieselMistake.js`, tested, same shape as the tea): after `startAfter` 240 s a free Jess has `chance` 0.004 a second (`cooldown` 600 s) to pick a parked, unattended quad within 35 m that is under 60% full and has room for 3 L, lowest first. She walks to the back of it, pours 3 L of diesel over `pourSeconds` 6 (the `pranking` state now takes a `stay`, 2.6 s as before for pranks) and carries on; if the quad is taken or she walks off the pour stops. Amy within 18 m says `dieselCaught` 85% of the time and Jess answers. Nobody stops her: you only find out when it runs badly.
- Lines (`dieselLines.js`): Amy warns when you pour it, says what's wrong when a quad runs badly or dies on it, and says to drain it if you keep cranking; Jess blames you. Eleven situations, about thirty lines, recorded. Amy has no `dieselPour` or `dieselCaughtReply`; the characters test lists them as Jess-only.
- The bowser is the petrol tank's model, 0.8 scale, darker, with a red label, and a collider scaled the same. It stands at (-61, 45.6), the can by it. Pouring diesel into a can that holds petrol (or the reverse) mixes them, so a half-filled can is a half-wrong one.
- Checked headless: the game loads without errors, the bowser and can show by the petrol tank, and with every quad low and the debug flag on, Jess went to the green, poured, and left it at 0.6 diesel (2 L of petrol plus 3 L). Found along the way: my first soak function had the same name as one in `heat.js` and silently replaced it in the engine, which would have broken the heat soak; renamed `stepDieselSoak`. Not checked: a quad running on diesel in play (the unit tests cover the engine), the smoke colour by eye, the girls' lines in play, or Amy's catch line. Not done: the tractor itself (TODO 6) and its use of the bowser; until then the bowser only feeds the can.

## Talking to the quad (TODO 2)

- Three new situations about a quad, written out for the colour and each nickname like the claims (`quadLines.js`): `startPat` and `startCheer` (Jess only) and `startSorry` (Amy only). Jess says `startPat` on her first crank of an attempt (70%) and `startCheer` when it catches (70%, else the old `startCaught`). Amy says `startSorry` (80%) when `StartingAttempt` raises the new `flooded` event. Both go through `sayQuad`, so Jess uses the nickname once it has been given and Amy holds to the colour until she slips.
- Flooded means plug fouling over `floodedFouling` 0.4 or richness over `floodedRich` 0.2 (the same thresholds the crank hint uses), judged after a failed go, once per attempt. Those two are in `DEFAULT_STARTING_PARAMS`; that set has no tuning ranges, so they are not in the panel.
- Not done: the pat. Jess says it but there is no tank-patting animation. Not checked in the running game: how the lines sound in play, or how often a girl floods one. The game loads without errors; unit tests cover the event and that every line exists for every quad and nickname.

## A head start on your usual quad (TODO 3)

- Your usual quad is the one you've spent most seconds at the controls of, once that's at least `usualAfter` 30 s (`src/npc/headStart.js`, pure, tested). It only counts you riding, not sitting on the back.
- The trigger is you on foot, on a cold day (the same test as warming their hands), that quad off and unattended, you between `minRange` 5 m and `noticeRange` 24 m of it and closing at over `closeSpeed` 0.8 m/s (smoothed). It is rolled once per approach and again only after you've walked off beyond 1.5 times the notice range. Nothing before `startAfter` 90 s.
- Favoured girl (the existing mood from favour.js): if she's free, within `girlRange` 40 m and no further from the quad than you are (plus 2 m), she goes with an 85% chance and runs the ordinary starting routine, so she can flood it or give up like any other go. Neglected girl: with a 70% chance she starts her own quad (the one she claimed) instead, if that's off and free, and says so pointedly. Girls who are even with you do nothing. Each girl waits `cooldown` 240 s before doing either again.
- Lines: `headStartGo` and `snubGo` for each girl (`headStartLines.js`). When she gets it running, the existing `startWarm` line is her handing it over.
- Not checked in the running game: how often it fires in a real game or how the lines sound in play. Checked once headless with the approach faked: a favoured Amy walked to the usual quad and had it running.

- It starts when both girls are free, within `together` 7 m of each other, you are on foot, and you have been between `hearMin` 4.5 m and `hearMax` 14 m of the nearer one for `settle` 2 s without being noticed (chance 0.4 per second after that, nothing before 60 s into the game, then `cooldown` 150 s). All in `DEFAULT_GOSSIP_PARAMS`, in the panel as "Talking about you".

## Shirts the colour of her quad (TODO 5)

- A girl's favourite is the quad the ownership deal gives her at the start of a game (`ownership.assign`), so the shirt can't be fixed in `characters.js`. The game recolours it right after the deal, using the spec's `bodyColor` as it is, collar included.
- `outfit.js` keeps the untouched texture on the material (`userData.baseMap`) and `reshirt` recolours from that, then refits the garments, so colouring twice never compounds. `Companion.setShirt` keeps `this.outfit` current so a figure change refits the right colour.
- Checked once headless at seed 3: Amy on green and Jess on red, shirt hex matching `bodyColor`, no console errors. Not looked at by eye in the game, so how the yellow and silver-ish shades read on the model is untested.

## Jumpers from the barn (TODO 6)

- Pure logic in `src/npc/jumpers.js` (tests in `tests/jumpers.test.js`), driven from `Game.updateJumpers` the way tea is: it says who is to fetch or hang one, the game walks her to the rail with `sneakTo` and does the dressing when she gets there. "Cold" is the same test as warming her hands (`weather.warmth < 0.5` or rain over 0.25), but she has to have felt it for 25 s first, so a passing shower doesn't have her trotting to the barn, and 60 s of warm, dry weather before it comes off, so one doesn't flap on and off. A girl who's busy waits until she's stood about.
- She walks back to the rail to hang it up rather than dropping it, so the rail always shows what's really left. The rack (`src/world/jumperRail.js`, `layout.jumperRail`) is a freestanding clothes rack by the barn's south wall, clear of the bench, the ladder and the bay: a bar along the wall on two uprights, the jumpers on hangers edge-on to the wall. A girl stands at its east end. The debug panel's two jumper buttons (`Jumpers.toggle`) send a girl for one, or back to hang it up, whatever the weather; a jumper asked for that way stays on until asked to come off.
- The jumper is a `skinnedGarment` like the shorts: torso and arms of her own mesh, pushed 3 cm out (2.4 cm on the sleeves, so it's oversized), a hem 11 cm below the shorts' waistband. It stops at the wrist. The neck is round and low enough only to show the polo's collar and the top button: 6 cm below the neckline front and back, within 7.5 cm of the middle of her neck; shoulders and sleeves are covered right up (earlier goes at 17 cm and 10 cm were far too big). The body mesh is flat shaded, so its corner vertices each carry a different normal; at 3 cm the faces pulled apart into slits showing the polo through. `skinnedGarment` takes `{ smooth: true }`, which pushes each vertex along the average normal of everything at the same spot. Only the jumper uses it, so the shorts and collar haven't moved.
- The polo collar and placket stay on under it. `withJumper` adds or removes the colour on the outfit, so she goes back to exactly what she had, and `reshirt` and a figure change refit with the jumper still on.
- Colours are dull on purpose: grey `#7b7d80` and beige `#b7a98c`. Two lines each, `jumperOn` and `jumperOff`, in `jumperLines.js`: Jess says it smells of the barn, Amy says she looks like a sack.
- Checked once headless: both girls in jumpers close up, the rack from inside the barn, and in rain a girl walking to the rail and putting the grey one on with no console errors. Not checked: a jumper on a girl who's sitting on a quad or driving (the sleeves follow the same skeleton as the shirt, but I haven't looked at the arm poses), and the full hang-up trip back.
- Girls walk in a straight line, so going to the rack from outside went through the barn wall. `Companion.sneakTo` now takes `via` (waypoints, here two points leading in through the big doors) and `out` (a point to walk to afterwards, back outside). Checked once headless: Jess from the yard went in by the doorway, dressed, and came back out by it.

One go per getting off, `cooldown` 360 s per girl, nothing before 120 s. It only counts a quad you have been riding, so a quad that was never yours, or one you switched off, is safe.

When it ends, `endDrive` doesn't dismount: it starts a second drive in HerDrive's `park` mode along the route back to the pose the quad had when you left it (the same point in line, then the spot), at `returnSpeed` 4 m/s, and she gets off there. She ends up within about 2 m of where it stood, not exactly on it.

## Stuck throttle when you get off revving

- Getting off a quad that is standing still (under the speed that makes you jump) with the throttle at `revHoldMin` 0.15 or more now leaves it revving at that throttle: `Quad.holdRev`, using the same ghost state as the runaway, so the throttle holds until it stalls or tips, you get on again or something switches it off. It uses the throttle value you had when you got off, so a blip of 0.6 stays at 0.6.
- It will pull away if it has gears to take it, like a stuck cable would. The girls treat it as a runaway because it is in the ghost state; I didn't look at what they make of it in play.
- Checked once headless by calling `holdRev(0.6)` on a running quad: throttle stayed 0.6 and rpm about 3,800 over three seconds. Not checked: getting off with the real key, or how the girls react.
- The parking brake stays on if it was on: the wheel brake only held for a rider's input before, so a stuck-throttle quad with the brake set crept off; it now holds for the ghost state too. Checked once headless: brake on, throttle held at 0.6, rpm about 2,600, moved 0 m in 12 s. Rev held without the brake still pulls away.

## The tractor (TODO 8)

Settled with Jamie before building: the item as written, in one go (the trailer, the girls driving it, neutral lights and gears jumping out, and creeping are 9, 10, 12, 14, 17 and 18); built on the quad physics as a new spec so it looks like a proper old tractor but reuses the vehicle code; the controls below; kyles's recordings for the sound; a few lines from the girls. Sitting on the back or the wheel arch is 11, so the girls don't ride it yet.

- **It's a unit, but not one of the girls' units.** `TRACTOR_SPEC` (`quadSpecs.js`) is built by `QuadUnit` (the spec's `tractor: true` picks the tractor's body, wheel layout and drivetrain). `game.units` has it, so you can ride, fuel, hear and wash it; `game.girlUnits` is the quads only, and every place the girls look at quads (driving, nicking, tea, warming, ownership, names, faults, the wear tally, the bench, the debug buttons for them) uses that. It isn't in the fleet pick, so it's there every game, parked in the yard at `LAYOUT.tractor` (-48.5, 70.5), with its own seeded idle, roughness, fuel and hours (`seed ^ 0x7ac70`). It gets no faults and no pods. The toolbox says it's too much for it; Jess's diesel mistake and the quads' petrol can't reach it.
- **Physics.** `QuadPhysics` takes a layout (wheels with their own radius and mount height, the chassis box), so the tractor has a 1.9 m wheelbase, 0.72 m rear and 0.42 m front wheels, rear wheel drive, 2,000 kg, 38 degrees of lock, and brakes about seven times the quads'. `Quad` takes a ground offset (0.8 m for the tractor, 0.36 for the quads) in place of the constant. No tip-over handling beyond the quads' `T`. The model (`tractorModel.js`, procedural like the quads, with a proxy box twice the size) has a bonnet, grille and lamps, a pan seat between the mudguards, a raked steering column, a drawbar, and the controls it animates: gear lever in its gate, hand throttle, clutch pedal, stop knob.
- **Starting.** A diesel with `compressionIgnition` (`engine/compression.js`, tested): no mixture or choke (`coldEnrichmentNeed` 0), and the catch chance depends on the glow plugs. `K` turns the key on and heats them at 1/`glowSeconds` (7 s) a second; the GLOW lamp lights once they're hot enough for the engine's temperature, which is at once above `glowWarmAbove` (0.6) and after the full seven seconds stone cold. Cranked on cold plugs it keeps `glowColdCatch` 4% of its chance (rising as they heat); once ready it catches within a second or two (`catchChance` 0.85). The plugs cool in 14 s. The lamp is lit when ready, as the item says ("wait for the light"), not lit while heating. The CHARGE lamp is lit with the key on and the engine not running. On catching it throws a cloud of black smoke (`catchBlack`), and snapping the throttle open gives a black burst (`burstBlack`, `throttleBurst` 1.7), both new smoke params. The exhaust flap (`exhaustFlap`) lifts a bit with each firing pulse and decays in about 70 ms, held part open by throttle, with a rattle on top.
- **Stopping.** The key only switches the dash and the plugs. With it off and the engine running, `keyPulled` is set: the dash goes dark and the engine carries on until `O` (the stop knob, `stopKnob()`), or it stalls or runs dry. Turned back on, it's simply on again. `tractorWatch.js` (tested) has a girl within 18 m laugh at it after 3 s with the key off and the engine running (Jess 70% of the time, Amy otherwise; once every 90 s).
- **Controls.** `Shift` clutch, `1` `2` `3` gears, `B` reverse, `0` neutral, `Q` / `Z` hand throttle up and down (0.35 a second, it stays where it is, even after you get off), `W` foot throttle, `S` brake, `O` stop knob (moved from `X` later, see "Tractor odometer, hand throttle, glow lamp"). The lever only moves with the clutch down (`changeClutch` 0.7), except neutral, which can always be found, so you can always take it out of gear to get off. The starter won't turn in gear with the clutch up (a note says so). `B` and `X` were free while riding (`V` is the dash lean, and `X` is only the winch when looking at the green), and the quads' choke keys are the hand throttle here. A readout at the bottom of the screen gives the gear, clutch and hand throttle, since the levers aren't on the dash.
- **Gearbox** (`manualDrivetrain.js`, tested, same interface as the quads' CVT drivetrain so nothing else cares). Top speeds 2.4, 4.8 and 8.5 m/s in the three gears, reverse 2.6; drive force 100%, 68% and 46% of 5,200 N; the clutch pedal goes down at 8 a second and comes up at 1.6 a second (about 0.6 s), because it's a key. Pulling away from rest drags the engine by `launchLoad` 0.25, 0.5 and 0.95 for the three gears, times the clutch bite, falling away with speed, and 90% cancelled by full throttle. That goes in as the engine's drivetrain load, with `loadDragRpm` 3,200, so third from rest with the throttle shut stalls it and first never does (`tests/tractorDriving.test.js` runs the real engine and gearbox to check: first never stalls, third at 0 stalls, third at 0.6 throttle and second at 0.25 don't). Braking while in gear loads the engine the way it does the quads, so stopping without the clutch stalls it. The gear speeds are caps on the drive, not tied to engine revs: in first at full throttle the engine still revs to the top. All the numbers are in the tuning panel under Tractor/Gearbox.
- **Dash.** A new `tractor` dash style: a rev counter (0 to 3,000 rpm with a green band at 1,400 to 1,900, which reads with the key off, as a cable-driven one would), an hour meter, a small fuel gauge, GLOW and CHARGE lamps and the key, on the scuttle tilted up at you. No pod fits it.
- **Fuel.** 45 litres of red diesel (`startDiesel` 1; the engine's `runsOnDiesel` stops it soaking it up), 12 to 34 L at the start. Fill it from the dark green can like a quad. Petrol or pre-mix in it isn't modelled (it just takes it, and Amy says nothing).
- **Sound** (`engine-bank-tractor`, made by `tools/slice_engine_audio.py engine-bank-tractor`): kyles's 637890 (steady running 14.8 to 30.6 s for the idle, the driving stretches 34 to 60.5 s for revs, shutdown 61.4 to 63.6 s) and 637891 (a second idle at 6 to 11 s, starter and cranking at 1.1 to 2.9 s). 109 idle grains between 716 and 1,428 rpm by the pulse-rate measure, 64 accel and 34 decel. The engine uses the 'pulse' sound (every firing plays a recorded idle firing at its own speed, so revs are firings coming faster), idle 760 to 840 rpm, top 2,200. 637892 wasn't used (mostly passes with Doppler).
- **Lines.** `tractorLines.js`: `tractorKey` (the key that doesn't stop it), `tractorGlow` (a girl says something once you've waited 2.5 s for the plugs: Amy "Wait for the glow light first", Jess "Come on, come on") and `tractorCaught` (it catching, 70% of the time). Three situations, three lines each, for both of them, re-recorded.
- **Checked:** unit tests (compression 11, gearbox 16, tractor driving 7, watch 9), the game loads with no page errors, the tractor stands in the yard and is the right way round, and a headless run in the seat: cold start (the starter in gear refused with its note, the lamp coming on at seven seconds, a start with black smoke), pulling away in first, second and third, braking in gear stalling it, a stop knob stopping it, the key off with the engine staying on, the dash by eye with the lean-in key, the bank loading and being given to the tractor's audio. **Not checked:** how it sounds (in particular the pitch and loudness at 2,200 rpm, since the recordings only reach about 1,400 by pulse rate, and the starter loop), how it handles on a slope, in the bogs or against the winch (it's heavy: 2,000 kg against 300), the girls' lines in play, the exhaust smoke colour against fog or rain, or the shake from the vibration block, which I took from the green quad without trying. Getting off leaves the hand throttle where it was, with the parking brake holding it, which is 18's starting point. Left alone: the girls and the tractor (11, 14), a trailer (47), and neutral lights and jumping out of gear (17).

### The tractor, after first feedback

- Idle: no hunting, wandering, stumbling or misfiring (`roughHunt`, `erraticHunt`, `huntRpm`, `stumbleRate`, `misfireBase` all 0 in its engine block) and a roughness of 0.05 to 0.08, so a warm or cold idle holds exactly its idle rpm (a test checks the spread is under 5 rpm over 30 s). The beat is still very slightly uneven (`cycleJitter` 0.03).
- Rear wheels moved out to 0.88 m and the mudguards made wider (0.58 m) and bigger (0.17 m clear of the tyre) so the wheels, with the body shaking, no longer poke through them.
- Wheels shake with the body (`wheelShake` 1.3 on the spec): each wheel takes the body's heave, pitch and roll at its own position. Only the tractor does it.
- Your arms aren't drawn on the tractor (the empty `riderHands` group is only there because `Quad` wants one).
- Dash redone as a black binnacle with three chrome-bezelled round dials: water temperature, the rev counter (hour meter inside, PTO speed marked) and fuel, with GLOW, CHARGE and OIL lamps (the last two lit with the key on and the engine stopped) and the key.
- Help panel trimmed to about two thirds of its length (it now also scrolls if the window is still too small).
- Not checked: how the wheel shake looks in motion.

### The tractor, second round

- No fuel tap: `noTap` in its fuel params (the whole tank is always reachable, no reserve, `F` does nothing and isn't in its prompt or help, and the tap part on the model is hidden because `Quad` still poses one).
- Idle 430 to 470 rpm (spec `idleRpmWarm` 450, `stallRpm` 280). `loadDragRpm` came down from 3,200 to 1,400 to match, or first gear would stall it from rest: the tests still say first never stalls, second stalls with no throttle but not with a little, third wants throttle.
- Hand throttle: `handFloor` 0.22 in the gearbox params. At 0 it's idle; the first notch jumps to 22% throttle (about 840 rpm) and the rest of the travel goes from there to full. Checked headless: idle 463, one tap of `Q` (a tenth of the travel) gave 988 rpm. If you meant something other than this by "starts somewhere more sensible", say.

### The tractor, third round

- The hand throttle goes back to plain steps (0.35 of its travel a second, linear from idle to full); `handFloor` is gone. Instead it's left at 0.15 when the game starts (`handStart` in the gearbox params), about 710 rpm, so the tractor doesn't spawn with the lever at the bottom.
- Much more black smoke: a steady dark plume even at idle (`warmDensity` 0.5, `warmGray` 0.22), a thick black column whenever it's working (new `throttleDensity` 1.4 and `throttleBlack` 0.8 smoke params), a huge burst on a blip (`throttleBurst` 4), up to 15 puffs when it catches, opacity 2.2, puffs lasting up to 7 s and growing to 3.6 m. Looked at once headless with the hand throttle up: a dense black column. Not checked: how it looks in fog or rain, or that it isn't too much from the seat.


## Manual quads (TODO 9)

Settled with Jamie before building: the red, green and yellow (the yellow so there is one two-stroke manual); four forward gears and reverse, no hand throttle; the tractor's controls (`Shift` clutch, `1` to `4`, `0` neutral, `B` reverse; `Q` / `Z` stay the choke); the girls shift for themselves and leave it in neutral on getting off; a ridden quad you leave moving or revving keeps its gear; the dashes say N, D or R. (The first version also put the gear and clutch in a readout at the bottom of the screen; it came out again, see below.)

- **One gearbox, two users.** `ManualDrivetrain` gained a fourth gear (`gears` 3 for the tractor, 4 for the quads, `topSpeed4`, `torque4`, `launchLoad4`) and `Quad` split `tractor` (hand throttle, stop knob, its model's levers) from `manual` (clutch and gears). A gear's top speed is the lower of its ratio and the quad's `maxSpeed`, so the tuning panel and the bench's limiter still limit the top gear. The two-stroke's power band (`powerbandRpm`) now shapes the manual's torque too, since the CVT drivetrain's torque curve isn't used.
- **Ratios** (m/s, per spec): red 4.5, 8.5, 12.5, 17; green 4, 7.5, 11, 15 (and the gentlest launch load); yellow 5, 9.5, 14.5, 20. Drive force 100%, 85%, 70% and 60% of the quad's maximum (green 100, 90, 75, 65), reverse 3 to 3.5 m/s. Launch load is lowest in first, so first never stalls from rest with no throttle (a test runs the real engines for all three), and fourth stalls without a lot of throttle. Engine revs still aren't tied to the road speed, as on the tractor, so the revs stay up through the changes.
- **Controls.** On a manual quad `W` is only throttle and `S` only brake; which way it goes is the lever's (`reverse` follows the gear). The starter interlock is the tractor's. The choke, fuel tap and pull-start keys are as before, and the girls can crank one by taking it out of gear themselves.
- **The girls** (`autoShifter.js`, pure, tested): from throttle, brake, speed and whether she wants reverse she picks a gear (up at 85% of a gear's top speed with the throttle open, down below 70% of the gear below's), holds the clutch down for 0.4 s for each change with the lever moving part way through, and dips the clutch when stopped or braking slow so it doesn't stall. She never crunches a gear or stalls it; that is for later (the other skills, 33). She goes into reverse only once stopped. Her tunables are under "Her gear changes" in each quad's tuning folder.
- **Getting off.** When she stops driving, the quad goes to neutral with the clutch up. When you get off, nothing changes: still moving or revving it carries on in the gear it had (the runaway and stuck throttle are as before); stopped, it is simply left in gear, as the tractor is.
- **Dashes.** The LCDs showed H for forwards; they now show D (the loan quad's column reads D, L, N, R, P), and the pod shows D as well as N and R. The lamp dashes already had R and N lamps, now driven by the lever on a manual quad.
- **Checked:** unit tests (gearbox ratios, no stall in first on all three, her shifter) and once headless with a stand-in driver on each of the red, yellow and green: starts in neutral, goes 1 to 4 as it speeds up, drops to first and holds the clutch down as it stops on the brake, and is in neutral when the driver is removed. Also the status readout and keys on the red from the seat. **Not checked:** a real girl driving one (my attempts had her walk to it and never get there in the time), the red from the seat at speed, how the shifts feel, the sound through a change, the gear letter on the dashes by eye, or a manual quad on the steep bank.

### The tractor's tickover, evened out

Jamie: it didn't sound even and thumpy like the recording. Measured and fixed; idle kept at 450 rpm (it starts with the hand throttle a little open, about 710).

- **What the recording is.** kyles's tractor is a four-cylinder at about 1,000 rpm: firings at 33 a second in a cycle that repeats every 0.121 s, one cylinder louder than the rest (autocorrelation of the envelope 0.54 at one cycle).
- **What went wrong.** The slicer followed single beats and landed on whichever cylinder was loudest nearby, so idle "cycles" came out 0.084 to 0.168 s (three, four or five firings) and started on different cylinders. The pulse sound then played a random one each cycle, stretched by a different amount each time, so the loud beat moved about and the pitch wobbled. At 450 rpm it picked from the eight longest, the worst cut. Rendered offline through the real worklet, the regularity at its own cycle was -0.06 at idle and -0.01 at about 710 rpm, against the recording's 0.54.
- **The fix.** `idleCycles` in the bank's config: `whole_cycle_cuts()` cuts the idle into whole cycles, each placed where the next cycle best matches the average cycle and then moved to 6 ms before its strongest beat (114 to 129 ms long; anything over 6% off its neighbours is marked not ok). The bank gets `readThrough`, and the worklet's pulse sound then plays idle grains in recording order, jumping only at a not-ok grain or the end of a stretch. Re-cutting alone or reading in order alone each did little (0.16 and 0.15 at idle); together, built: 0.45 at idle, 0.68 at 710 rpm and 0.82 at 1,325, as loud as before within about 10%. The rev grains are unchanged (the pulse sound doesn't use them). The bank's WAV is unchanged; only its index moved.
- **Shake.** The vibration model took one kick per engine cycle, a single's thump: 3.75 a second at 450 rpm, ringing the body at its own 6.5 Hz. New `firingsPerCycle` (vibration params) spreads each pulse into that many kicks evenly through its period, carrying the late ones into the next update; the tractor has 4, so it shudders at 15 Hz at idle. Its shake now eases off above 600 rpm (`idleRpm` 600, `smoothRpmRange` 1,000), or the four-fold kicks held the body about 10 mm off centre at 1,300 rpm. By the numbers at idle: 1.6 mm of shake at 15 Hz (was 2.6 mm at 7.5 Hz) and more in the bars (1.7 mm from 0.6).
- **Checked:** unit tests (whole cycles in the bank and `readThrough`; four even kicks a cycle across updates), and offline renders (`.scratch/tractor-sound/`, untracked: the recording, the game before, the prototypes and the built version). **Not checked:** how it sounds in the running game alongside the quads, or how the 15 Hz shudder looks at 60 frames a second.

### Manual quads, after first feedback

- **They stall when slowed in a high gear.** New `lugSpeed` in the gearbox params (0.6 on the three quads, 1 on the tractor, which behaves as before): the drag on the engine now carries on up to 60% of the gear's top speed rather than only from rest, and braking with the clutch out only loads the engine below that speed (so a hard stop from speed doesn't stall it until it has slowed right down). Launch loads are now 0.1 to 0.15 in first (it never stalls from rest, or at any speed) and 0.6 to 0.7, 0.85 to 0.9 and 1 in second, third and fourth. The old engines shrugged this off, so the three specs also got a much bigger `loadDragRpm`: red 9,000, green 7,500, yellow 32,000 (a two-stroke idling at 1,800 takes a lot of dragging down to its 350 stall). Throttle held open saves it (relief 90%).
- Measured with the real engines, clutch out, a held speed: in third and fourth at 15% of the gear's top speed with no throttle, all three stall. Second stalls on the yellow, red and green at rest. Fourth at half its top speed doesn't stall. The yellow is the most stally (fourth at 30% of top speed too), the red and green need to be a lot lower. Left alone: how it feels to ride with real keys, and whether the red's second at rest is too easy to stall for pulling away (it only does it with no throttle at all).
- The girls' shifter is unchanged: she downshifts before the lug region is deep and dips the clutch when braking slow, so she doesn't stall (checked again headless on all three: 1 to 4 and back to first with no stalls).
- `tests/quadCharacter.test.js` applies a full braking load to every spec to check nothing stalls on hard stops; the manual quads are now excluded from that, since stalling there is the point.
- **No readout.** The gear and clutch line at the bottom of the screen is gone, for the manual quads and then the tractor too (you go by feel; the tractor's hand throttle setting is only on its lever). This supersedes the tractor readout described under TODO 8.


## Hay ride (TODO 10)

Settled with Jamie before building: a kinematic trailer, not a physics body; hitched by `J` with the drawbar at its ring; the girls walk over and climb on by themselves once you're stopped; one slides off on a hard bend, runs after you and climbs back on; about four situations of lines, three each.

- **The trailer** (`vehicle/trailer.js`, pure, tested; `hayTrailer.js`, `trailerModel.js`). Not a Rapier body: the axle is dragged along behind the tractor's drawbar pin on a rigid 2.6 m drawbar (`followHitch`), which is all that's needed for the off-tracking swing on a corner and means it can't flip the tractor or snag on anything (and it doesn't collide with anything either: it goes through hedges and bales). Its body sits on springs on the axle (`springRate` 140, `springDamping` 7, driven by the axle's vertical acceleration from the terrain under both wheels), and pitches and rolls with the ground under the wheels and the pin. `jolt` is the spring's vertical speed, which is what throws the girls about. It stands in the yard at `LAYOUT.trailer` (8 m behind the tractor, pointing at it). 47 (the tipping trailer) can add carrying and handling on top of this.
- **Hitching.** `J` while riding the tractor: hitches when the pin is within `hitchRange` (1.1 m) of the ring, with a prompt when it is; unhitches when you're slower than 0.5 m/s; otherwise a toast. The trailer snaps onto the line behind the pin in a frame, so up to 1.1 m of lurch on hitching.
- **Who's aboard** (`npc/hayRide.js`, pure, tested). Active when the trailer is hitched and you're riding the tractor. A girl who is stood about with nothing else on, within 40 m, sets out 1 to 4 s after the tractor has stopped (under 0.6 m/s) and takes the free side nearer her; two girls, two seats, so no more than two. She walks to beside the wheel, climbs up (`companionHayRide.js`, new states `boarding`, `onTrailer`, `sliding`; the other behaviours only start from `idle` so they leave her alone) and sits on the top bale at the edge of the well, facing forward. She waits at the trailer if it's still moving. When you get off or unhitch each one climbs down after up to 0.9 s.
- **Bumps.** Over `bumpJolt` (0.5 m/s of bounce) one of them shrieks (Jess 65% of the time), and both throw their arms up for 0.7 s. At most once every 14 s. On flat grass the jolt stays near zero, so bumps only come with rough ground.
- **Lying back.** After `lieAfter` (7 s) of going at over 1.2 m/s, nearly straight (under 0.1 rad/s) and smooth (jolt under 0.25), there's a 50% chance one lies back (the pivot at her hips leans back 1.15 rad, hands behind her head), then another try after the same time. A bump over 0.6 or a sideways push over 1.2 m/s squared sits her up again.
- **Falling off.** Each girl has a grip from 1 to 0. The sideways push toward her edge is speed times the trailer's turn rate (in a left turn everyone is thrown to the right, so the girl on the right is on the outside), plus a share of the jolt. Over her limit (`holdPush` 2.4 m/s squared, 1.5 lying back, times her own nerve of 0.85 to 1.3) the grip drains at 0.45 a second per m/s squared over, and comes back at 0.5 a second when calm. At zero, and not in the first 12 s aboard, and not within 90 s of the last fall, there's a 60% chance she goes; otherwise she hangs on with 0.4 grip left. For scale, a left bend at 6 m/s with the trailer turning at 0.5 rad/s is 3 m/s squared, a gentle one at 3 m/s and 0.3 rad/s is 0.9, which never drops anyone. She slides off feet first, lands 3.2 m from the middle on her side, lies in the mud for about 1.5 s, gets up, and runs after the trailer at 1.7 times her walking pace and climbs on when it is slower than 1.2 m/s. The other says something 1.2 s after the landing. There's no mud on her afterwards.
- **Lines** (`hayRideLines.js`): `hayBoard`, `hayBump`, `hayLie`, `hayFall` (the one who fell, on landing) and `hayFallOther`, three lines each for each girl, recorded. Tunables are under Hay ride, Hay ride seat and Trailer in the tuning panel.
- **Checked:** unit tests (the hitch following, off-tracking in a turn, springs and bumps, the sign of the sideways push, boarding, leaving, bumps, lying back, falling in each direction with the grace period, cooldown and the lying case); the game loads with no page errors; headless, driving the tractor by teleporting it along a heading: the prompt and hitching, both girls walking over and climbing on, the trailer following in a straight line and swinging round a left turn at 0.9 rad/s, a forced lie back and a forced slide off (fall, lie, rise, walk back and board in about 3 s), screenshots of the seating and the fall. **Not checked:** driving it with real controls, a real fall from a real bend (my turn at 3 m/s didn't push hard enough to drop anyone, which suggests it wants a fast third-gear bend), how the lying pose and the arms behind her head look from close up, how the landing looks on a slope, the girls' lines by ear, or the trailer in a bog. Left alone: the girls on the mudguard (11), a chain to the quads (12), carrying things and tipping (47).

## Tractor to the rescue (TODO 12)

- **The chain** (`src/items/chain.js`, `chainPull` pure and tested): same pattern as the green's winch, but with no reel. `X` looking at the tractor on foot takes it off the drawbar pin (1.5 m behind its middle), `E` on a quad hooks it to the end nearer the tractor, `E` on that quad unhooks it. It is a stiff spring (40 kN/m, at most 28 kN against the winch's 6.5 kN) that only pulls once the slack set at hooking is used up, so you back the tractor up close and drive off. The hook only reaches 5 m from the drawbar, otherwise it pulls out of your hand. The hooked quad has its brakes off (`towed`), which is what makes towing a dead one home work. A quarter of the pull drags back on the tractor (`reaction`); at 2,000 kg it barely notices. The trailer can't be hitched while the chain is out, and the chain can't be taken while it is.
- **Sunk too deep for the winch.** The winch could pull any quad out (6.5 kN against 3.2 kN of suction at most), so nothing was too deep for it. The bog's hold now climbs from sink 0.8 (`deepAt`) to a further 2.8 times the quad's weight at full sink (`deepSuction`): about 11 kN for a 290 kg quad, above the winch and well under the chain. Below 0.8 nothing changes, so the earlier bog tuning stands (a test checks the ordering winch < deep hold < chain).
- **Checked:** unit tests; headless, a red quad forced to sink 0.85 in the field bog: the chain, dragged by the tractor at 1.5 m/s, pulled it clear in about 6 s. **Not checked:** the winch failing on a deep quad (my headless run didn't make the winch pull at all, so that run proves nothing about it), driving the tractor with real controls against it, how the chain looks, towing a quad across rough ground or uphill, or whether sink really reaches 0.8 and over in play (wheelspin has to dig a long time; sitting in a bog only settles it slowly). Left alone: the girls using the chain, and any lines about it.

## Puddles stalled quads too easily, and a drowned quad now drips

- Small puddles were stalling quads at ordinary speeds. `waterDepthFrom` is now 0.55 (was 0.35), `waterSafeSpeed` 4 m/s (was 3), `waterGulpRate` 3.5 (was 6) and `waterStallAt` 0.75 (was 0.65). By the arithmetic in `tests/water.test.js`: a quarter-second crossing of a deep puddle at 6 m/s takes in about 0.1 of water, at 10 m/s about 0.9 (it drowns), and a shallow puddle takes none at any speed.
- A quad with water in it (above 0.03) now sheds drips from under the front and seat (`dripRate` 14 a second at full water, in the mud tuning), through the existing spray points, until it has drained and will start again. Same drops as puddle splash, so they're small and brief.
- **Checked:** unit tests, and the game loads without errors headless. **Not checked:** how the drips look, or the feel of the new numbers in play.
