## Smoke hanging in still air (TODO 15)

- `src/world/haze.js` (pure, tested in `tests/haze.test.js`) keeps one haze level, 0..1, for the yard. Each running engine within `area` 30 m of the yard's middle adds `fillRate` 0.03 a second times its output (`idleOutput` 0.3 at idle up to 1 flat out; two-strokes 1.5 times, the diesel 1.4 times). The air clears at `clearBase` 0.002 plus `clearWind` 0.05 times the weather's wind squared, plus `clearRain` 0.04 at full rain. So the level climbs towards fill over clear: foggy (wind 0.3) two idling quads reach 0.6 in about two minutes and take a few more to clear; overcast (wind 1) the same idling stays under the cough level; rain scrubs it. I used the weather's wind multiplier directly, so no separate "calm day" setting was needed: fog is the calm one and sunny (0.6) is in between.
- Girls: a free girl in the yard with haze at `coughAt` 0.35 for `reactAfter` 4 s waves it away (the existing `waveOff`) and says `hazeCough`, then not again for `coughGap` 75 s. Both girls do it; Amy is the one who complains, Jess jokes. The lines are in `smokeLines.js`.
- Look: `src/effects/hazeLayer.js`, 14 big camera-facing sprites (9 to 17 m across) at 1 to 3 m over the yard, drifting slowly with the smoke's wind, opacity up to 0.24 times the level. Sprites, not points, because point sprites are capped in size. The yard only; no haze over the rest of the farm.
- Checked headless once, on a foggy seed with the level forced to 0.8: the game loads without errors and the haze is a faint grey-blue wash that is hard to tell from the fog itself, so I raised the top opacity from 0.16 to 0.24 without looking again. Not checked: the look in sun, whether the blobs show any edges close up, the real fill time with the game's quads, the girls' lines in play. The new lines are not recorded yet, so they use browser speech until the banks are rebuilt.

## Showing off (TODO 16)

- `src/npc/showOff.js` (pure, tested in `tests/showOff.test.js`): `ShowOffRun` is a small state machine (setup, run, look) that `HerDrive.driveShowOff` runs as a drive mode, like doughnuts and the jump. Setup finds a clear point 32 m from you (the way she is already facing you if it can, otherwise trying either side and behind with `isClear`) and gets there at her usual pedal feel; the run goes at a mark 4 m to one side of you at 9 m/s. A wheelie starts 17 m from the mark: full throttle and the nose up for 1.8 s. A skid works out where to stand on the brakes (speed squared over twice 6 m/s squared, plus 1.2 m) and locks them from there. Then she stops and looks for 3 s.
- Wheelies are visual only. `quad.wheelie` (radians, 0.5) tips the body and lifts the front wheels about the rear axle, eased in and out; the physics doesn't know. A real wheelie would need a pitching torque model and a way to catch her before she flips, and it isn't worth it for something that lasts two seconds. A wheelie is only picked on a two-stroke (70%); any other quad skids.
- Whether you saw: `youSaw` wants her inside a cone of about 57 degrees either side of where your eyes point (the camera) and within 80 m, for a total of 0.35 s from the start of the trick to the end of the look. Seen, she's pleased and stops. Unseen, she goes round to the other side and does it again, three goes at most, then says you never look. The cone is the camera's, so it works riding or on foot.
- Deciding: `boredShowOffChance` (Jess 0.12, Amy 0.04), only if her drive confidence is at least 0.5, you are within 70 m, and there is a free non-tractor quad. She can say no room and give up if no clear run exists (the yard is cramped, so she mostly does it in the field). If something shows up ahead for 1.5 s she brakes and gives up.
- Lines in `showOffLines.js` (deciding, the trick, seen, again, missed), recorded into both banks.
- **Checked:** unit tests, and headless once: Jess on the yellow two-stroke, placed in the open field 27 m back from you. The wheelie lifts to 0.5 rad as she passes, she stops beside you, and when you are facing away she goes again (twice, then the third time stops after the look); the skid stops her about 4 m off with you facing her, and she calls it seen and stops. **Not checked:** how the wheelie looks (the screenshot caught the game's start overlay), whether the lifted nose clips her figure, whether the skid shows any slide, Amy's version, and the lines in play. She stalled on the run-up in the cramped yard in an early run (a manual two-stroke braked to a stop by the obstacle check), which is why obstacles give up the go rather than just braking.


## Neutral and jumping out of gear (TODO 17)

Not asked before building: the item as written. Jamie, part way through: no neutral light on the tractor (you can see the lever); the quads' dashes already light N from the lever, so nothing was needed there.

- **False neutral** (`manualDrivetrain.js`). `pedalDown` counts how long the pedal has been down far enough to change gear (0.7). A change made before `rushTime` (0.2 s) has a `falseNeutralChance` (45%) of missing the gear: the lever drops to neutral, `select` returns `'missed'` and the player gets a note. Holding Shift for a moment first is always clean. The girls' shifter passes `careful`, so she never misses (clumsy novice shifting stays with the other skills, 33). Pressing the clutch and the number together, which is what most people do, is the rushed case, so expect to miss now and then until you learn to hold it.
- **Worn gearbox.** `pickWornGearbox` (seeded, `seed ^ 0x6ea4`, so `?seed=` repeats it) picks one manual vehicle from the fleet's manual quads and the tractor, one of its forward gears above first (first is for climbing the bank, so it would be unfair there) and a wear of 0.5 to 1. In that gear, with the clutch engaged, throttle at least `jumpThrottle` (0.5) and the nose up by at least `jumpSlope` (sine 0.1, about 6 degrees), it jumps out at `jumpRate` (0.6) a second times wear times how much steeper than the minimum (up to double). So a fully worn gear on a 12 degree bank goes in about a second, a half worn one in two to three. It goes to neutral, with the note "It jumped out of gear". Level ground and the clutch down never trigger it. The game logs which one at the start.
- **Checked:** unit tests (rushed and clean changes, careful driver, timer reset, the jump and its conditions, rate against wear, the pick). **Not checked:** how it feels on the real keys (the 0.2 s and 45% are guesses and may be too harsh), a worn gear on the actual bank, or a girl driving a worn one (she'd just reselect, but I haven't watched it). Left alone: no girl line when it happens, nothing at the bench to fix it, and nothing tells you which vehicle is worn.

## Creeping in low (TODO 18)

Settled with Jamie: a straight line at a pace the hand throttle sets, working all the way below walking pace, built on the same idea as the quads that carry on after you jump off; no bale throwing (too much for now, so no loose bales and nothing for the girls to load); a girl notices it, runs after it and stops it, but sometimes it reaches the hedge; works with or without the trailer.

- **When it creeps** (`vehicle/creep.js`, pure, tested in `tests/creep.test.js`): the tractor, running, nobody on it or driving it, in first gear, hand throttle at `minHand` 0.05 or more, parking brake off, upright, not on the winch. Neutral, second and reverse never creep, so leaving it in neutral (what the girls do when they get off) still parks it. Before this the parking brake held an unattended tractor whatever the throttle said; `Quad.fixedUpdate` now releases it while creeping.
- **How fast**: `creepSpeed` is the hand setting times `speedPerHand` 2.4 m/s (first gear's top speed), never under `minSpeed` 0.15. At the start setting of 0.15 that is 0.36 m/s, about 1.3 km/h; a girl walks at 1.3 m/s, so she catches it easily. It is done by a `speedLimit` argument to `ManualDrivetrain.update` that lowers the gear's top speed (the drive fades to nothing as it nears it), not by braking: braking while coupled stalls the tractor. The throttle itself is still the hand setting, so the engine note follows the lever as before. I haven't measured how closely it settles on the limit in the running game.
- **Against the hedge**: held under `stuckSpeed` 0.1 m/s for `stuckTime` 4 s while creeping, it stops itself with the stop knob (so a girl who finds it finds it stalled, with the throttle still open).
- **The girls** (`npc/tractorRunaway.js`, pure, tested; lines in `tractorRunawayLines.js`): after `noteDelay` 4 s of creeping the nearest free girl within `noteRange` 30 m remarks on it, once. `distanceToHedge` ray-casts along its heading to the field square (`fieldHalf` less the hedge thickness). At `noticeDistance` 18 m from it, plus or minus up to `noticeSpread` 8 m, the nearest free girl (Jess about half the time when it's close) runs after it at 2.4 times walking speed. Within 2.4 m she shuts the throttle, puts it in neutral and pulls the stop knob; there is no climbing-on animation. `missChance` 0.25 a time, nobody is looking and nobody goes, and when it stalls the nearest girl says so and the other blames her two seconds later. Everything is in the tuning panel under "Tractor creep" and "Tractor runaway".
- **Checked:** unit tests only (the rules, the speed, the stall timer, the hedge distance, the events, the drive cut-off at the limit). **Not checked:** it running in the game: the real speed the tractor settles at, the girl's run, or the hedge stall.


## Top revs each game and the rev limiter (TODO 1)

- **Review:** the tops already in the specs were set against recordings in "Rev limits, roughness and fuel variation" and the sound passes since. Nothing was out of line, so they stay as the centres: red 6,200, blue 6,800, yellow 10,500, green 5,600, orange 11,500, kids' 9,800, loan 7,000, tractor 2,200.
- **Per game:** each spec has a `topRpmRange`, about 4% either side of its centre (the loan quad and tractor narrower, 1.5% and 2.3%, as a new machine and a governed diesel). `applyTopRpm` in `quadVariation.js` picks it in steps of 50 from its own stream (`seed ^ 0x70b5`, the tractor `0x70b6`), so a seed keeps every other pick. The redline keeps its gap below the top. The drivetrain's torque curve and the dash already read the engine's own `maxRpm`, so nothing else needed changing. Fuel burn per revolution is left alone, so a quad that revs 4% higher drinks about 4% more flat out.
- **Toolbox:** a sixth setting, the rev limiter (`revDownClicks` 6, `revUpClicks` 6, `revStep` 5% of the quad's own top per click, so about 30% either way; all in the tuning panel. First built at 200 rpm and three clicks, widened because Jamie wanted it to go relatively far). It adds to the throttle stop's own rev gain and moves the redline with it, and "Back to stock" returns to this game's picked top, not the spec's centre. It changes revs only; top speed stays the throttle stop's job.
- **Checked:** unit tests only (ranges contain the centres, redline gap, seed repeat, limiter limits and exact restore). **Not checked:** how the higher and lower tops sound or feel in the running game.
- **Over-revving wears it:** `wearOverRevBoost` 30 times how far the revs are over `designRpm` is added to the wear multiplier in `engineModel.js`, next to the work and cold-thrash boosts. `designRpm` is each engine's own top as picked for the game (`applyTopRpm`), so the seed's pick is free and only the toolbox's rev limiter or the throttle stop (2% a turn) take it over. Flat out that is about 2 times the wear at 10% over and 4 times at 30% over, against the same engine at its design top. Lowering the limiter doesn't reduce wear below stock. An engine with `designRpm` 0 (none set) has no over-rev wear. The girls don't comment on it yet (left out on purpose). Not checked: how fast a +30% quad actually needs a service in play.

## Reading the plug (2026-10-07)

Item 2; the item settled everything, so I chose the details without asking.

- Pure logic in `readPlug` (`src/workshop/benchJobs.js`, tests in `tests/bench.test.js`). Five readings from the engine's real state: oily (a two-stroke with wear over `plugOilyWear` 0.35, or `oilRich` over 0.3), rich and sooty (fouling over `plugRichFouling` 0.3, or `rich` over 0.2), lean and white (mixture under minus `plugLean` 0.15, the value it last ran at), worn (a four-stroke with wear over `plugWornWear` 0.4) and otherwise healthy tan. Checked in that order, so an oily plug beats a sooty one. Tunables are under "Workshop bench".
- The toast on the look step now gives only the colour. What it means is for you to work out, or for a girl to say. This replaces the old four-step fouling description, which said "it was drowning" outright.
- Girls: `onPlug` in `companionVoice.js` runs when you look at the plug, within her voice range. She names the true reading with probability `diagnoseAccuracy` (Amy 0.9, Jess 0.5, the same measure as listening to a running engine), otherwise one of the other four (`guessPlug`). Both say it, so Jess can contradict Amy. Five new lines each in `benchLines.js` (`plugHealthy`, `plugRich`, `plugLean`, `plugOily`, `plugWorn`), recorded.
- Left out: a four-stroke worn plug and a healthy one look alike to anyone but Amy, by design. Nothing sticks the reading to the quad afterwards (a logbook is item 20). Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how it reads in the running game, or how often a quad actually turns up lean enough to read white (lean mostly comes from a clogged air filter or starved fuel, so it may be rare).

## Horns (TODO 3)

Item 3; the item settled what happens, so I chose the key and numbers without asking.

- **Key:** hold `L` (free, and `H` is help). It works on any quad and the tractor, with the engine off too. Held down, the horn sounds; the sound is built the first time a horn is used (`setHorn` in `rideAudio.js`) and goes through the quad's own sound path, so a girl's beep has distance, Doppler and the barn's echo like her engine.
- **Voices:** `hornVoice` in `src/npc/horns.js`. Quads: two buzzy sawtooth tones near 620 and 780 Hz, quiet (0.22), low-passed at 2.6 kHz, with a 95 Hz wobble on the level; each quad's pitch is shifted up to 10% by its id so they don't all match. The tractor: 196 and 247 Hz, 0.55, less buzz. A spec can give its own `horn`. `hornVolume` sits in the Sound tuning.
- **Answers:** a fresh press (quiet for 0.15 s first) within 35 m of a girl sat on or driving a quad makes her beep back after 0.4 to 1.1 s, one or two beeps, then she won't answer again for 3 s. Jess always answers, Amy 60% of the time. Holding the button down is one beep, not a stream of them.
- **Creeping up:** Jess driving between 2 and 9 m behind Amy's rear arc (dot product 0.7 or more), with Amy driving at 2 m/s or more, has a 15% chance per second of blasting the horn for 0.7 s. First one no earlier than 120 s into a game, then 240 s apart. Amy "jumps" 0.12 s in, which is only her line (`hornJump`) for now: her quad doesn't swerve. The tunables are under "Horns".
- **Not done:** the quirk for a horn that works when it likes is item 6. No battery cost for the horn.
- **Checked:** unit tests only (answers, ranges, creep-up geometry and timing, voices, lines). **Not checked:** what the horns sound like in the running game, or how often Jess gets into position.

## L plates (TODO 4)

Item 4; the item settled what happens, so I chose the numbers and what "confident" means without asking.

- **Logic:** `src/npc/lPlates.js` (pure, `tests/lPlates.test.js`). Plates are in one of three places: nowhere, on Amy's own quad (the one she claims, `claimedSpec`), or on the tractor for good.
- **Jess tapes:** from 120 s in, at 5% a second while she's idle and Amy has a quad. **Amy peels:** the plates have to be on and Amy idle within 4 m of the quad; she then takes 25 to 90 s to do it. **Back again:** 120 to 360 s later Jess tapes them to Amy's quad again, as many times as it takes.
- **Confident:** Amy's mean skill (starting, choke, cornering, mud, `skills.js`) reaching 0.7. She starts at 0.44, and each right-moment piece of coaching is worth 0.12, so it takes about eight good pieces of advice across the four skills. Her own practice gets there far more slowly. Once Jess has made them at least once, Amy idle and the tractor free, she sticks them on at 10% a second. They then stay put.
- **Look:** one plate, a white square with a red L and tape at the top corners (`src/vehicle/lPlateModel.js`), on the back of the bodywork below the rear rack, or the front of the tractor's grille. Only one set exists, so they are never on both. The kids' quad and any quad with an odd rear may not line up exactly.
- **Lines:** `lPlateLines.js`: Jess `lplateTape`, `lplateCheek`, `lplateTractor`; Amy `lplateFound`, `lplatePeel`, `lplateStick`. The other girl answers a few seconds later.
- **Left out:** Jess doesn't take them off the tractor, and the plates don't turn up on a quad Amy has stopped claiming until she next claims one. Checked: unit tests only. **Not checked:** how the plate sits on each quad's rack, or the pacing in a real session.


## Gear on the digital pods

A digital pod fitted to a manual quad (red, green, yellow) showed "D" when in gear. Both digital pods now show the real gear (R, N, 1 to 4) from the dash state, and keep R, N and D on the automatics (`gearShown` in `src/vehicle/dashState.js`, unit tested). Not checked by eye in the running game.

## Wider yard spacing

The girls kept getting stuck on the parked quads. The yard slots in `LAYOUT.spawn.quads` are now two rows 6.7 m apart with 4.4 m between quads along each (before, 3.4 m, with a second row only 3.8 m away), listed alternately so a smaller fleet uses both rows, and the random shift (`quadJitter`) drops from 2.5 m to 1.4 m so neighbours can't be nudged into each other. Nothing else is placed near the new spots. Checked: unit tests only; not by eye or by watching the girls.

## A history (TODO 5)

Settled with Jamie: the hour meters and odometers already exist (every quad has them whether or not a pod is fitted), so this item only seeds the rest. Starting wear is a per-quad range, faults at the start are fairly random, one to two of them.

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/history.js` (tests in `tests/history.test.js`), seeded from `seed ^ 0x4157` so `?seed=` repeats it. `game.js` `applyHistory` applies it after the usage readings, so it doesn't disturb the layout or the other seeded streams.
- Wear: each spec has a `wearRange` (red 0.1 to 0.35, blue 0.08 to 0.3, yellow 0.1 to 0.35, green 0.25 to 0.55, orange 0.04 to 0.2, kids 0.02 to 0.12, loan 0 to 0.04, all under the 0.8 where an engine turns erratic). Seventy per cent of where it lands follows where the quad's hour meter sits in its range, the rest is luck. The wear ledger counts it as nobody's, as before. The tractor has none yet.
- Faults: one roll per game picks the fleet's chance of a starting fault between 0.15 and 0.6, so some games are mostly sound and some a mess. Each quad then rolls against it, gets one fault (a second, different one 25% of the time) at a level of 0.15 to 0.55, using `Faults.leave`. They behave and get fixed like the ones that develop.
- Tinkering: a quarter of quads (not the kids') come with the throttle stop out 1 to 3 turns, the mixture 1 to 3 clicks rich, or both. It is applied through the toolbox's own `applyTune`, and `unit.leftovers` seeds the workshop, so opening the toolbox shows the quad as it is with stock meaning the factory settings, and Reset undoes it.
- Who had it out: 10% nobody, otherwise Jess about half the time and Amy the rest.
- What the girls know: each girl settles once per quad what she believes, right with her `diagnoseAccuracy` (Amy 0.9, Jess 0.5). A wrong rider swaps to the other girl; a missed fault or tinkering is simply never mentioned. Stood by a quad, `watchHistory` brings up one topic at a time (90 s apart, each once per quad, `historyChance` 0.02 a second) and only while it is still true: a fixed fault or put-back stop isn't mentioned. Lines are in `historyLines.js`, written for the colour and each nickname like the other quad lines.
- The `DEFAULT_HISTORY_PARAMS` apply once at the start of a game, so they aren't in the live tuning panel (`HISTORY_PARAM_RANGES` is there for when a debug "new game" exists).
- Meters: checked in the code that the hours (engine running), odometer (distance) and trip all advance in `Quad.updateDash` for every unit every frame, ridden or not. The debug panel's engine folder now shows hours, odometer and trip for the quad you're on or looking at. The history itself is too long for the panel, so it is only logged to the console at the start of a game (in the per-vehicle block below), until the console item (TODO 57) can query it.
- Not checked in the running game: how the girls' history lines land, whether the starting faults feel right on a first ride, or that the debug readouts move (unit tests and a production build only). The voice banks needed re-recording for about 340 new lines.

## Tractor odometer, hand throttle, glow lamp, stop knob key

- Odometer: the tractor now starts with 5,000 to 15,000 miles (`odoRange`), pulled towards where its hours sit in 3,500 to 7,500 (`pickRelatedReading`, the same 70% pull as quad wear). Its dash has no odometer, so it is only in the debug panel; a pod could show it if one is ever fitted to the tractor (not done: the pods only go on the quads for now).
- Hand throttle: left anywhere from 0.05 to 0.45 each game (`handStartRange`, seeded with the tractor's other variation), where it was a fixed 0.15. The gearbox's `handStart` stays as the fallback.
- Glow lamp: the real way round. It is lit while the plugs heat and goes out when they're ready, and stays out on a warm engine, a running one or with the key off. `EngineModel.glowLit` is the lamp; what was `glowLamp` ("plugs ready") is now `glowHot`, which the girls' starting and the waiting remarks use. HUD help and Amy's `tractorGlow` lines say "wait for the light to go out" (those three lines need re-recording).
- Stop knob: `O` instead of `X`, which is the tow chain and the winch. I didn't use `C` because it holds the look-back view while riding, and you pull the knob while riding. `O` was free in the game (the toolbox uses it only with the panel open).

## Quirks (TODO 6)

Settled with Jamie: build all ten, one to three per quad from the seed, only on quads with the part, the girls warn from what they know, and nobody fixes them (the stiff tap frees up with use).

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/quirks.js` (tests in `tests/quirks.test.js`; `QuirkSet` per quad, `quad.quirks`; tunables under "Quirks" in the tuning panel, live, since they are read every frame). Picked from `seed ^ 0x9a1c` after the pods are placed. One quirk 50% of the time, two 35%, three 15%, never both fuel gauge ones. The debug panel's engine folder lists each quad's quirks; the console prints them with the rest of the start values (below).
- Where they can land (`featuresOf`): the two fuel gauge quirks and the cold needle only on a quad whose dash shows them (the blue and loan quads' LCDs) or that starts with a pod fitted. The senders live on the quad, so a pod moved onto another quad later reads that quad's lies, but the quirk isn't redealt. The tractor has none for now (it has no choke or tap, and the item is about the quads). The kids' quad has no split seat.
- Gauges (`QuirkSet.dash`, applied to the dash state in `Quad.updateDash`, so the dashes, pods and the girls reading the lamp all see the lie): reads high is level to the power 0.6 (a quarter tank reads 44%, and empty and full are right, so the lamp comes late); sticks holds a value picked at the start (0.2 to 0.9) and, moving, has a 1% a second chance of a knock that jumps it to the real level before it sticks again; the cold needle is capped at engine temperature 0.12 with no red zone.
- Choke: with it running, shake (the vibration model's intensity, 0..1) adds a step of the lever each 4 s at full shake, up to 0.5, so a quad shaking hard gets noticeably choked in a minute or two.
- Tap: needs three presses of `F`, each within 2 s of the last, and each turn takes 0.35 off the stiffness, so presses needed run 3, 2, 2, 1 (it rounds). A note says "The fuel tap is stiff: keep working it". The girls' own tap turning isn't affected. The note channel (`quad.say`) used to show only for the manual quads; it now shows for all.
- Seat: rain over 0.25 outside cover adds 0.08 a second of wet and it dries at 0.0015 a second. Sitting on it at over 0.3 soaks you (a toast and the nearest girl within 10 m reacts, `seatSoaked`) and takes 0.3 off the wet, so it doesn't repeat every time you get on. Girls' own seats are not modelled.
- Horn: pressing rolls whether it works (55%); a working one cuts out at 0.7 a second and a dead one splutters in at 0.9 a second while held. The girls only answer a beep they actually hear. Applies to the girls' beeps on that quad too.
- Brakes: full braking pulls the bars 0.05 rad left (the same channel as a puncture's pull, so the two add up). The squeal is a pair of sawtooth whistles through a narrow band-pass, wobbling a little and falling in pitch as it slows (built the first time it's needed, in `rideAudio.js`), when braking over 1.5 m/s.
- Headlight: the lens dims by up to 85% for about 0.12 s every 1 to 4 s on average 2.5 s. There's no light cast, only the lens glow, as for the lights today.
- The girls: `watchQuirks` in `companionVoice.js`, like the history. Each settles per quad what she believes with her `diagnoseAccuracy` (Amy 0.9, Jess 0.5), and with probability (1 - accuracy) / 2 is convinced of one the quad hasn't got. She warns once per quirk, a minute apart, stood by it or within 10 m as you get on it (three times as likely). Lines in `quirkLines.js` say "that one" rather than the colour, so there are only 22 of them.
- Not checked in the running game: how any of it feels or sounds (the squeal and the horn's stutter are first guesses), the choke creep rate, how often it rains onto an open seat in a real game, or the lines. Unit tests and a production build only. Re-record the voice banks for the 22 new lines.

## Start-of-game console readout

The several one-line logs at load (`This game:`, `History:`, `Quirks:`, `Pods:`, `Worn gearbox:`, `Tractor:`) are one block per vehicle now, from `Game.logStart` using `src/debug/startReport.js` (tested): where it stands, idle, top and redline revs, roughness, starting litres and tap, hours, odometer, trip, the pod fitted, front plate, wear, starting faults, tinkering found, who had it out, quirks and a worn gearbox gear, plus the tractor's hand throttle. Anything new that is random per vehicle at the start should be added to it. `Layout seed` and `Weather` stay as their own lines.

## Tyres (TODO 7)

Settled with Jamie: per-wheel pressures, per-axle brand and tread, found out by the gauge and by feel, a puncture shows on the gauge, no swapping tyres, no new girl lines.

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/tyres.js` (`TyreSet`, tests in `tests/tyres.test.js`; tunables under "Tyres" in the tuning panel, read when the tyres are dealt). Dealt from `seed ^ 0x7e55` in `game.js`. Wheel order is the physics': FL, FR, RL, RR.
- Pressure: stock 4 psi plus a shared bias of up to 0.5 either way and up to 0.4 each wheel; one wheel in four games is a further 0.8 to 1.6 out. Rounded to 0.1, between 1.5 and 7.
- Brands per axle, matched 40% of the time: Trackmaster (grass 1, mud 1), Mudclaw (grass 0.93, mud 1.2) and Roadrunner (grass 1.06, mud 0.88). Tread per axle, 0.6 to 1 at the front; the rear 0.55 to 1, or bald (0.2 to 0.4) one game in four. No tread costs up to 15% grip on grass and the track and up to 40% in mud and puddles.
- Where it acts: `QuadPhysics.applyControls` multiplies each wheel's grip by `TyreSet.grip(i, mud, puddle)` (pressure, brand, tread, blending to the mud values by how muddy or puddled the ground is). Stiffness is set per wheel from its pressure in `applyParams`, and rolling resistance from the mean pressure. The psi effects that used to be applied to the whole quad's params in `applyTune` moved here, with the same constants (`psiGrip`, `psiMudGrip`, `psiRolling`, `psiStiffness` still live in the toolbox tunables). The one thing that no longer follows pressure is the engine load's fallback grip estimate (`tyreGrip` and `mudGrip` in `engineLoad.js`), which is only used without the per-wheel info.
- The toolbox: the Tyres row's value is the four gauge readings; the setting is still the stock 4.0 psi and moving it pumps all four to that value (so it also levels them). Reset to stock levels them to 4.0. `T` (pump and patch) and the bench tyre job pump them up too, to the toolbox setting or to stock respectively, whether or not there was a puncture. A puncture takes up to 75% off its wheel's gauge reading as it goes flat, but not off its real pressure, so the grip doesn't improve when a tyre goes flat (the puncture's own pull and drag still apply).
- Debug panel: a Tyres folder for the quad you are on or looking at (the four pressures, draggable, plus the gauge, brands, treads and per-wheel grip on grass and in mud), and the console block per vehicle now has a tyres line.
- Worked out from the averages, not measured in play: a quad's mud grip comes out about 8% down on before and its grass grip about 4% down, with a wide spread between quads (a bald rear in mud is about 30% down). Not checked in the running game: how any of it feels, how visible the pull from uneven pressures is, or whether the bogs got harder. All the values are first guesses.

## The day's temperature (TODO 8)

Settled with Jamie: bands from frosty to a warm sunny day of up to 22 °C, fixed for the game (time changes later), the effects on engines, frost on the seats, no hats, a few girl lines.

- Pure logic in `src/world/weather.js` (`pickTemperature`, `chillOf`, `bandOf`; tests in `tests/temperature.test.js`). Picked after the weather kind, from `seed ^ 0x7e3b`, so the rest of a seed's weather doesn't move. Bands: frosty -5 to 0, cold 2 to 6, mild 8 to 14, warm 16 to 22, to the nearest half degree. Odds by kind: sunny 15/20/30/35%, sunny intervals 10/25/35/30%, overcast 10/35/55/0%, drizzle and rain 0/35/65/0%, fog 35/40/25/0%. `?temp=<degrees>` forces it (`Weather.temperature`).
- Chill: 0 at 12 °C and above up to 1 at -5 °C (`Weather.chill`, `World.chill`, passed to the engine as a control). At full chill: the choke an engine needs is 1.5 times (`chillChoke` 0.5), warm-up takes 1.6 times as long (`chillWarmUp` 0.6), the battery drains twice as fast on the starter and with the key on (`chillBattery` 1) and the glow plugs take 1.5 times as long to heat (`chillGlow` 0.5). All in the engine params, so in each quad's tuning panel. Engines already started every game stone cold, so there was nothing to change there.
- The sun: `Weather.warmth` (which heats the engines) gets 0.01 per degree away from 12, so a frosty sunny day takes the edge off the sun (down to 0.83 at -5) and a warm dull day adds to it. It is unchanged at 12 °C and on a sunny day above it (already capped at 1).
- Frost: `Weather.frost` is 0 above 0.5 °C and 0.4 at freezing up to 1 at -3.5 °C or colder. Each quad's seat (`seatFrost`, `src/vehicle/seatFrost.js`) is tinted a bluish white and matte by that much, until someone sits on it, which clears it over 4 seconds. Sitting on a frosted seat (over 0.3) gets a toast and a remark from the nearest girl within 10 m. The seat material is now its own per quad (it was shared on the quads with the plain seat), and the mud coat's copy of it is what gets tinted. The tractor's seat isn't frosted.
- The girls: an early remark in `watchWeather` (once per game, as for the sky) when the day is frosty, cold or warm and not raining; a mild day says nothing about it. `temperatureLines.js`, two lines each of `weatherFrost`, `weatherCold`, `weatherWarm` and `seatFrosty` for both girls.
- Console and debug: the `Weather:` line has the temperature, band, chill and frost; the debug panel's live list has `Day temperature`.
- Not checked in the running game: how the frosted seats look, whether the chill numbers (first guesses) make cold starts a pleasant struggle or a chore, and the girls' lines. Hats weren't built (the item's hats are dropped for now), and the temperature doesn't change through the game.

## The handbrake (TODO 16)

Settled with Jamie, all as recommended: only empty quads lose the automatic hold, every quad starts braked, the existing rates for the girls, no rescue, blame from a real tally, "it" lines, brake state in the debug panel and console.

- `Quad.update`'s `idle` (the hold that applies `parkingBrake` and `setParked`) now reads: with someone at the controls (rider's input, a ghost carrying on, a driver or the tractor's creep), stopped with no throttle, as before; with nobody, only if `parkBrake` is set, or it is the tractor. Before, any stationary quad held itself. An empty quad with the brake off has only rolling resistance and the physics' damping, so it stays on the flat and rolls on a slope as far as the ground lets it.
- Every quad starts with `parkBrake` on (the tractor too, harmlessly). Amy's `parkBrakeChance` is 0.9 now (it was 1), Jess's stays 0.5. When you get off a quad without the brake, a one-off toast says `Space` sets it. Getting off at speed leaves the brake as it was and counts as not braked.
- `src/vehicle/handbrake.js` (pure, tests in `tests/handbrake.test.js`, tunables under "Handbrake"): `RollWatch` calls an empty, unbraked quad that goes faster than 0.5 m/s rolling away, once until it has been nearly still for 3 s. `quad.lastLeft` (`{ who, braked }`, set when you or a girl get off the front seat) says whose fault it is; no `lastLeft`, or one who braked, blames nobody. A tally per person is kept. Pillion girls count as attending.
- Reactions: the girls within 18 m (not driving) respond through `rollReactions`. Blamed on you, the nearest says `handbrakeYou`; on a girl, her friend says `handbrakeJess` or `handbrakeAmy`, and 2.5 s later she owns up (`handbrakeOwn`) if she's near; if only she's near she owns up at once; no known culprit, the nearest says `handbrakeRoll`. Nothing physical happens (the girls don't chase it), so once it stops in the hedge or bog the existing stuck behaviour applies. `handbrakeLines.js`: each girl has the four lines she can use, two each (Amy cross then sorry, Jess gleeful then not sorry).
- Debug and console: each quad's `parkingBrake` line in the engine folder (on or off and who last left it), and a `parking brake` line in the start block.
- Not checked in the running game: how far and fast a quad actually rolls on the bank and the yard slopes (the physics decides), whether the starts are on slopes steep enough for the brake to matter, or the girls' lines. Unit tests and a production build only.

## Headlights that follow the revs (TODO 19)

- `src/vehicle/headlight.js` (pure, tests in `tests/headlight.test.js`; tunables under "Headlights" in the tuning panel, shared by every quad): `headlightLook` gives a brightness (0..1) and colour from the engine each frame, replacing the old on-with-the-key lens. Running: 0.3 at idle rising to full by 55% of the way up the rev range, from a yellow (0xffb347) to a near white (0xfff2cc). Cranking and not yet running: 0.15 and yellow. Stalled with the key on: out (a magneto has nothing to give). Key off: out.
- Flicker: from engine roughness plus erratic over 0.2, up to 0.6 above that, the light dips by up to 70% with two wobbles at different rates, so it stutters rather than pulses. It multiplies with the flickering-headlight quirk's dips.
- Steady: specs with `steadyLights: true` (the loan quad, and the tractor) stay full and white whenever the key is on.

A real beam would be its own item. Not checked in the running game: how the dimness reads in daylight (it may be hard to see at idle in the sun) or the flicker rate.

## Standing still on a slope (TODO 46)

- Cause, found with a scratch Rapier run (the player's character controller settings on a heightfield slope, no input, 120 Hz): while grounded the player asks for a downward speed of 1 m/s each step to stay on the ground, and Rapier slides the controller a little along the slope in answer. It is a steady creep, about 2 mm/s at 10 degrees, 6 mm/s at 30 and 9 mm/s at 50, so 0.1 to 0.5 m over a minute standing there.
- Fix: `src/player/standing.js` (pure, tested): grounded, no movement key down, not stumbling and under 0.05 m/s horizontally counts as standing still. Then the horizontal speed is zeroed, no downward push is asked for, and any horizontal movement the controller computes is thrown away (the height still follows the ground). The same run with that gave no drift on any slope from 10 to 55 degrees. Walking, jogging, stumbling, landing and being in the air are untouched.
- Not checked in the running game: that standing on the jump ramp or on a quad's collider feels right, which only the scratch run covered in the open.

## Jogging, and bringing the quad home (TODO 40)

- `src/npc/homeParking.js` (pure, tests in `tests/homeParking.test.js`; tunables under "Home and jogging").
- Jogging: in the walking state, a walk that isn't a prank or a wander jogs once she is more than 10 m from where she is heading, and drops to a walk within 4 m (so she doesn't flip at the edge). A jog is 2.1 times her walking speed (about 2.7 m/s) and plays the walking clip sped up to at most 2 times. There is no running clip, so it is a fast walk, not a real jog; a proper jog animation belongs with TODO 45.
- Bringing it home: in `endDrive`, where she would get off (not onto the back, not when you asked her to stop, not when she was parking, tidying or had no room, not with a passenger on, not the tractor, not if the quad is within 22 m of the middle of the row), `bringsItHome` rolls 0.65 (the item said 60 to 70%). If it comes up she picks a spot in the open ground east of the row (x about -43.5 plus or minus 3.5, along the row's length plus or minus 14 m, at least 3.5 m from every other quad, noses to the barn give or take 0.5 rad; ten tries, else she gets off where she is) and drives it there with the same slow, careful `park` route and slot stop the tidying uses, then gets off. Done by reusing the nickname job's trick of ending the drive and going straight back into driving with `parkWanted` set; her fear-speed and pauses are switched off the same way. `park.home` stops the end of that drive carrying on into tidying the row or complaining about a crooked quad.
- The girls' words: the end of the first drive still says its usual bored, scared or gave-up line, and the final getting off says its usual line. Nothing new was recorded.
- Not checked in the running game: how the jog reads without a run clip, whether the spot east of the row is always clear of hedges, props and the tractor (it only avoids quads), and how a quad being left there interacts with Amy's tidying of the row (it should just join the row next time). Unit tests and a production build only.

## Different controls (TODO 9)

- `src/vehicle/controls.js` (pure, tests in `tests/controls.test.js`; tunables under "Controls" in each quad's tuning folder). Each spec names a `reverseStyle`, `chokeStyle` and `tapStyle`; a test checks they exist, and builds the model for every choke and tap combination.
- Reverse: the red is `bars` (B with A or D held, creeping at most), blue and loan are `button` (S held and stopped, then B), green is `knee` (stopped), yellow, orange and kids are `none`. The old "hold S at a standstill" reverse is gone, so an automatic's S is only a brake until you press the button. `reverseDelay` went with it. The red and green are manual with a reverse gear, so for them the style is a condition on selecting it with B rather than a different key.
- Choke penalties, chosen so each costs something you feel and nothing is shown on the HUD: thumb free; knob (7 m/s, 45% steering while a key is held); plunger (1 m/s, 20% steering). Out of reach at speed gives a toast saying why. Beside the quad everything is in reach. The choke creep quirk still works the same.
- Pushing: hold `V` on foot by a quad (not the tractor) and it is shoved at 1 m/s along its length, away from you, backwards from the side. It keeps going while you hold `V` and stay within 4.5 m even if you look away. Refused with the parking brake on. A pushed quad doesn't count as rolling off for the handbrake blame. Any quad can be pushed, not only the two-strokes.
- Taps: left, right, rear left (the green's, on the tank side towards the rider; it was under the tank at first and couldn't be seen), low left, low right; handles are a chrome or black lever, a coloured knob with a pointer, or a T-handle. The tractor is untouched.
- The girls' driving is unchanged: when they drive the yellow, orange or kids' quad they still reverse as before, as otherwise they'd be stuck for good. Jess and Amy have no new lines.
- Not checked in the running game: how the knob, plunger and tap models look and sit against each dash and body (only that the game loads without errors), and how pushing feels.

## The mini moto (TODO 10, part one)

Jamie's calls: do both the mini moto and the old three-wheeler, one at a time, the moto first. The moto rides like the quads (the self-balancing option, not a falling-over model), is solo, turns up at random like the other extras, is a 50cc pull-start two-stroke, and uses the kids' quad's sound for now. The ATC will use the yellow's sound, since both are manual two-strokes.

- **Physics** (`MOTO_LAYOUT` in `miniMotoModel.js`): the same raycast vehicle with four wheels, but two are in a line (front at z 0.5, rear at z -0.45, the rear driven) and two are hidden outriggers either side (`outrigger: true`, 0.38 m out, `driven: false`, `gripScale: 0.35` so they don't scrub in corners). A first try with the outriggers 0.24 m out and the default centre of mass tilted 27 degrees within a second and was on its side within three: the roll stiffness from the outriggers (2 k x squared) was barely more than gravity's push over. Now 0.38 m out, stiffness 60 (the quads are 28), and the centre of mass 0.22 m below the chassis origin, which gives a static tilt of about 4 degrees on the parking slope, 1 to 3 degrees at speed and about 10 m/s in 4 s.
- **Looks**: the body leans into corners for show (`userData.leans`, 0.3 rad at full lock and 6 m/s or more, eased over about 0.2 s) in `quad.js`; nothing is simulated. The bars, dash (key switch) and thumb choke are the quads' own, scaled to 0.85. The headlight turns with the bars, the fork legs with the front wheel.
- **Flat tyres**: a puncture can only land on the two real wheels (`Faults.tyreWheels`). The tyre gauge in the toolbox still lists four readings, so two of them are the hidden outriggers'. Not tidied.
- **Fleet**: the extras are now three, with chances 30, 40, 20 and 10 per cent of none, one, two or all three, and the line-up is capped at seven (`maxVehicles`) so a full house still fits the seven parking slots.
- **Names**: the girls' quad nicknames (Jess names it Pogo or the bumblebee) add lines to be recorded, as the loan quad's did.
- **Checked**: unit tests; one headless run on seed 2: the moto loads without errors, shows its prompt, starts on the starter, reaches 10 m/s and stays upright through left and right turns. Not checked: a girl riding it (seat height, legs on the pegs, whether her riding pose looks right on a bike this small), the pull-start by hand, how it sounds, being pushed, a puncture, and the bog and mud look from the hidden wheels' spray.

- **Second pass**: no starter motor (`noStarter` engine param: `R` does nothing, the HUD hint is gone and a girl goes straight to the cord whatever the battery); a pod fits on the bar clamp; the lamp has a shell and brackets and the loose side panels became an airbox cover joined to the frame. Wheels now shake on every quad (`wheelShake` default 0.6 of the body's heave, pitch and roll; the tractor stays at 1.3). The outriggers' grip went from 0.35 to 0.05, and the moto keeps its steering at speed (`steerSpeedReduction` 0.02) with the driven rear losing half its side grip when spinning. Measured at 10 m/s entry, full left: radius 6.2 m coasting and 6.3 m on full power, so the sim doesn't show power-on understeer; Jamie feels one, so the handling still needs a real two-wheeler model (lean-driven turning). Not done.

- **Idle and clutch**: a real pit bike idles fast, so the moto now idles at 1,800 to 2,200 rpm (`safeIdle` 1,400, warm idle 2,000), with a centrifugal clutch biting at 3,200 and locked at 5,000 (it was 2,200 and 4,000, which was too near the idle). Lower `idleRange` and `idleRpmWarm` in its spec to settle it. The ATC, when it's built, should idle around 1,300 to 1,800.
- **Asking the girls**: the existing wheel menu already offers "Drive it", "Follow me on it", "Start it" and "Stand next to it" for the moto (no "Take me for a ride" or "Get on it", as there's no back seat), so nothing new was needed. Checked once headless: Amy walked to it, got on, started it with the cord and rode it round the yard. Not checked: Jess, her legs on the pegs close up, or how she takes corners (the handling is item 59).
- **ATC**: solo, and the yellow's sound. Part two.

## The old three-wheeler (TODO 10, part two)

Jamie's calls: solo, the yellow's sound, an idle that's realistic but easy to turn down, girls can drive it from the wheel menu, and a proper two-wheeler or ATC rollover model is a separate item (59).

- **Physics** (`ATC_LAYOUT` in `atcModel.js`): the raycast vehicle with three wheels, one at the front centre (z 0.62) and two at the back (0.9 m track, z -0.55), the rear pair driven. The front wheel carries about twice the load of a rear one, and with the same stiffness it sat 12 degrees nose down at rest, so a wheel can now have a `stiffness` multiplier in the layout (front 2): it rests within 2 degrees of level.
- **Tipping**: no special code, just a narrow stance, a centre of mass at -0.06 (the quads' default is -0.08) and the steering lock cut with speed more than the quads' (`steerSpeedReduction` 0.12). Full left lock from a 13 m/s roll-in rolled it to 96 degrees over about 2 s and it was flagged tipped; from 9 m/s the tilt stayed under 7 degrees. A first try with a higher centre of mass and the quads' steering tipped it at 9 m/s; it tripped over its own front wheel and that felt unfair.
- **Engine**: a worn 200cc two-stroke: wear 0.35 to 0.65, 800 to 2,400 hours, idle 1,300 to 1,800 rpm (`idleRange`; warm 1,550), rough (0.45 to 0.7), no starter motor (`noStarter`) so only the cord and the girls go straight to it, a manual four-speed box (4, 8, 12 and 16 m/s) and a pull-knob choke. Sound: the yellow's two-stroke bank with a longer pipe.
- **Tyres and flats**: the tyre set and the faults now follow the layout's real wheels (`TyreSet.useLayout`, `Faults.wheelSides`): the ATC's gauge reads "F, RL, RR" and the moto's "F, R", a flat tyre lands on a real wheel and pulls to its side. The moto's hidden outriggers no longer show up in the toolbox. This also fixes the moto's tyre note above.
- **Fleet**: four extras now, 28, 35, 22, 10 and 5 per cent of none to all four, still capped at seven vehicles.
- **Checked**: unit tests; headless on seed 4: it loads without errors, Amy walked to it, started it on the cord, drove it through the gears at 5 m/s, and the screenshot shows her on it. Rollover as measured above. Not checked: the cord start by hand, shifting by hand, the girls tipping it (her driving brain doesn't know it tips), either girl's legs and feet on a seat this size, Jess, how it sounds, and bogs and mud with three wheels.
- **Look** (second go): the first model was boxes and clipped its own tyres. Now the tank and seat are lofted, the fenders are moulded shells (`shellGeometry`, the quads' own method) standing 0.15 m clear of the tyres, the front fender turns with the wheel, the barrel is finned, and the colour is a chalky, sun-bleached Honda red. The tyres are 0.3 m wide. Checked from three angles headless; no clipping seen.
- **Pods** fit both the mini moto and the ATC (each model has a `podMount` on the bar clamp): `fitPod` returns true on both and the pod shows in a screenshot.
- **Roughness showing 1.0**: not a regression. At the start every engine is cold, and the live roughness adds a cold term (0.55 to 0.7 on the quads, in `coldRoughness`) to the build roughness and wear, so anything with the two summing past 0.4 clamps to 1.0 until it warms. Checked on seed 4 at the commit before this work (`25d41ab`) and now: the same, blue 0.24 + 0.17 + 0.62, yellow 0.45 + 0.24 + 0.6, green 0.56 + 0.40 + 0.7, orange 0.89 + 0.19 + 0.6, all 1.00. The per-vehicle build roughness (`baseRoughness`) is still random and different for each. Left alone; see the summary for the options.

## Engine oil on the four-strokes (TODO 11)

Item 11; the item settled the shape, so I chose the details without asking.

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/sump.js` (tests in `tests/sump.test.js`, plus the bench job in `tests/bench.test.js`). A `Sump` on each quad with a sump (`hasSump`: not the two-strokes, the tractor or anything that runs on diesel; the tractor is left for a later item) holds a level, 1 being the top mark and 0.3 the bottom one. It only drops while the engine runs, with how hard it's working (the engine's `lubeWork`, the same measure as the two-stroke's lubrication). Tunables are in `DEFAULT_SUMP_PARAMS`, shown in the panel as "Engine oil".
- One burner a game, picked from the seed (`pickBurner`, `?seed=` repeats it) from the quads that have a sump. It starts between 0.45 and 0.8 and loses 0.0012 a second at full work, so from half-full to starved (0.25) is about 7 minutes at half work (the test allows 5 to 15). The others start between 0.55 and 1 and lose 0.00002 a second, hours to go from top to bottom, so now and then one is low by chance. Levels are dealt in `game.js` after the quirks, from their own seeded stream.
- The engine reads it through the machinery the two-strokes already had: `stepLube` takes a `lube` factor, and `sumpLube` makes it 1 down to `sumpOkLevel` (0.25) and falling to 0 at empty. Starved, the scuffing, heat on the gauge, nipping up (roughness, power loss, erratic) and seizing are the two-stroke's own, so run dry at full work it seizes in about the same 70 seconds-ish a two-stroke does on straight petrol (I didn't time it in the running game; the unit test only checks it takes more than 20 seconds and less than 600). Above 0.25 nothing happens, so a sump at the bottom mark is fine and a girl topping it up is being careful rather than rescuing it.
- New: tappet tick, from `sumpTickFrom` 0.28 down to `sumpTickFull` 0.1. It adds up to 0.12 roughness and emits the existing `coolTick` sound events while running, at up to 9 a second, so it reuses the cooling-metal tick sample. I haven't heard whether that reads as tappets rather than a cooling exhaust.
- Burning oil: a burner with the throttle shut and the wheels turning the engine over passes `oilBurn` to the engine (more the faster it turns and the lower it is), which thickens and darkens the exhaust smoke the same way too much oil does. It's grey, not properly blue: the smoke has a single tint per quad and I didn't add a second one. It doesn't foul the plug or trigger Amy's "far too much oil" line.
- The dipstick: `Y` on a quad you're looking at (`Game.dipstick`). A word reading from `readStick`, which is never out by more than `readNoise` 0.04. Holding `Y` for half a second with the quad within 8 m of the oil bottle pours 0.06 of a level a second, so about fifteen seconds from the bottom mark to the top. There's no carried bottle: you bring the quad to the bottle. The bench has a "top up the engine oil" job (key 5) that fills to the top mark, or to 0.75 after a short pour. The two-stroke job is still two-strokes only and the new one four-strokes only. Strip down at the toolbox now works on any engine that's scuffed or seized.
- The OIL lamp: the red's dash had an "OIL °C" lamp that was only its temperature warning. You said it should be oil pressure, so it's now just "OIL" and no longer lights for heat (the red has no other overheat lamp; its temperature is on the pod or the engine's feel). `oilPressureLamp` lights it with the ignition on and the engine not running, like the tractor's, and while running with the sump under 0.18: flickering at idle (pressure's lowest there) and out when revved unless it's under 0.11. The tractor's own OIL lamp is untouched. Both accessory pods have an OIL lamp too (a fifth lamp on the analogue pod, an OIL word beside MPH on the digital one, lit with the key on and the engine stopped, then only when the sump is low), so a fitted pod shows it on any quad; the quads' own dashes other than the red's have none, so without a pod the sump only shows as ticking, power loss and the girls' remarks. Not checked by eye.
- The girls: Amy checks the dipstick once each time she starts driving a quad (nine times in ten) and tops it up with no ceremony if it's under 0.45 ("That's low on oil. There, I've topped it up."), otherwise sometimes says it's fine. Jess never checks. The girls' ticking line (`engineTicking`) is new for both; the existing hot, nipping and seized lines already fit. Lines are in `oilLines.js`. Amy tops up instantly rather than walking to the bottle.
- Debug panel: the engine folder has a draggable engine oil level and whether it's the burner, and the start-of-game console report prints each quad's sump and which one burns.
- Checked: unit tests and a production build. Not checked in the running game: how the dipstick toast and the pour feel, the tick sound, the smoke look, the lamp flicker on the red's dash, or the girls' new lines (recording in the background). Left for later: a carried oil bottle, oil in the tractor (it has an OIL lamp but no sump), and Amy refusing a quad she knows is low (item 31).

Each girl then gives at most one piece of advice every 20 s, and nothing in the first 12 s of sharing.

The driver answers 2.5 s later with the usual `taught_`, `coachWrongTime` or `coachNagged` line.

Jess gives mud only, 0.7 a second, and is bad at it on purpose: 1.5% a second she pipes up with any of the four when nothing's wrong (nearly always the wrong moment), and 35% of the time she says it again 4 s later (nagging). So Jess teaches mud, loudly, and sometimes costs Amy a slip of patience.

## Left running (TODO 15)

The item settled it; I chose the details without asking. Nothing new makes a girl wander off: after she starts a quad (head start, or you ask her to) she already goes idle with it running, and the heat and fuel work already finish the job. What was missing was anyone noticing, and being right.

- `src/npc/leftRunning.js` (pure, tests in `tests/leftRunning.test.js`; tunables under "Left running"). The game says each frame, for each quad, whether it's running, whether anyone is on it or starting it, and who is at the controls (you, a girl driving, or a girl having a go at starting it). The last of those to have it is the one to blame, so a girl who starts it for you and goes off is the culprit, and so are you if you step off without switching it off. A passenger alone doesn't change the blame. After `askAfter` 75 s running unattended it counts as left, once per stretch, in a tally per person.
- A girl stood about or resting within `leftRunningRange` 8 m says who did it (`leftRunningYou`, `leftRunningYouAgain` once your tally reaches `againAfter` 3, `leftRunningAmy`, `leftRunningJess`), at `leftRunningChance` 0.05 a second, once per quad every four minutes. Same shape as the wear blame: each girl has her own table, and the situation named for her own name is her owning up. Amy minds the petrol; Jess is unbothered and tells on Amy with relish.
- Left out: a girl switching it off herself, and the tally feeding into who she favours. Both are easy to add on top.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked in the running game: how long 75 s feels, how often the lines fire, or how they sound.

## Liking them running, the seat's buzz and side-saddle (2026-10-07)

Not a TODO item: Jamie asked for it alongside item 15. Asked first; the answers were the recommended ones.

- `src/npc/contentment.js` (pure, tests in `tests/contentment.test.js`), one per girl on `companion.content`. Tunables are in her companion params, so they're in each girl's panel and can differ per character.
- Stalls: a quad stalling within her voice range adds `sadOnStall` 0.4 (0.8 if it's under her), fading at `sadFade` 0.012 a second, so about a minute and a half. She says `stallSad` (unless the existing stalled-while-riding lines already did) and, when it catches again with her still down, `stallRelief`. A stall while she was driving knocks her driving confidence by `stallConfidenceKnock` 0.04.
- Liking them running: her mood (`joy`) follows the share of the quads running. With 70% or more going and her stood or resting within 8 m of a running one, she says `likeAllRunning`; with half or fewer, `wishAllRunning`. The bored 'start' weight is multiplied by 1 plus `startUrge` 1.5 times the share not running, so with all of them off she starts one 2.5 times as often as when all are on. The chance she switches off an idling quad she's sat on is cut by up to `keyOffCut` 0.8 of her joy. She still never starts one you've asked her to leave.
- The buzz: the body-shake level from `unit.vibration.body` (the one the shaking comments already use) counts once it's over `buzzFrom` 0.5, capped at 2, as shake-seconds per quad. After `buzzSaysAfter` 25 of them on one quad she says `buzzLove` (once per sitting). Her favourite quad moves to the one with the most, once it leads her current one by `buzzSwitchLead` 40, and only to one she has favourite lines for. A warm idle is about 0.6 on that measure, so a few minutes on any idling quad counts; the rough two-strokes count for more.
- Side-saddle (`src/npc/sideSaddle.js`, tests in `tests/sideSaddle.test.js`): each time she's sent to the back she picks it with `sideSaddleChance` (Amy 0.4, Jess 0.1, and a side at random). She turns her hips `sideSaddleTurn` 1.0 rad towards the side her legs hang, the back twists 80% of the way round again to face forward, and her legs come together rather than astride. The turn is on the root, so the seat offset is rotated with her to keep her hips on the seat.
- Slipping: only above 3 m/s. A sideways push over `slipLateral` 5 m/s squared (thrown the way her legs hang; half as much counts the other way), or a bump or landing over `slipBump` 25, builds slip at `slipRate` per second per unit over, and she recovers at 0.5 a second when it eases. Slip 0.5 is a yelp (`sideSaddleWobble`), 1 is off (`sideSaddleSlip`): she goes into the dismount and lands on the ground beside where the quad was, which carries on without her. With you riding in front her arms are round you and the limits are 1.6 times higher, so she holds through most of what would throw her alone. Nothing falls off while astride.
- Left out: a tumble animation (she just slides into the usual dismount), her hands taking a different hold side-saddle (the arms still reach the same targets, only the body is turned), and any effect on you riding off without her afterwards beyond the lines she already has.
- Checked: unit tests and a production build. Not checked in the running game, so the pose is the biggest unknown: whether the turned hips sit right on every quad's seat and look sensible, whether the arms reach you from the twisted body, whether the slip thresholds are about right for real riding, and the girls' new lines.

## A dripping carb (TODO 17)

The item settled it; I chose the details without asking.

- `src/vehicle/carbDrip.js` (pure, tests in `tests/carbDrip.test.js`; tunables under "Dripping carb"). At the start of each game `proneShare` 0.4 of the quads with a fuel tap get a sticky float (always at least one, on its own seeded stream so `?seed=` repeats it; the tractor has no tap and is left out). A prone quad parked (engine off, nobody on it or starting it) with the tap on ON or RESERVE sticks at `stickRate` 0.004 a second, about once in four minutes. While stuck, with the tap open, it loses `dripLitres` 0.005 L a second from the tank (0.3 L a minute) and its patch grows at `puddleGrow` 0.012 a second, so about 80 s to full size. The tap OFF stops the drip but leaves the float stuck; the patch dries at `puddleFade` 0.002 a second (about 8 minutes). Running the engine frees the float.
- The next start floods: while it's parked the plug fouling is held at least at patch size times `floodFoul` 0.6. That's the existing fouling, so the existing ways to clear it work (cranking with the throttle open, waiting), and Amy's own "flooded" lines fire when she's trying to start one past 0.4.
- The patch is a dark disc on the ground where it began to drip, scaled with its size. It stays put if the quad is moved, and dries there.
- A girl idling or resting within `dripSmellRange` 4 m of a patch of 0.08 or more says `dripSmell` at `dripSmellChance` 0.15 a second, once per quad every four minutes (lines in `dripLines.js`). Amy tells you to turn the tap off; Jess blames the tap, then Amy.
- Left out: a girl turning the tap off herself, Amy's walk-round noticing it, and blame going into a tally. All easy to add on top.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked in the running game: how the patch looks, or how often a float sticks in a real session.

## Silencer packing blows out on the two-strokes (TODO 18)

The item settled it; I chose the details without asking.

- `engine.silencerBlown` (0..1), only on engines with `strokes === 2`. It grows while running at `silencerBlowPerMinute` 0.05 times 0.33 to 1 by how far up the revs it is, so about 20 minutes at full revs and an hour idling. `engine.exhaustLoud` is what the audio gets for its `loud` flag: 1 with the silencer off, otherwise blown times `silencerBlownLoud` 0.6. The existing `silencerLoud` and `silencerBright` sound params do the rest, so louder and raspier together.
- Repacking: a bench job `packing` (hold to undo the silencer, pull the old packing, stuff the new one in a window, bolt on), two-strokes only. Botched, it leaves `packingBotched` 0.3. A toolbox service (`V`) also clears it, and a quad isn't counted as serviced until it's repacked.
- At the start of the game a two-stroke's blown level is 0.8 of its wear, up to 0.6.
- The girls (`silencerLines.js`, `silencerBlown`): once per quad every five minutes, near it while it runs. Amy notices at `blownNoticeAt` 0.25 and wants it repacked; Jess only at 0.6 and loves it.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how the blown level sounds, or whether 20 minutes is the right pace.

Opacity follows the lamp's brightness and colour, so a dim idle light gives a dim beam and a flickering one flickers. No real light: one per quad would be costly, and changing the light count recompiles the shaders.
