## Amy's logbook (TODO 20)

The item settled the idea, so I chose the details without asking.

- Pure logic in `src/workshop/logbook.js` (tests in `tests/logbook.test.js`): `Logbook.write` turns "what happened" into a line like "Blue, belt slipping." and drops repeats of the same thing about the same quad inside `repeatSeconds` (120), so a quad she walks round every ride doesn't fill the page. The page keeps the last `maxEntries` (40). Time is seconds since the game began, shown as m:ss. Tunables are under "Logbook".
- What goes in: her walk-round findings and oil top-ups (she did those herself, so always written), and the plug you pulled and the bench jobs you finish (written only if she is within `writeRange`, 15 m, which is her voice range for the bench lines, so she has to have seen it). Jobs and plugs are marked "(you)"; a botched job says "not well". The plug is written as what it really is (`readPlug`), not what a girl guessed, which is the point of the book being more honest than Jess.
- The prop and the page are in `src/workshop/notebook.js`: a red notebook on the bench, 0.9 m along from the toolbox, read with `E`. The page is a card low on the screen like the bench panel, so the barn stays in view, and shows the newest entries at the bottom.
- Left out: Jess's mistakes (she writes nothing), quirks and history (Amy doesn't know them for certain, so a line would be a lie or a spoiler), faults she hears or smells away from the bench, and the notebook surviving a reload. A girl can't pick it up or tease you about it yet. Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how the notebook looks on the bench, whether the card reads well, or that the interact box beats the bench's own.


Starting entries (added after Jamie asked for them): `startingCandidates` and `startingEntries` in `logbook.js` pick `startMin` to `startMax` (2 to 5) from what is true of each vehicle at the start: history faults, tinkering, quirks and the last rider. Each is kept with `startKnows` (0.85), so she misses some and never writes what isn't true, and at most one note of who had a quad out is kept because nearly every quad has one. They come from the seed (`seed ^ 0x109b`), show as "Yesterday", and don't count towards the two-minute repeat rule, so a walk-round finding of the same fault is still written. Plug readings aren't pre-written, since the plug is read at the bench. Decided with Jamie: quirks are included, and Jess writes none. Checked: unit tests only; not how it reads in the running game.


## Fumes in the barn (TODO 21)

The item settled it; I chose the details without asking. It copies the yard haze (TODO 15) but for the barn's own air.

- `src/world/barnFumes.js` (pure, tests in `tests/barnFumes.test.js`; tunables under "Fumes in the barn"). One level, 0..1, fed only by running engines whose position is inside the barn's walls, with the same output shape as the yard haze (throttle, two-stroke 1.5 times, diesel 1.4 times). `fillRate` 0.03 is the same number as the yard's but the barn is a small box, so one idler passes the cough level (0.35) in about 40 s. It clears out of the open doors at `clearBase` 0.012 a second plus a little wind: from full it takes a few minutes to go. Rain doesn't clear it, being under a roof.
- Girls: one stood about or resting inside, with the level at `coughAt` for 3 s, coughs (`fumesCough`) and walks out to the yard in front of the doors, not again for 40 s. Girls driving or doing something else aren't moved.
- Amy: at `switchAt` 0.5 she walks to just outside the doors and, 3.5 s later, switches off the engine ("reaches in"), but only if nobody is on it or starting it; if that changes on the way she stops. If every running engine in there has someone on it (you), she says `fumesTell` instead, at most once a minute. Jess only coughs.
- Look: the yard's `HazeLayer` again, with a `look` option for blob size, height and wander: 14 blobs 3.5 to 6.5 m across, 2.6 to 4.8 m up, inside the barn, opacity up to 0.3. The walls hide them from outside, so it shows through the doorway. It doesn't drift out of the doors.
- Checked: unit tests and a production build only. Not checked in the running game: the look of the haze, Amy's walk to the doorway, or the timings. The new lines are recorded in the background and use browser speech until the banks are committed.


## The barn find (TODO 1)

Jamie's answers: it takes an additional slot (not one of the seven yard slots), 20% of games, bench jobs work where it stands, the charge takes about five minutes, a botched job still counts as done (it leaves the usual rough fault), and the tarp is a first job of its own. It is called the "rusty quad" everywhere it shows up.

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/barnFind.js` (tests in `tests/barnFind.test.js`; tunables under "Barn find"). `pickBarnFind` takes its own stream (`seed ^ 0xba2f`, so the rest of a seed is untouched): a 20% chance, a donor from the five regular quads, and `minJobs` 3 to `maxJobs` 4 of the six jobs (a pull-start-only donor never gets the battery). `BarnFind` holds the state: tarp, jobs done, the charger, and `startBlocked` until the tarp is off and every job is done.
- It's a `QuadUnit` like the tractor: in `units` (so you can ride and fuel it and the barn fumes count it) but not `girlUnits`, so the girls never choose it, it has no pod, quirks or history, and no random faults roll for it. Spec: the donor's with id `rusty`, brown (0x6b4426), hours 1,900 to 2,800, 4,200 to 7,800 miles and wear 0.55 to 0.8. It stands at `LAYOUT.rusty` (back of the barn, clear of the bay, the bales and the ladder), brake on.
- Starting: `EngineModel.startBlocked` zeroes the chance of catching however it's cranked. Cranking it before it's ready toasts the first remaining cause. Some of the jobs have a real effect before they are done: the tyre job's start puts a puncture and a near-flat tyre on it, the carb one a gummed carb fault, the fuel one fouling, the battery starts at 3%. The seized brake just holds the parking brake on until freed.
- Bench: `Bench.unitInBay` falls back to the rusty quad while it still has work. Four new jobs in `benchJobs.js`: `tarp`, `fuel` (a windowed flush), `brake` (a windowed hold on the caliper) and `battery`, which is only the clip-on; `BarnFind` counts it once the charger has had `chargeSeconds` 300. The charger raises the battery steadily to 95%. The bench list for it is the tarp plus what it needs, with a tick on those done and a percentage while charging.
- Look: `rustyLook.js` copies each material (the quads share some) and pulls it towards rust on the metal and dust on everything; the tarp is a lumpy blue box over it that disappears with the tarp job. No assets.
- First start: on the first catch with everything done, the exhaust burst is held on for `smokeSeconds` 4, and both girls (in earshot) cheer (`rustyLines.js`, `rustyTarp` and `rustyRuns`). They also react when the tarp comes off. Amy then switches it off by the usual fumes rule, which I left alone.
- Left out: the girls riding it or having a view on it afterwards, a wheel entry for it, hands-on animation, and the logbook's starting entries mentioning it. Checked: unit tests, a production build, and once headless on seed 13 that the game loads with no errors, the rusty quad is in place under its tarp, won't start while blocked and does start (with the first-start flag set) once every job is done. Not checked: how it looks (dust, rust, tarp, placement against the bales and ladder), the bench list in play, the feel of the new holds, and the new lines (browser speech until the banks are committed).

Follow-up: draining the varnished fuel empties the tank, float bowl and oil (`FuelSystem.drain`, oil set to 0), so after the job you have to fill it from the petrol can, or the pre-mix can for the yellow and orange, before it can start. The toast says so. A rusty quad whose plan has no fuel job keeps its ordinary starting fuel.

## The diesel UTV (TODO 2)

Settled with Jamie before building: always in the game, parked by the paddock (a unit like the tractor, so not one of the girls' quads); an automatic with a dash lever for reverse; girls ride in it but don't drive it yet, and you can't ride in the bed; the girls squabble over the middle seat and one rides in the bed, and tipping it slides her off; red diesel only, petrol makes it run badly; Deere-style green. The model was to be accurate and detailed.

- **It's a unit, built from `UTV_SPEC`** (`quadSpecs.js`; `QuadUnit` reads `utv`). `game.units` has it, `game.girlUnits` doesn't, so the girls' driving, nicking, tea and the rest never see it. Placed at `LAYOUT.utv` (-52.5, 40.5), between the paddock and the quads' rows, where you start can see it. No pods, no faults, no history, no toolbox tinkering (the toolbox only pumps the tank out). The quirks module leaves it out of the choke and split-seat quirks.
- **Physics** on the quad physics with its own layout: 1.96 m wheelbase, 1.32 m track, 0.34 m tyres, 760 kg with a driver, four-wheel drive, 11.2 m/s top (25 mph), 3.4 m/s reverse, 32 degrees of lock. A touch over twice a quad's mass with about twice the drive force, brakes and inertia, and a softer rest length (0.2 m) with 0.22 m of travel. Not tuned against anything: these are first values. The steering wheel turns 6 times the road wheels' angle (`steerGain` in the model's userData, the quads' bars stay at 0.85).
- **Engine**: a three-cylinder diesel with the tractor's glow plug start (`compressionIgnition`), 850 rpm idle, 3,200 max, 3,100 redline. Two new engine params: `keyStops` (the key switches a running diesel off, where the tractor carries on until its knob) and `petrolFussy` (the soak numbers in `diesel.js` run on the petrol share of the tank, `contaminant()`, instead of on the diesel share). With the existing numbers a tenth of petrol is a rough running engine, a fifth stalls it and it won't restart. A diesel no longer gets a run-on when the key is turned off. Its sound reuses the tractor's recordings (`engine-bank-tractor`) with `pulseRefRpm` 850.
- **Fuel**: 30 L, 3 L reserve, no tap, red diesel from the bowser and can. Pouring a petrol can in makes whoever's near shout (`utvPetrolWarn`, via `mixWrongFor`), and once the engine is soaked past 0.2 and running, someone near says `utvPetrol` once. The toolbox on the UTV pumps the tank and carb out (`Quad.drainFuel`, soak cleared) if there's any petrol about; after that it needs red diesel.
- **Controls**: `T` tips the bed (stopped, from the seat or within 3.2 m on foot), `B` is the dash lever, a new reverse style `dashLever` that wants it under 0.5 m/s (`controls.js`). `utvBed.js` (tested): 7 s up, 5 s down, won't rise above 0.8 m/s, lowers itself if you drive off above 2 m/s, the tailgate drops once the bed is 8% up or while someone sits on it, and whoever is on it slides off from 55% up.
- **The model** (`utvModel.js`, procedural like the others, not tested in unit tests since it needs a canvas): a ladder frame with wishbones and coil-overs, a bonnet with stripes, grille and headlamps (lit by the headlight code), a bumper with tow loops, front fenders, a floor with ribs, a dash panel with a glovebox, grab handle and cup holders, a bench in grey cloth with seams and the three belts stowed down the back, a black roll cage with a cab guard and side bars, a moulded roof, the steering wheel and column with the dash on top, a brake and accelerator pedal, a parking brake handle and the dash lever, a silencer and tailpipe, a hitch receiver, tail lamps, yellow rims on chunky tyres, and a black ribbed bed with wheel wells, tie-downs, a front wall and a tailgate on a hinge. The dash (`utv` in `dash.js`) is a speedo with an hour meter, fuel and water dials, GLOW, CHARGE and OIL lamps and R / N / D.
- **The girls** (`utvRide.js`, tested; `utvMount.js`): the same machinery as the hay trailer and the tractor's mudguards (`boardTrailer`, `onTrailer`, `sliding`), with three slots: `left` and `mid` on the bench, and `bed`, which is the open tailgate (she sits facing backwards with her legs hanging). `standPoint` and `slideOffTrailer` take the slot and a direction now. Stop with it running and there's a 60% chance a girl sets off; with both near it's both 45% of the time. Two coming means a row over the middle 70% of the time, Jess winning 60%; the loser sits at the window, or goes to the bed (Jess 35%, Amy 2%). One coming on her own goes to the bed 30% of the time for Jess and 3% for Amy. The other girl says something about the bed once, 1.5 to 3.5 s later; the one in the bed complains every 18 to 40 s above 6 m/s. They climb out when you get off unless you asked them in from the wheel (Amy refuses the bed), and get bored and leave after 2 to 5 minutes. About 60 new lines (`utvLines.js`: eleven situations, three lines each), recorded.
- **Left out**: the girls driving it (it'd want the pedal-to-lever hand work of `companionDriving.js`), you riding in the bed, a payload (bales, the can, the toolbox in the bed), 2WD and 4WD (TODO 3), a trailer on its hitch (TODO 6), and a winch.
- **Checked**: 34 unit tests (`utvBed`, `utvRide`, `utvEngine`, `utvMount`); the game loads with no page errors; headless screenshots of the UTV from the front, back and the seat, with both girls on the bench, one on the tailgate, and the bed tipped with her sliding off and Jess's line showing. **Not checked**: how it drives and handles (nobody drove it), the engine's sound, the glow start in play, the girls boarding by themselves on a stop or squabbling, the lamps on the dash lit, the petrol soak in play, and the girls' lines by ear.

Follow-up: the bench is 1.14 m wide so it sits between the cage posts instead of through them; the rear hoop and cab guard moved forward to z -0.58 with their feet on the seat base, and the bed's wheel wells start behind them. The seat belts stand off the cloth (they z-fought it). The tail lamps moved from a floating spot on the frame to the rear ends of the bed's sides, so they tip with it. The dash lever stands on a plate on top of the dash. The dash has a red PARK lamp (parking brake, lit with the ignition on). Checked by screenshot from the side, rear and seat.

## 2WD and 4WD (TODO 3)

Settled with Jamie before building: only the red and blue get the switch (the green and loan quads stay fixed four-wheel drive, the rest fixed rear-wheel); `G` flips 2WD and 4WD with a physical switch on both; the blue gets a diff lock on `U`; 2WD is the default; it persists per quad until a new game; the girls forget it.

- **Logic** (`driveMode.js`, tested): `DriveSwitch` holds `2wd`, `4wd` or `lock` (the lock only where `hasLock`, and it implies 4WD, so `G` from the lock goes straight to 2WD). `driveEffects(mode)` gives what the mode does that step. Specs opt in with `driveSwitch: true` (and `diffLock: true` on the blue); `QuadUnit` passes it to `Quad`, which makes `quad.drive`. Quads without one keep the spec's fixed `fourWheelDrive` and never see any of this.
- **What it does**: 2WD drives the rear axle only (`QuadPhysics.frontDriven` overrides the spec's `fourWheelDrive`, which the physics already knew how to do) and the bars respond 1.2 times as quickly. 4WD drives all four and costs 4% of the drive (`fourWdLoss`), at the bars' own speed. The lock costs the same 4%, takes 12% more drive before the tyres give up (`lockGrip`, through a new `Traction.gripScale`), responds at 0.8 and only turns 88% of full lock. I didn't measure these in the running game (see below), just picked values with the right ordering; the tuning panel's "2WD and 4WD" folder has them all.
- **Switch and pods**: a rocker in a little housing inboard of the right grip on the red and blue (`driveSwitchRocker`, eased with the mode), and a red button beside it on the blue (`diffLockButton`, sinks when locked). The stock dashes show nothing (neither quad's dash had a 4WD lamp to begin with; I first added one to the red and the blue and Jamie had them taken out again): the switch shows the mode. The accessory pods carry it instead: the analogue gets a green 4WD lamp (and a red LOCK on the blue) and the digital a 4WD label with a bar under it for the lock, only when the fitted quad has a switch (`fourWd` and `diffLock` in the dash state are `null` for one that hasn't). The loan quad's always-lit 4WD label is untouched.
- **The girls**: `updateFourWd` in `game.js`. Taking the wheel of a switched quad, she remembers with her own `remembers4wd` chance (Amy 0.65, Jess 0.25; the default is 0.4) and flips it to 4WD if she does. Otherwise a `StuckWatch` waits for 2.5 s of throttle over mud (0.35 or more, track mud counts) at under 1.2 m/s in 2WD, then she flips the switch. A girl in 4WD already, or moving, never trips it. Amy tells Jess off (`fourWdToldJess`) and Jess crows at Amy (`fourWdToldAmy`), each only if the friend is within 18 m (the handbrake's hearing range), then the one who forgot owns up (`fourWdOwn`). Lines are in `fourWdLines.js`.
- **Left out**: the girls putting a quad back to 2WD when they get off (it stays as left), and the girls using the diff lock. The UTV and tractor have no switch.

## Jetting the two-strokes (TODO 4)

The item settled the behaviour; I chose the numbers and where it lives without asking. The item's "goes with 26" is the day's temperature, which is already in (TODO 8 of the last list).

- Pure logic in `src/engine/jetting.js` (tests in `tests/jetting.test.js`; tunables under "Jetting a two-stroke" in `engineParams.js`, so in the tuning panel). The engine's `jet` param is whole sizes from stock. The right size for the day is `(12 - °C) * 0.12`, so about +2 at -5 °C, 0 at 12 and -1.2 at 22; the stock jet is therefore right at 12 °C only. `jetError` is the fitted size less that, negative lean.
- Effects, all two-stroke only: the error adds `jetMixture` 0.06 a size to the mixture, so the existing lean and rich running (roughness, power loss, smoke, fouling) do most of the work. Spot on (within a quarter of a size, fading to nothing at a whole size out) takes `jetRoughness` 0.05 off the roughness and adds `jetPower` 6% to the power. Lean enough to hurt starts a whole size too small and is full at three: it pushes the dash heat up with the work, and adds scuffing at starved squared times work over `jetSeizeSeconds` 160, so a jet of -1 on a frosty day (full lean) seizes in about 160 s flat out and a stock jet on the same day (about half lean) in about ten minutes flat out. Idling is 0.15 of that. Scuffing feeds the existing nip-up and seizure, and a strip down at the toolbox frees it as before. The day's temperature reaches the engine as a new `dayTemp` control, from `World.temperature`.
- Because the stock jet is right at 12 °C, a stock quad on a frosty day is a little lean (about 10% power down) and on a warm one a little rich, and the bonus is there for whoever fits the right jet. Games don't start with the jet changed.
- Toolbox: a "Main jet" row (`tuneUp.js` setting `jet`, in `stockSettings`, so Back to stock resets it), shown only on a two-stroke and skipped by the up and down keys on the others. It says what the day wants and whether the jet is spot on, a bit lean or rich, or too lean. Range is `jetSmaller` and `jetBigger`, three each.
- Girls (`jetLines.js`): `jetLean` when you step to a jet that's too lean for the day (Amy warns, Jess shrugs it off), `jetRight` when you hit it. Recorded.
- Left out: the bench job version of changing a jet, and any dash or sound for a lean engine beyond what lean running already gives. Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how the row looks in the panel, how a lean two-stroke or a spot-on one feels and sounds, or whether the seize times are the right pace. All the numbers are first guesses.

## The yellow's big-bore kit (TODO 7)

The item settled the behaviour; I chose the numbers and the form without asking. It's a bench job, not a toolbox row, because "fitted at the bench" is a one-way change and the toolbox's rows are all dials you can turn back.

- Pure logic in `src/engine/bigBore.js` (tests in `tests/bigBore.test.js`; tunables under "The big-bore kit" in `engineParams.js`, so in the tuning panel). The engine has `bore` (0 or 1) and `boreFit` (1 for a good fit, `boreBotchedFit` 0.55 for a botched one). Only the yellow has `boreKit: 1` in its spec, so only it can be offered the job. Fitted once; there's no taking it out.
- Effects: power times `1 + borePower (0.18) * boreFit`; heat load of working times `1 + boreHeat (0.45)`, so it runs hotter but still only pinks and overheats the way the air-cooled quads do; wear rate times `1 + boreWear (1.6)`, 2.6 times the usual; and the right jet for the day moves `boreJet` 0.6 sizes bigger, so the stock jet is a little lean on it and the toolbox's jet row and its advice take that into account. A botched fit keeps all the wear and heat, which is the point of botching it. The kit's new piston and rings take `boreWearRemoved` 0.2 off the existing wear.
- Bench: job `bore`, five steps (head off, old barrel off, rings on, new barrel over the rings, head nuts up evenly in a window), the eighth in the list. Shown on every quad's list, "(yellow only)" on the others and "fitted" once done. The logbook writes "big-bore kit fitted".
- Girls (in `benchLines.js`): opening the bench on the yellow with the kit still to fit makes a girl say something about the catalogue (`boreCatalogue`, Amy disapproving, Jess pushing it); `boreDone` (Jess thrilled, Amy warning about the heat and wear) and `boreBotched`. Recorded.
- Left out: a catalogue prop on the bench (it's only in the lines), the kit's sound (the exhaust note doesn't change), and the girls knowing it's fitted when they ride it. Checked: unit tests only. Not checked in the running game: how the bench job reads, whether 18% power and 2.6 times the wear are good numbers, or how the lines sound. All the numbers are first guesses.

## The orange's coolant leak (TODO 5)

The item settled the symptoms; I chose the rest without asking. The item says "as a quirk", but quirks (`quirks.js`) are permanent and nobody fixes them, and this is fixed at the bench, so it isn't one of the ten: it has its own seeded chance instead.

- Pure logic in `src/engine/coolant.js` (tests in `tests/coolant.test.js`; tunables under "The orange's coolant leak" in `engineParams.js`). `EngineModel` has `coolant` (1 full, 0 dry), `coolantLeak` (this game's multiple of the usual rate, 0 for none), `coolantPuddle`, `steam` and `boil`, only for `waterCooled` engines, which is only the orange. The game picks them at the start from `seed ^ 0x2b6d`: `coolantLeakChance` 0.5 of games leak, at 0.6 to 1.4 times `coolantLeakRun` (0.00035 a second running, so about 48 minutes from full to dry at 1), starting between 0.55 and 0.95 full. Other games it's full with no leak. The console prints which.
- It leaks while running, and while parked only at a quarter of that while the metal is still hot (soak over 0.3). Once down below 0.92 a damp patch grows at 0.01 a second while it leaks, and dries at 0.003 (a green translucent disc under the quad, following a parked one while small and staying put once it's grown).
- The radiator's share of its cooling (`coolingEffect`) is full from 0.5 and nothing at 0.1. In `heat.js` that scales the water-cooled load discount (`heatWaterScale`, 0.6 down to 1, so it heats like an air-cooled engine), the fan's cooling, and how much of the heat the dash gauge shows (a quarter up to all of it). It still never pinks: pinking is air-cooled only. Idling in full sun on a dry radiator, heat heads for about 0.8.
- Steam: `steamLevel` is low and hot (level under 0.4, heat from 0.35) or what's left of a boil-over. It comes off the radiator (a second `ExhaustSmoke` on the orange, white, rising, spawned by `steam()` from a point on the nose) at up to 40 puffs a second.
- Boil-over: heat over 0.5 with the radiator down to half its cooling, once per top-up (`boiled`). It sets the level to at most 0.06, `boil` to 1 (dying over 14 seconds, so heavy steam for a while), and raises a `boilOver` event. In a full-sun idle the engine's heat lags (240 s time constant), so it can run dry before it gets hot enough; the boil-over doesn't need any coolant left, only that it hasn't boiled since a top-up.
- Girls (`watchCoolant` in `companionVoice.js`, lines in `coolantLines.js`): within `coolantJumpRange` 7 m of a boil-over a girl who is on her feet jumps back (`Companion.jumpBack`: 3.2 m/s away from the quad easing off over half a second, arms up) and shouts `coolantBoil`; within 6 m she says `coolantSteam` when it's steaming and running (once per 90 s), and `coolantDamp` stood by a parked orange with a visible patch (once per four minutes). Amy says what it is and to switch it off; Jess is scared first. Both banks re-recorded.
- Bench: a seventh job, `coolant`, for the orange only (let it cool, nip the clip up in a window, pour to the line in a window, bleed it). A good clip stops the leak for the rest of the game; a loose or over-tight one leaves 0.4 of it. A short pour leaves 0.7 of the coolant, a good one fills it. It clears most of the damp patch. The yard toolbox has no way to do it. The logbook writes "coolant topped up".
- Left out: checking that the engine has cooled before the cap comes off (the job's first step just says to let it cool), the girls topping it up, Amy's walk-round noticing the level, a coolant level on the dash, and the steam's sound (a hiss would suit it). Checked: unit tests and a production build. Not checked in the running game: how the steam, the patch and the girls' jump back look, whether 48 minutes is a good pace, or whether boiling over happens often enough in practice (it needs sun or hard work as well as low coolant; a dull or wet day keeps even a dry orange from boiling). All the numbers are first guesses.

## The mini moto's, ATC's and UTV's own sounds (2026-10-07)

Shopped for on Freesound (CC0 only) and picked by ear by Jamie from a listening page; the shortlist, with every candidate and where its idle, revs and start are, is `docs/briefs/vehicle-sounds.md`. Jamie picked the idles; the revs were left to me.

- **Picks.** Mini moto: a Derbi GPR 50 with a race kit (127592). ATC: a Piaggio Ape idling (239927). UTV: a Gator idling, recorded on the vehicle (149193). Also picked, for later: the Land Rover (TODO 15) on craigsmith's Jeep rumbling idle (438105); the green's idle on a Yamaha Ténéré 250 (510231, TODO 31), revs maybe from kyles's Honda ATV (450136); the yellow's idle on a Yamaha DT 125 (175964, TODO 40) and two-stroke revs from a Honda CR85 onboard (704792, TODO 32 and 40).
- **Revs (my choice).** Moto: the Derbi's own blips and riding off. ATC: the DT 125's blip and an old Vespa PX 200 ridden onboard (158891), which are worn small two-strokes like it. UTV: a small Bobcat loader's diesel working (174237). The pulse sound, which every vehicle uses, builds revs from idle grains, so these are only heard in the tuning panel's "recorded" sound.
- **Slices.** The Derbi and the Ape don't fire evenly at idle: the Derbi bangs every other turn (a burst every 63 ms, about 1,900 rpm) and the Ape lumps along with a strong beat every 110 to 116 ms. Played one grain per firing, each firing would get only the start of a lump and the lumpiness that made them worth picking would go. So the slicer cuts each lump whole (`idleCycles`, as for the tractor) and splits it in equal slices (`cycleSplit`: 2 for the Derbi, 3 for the Ape), marked with their `slice` number, and the bank is read in recording order (`readThrough`). The engine's own firings then replay the lumpy pattern at its own rate: the moto's render keeps a beat every 60 to 80 ms and the ATC's every 118 ms.
- **Cleaning up.** Read in order at every speed, the moto would bang every other turn flat out too. A worn two-stroke four-strokes at idle and cleans up as it revs, so above `pulseCleanUp` (1.3) times the recorded idle speed, the worklet plays only slice 0, the bang (`leads` in `engineWorklet.js`). For the ATC that's about 2,100 rpm, for the moto about 2,500. Tested in `tests/engineWorkletSlices.test.js`.
- **The Derbi's idle.** The stretch I first labelled its idle (55 to 66 s) is the engine off: it stops at about 54.5 s. Its idle is the purr between blips at 32.5 to 35, 41 to 44 and 51 to 54 s.
- **Borrowed one-shots.** The moto and ATC keep the quad recording's starter and shutdown, the UTV the tractor's. The slicer scales every source by the bank's idle loudness, so the quiet Ape would have made the ATC's starter 6.7 times louder. `fixedGain` pins a borrowed source to the gain it had in the bank the vehicle used before (4.851, 6.283, and 1.503 and 1.042).
- **Loudness**, from offline renders of start, idle and blips through the real engine model and worklet (`.scratch/pp/sweep2.mjs`, which finds any vehicle by id), matched to the old banks at idle: moto `recVolume` 0.3 (idle -24.1 dB, was -24.2), ATC 0.42 (-19.8, was -20.0), UTV 0.72 (-17.4, was -16.8). Flat out the moto is 5.6 dB louder than before (-9.2 dB) even with its `pulseRevLoudness` down to 0.2, as every firing is now a bang; I left it, a race-kitted 50 is loud. The engine model stalls the UTV after a full-throttle blip in that harness on either bank, because it doesn't feed fuel as the game does; not a sound problem.
- **Sizes.** The ATC's bank is trimmed to 5.8 MB (Ape 2 to 28 and 30 to 50 s, Vespa 48 to 60 s), the UTV's 3.7 MB, the moto's 2 MB. All banks load at the start, so these add about 11.5 MB to the first load whether or not the moto and ATC are in this game.
- **UTV speed.** The Gator idles at about 970 rpm and the UTV did at 830 to 880, so its grains played about 12% slow and lower. Changed the same day, below.
- Not checked: how any of it sounds in the running game (only the offline renders and their numbers), the slices' joins by ear, and the revs in the "recorded" sound.

## The little tipping trailer (TODO 6)

Settled with Jamie before building: a second, small trailer (the bale trailer stays tractor-only); hitches to the tractor and any quad with a rack; handling changes kept cheap; bales left out (they aren't pick-up items yet); girls can be asked in through the wheel as well as hopping in by themselves.

- **It's the bale trailer's cousin, not a physics body.** `TipTrailer` (`vehicle/tipTrailer.js`, model in `tipTrailerModel.js`) reuses `TrailerMotion` (the axle dragged behind the hitch on a rigid 1.7 m drawbar, the body on springs) so it can't flip a quad or be driven into, and the UTV's `UtvBed` for the tub (`riseSeconds` 4, `lowerSeconds` 3, tips from standing, lets itself down above 2 m/s, `slideAt` 0.5). It stands by the paddock at `LAYOUT.tipTrailer` (-49, 33.5) with its tongue towards the yard.
- **Who can pull it.** `canTow(quad)`: the tractor (drawbar pin, 1.5 m back) or a quad with a rear rack (the ones with a `mugAnchor`: the red, blue and green utilities; hitch 1.25 m back and 0.48 m up). The mini moto, ATC, race quads and UTV can't. `J` hitches when the hitch is within `hitchRange` (1 m) of the ring and unhitches when you're under 0.5 m/s. `J` on the tractor now works for either trailer; one at a time per puller.
- **Handling, cheap** (`vehicle/towing.js`, pure, tested). No physics: `towLoad` is 90 kg for the tub, 55 per girl in it and 18 per can or toolbox; `towEffect` takes 0.16% of the puller's drive and 0.09% of its brakes per kg (floors of 45% and 60% left), so an empty tub costs a quad about 14% of its drive and 8% of its braking, and a girl and three things about 29% and 16%. The tractor feels 15% of that. `Quad.tow` holds the two factors, applied to the drive force and the pedal brake in `quad.js`. Reversing just follows the hitch pin: no jackknifing. Tunables under Towing, Tipping trailer, Trailer tub and Trailer riders.
- **Carrying.** `E` at the tub with a jerry can or the toolbox in your hands stands it in one of three spots (`load`); you pick it up again the usual way. Things in the tub ride its tilt. `T` (stopped, from the seat of the puller or within 3 m on foot) tips the tub, and from half way up everything in it slides out behind in half a second, landing on the ground as an ordinary dropped item. The pranks and requests code reads an item's world position now, in case it's in the trailer.
- **The girl in it** (`npc/trailerRide.js`, pure, tested; the sit, climb and slide states are the bale trailer's in `companionHayRide.js`). One seat, on the tub floor. Stopped with it hitched and the engine running, a girl who's stood about within 30 m has a 40% chance to hop in after 2 to 6 s: Jess when both are near (85%), Amy alone 8%. The other girl says it's against the rules; a hard bump (over 0.45 m/s of bounce) gets a shriek, no more than one in 10 s; above 4 m/s she complains every 15 to 35 s; she climbs out when you get off or unhitch, or after 90 to 240 s. Tipping the tub with her in it sends her off the back into the mud, and the other girl laughs. **Through the wheel:** a "Trailer" slice on either girl's wheel (get in, get out); asked in, she goes at once and stays until asked out. Amy goes in when asked.
- **Lines** (`trailerLines.js`, merged into the main sets): `trailerBoard`, `trailerAsked`, `trailerTell`, `trailerBump`, `trailerShout`, `trailerTipOut`, `trailerTipLaugh`, three lines each for each girl, recorded.
- **Checked:** unit tests for the towing numbers and the ride logic; headless, with the red quad backed to the ring: the prompt, `J` hitching, Jess asked in (she walked over and sat down), a can and the toolbox loaded, a drive and bend with her aboard (the towing factors dropped to 0.71 drive and 0.84 braking, and came back to 0.86 and 0.92 once she and the things were out), then `T` tipping the tub, the girl sliding off with Jess's line, the cargo gone from the tub. **Not checked:** the hitch height against each rack quad's real bumper, how it feels to drive, reversing it, the tractor pulling it, Jess hopping in by herself, the girls' lines by ear, or the tub in a bog. Left out: bales, the UTV's receiver, and the girls driving with it on.

## Diesels don't foul plugs (2026-10-07)

- The tractor (and the diesel UTV) could stall "flooded" and refuse to start with a fouled plug, because the engine model's fouling followed a rich mixture (a choked air filter, too much oil, a drip-flooded carb) whatever the engine. A compression-ignition engine now has its fouling held at 0 each step. Rich running still does what it does to a diesel otherwise (black smoke, hunting, lost power), it just can't drown it.
- Left alone: the bench plug job and the girls' "flooded" and plug lines still exist for a diesel's glow plugs if something sends them there; nothing in the model produces a fouled reading any more. Checked: unit tests only (new case in `tests/compression.test.js`).

## Diesels don't pink or get a dirty carb (2026-10-07)

- Pinking is a petrol engine's detonation, but the tractor and UTV are air-cooled in the model so they pinked in the heat like the quads. It is now held at 0 for a compression-ignition engine (`updateHeat`). They still get hot, they just don't rattle or lose power from it.
- A diesel has no carb, so its dirty-carb fault never starts (`faults.prone.carb = 0` in `Quad`). The sticky-float drip (`carbDrip.js`) now leaves out the UTV as well as the tractor, by `compressionIgnition` rather than by name. Checked: unit tests only (`tests/compression.test.js`).

## Girls on the tractor and in the UTV by themselves (2026-10-07)

- The quads already let a bored girl sit on one or hop on the back of her friend's as it sets off. The UTV and tractor only took a girl when you were driving. Now, with nobody in or on the vehicle, a girl stood about within 30 m climbs into a parked UTV or up on a parked tractor by herself (`idleBoardRate` 0.004 a second per girl, about once in four minutes; tuning panel under UTV riders and Mudguard ride). She is marked as asked, so she stays put with you off it, until the usual boredom takes her down (UTV 120 to 300 s, mudguard 90 to 240 s). Get in and drive and she's already aboard. The UTV seat comes from the same `planSeats` as when you stop for her. The tractor needs no trailer hitched; the UTV's bed must not be tipped.
- A girl driving the tractor herself: her friend, if stood about, climbs up on the arch as the driver is getting on, starting it or ticking over, with `joinChance` 0.5 (one roll per go, `MudguardRide.girlDriving`), after 0.5 to 2 s so she is walking over before the driver is ready to go. The driver waits for her (`friendOnBack` counts a girl boarding or sitting on the arch, so the "friend coming" pause and the "friendOn" line apply). She comes down when the go ends.
- No new lines: they use `mudBoard`, `utvBoard` and `utvBedGo`. The girls don't drive the UTV yet, so there is no UTV version of the join; it comes with item 37.
- Checked: unit tests for all of it (`tests/mudguardRide.test.js`, `tests/utvRide.test.js`). Not run in the game: the walk over and climb, the driver's wait for her, and how often it happens in real play.

## Amy never turns it off (2026-10-07)

The only place a girl did it was Jess drowning Amy out (`drownOut.js`), where Amy reached over and switched it off, and then (first fix) Jess did. Now the engine stays running: the throttle is held for `revFor` 1.1 s, Amy tells her to stop `tellAfter` 1.0 s in ("Jess! Stop revving it!", `drownTellOff`), Jess lets go and protests ("Fine. Fine! Stopped.", `drownProtest`). The `reachRange`, `amyFree` and `switchOffAfter` bits and the "Right. Off." lines are gone; `drownTellOff` and `drownProtest` lines were reworded and re-recorded.

Say if any of those should go too.

## Steam from hot engines (2026-10-07)

Chosen without asking: the item gives no cause, so I used the two real ones, an engine cooking and rain on hot metal.

- `hotSteam` (`engine/heat.js`, pure, tested) gives 0..1. Cooking: a running engine over heat 0.7 (`steamFrom`) makes a thin wisp, up to 0.5 of the orange radiator's full steam (`steamCooking`), so it shows just as the pinking is getting bad. Rain: falling rain, or wet ground counted at 0.4 of it (`steamWetGround`), on metal hotter than 0.3 (the greater of running heat and soak, `steamWetFrom`) gives up to 0.9 (`steamWet`), running or switched off, so a quad you've just worked hard steams in a shower and stops as it cools; an engine only idling (soak 0.25) never does. Under the barn roof there is no rain to boil off. All the params are in the engine's Heat group in the tuning panel.
- `QuadUnit.updateHotSteam` draws it with a second `ExhaustSmoke` in the orange's steam look (pale, quick, rising), made the first time any is wanted so the quads that never get hot don't carry one, from the rider anchor (under the seat on a quad) or the exhaust. That means it comes from under the seat on the tractor, UTV and Land Rover too rather than the bonnet; their engine spots weren't worked out. Diesels have heat and soak like the rest, so they steam in the rain after work. The orange's radiator steam is separate and unchanged.
- Checked: unit tests for the levels. Not looked at in the game: how it reads against the exhaust smoke, the spot it comes from on each vehicle, or how often it shows in a real shower.

## A steady toolbox panel (2026-10-07)

- The buttons were a wrapping flex row with `display: none` for the ones that didn't apply and labels that changed length, and the card was centred on the screen, so anything appearing or changing (strip down, the filter percentage, the pods, the condition line wrapping) shifted the rest.
- Now the buttons are a two-column grid in a fixed order (service, air filter, tyres, belt, cable, carb, pump out the tank, strip down, silencer, back to stock, done), each in its own cell. One that doesn't apply (no carb on a diesel, no belt on the tractor, nothing to strip, no silencer) is `visibility: hidden`, so its cell stays empty and the rest don't move. The pods are a three-column grid with short fixed labels, and hide the same way on a diesel. Long labels and readings cut off with an ellipsis rather than wrapping, the condition line has room for two lines, and the card is pinned to the top of the screen, so a change in height never moves what's above it.

## Servicing the tractor and UTV (2026-10-07)

Settled with Jamie: the toolbox in place, a sump for the diesels that can burn oil, no girls involved.

- **The toolbox opens on both.** The panel (`workshop.js`) used to be refused with "nothing in it fits a diesel". It now opens, and shows only what a diesel has: idle screw, clutch springs and tyre pressures (rows marked `petrolOnly`: mixture, throttle stop and rev limiter are left out, as the jet already is on a four-stroke), the service, air filter, strip down, throttle cable and puncture jobs. Hidden: carb clean (no carb), the silencer, the pods (they fit the quads; the tractor gets them in item 31), and the belt on the tractor. The UTV keeps its CVT belt. The tuning maths (`tuneUp.js`) was already generic, and a test now puts both through the idle screw and back to stock.
- **Petrol pump-out** moved into the panel as the same button, relabelled ("Pump the petrol out of the tank"), enabled by the old `utvTainted` test. The separate UTV-only toolbox prompt is gone.
- **Sumps.** `hasSump` is every four-stroke, so the tractor and UTV each have oil, start somewhere between 0.55 and the top mark like the quads, lose a trickle as they work, tick when low and seize if run dry (the engine's lubrication no longer skips diesels). They are in the draw for the game's one oil burner, so one game in about three the burner is a diesel, with blue smoke on the overrun. `Y` reads the dipstick and, by the oil bottle, tops up. Both now show the Y chip.
- **A diesel's service** also fills the sump, because they can't go on the bench (the quads' oil is the dipstick or the bench). The button says "Service it (oil, wear)" and counts as not done while the oil is under the top mark.
- **The tractor no longer gets the belt fault**, since it has a clutch and gearbox and the panel has no belt button for it.
- Not done, and left for later: the diesels' own jobs on the bench (glow plugs, fuel filter, bleeding), and the girls knowing about their oil. Checked: unit tests only (sump, tune-up on the tractor and UTV). Not opened in the game: the panel on either vehicle, how the hidden rows and buttons look, the dipstick from beside the tractor.
