It never starts with a pod, so the seeded placement is unchanged. Checked: unit tests only; not tried at the bench in the running game.

## The UTV idles at its recording's speed (2026-10-07)

Jamie found the UTV rough and heavy, like the tractor. Two causes, one only in my renders:

- **The renders.** `sweep2.mjs` never told the engine model what was in the tank. A diesel engine (`runsOnDiesel`, `petrolFussy`) reads a missing `diesel` control as all petrol, so the render ran it soaked in petrol: roughness 0.32, misfires, beats 31% uneven, and a stall after full throttle. The game fuels it with red diesel, so none of that was in the game. Renders of the UTV or the tractor need `diesel: 1` and `fuel: 1` in each plan step, and `baseRoughness` from the spec's `roughnessRange`. Fuelled properly, its idle beats were even (0.8% spread).
- **The game.** Each idle grain is one whole cycle of the Gator at 970 rpm, and the UTV idled at 830 to 880, so every cycle was stretched about 12%: a slower beat (141 ms against the recording's 123) and a darker tone (spectral centroid 257 Hz against 313). The UTV now idles at 940 to 1,000 (`idleRange`, `idleRpmWarm` 970, `pulseRefRpm` 970), about what a small three-cylinder diesel ticks over at. Rendered: a beat every 122 ms and a centroid of 294 Hz, idling at -16.9 dB (it was -17.4). The clutch still bites at least 400 rpm above idle (`CLUTCH_MARGIN_RPM` in `quadVariation.js` raises it each game), so it doesn't creep.
- **Revs, not changed.** The engine model fires once per cycle, so in the pulse sound each firing plays the start of a whole Gator cycle, its loudest beat, cut off at the next: as the revs rise it sounds like a big single rather than a three-cylinder. `pulsePitchFollow` 1 squeezes the whole cycle into each engine cycle instead, so all three beats sound, but it pitches it up with the revs (the centroid at quarter throttle went from 375 to 873 Hz in the renders). Left at 0 for Jamie to try in the tuning panel; renders of both are in `.scratch/banks/render-utv-fixed*.wav`.

## The green's and yellow's own sounds (2026-10-07)

Jamie asked for the green and orange; partway through he moved the new two-stroke sound to the yellow and kept the orange as it was (its roughness suits it). Picks from the shortlist (`docs/briefs/vehicle-sounds.md`): the green idles on a Yamaha Ténéré 250 (510231), revs left to me, maybe the Honda ATV (450136); the yellow idles on a Yamaha DT 125 (175964) without its revving, revs on a Honda CR85 onboard (704792).

- **Why the green sounded bad.** It idles at 800 to 900 rpm and its old idle, a CRF250, runs at about 2,200. The pulse sound stretches a firing recorded faster than the engine turns to fill its cycle, down to 0.45 of its speed at most, so the green played its CRF250 more than an octave low. The Ténéré runs at about 1,750, so filling would still drop it about an octave. New tunable `pulseStretch`: the stretch is natural speed to this power, 1 filling the cycle as before, 0 playing the firing at its own speed and leaving a gap. The green uses 0.5: each firing plays at about 0.7 of its speed and leaves a short gap, as a big slow single thumps. Renders of 0, 0.5 and 1 are on the listening page.
- **Real revs in the pulse sound.** The pulse sound built every rev from idle firings, because rev recordings from other machines, resampled to fit each cycle, measured worse (see "Engine sound from idle"). Played at their own speed instead, a recording's rev firings keep its resonances where they were, so they can be used: `pulseRevLo` to `pulseRevHi` (engine rpm) blends from idle firings into the bank's `accel` firings (or `decel` with the throttle shut) on a second layer, each picked by rpm and cut off at the next firing like the idle ones. The rev EQ, which makes idle firings sound like revs, fades out as the real ones come in. `pulseRevGain` sets their level. Both default off, so only the green and yellow use them. Tested in `tests/engineWorkletSlices.test.js`.
- **Banks.** `engine-bank-green`: Ténéré idle (1 to 15 and 42 to 52 s), Honda ATV pulling away (10 to 30 s, about 2,000 to 7,300 rpm). `engine-bank-yellow`: the DT 125's idle (4 to 6.3 s, about 1,900 rpm) and its high idle (6.5 to 14.5 s, about 3,300), both as idle firings so the pulse sound picks whichever is nearer the revs, and its blip after that left out; the CR85 climbing, held high and coming off (13 to 70 s, about 3,300 to 15,000 by the bank's measure). The DT 125 is blurred and roomy, so its idle checks are looser (`idleTail` 1, `idleOdd` 0.4). Borrowed starters keep their old gains (`fixedGain`).
- **Settings, from renders** matched to the old sounds' loudness: green blends from 1,600 to 2,600 rpm, `pulseRevGain` 1.45, `recVolume` 0.58; yellow from 3,500 to 5,000, `pulseRevGain` 1.4, and `pulseTone` 9,000 so the CR85's rasp isn't smoothed off. Rendered green: idle -20.1 dB, flat out -10.3 (old sound at its own volume: about -19 and -10). Yellow: idle -19.6, flat out -7.0 (was -19.9 and -7.7). The yellow is much darker than before (spectral centroid about 500 Hz flat out, against 950): the old one was brightened by the rev EQ, the new one is the CR85 itself (about 580 to 660 Hz in the recording). The green's revs measure like the Honda ATV recording (about 330 Hz).
- The CRF250 stays in `engine-bank` as `idle3`, unused unless picked in the tuning panel.
- Not checked: how it sounds in the running game, the blend between machines by ear, and the gaps in the green's idle with its roughness and misfires.

## Amy won't ride it (TODO 3)

The item settled the shape, so I chose the details without asking.

- Pure logic in `src/npc/wontRide.js` (tests in `tests/wontRide.test.js`, tunable `amyWontRide` under "Amy won't ride"). Amy has a `FaultKnowledge`: quad id to the walk-round findings she knows about. `canDrive` (the one gate every drive choice goes through: bored drives, races, convoy, line-up, offering a ride, the wheel) now also asks `wontRide(quad)`, so she is never given or picks a quad she knows is faulty.
- Where she learns: her walk-round (`updateOilChecks`), and the faults in the starting logbook entries (the notebook seeds her knowledge from the same entries it prints). Not from hearing a fault: she has no hearing model, so that part of the item is left out.
- She forgets by looking, not by event: each time the gate asks, the fault is checked against the quad's real state (`stillWrong`, same thresholds as the walk-round) and dropped if it is gone. So a yard fix, a bench job or pumping a tyre all count with no hook in any of them, and a fault that comes back later is new to her. A rough yard fix leaves the fault under the walk-round's `faultFrom`, so it counts.
- When the walk-round finds something as she sets off, she says a "Not on that" line (`wontPuncture` and so on, five of them in `walkLines.js`) instead of the finding, pulls up and gets off. Not while a race, drag race, convoy or recall is on (`eventOn`): she says the usual finding and rides. Jess never learns anything, so is unchanged. The 15% of rides Amy doesn't walk round, and the 55% and 85% find chances, still apply, so she can ride a faulty quad she hasn't noticed.
- Left out: telling you why a "drive" choice is missing from her wheel (it just isn't offered), her telling the other girl, and her declining when you ask her outright. Checked: unit tests only. Not checked in the running game: how the pull-up and get-off looks straight after she sets off, or the five new lines (recording in the background).

## The ground changes (TODO 4)

- `src/world/groundWear.js` (pure, tests in `tests/groundWear.test.js`, tunables under "Ground wear"): a 2 m grid over the hedged field holding a wear value 0..1. Every wheel in contact on the move adds wear (`quad.js`, so the girls' quads and yours count alike), scaled by the quad's mass, tripled by wet ground and increased by wheelspin. Reading it back at the same spot gives what was added (the deposit is normalised against the bilinear weights, or a streak would read a quarter as strong between grid points). About 25 passes of a quad softens a strip of grass.
- Wear makes the ground soft (`mudAt`, so less grip, spray, ruts and mud coat, as on the track) from 0.3 to 0.65, and a bog from 0.72 on, judged on wear times a wetness factor (0.7 dry to 1.2 soaked). Dry, nothing worn ever bogs, because the factor tops out below the threshold; in rain a bog starts from about 0.7 wear and heavy rain makes it from less. That is the "rain swells them, dry spells shrink them" rule, and it needs no second mechanism: the weather's wetness already rises over about 40 s of rain and falls over ten minutes. Wear itself fades at 0.0008 per second dry (a soft patch is grass again in about ten minutes) and a quarter of that in the rain, so a bog made on a wet day outlasts the shower.
- It gets there by `World.mudAt` and `World.bogAt`, which now take the larger of the farm's fixed values and the wear's, so grip, sinking, the girls' waypoint checks and the ride sounds all see new bogs with no other change. The girls' race route also plans round them (`World.bogHazards`, up to 24 of the deepest cells, each 3 m radius).
- Drawn in the terrain shader from an 80 by 80 texel `DataTexture` (soft in red, bog in green, refreshed twice a second while anything is worn or recovering): churned brown for soft ground, dark and glossier for a bog, with the edge broken up by the detail noise. No canvas repaint.
- Not done: the ruts are not physically deeper. The Rapier heightfield and the mesh grid are built once and the ribbons are laid on that grid, so dipping the ground would need the collider rebuilt, which I judged not worth a hitch. A new bog is a dark patch with no dip or glossy skin like the three fixed ones. The three fixed bogs don't change size with the weather, only the existing 15% deeper when wet. The yard, the drag strip and the barn are exempt; the bigger field (TODO 9) will need `fieldHalf` only, as the grid is built from it.
- Checked: unit tests, and the game loads without errors (no shader compile errors). Not checked: how it looks in play, or how fast the girls' routes wear in practice; the numbers are picked, not tuned.

## Running in (TODO 5)

- `src/engine/runningIn.js` (pure, tested). A rebuilt engine gets `runIn` 1 (how much running in it still wants) from: `freeSeized` when it was really seized (not just scuffed), `fitBore`, and the barn find's first start in `game.js`. It counts down over `runInSeconds` 600 of running; idling only ticks `runInIdleShare` 0.5 of it, because ten minutes of tickover isn't running in.
- Thrashing is a smooth step of revs (0 idle, 1 flat out) from `runInGentleRpm` 0.6 up. While it's new, wear is multiplied by `1 + runInWearBoost` (5, so 6 times) times thrash times how much running in is left, so it eases off as it beds in. The test has a new engine flat out wearing more than 3 times as fast over a minute, the rest of the factor being the work boost it already shares.
- "Never runs quite as sweetly": thrashing leaves `scar` at `runInScarPerMinute` 0.012 a minute, capped at `runInScarMax` 0.1, added to roughness and to the condition that makes it erratic. `service()` zeroes wear but not scar, so it is the one part of an engine's state a bench can't clean; a new big-bore piston or a strip down after a later seizure keeps it.
- The tally needed no change: the extra wear lands on whoever was riding, so `WearLedger` already names the culprit. The scar itself isn't tallied separately.
- The girls: while a rebuilt engine has more than `runInNoticeFrom` 0.15 left and is worked past `thrashWork`, Amy says `thrashRunIn` (to Jess) or `thrashRunInYou`, and Jess cheers you on. It takes the place of the cold-thrash lines, as it's the worse crime. Lines in `thrashLines.js`, recorded.
- Not checked in the running game: how it feels to ride a fresh engine gently for ten minutes, or how often Jess trips the lines. Numbers are picked, not tuned. Nothing on the HUD says an engine is still running in; the dash might want that later.

## The collar lowered, and the jumpers remodelled (2026-10-07)

Jamie: the girls' collars sat too high, and should line up with the base of the neck; the jumpers looked strange, worn and on the rack.

- **The collar.** The model's shirt colour runs up her neck like a roll neck, to 1.25 m with her neck bone at 1.20 and her jaw at about 1.28, and the collar was fitted to the top of it, so it sat nearly under her chin. The body is low-poly: the triangles that carry the shirt up her neck run from the tops of her shoulders. `lowerNeckline` gives the skin's palette cell to any shirt triangle at the front or sides that touches her neck (a corner within 7.5 cm of its middle), stays within 10.5 cm and reaches more than 3 cm above the neck bone. Those further out at the back are left alone: they reach past the collar's flap and showed as skin patches on her shoulders. The collar now follows the shirt's top all the way round (the highest shirt point in each slice of angle, smoothed), so it sits at the base of her neck at the front and a little higher at the back, as a polo collar does. Once per body geometry, which both girls share.
- **The worn jumper.** It was her body mesh pushed out 3 cm with flat shading, so it came out angular, with sharp facets over her bust. `skinnedGarment` now takes `soft` (the corners at each spot welded into one vertex, smooth normals: one soft shell), `shape` (moves each vertex in root space) and `shade` (vertex colours). The jumper is soft, 2.6 cm off her body (sleeves 2.1), and below her bust the front falls straight instead of following her under it, easing back towards a snug ribbed hem (up to 5 cm forward, measured from her chest's furthest-forward point across a 12 cm window so it bridges between her breasts). Cuffs, hem and neck are ribbed bands 24% darker. The crew neck still shows the polo's collar and top button.
- **On the rack.** The hanging jumpers were boxes. Each is now one smooth knitted shape built from rings (`hangingJumperRings` in `src/world/jumperRail.js`, pure and tested): a flattened body that is fuller through the middle and gathered at a ribbed hem, with soft folds that deepen lower down; shoulders sloping from a ribbed round neck that dips at the front; sleeves leaving the shoulder seams and hanging beside the body with a slight bend into ribbed cuffs. A wire hook over the bar and wooden hanger arms that follow the shoulders inside it. The hem and cuffs are closed with a dark inside so they don't look hollow from below.
- Checked headless (overlay hidden, camera placed by script): both girls' necks from the front, side, back and above; both in jumpers from the front, back and close up; Amy in a jumper sitting on the red; the rack from four angles; no page or console errors. Not checked: Jess sitting in a jumper, a girl in a jumper riding on the back or front rack, and the shirt colours other than the ones her favourite quad gave her this seed.

## Spitting back (TODO 6)

- Pure logic in `src/engine/spitBack.js` (tests in `tests/spitBack.test.js`; tunables under "Spitting back through the carb" in `engineParams.js`). The engine model decides, as with the overrun pops, so sound, smoke and the girls share one `spitBack` event.
- When: the throttle snapped open (`isSnap`: at least `spitSnapTo` 0.6 open and `spitSnapStep` 0.4 above a slow copy of itself that follows over `spitFollow` 0.15 s, so a roll-on never counts, whatever the frame rate) on a running engine that's lean and cold. Propensity is leanness (0 at `spitLeanFrom` 0.05 up to 1 at `spitLeanFull` 0.35) times coldness (0 at `spitColdBelow` 0.7 temperature up to 1 at stone cold), so it needs both; the chance of a snap spitting is `spitBase` 0.8 times that, and it waits `spitCooldown` 1.5 s after one. It never happens on a diesel. A warm engine or one with the choke on never does, which is the point of it: it teaches choke discipline without a new fault.
- What it sounds like: a soft, wide, low whuff (noise through a one-pole low-pass plus a 130 Hz thump, about 35 ms decay), against the pop's sharp crack (3 ms decay, 90 Hz thump). Both the synth and the recorded-engine paths have it (`spitVolume` in the audio params).
- Smoke: `ExhaustSmoke.spit` throws up to `spitPuffs` 5 pale puffs upwards from the rider's anchor (the seat), not the pipe.
- The girls: the one sat on the quad (riding or driving) gets `Companion.startle` (the arms-up pose of the boil-over jump, without moving her) and `spitBackOn`; one within `spitHearRange` 12 m says `spitBackNear` with a 0.6 chance. Amy says what it is and to use the choke; Jess is startled, then amused. Both banks re-recorded.
- Checked: unit tests only (the full suite passes). A cold engine can't be had unchoked and idling without stalling in the tests, so they warm it, start it, then drop the temperature with a little choke on. Not checked: how it sounds, how the puff and the girl's jump look in the running game, or how often it happens when you start a cold quad and ride off with the choke part-way out. The numbers are picked, not tuned.

## Shouting a fault (2026-10-07)

Item 7. The item settled the idea; I chose the rest without asking: it is signs a rider can't see from the saddle, shouted only while you ride, and no new faults.

- `src/npc/shoutFault.js` (`FaultShout`, tests in `tests/shoutFault.test.js`, tunables under "Shouting a fault"). A girl who is off your quad and free to look (following, stood about, or on her own quad) shouts when your running quad is moving over `minSpeed` 2.5 m/s, she is within `seeRange` 28 m, and she is behind you or alongside (no more than `aheadSlack` 1.5 m in front of your middle: ahead of you she sees your face, not your back). She needs it in view for `amySeeFor` 1.2 s or `jessSeeFor` 2.6 s, looking away resets that, she shouts at most once every `gap` 12 s, and the same thing at most once every `sameGap` 70 s. Worst first when several show: steam, hanging, smoke, wobble, tyre.
- What counts as seen: steam off the radiator (`steam` 0.25), blue smoke from too much oil (`oilRich` 0.5) or black from diesel (`dieselSoak` 0.3), a sagging tyre (puncture level 0.35, before you feel it through the bars), a flat one wobbling on the rim (0.7), and a blown silencer "hanging off" (`silencerBlown` 0.5). A two-stroke's ordinary smoke is not a fault. "Wobble" is the flat tyre at its worst; I added no wobble of its own.
- The lines (`shoutFaultLines.js`) are all shouted, so they carry further, and are separate from the saddle remarks in `watchFaults`, which stay for when she is on or by the quad.
- Checked: unit tests. Not checked: how it reads in the running game, whether 28 m is the right distance, or how often it fires against the saddle remarks. The numbers are picked, not tuned. The lines still need recording.

## The collar, cut on a clean neckline; a rounder bust (2026-10-07)

Jamie: the collar's top should sit where the neck meets the shoulders, where the placket starts, and it had become far too wide; then, skin showed on her shoulders between the shirt and the collar; and make the busts a little rounder.

- **Why recolouring triangles wasn't enough.** The body is so coarse that the triangles carrying the shirt up her neck run from her shoulders to near her jaw, so whole triangles give a ragged, lopsided neckline: after the last change the shirt still reached 1.25 m on her left and stopped out on her chest at the front.
- **The neckline cut.** `cutNeckline` widens the girls' texture to twice its width, with a strip in the new half that is shirt colour in its lower half and skin in its upper half (`withNecklineStrip`; the palette's own colours, so recolouring the shirt recolours it too). Every other UV is squeezed into the left half, and each shirt triangle round her neck is mapped across the strip by its height above the neckline at its angle, so the shirt changes to skin exactly on the line, wherever it crosses a triangle. The line (`necklineAt`) is 3.6 cm above the neck bone round the sides and back, dipping 4 cm at the front where the placket starts. Height above the line counts distance out from her neck five times over beyond 6.5 cm, so the line stays on her neck and the tops of her shoulders stay shirt; at twice that, a wedge of skin showed on each shoulder.
- **The collar.** A short stand round her neck from just under the neckline, a crease (its top edge) 1.1 cm above it, and a flap lying back on the shirt no more than 3.6 cm out (`COLLAR_REACH`), lifted on to whatever is under it rather than following her shoulders outwards. Its two ends meet over the top of the placket (`COLLAR_GAP` 0.12 rad), and the placket starts just under them. The old thickened band of shirt under the collar is gone.
- **Two gotchas.** Three.js's `BufferGeometry.clone` shares `userData` with the original, so a flag set there on Amy's geometry was also set on Jess's and her neckline was never cut; geometries cut are kept in a WeakSet instead. (`figure.js` keeps `bindPositions` in the same shared object; harmless, since both girls start from the same positions.) And her hair can be its own mesh on the same material, so every mesh on the texture is found before any is changed, or the hair stays unsqueezed and samples the strip (Jess's hair came out the colour of her shirt).
- **Rounder bust.** The bust's forward push used the same smooth taper as its swell, which on this mesh came out conical. It now follows a dome, `(1 - e) ** 0.55` faded in at the edge, so it's full across the middle and falls away at the edge, and the region reaches less far below the apex (0.22 of a hips-to-neck length, was 0.27), so it's less of a teardrop. In profile she's fuller above the apex and rounder underneath.
- Checked headless: both girls close up front, three-quarter and back, from your eye height either side, and in profile; the jumper over the new collar. Not checked: the collar with other shirt colours, and how the strip looks from far off, where the texture's mipmaps blend its two halves.

## No collar; rounder still (2026-10-07)

After several goes the collar still wasn't right (asked for: buttoned up, closed at the front), so Jamie had it removed: the polo has no collar, and the placket starts at the top of the shirt.

- **What stays.** The neckline cut (`cutNeckline` and its texture strip), so the shirt stops on a clean line round the base of her neck instead of running up it like a roll neck. With nothing to cover it, the line dips only 1.5 cm at the front (`NECK_DIP`, was 4): deeper, it cut across a ledge of the model's old roll neck at her throat and showed as a slash of skin. The placket starts on the line at the front, with its top button just under it (`shirtFrontTop`, buttons at 10%, 45% and 80% of its length).
- **What went.** The folded collar (`addCollar`, `collarSection` and its test), which on this model kept either climbing her neck, spreading over her shoulders, leaving skin showing between its halves, or fitting to the inner of the two shells the model has at her throat.
- **Bust, rounder again.** The forward push's dome is `(1 - e) ** 0.35` (was 0.55) and the region reaches 0.2 below the apex (was 0.22). Doming the swell as well made the shape steeper than `tests/figure.test.js` allows (4.2 times a small move, against 4), so only the forward push is domed.
- Checked headless: both girls close up front, three-quarter and back, from your eye height, and in jumpers.
- Follow-up: the placket still started above where the shirt meets her skin, running up over the skin at the base of her neck. I had placed it at `necklineAt`'s front height, but the cut's boundary sits lower there (it is bent by distance out from her neck), and I judged it from a scaled-down contact sheet. `shirtFrontTop` now measures it: down the middle of her front, the first point whose texture colour is the shirt's. Checked at full resolution, front and three-quarter, on both girls.
- Jumper neck, smoother: the rule for where the jumper stops round her neck stepped from scooped to over-the-shoulders 8 cm out, which made a square notch, and its edge followed whole coarse triangles, which saw-toothed. The line is now a smooth curve (lowest at the front, a little low at the back), and `skinnedGarment`'s `smoothEdge` moves each point on the neck opening's edge towards the middle of its two neighbours along it, six times. Checked at full resolution on both girls.

## The Land Rover (TODO 14)

Settled with Jamie before building: the starting handle with its kick-back, yes; a manual four-speed; the girls ride beside you on a bench for three as in the UTV, not in the back, and don't drive it; a Series III 88-inch pick-up in Bronze Green with a white roof, always in the yard; a petrol quad's wear, faults, battery, choke, quirks and toolbox; its sound from the shortlist (`docs/briefs/vehicle-sounds.md`). Jamie's brief also asked for 2WD and 4WD, a diff lock, a hand throttle and towing the little trailer.

- **A unit like the UTV, built from `LAND_ROVER_SPEC`** (`quadSpecs.js`, `landRover: true`; model in `landRoverModel.js`). `game.units` has it, `game.girlUnits` doesn't, so the girls leave it alone except to ride in it. It parks at `LAYOUT.landRover` (-54, 71.5), north of the barn doors: the first spot (-50.5, 67.5) was on a straw bale, and it spawned on top and flipped, so a test now keeps its spot 4 m from every bale and 6 m from the other vehicles. Unlike the UTV it's dealt what a petrol quad gets, by hand since it's added after the fleet's dealing: wear from `wearRange`, tyres, one to three quirks (the Series dash counts as a gauge dash, so a sticking fuel gauge can land; the seat quirks can't, it's a vinyl bench), a sump, and faults with the belt switched off (`prone.belt = 0`). No fuel tap (a Series has none), so no sticky float, which I'd offered and Jamie had agreed to.
- **Starting handle** (`crankHandle.js`, tested). Stand within 1.2 m of the hole in the front bumper (ground distance) on foot and the prompt shows your grip: `1` thumb round the handle (the default, and it kicks back on 30% of swings), `2` thumb alongside the fingers (never). A swing is the engine's pull (`pullStart` with `crankHandle`): hold `P` and let go, 0.35 to 0.9 s for a good one, turning it over at 0.6 of the starter's speed for 0.7 s. A kick-back stops the turn, leaves your hand hurting for 4 s (no swinging), says why, and the nearest girl within 15 m says something (`landyKick`, three lines each). The engine reports a swing as `handCrankOn` rather than `starterOn`, and the worklet plays a one-shot clunk for it instead of the starter loop.
- **Gearbox**: the manual drivetrain, four speeds, and two new gearbox params: `crashGears` (2: first and second have no synchromesh) and `crashSpeed` (1 m/s). `select` takes the vehicle's speed and returns `crunch`, leaving the gear where it was, when a crash gear is asked for faster than that. Tested.
- **Hand throttle**: a knob on the dash, `]` out and `[` in (Q and Z are its choke, which the tractor hasn't got), moving 0.35 a second like the tractor's, and holding the throttle at least that open. `Quad.handLever`; starts pushed in.
- **4WD and diff lock**: the existing drive switch (`G`, `U`), on the yellow and red knobs by the gear lever, and lamps on the dash. Starts in 2WD.
- **Towing**: `canTow` and the trailer's hitch point now take any model with a `towHitch` (the Land Rover's jaw, 2.02 m behind its middle); it counts as heavy for the load, as the tractor does.
- **Dash** (`series` in `dash.js`): a speedo to 90 mph round 260 degrees with a five-drum odometer, a combined fuel and water gauge, and IGN, OIL, CHOKE, BRAKE, 4WD and DIFF lamps (the dash state gained `choke`). Checked at full resolution in a screenshot from the seat.
- **Sound** (`engine-bank-landy`): the Jeep's idle beats every 60 ms in groups of four, about 500 rpm, so the Land Rover idles at 520 to 580 (a Series 2.25 idles about there) and the idle is cut into whole four-beat cycles read in order; the cycles came out at 470 to 530 rpm. `pulsePitchFollow` 1, so as it revs each whole cycle is squeezed into the engine's cycle and all four beats sound. The starter borrows the quad's; the handle's clunk is one swing from craigsmith's crank-start car (31.95 to 32.45 s); the shutdown is the Jeep stopping (55.2 to 57 s).
- **Numbers, first guesses**: 1,350 kg, 2.23 m wheelbase, 0.38 m tyres, 7,000 N of drive, 14.5 m/s in top, 4,200 rpm, 45 litres burning 0.0003 l per revolution (about 40 minutes flat out), heavy slow steering. `recVolume` 0.6 is unmeasured.
- **Size and smoothness, after Jamie tried it.** Built to a real Series's measurements it looked small beside the girls, so the spec's `scale` is 1.15 (the model and its physics chassis together; the seats, stand points, starting handle and towing jaw follow, the people don't grow). It also ran too rough: the engine model is a single cylinder, and a misfire or a lumpy beat on a four loses a quarter of the cycle where on a single it loses the lot. So its roughness effects are about 40% of a quad's (`misfireRoughness` 0.06, `cycleJitter` 0.07, `pulseStrengthJitter` 0.18, `roughHunt` 0.12, `erraticHunt` 0.07, `stumbleRate` 0.2) and it starts less rough (`roughnessRange` 0.1 to 0.2). Wear still roughens it as it does a quad. Neither change checked in the running game.
- **Four cylinders, second go, after Jamie tried it again.** It still ran too rough, revving sounded strange, its tyres sat in the ground and it flickered behind the seats.
  - *The engine fires once per cylinder.* A new engine param, `cylinders` (default 1, the Land Rover 4), makes the cycle four evenly spaced firings, each its own pulse. Each firing's chances (catching, popping, stalling on water or diesel) and its kick to the revs are a quarter of a cycle's, so a misfire costs a quarter of the beat. In a seeded test at the same roughness the idle's rpm spread is under 70% of a single's. Smoke and the run-on are shared out the same way. Its vibration's `firingsPerCycle` goes back to 1, since the pulses already come four to a cycle.
  - *Roughness cut much further*: `wearRange` 0.04 to 0.12 (was 0.2 to 0.45), `roughnessRange` 0.04 to 0.1, `wearPerMinute` 0.0003, `misfireRoughness` 0.03, `cycleJitter` 0.04, `pulseStrengthJitter` 0.1, `roughHunt` 0.04, `erraticHunt` 0.03, `stumbleRate` 0.1.
  - *Sound*: the strange revving was `pulsePitchFollow` 1 squeezing a whole 230 ms four-beat cycle into the engine's cycle: at 3,000 rpm it played six times too fast, resonances and all. The idle is now cut into single beats (`cycleSplit` 4, 392 beats of about 55 ms, labelled 1,810 to 2,190 rpm, four times the engine's), one played per firing at nearly its own speed (`pulsePitchFollow` 0.25). In an offline render through the worklet the beat rate follows the firing rate (19 Hz at idle, 61 Hz part open, 140 Hz at 4,200 rpm) while the sound's centre of brightness only rises from about 1.7 to 2.5 kHz.
  - *Sitting in the ground*: the physics took the layout's wheel radius and the suspension length unscaled, so at `scale` 1.15 it stood on 0.38 m wheels while the model drew 0.44 m ones, 9 cm into the ground. A layout's own wheel radius is now scaled with the chassis (only the UTV and the Land Rover set one; the UTV is scale 1), and the spec's `suspensionRestLength` is 0.23 and `wheelRadius` 0.437. A test checks the physics ground meets the model's.
  - *Flicker behind the seats*: the rear window was a plane laid on the cab's rear panel, the same depth exactly. The panel is now four pieces round an opening with the glass set in it, and the seat backs are 4 cm further forward so their tops, leant back, stay clear of it.
  - *No room to get off*: the spots you step off to are a quad's (1 m to the side, 1.7 m behind, 1.8 m in front) and at 1.15 scale the Land Rover's physics box reaches 0.92 m to the side and 2.07 m fore and aft, so every spot was inside it. Each spot is now pushed out until you stand clear of the vehicle's own box (`vehicle/dismount.js`), never pulled in, so the quads are unchanged; the tractor's spot behind moves out from 1.7 to 1.88 m.
- **Idle pace and top speed, third go.** Jamie: the idle still sounded a bit quick, and it should top out at 50 to 55 mph.
  - *Idle*: the Jeep's beats in the bank run at a median of 500 rpm (474 to 528 for the middle 80%), but the Land Rover idled at 520 to 580, so every beat came 4 to 16% early and was clipped short. It now idles at 470 to 510 (`idleRpmWarm` 490), and a test keeps its range within 8% under and 5% over the recording's median.
  - *Gearing*: 4.5, 8, 14 and 24 m/s (was 2.6, 5, 9 and 14.5), even steps of about 1.75 like the real box's, with `maxSpeed` 24. The drive fades as (speed / top) squared, so flat out it settles just under 24 m/s, about 52 mph; not measured in the game. Torque shares unchanged. `steerSpeedReduction` 0.08 (was 0.05), so at 50 mph it has about 11 degrees of lock, not 15.
- **Half pace, fourth go.** Jamie: still far too fast, try half speed. A new audio param, `pulseDivide` (default 1), sounds only every Nth firing and gives it N firings' time, so with `pulseStretch` 1 each beat is stretched to fill it: the recording at 1 / N of its pace and lower. The Land Rover has 2. Offline through the worklet the warm idle beats at 9 Hz (was 19) and flat out at 70 (was 140), as loud as before. Found while checking: started cold it only catches on full choke, and the choke holds it at about 1,130 rpm through most of its five-minute warm-up, 2.3 times the recording's pace, which is probably much of what sounded fast. The choke is unchanged for now.
- **A third, and a steadier idle, fifth go.** Jamie: half pace sounded far better, maybe slower still, a third; and the recording wanders up and down while it idles. `pulseDivide` is 3. Mapped through the Jeep recording in half-second windows: its beat holds at 60 to 61 ms from 7 to 20.5 s, but the old 3 to 27 s cut took in the start, where the beat is 58 to 59 ms, and 21 to 27 s, where it jumps between 40, 48 and 55 ms and the level drops by a third. Following each beat from 6.5 to 21 s, the windows starting near 15.4 s have the evenest timing and strength (strength varying 7 to 9% against 14% over 7 to 20.5 s), so the idle is now just 15.4 to 18.4 s: 45 beats. There its pattern repeats every three beats (correlation 0.28 at 182 ms against 0.13 at four beats), so it's cut in threes (`cycleSplit` 3) and still read in order. Offline the idle beats 5.3 times a second, its loudness steady to about 5% from one 200 ms block to the next.
  - *Cutting off*: Jamie heard it hit a hard edge and cut off at idle. The worklet never slowed a beat below 0.45 of its speed, and a third of the pace needs about 0.32 (a 61 ms beat filling three firings, about 190 ms), so each beat stopped after about 135 ms with a dead gap before the next: silent 23% of the time in the render. That floor is now a setting, `pulseSlowest` (default 0.45, so the quads are unchanged), 0.25 on the Land Rover; re-rendered, nothing at idle is near silent.
- **Checked**: unit tests (crank handle, crash gears, the spec, towing, quirks, the crank's sound event, its parking spot); headless, the game loads without errors and screenshots from the front, back, side and seat. **Not checked**: driving it (gears, the crunch, 4WD in mud), the starting handle in play, the hand throttle, towing the trailer with it, the girls climbing in, its sound by ear or its loudness against the others, and the toolbox panel on it.

## The tractor's heavy noise (TODO 1)

Jamie: the tractor sometimes sounds right and sometimes has a heavy noise over it; at full throttle it sounds right only for a moment now and then.

- **Cause.** Its idle came from two recordings: 637890 (14.8 to 30.6 s, 126 cycles) and 637891 (6 to 11 s, 33 cycles). The pulse sound reads cycles in recording order and only jumps at the end of a stretch or a bad cycle, so it played 637890 nearly all the time, revved or not, with the odd few seconds of 637891. 637890 is the heavy one: it has a drone at 256 to 310 Hz that 637891 hasn't, and at full throttle it played about twice as loud (rms 0.39 against 0.18) and touched the soft clipper.
- **First fix, wrong.** I read it the other way round (637891 has more at 70 to 100 Hz, and I took that for the boom) and removed 637891. Jamie heard it boomy all the time, with the good bit gone. Rendering offline settled it.
- **Fix.** The tractor's idle is 637891's alone, at the level it played at before (`fixedGain` 1.042), which is the sound Jamie picked from renders of the four options. Its 33 cycles loop: every few seconds at idle, about every 2 s at full throttle. No other stretch of 637891 or 637892 sounds like it (637892's steady running was recorded much further off), so a longer one would mean a new recording.
- **Kept for another tractor.** `engine-bank-tractor-eq`: 637890's 15 s EQ'd to 637891's tonal balance by the slicer (`eqMatch`: third-octave spectra matched within 12 dB, so the drone comes down 12 dB at 300 Hz and 80 to 120 Hz go up 6 to 8 dB). It doesn't loop, but it keeps 637890's rattle. It's in the tuning panel's bank list and loads only when a vehicle asks for it (`requestBank` in `engineAudio.js`), so it costs nothing until then.
- **Offline renders.** `node tools/render_engine_sound.mjs <vehicle id> out.wav [--bank ...] [--set param=value]` runs the real engine model and worklet in Node through idle, full throttle, idle, half and full again, in a few seconds and without a browser. It feeds a diesel its diesel and takes the middle of the spec's roughness range, as the UTV's renders found they must.
- A test checks that every bank takes each kind of idle from one recording. All the others already did.
- Checked: unit tests and renders (`.scratch/tractor-sound/heavy-noise/`, untracked). Not checked: the tractor in the game by ear.

## The UTV's boom (2026-10-08)

Jamie found the UTV boomy too. Rendered with `tools/render_engine_sound.mjs`, its idle matches the raw Gator recording (149193) almost exactly (spectral centroid 309 against 311 Hz), so the boom is in the recording: it was made on the vehicle and has a broad hump from 80 to 140 Hz, centred near 110 Hz and about 10 dB above 160 to 250 Hz.

- New audio params `pulseBoomCut` (dB, default 0, so nothing else changed) and `pulseBoomFreq` (Hz, 110): a peaking cut (Q 1.4) on the pulse sound at every rpm. A test plays a recorded tone through the worklet: 12 dB at 110 Hz leaves under 0.4 of it, and a 600 Hz tone is barely touched.
- Rendered at 6, 10 and 14 dB, each with `recVolume` raised so it idles as loud as before (rms 0.14). Jamie picked 14: `recVolume` 1.12 (was 0.72). Centroid at idle 530 Hz (was 309), at full throttle 716 (was 498); full throttle is about 1 dB louder than before.
- Not changed: as it revs, each firing plays only the loudest beat of a Gator cycle, so it can sound like a big single ("The UTV idles at its recording's speed"). Not checked: the UTV in the game by ear.

## The east fields and the farm road (TODO 2)

- Agreed with you: the farm grows east only, keeping the grass field, its hedges, the south gate, the track, bogs, bales and yard where they were; north (the tree line) and south (the hill, TODO 3) are left for later. The rest of the bigger-farm idea is split into TODO 3 to 8 (the hill, buildings and start spots, the bell, a map, a cattle grid, the girls on tarmac).
- Shape (`layout.farm`, `layout.road`, `src/world/fields.js`, pure, tests in `tests/fields.test.js`): the farm is x -80 to 200, z -80 to 80 (280 by 160 m, 1.75 times the grass field). The road leaves through a gateway in the grass field's east hedge at z = 2, 16 m past the track's far bend, runs to x = 184 (4.6 m of tarmac) and ends in a hardstanding 8 m in radius. Hedges run either side of it to x = 176 with a 2.8 m grass verge (1.6 m at first: the hedge blocks and bushes then nearly closed over the tarmac in a screenshot), two 6 m gaps each side into the fields, and open ends round the hardstanding. The fields are 120 by about 77 m. Hedge lines are now a list (`fields.hedges`, each with its gaps) that `props.js` builds from, colliders in pieces of at most 20 m so they follow the ground.
- Terrain: the detailed square goes from 200 to 420 m (420 segments, still 1 m; about 177,000 vertices, from 40,000), centred, so it already covers the ground through the south gate. The ground canvas goes from 2048 to 4096 to keep about 10 pixels a metre. The rise towards the distant hills is measured from the farm's edge rather than the grass field's, which leaves every height inside the old farm, and outside it to the west, north and south, as it was. The road is laid level across, following the ground along its middle, with a 3 m shoulder. The neighbouring patchwork now starts 6 m beyond the farm's hedges in the ground texture as well as the outer ring (`patchwork.js`, shared), so the band of plain grass outside the hedges is gone; the outer ring goes out to 1000 m. No distant tree clumps within 40 m of the farm or on the ground through the south gate.
- Surfaces (`src/world/surfaces.js`, "Surfaces" folder): factors on grass. Tarmac grips 1.3 (1.15 wet, before the usual wet loss), stubble 1 dry and 0.85 wet. "Rolls freer" is the drag: rolling resistance is only a 0.4 brake impulse, so the factor (stubble 0.75, tarmac 0.6) scales the body's linear damping too, which is most of what slows a quad. Grip goes per wheel through `surfaceGrip`'s new `firm` argument (it scales the grass part, not the mud); the drag is judged at the quad's middle. Stubble wears into soft ground and bogs like grass; the road never does.
- Mud on the road (`src/world/roadMud.js`, "Road mud" folder): each tyre carries 0..1 of mud, picked up on ground over 0.3 muddy (a few metres of the track loads it), 2% wiped off per metre on grass, and on tarmac shed at 1 - e^(-d/20) a metre, which leaves under 5% after 60 m. What it sheds lands on a 0.5 m grid over the road as a smear, three times the mud shed. A smear counts as 0.75 of mud for grip (so `World.mudAt` sees it, and the ride sounds hear dirt) and is drawn in the terrain shader as brown, darker where thick. Rain washes 0.004 a second at full rain; dry, it fades over hours.
- Tyre squeal (`src/vehicle/tyreSqueal.js`, "Tyre squeal" folder; volume `tyreSquealVolume` 0.5 in the audio settings): on tarmac only, the largest of a slide (slip angle 0.08 to 0.3 rad, faded in from 2 to 7 m/s), braking (lever 0.6 to 1, at 0.7 of a slide's loudness) and wheelspin (0.2 to 0.7). The sound is noise through two narrow resonances near 900 and 1900 Hz plus two slightly detuned tones, the pitch wavering. Tarmac also rumbles more quietly than the track and has 85% fewer small bumps.
- The girls: their drives may go anywhere inside the farm's hedges (6 m in); her loops are still checked by casting at the hedges, so a loop stays in the field she's in unless a gap lines up. Wandering on foot stays in her field. Walking anywhere goes by the gateways: `fields.route` is a Dijkstra over the dozen gateway points (one each side of a gap), and `walkToward` heads for the next one. A parking drive (bringing a quad home, putting one in the row) gets the same gateway points put in front of its route. The runaway tractor's distance to the hedge is the first hedge along its heading, through the gaps (`fields.hedgeAhead`). Not done: a race or drag race called while a girl is out in the east fields doesn't route her back, so she's likely to time out and the race is called off.
- A bug found on the way: the ground-wear texture was written x-major but sampled with u along x, so worn patches were drawn mirrored across the diagonal (wear at (20, -10) showed at (-10, 20)). Grip and bogs were right; only the drawing was wrong. The grid is now a rectangle over the whole farm (140 by 80 cells) laid out row by z, and a test checks the mirror point is clear.
- Straw stalks (`buildStubble`, 45,000 instanced tufts in the drill rows) draw each face twice, once each way round, front side only: on a double-sided material their up-pointing normals flip on the back faces, and half the field's stalks came out dark in a screenshot.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless load with the overlay hidden (no errors or warnings, about 3.5 s to load): views down the road from the gateway, over the fields from above and across the stubble at eye height. Not checked: riding it (how the grip, the drag and the squeal feel and sound), mud smears building up in play, the girls actually walking or driving between fields, the frame rate with the bigger terrain on a slower machine, and the 4096 ground texture's memory (about 90 MB with mipmaps).

## Two different stubble fields, and hedges that don't show through (TODO 2, follow-up)

- Jamie: the two new fields were the same, and the hedges and bushes clashed and bled through all along them (and probably always had).
- The fields, agreed: both stay stubble (the dust, TODO 9, wants that) but differ in crop, drilling and lie (`layout.stubbleFields`). North is barley: ground #a7a07c, stalks 5 to 10 cm, less saturated, 1.6 times as thick, rows and tramlines along z, tramlines every 20 m. South is wheat as before: rows along x, every 24 m. The lie (`createFieldShape` in `fields.js`, used by `terrainHeight.js`): the north field has an elliptical hollow at (140, -44), 26 by 16 m and 1.3 m deep, painted darker and greener; the south field rises 4 m from 3 m off the road to its far hedge, faded in over its first 25 m from the grass field so the hedge between them has no step (a test checks there's under 0.3 m across each hedge a metre either side, and the road stays level across). Both are gentle enough to leave the girls' driving alone (the steepest is about 6%).
- The hollow holds the wet: `GroundWear` takes a `soak(x, z)` (0..1, 1 at the bottom of the hollow) and judges a bog there on wetness times 1 + `soakGain` (1) times soak, capped at 1, so half-wet ground in the hollow bogs as if soaked. In the dry it makes no difference.
- The hedges: the hedge, bush and oak leaf materials come out of their models as alpha-blended. Three.js draws those without writing depth, and each kind is one instanced mesh whose instances it never sorts, so a hedge block or bush further away could be drawn over a nearer one, all along every hedge. The leaf texture's alpha is cut-out (half the pixels 0, half 255, 2% in between), so `cutOutLeaves` (`assets.js`, tested) makes every blended, textured material a cut-out on load: no blending, depth written, `alphaTest` 0.5 with alpha-to-coverage for soft edges under the antialiasing. A plain cut-out made distant oaks look bare, because the smaller mipmaps average the alpha below 0.5; the alpha is scaled up by a quarter per mip level in the shader (Ben Golus's fix), which brought them back.
- This is probably what TODO 16 ("Fix the hedges and bushes around the side") was about; left open until Jamie has looked.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless load with the overlay hidden: the hedge along the road from the north field and the grass field's north-east corner (no blocks showing through, trees in full leaf at distance), the farm from above, and each field at eye height. Not checked: the hollow turning to bog in rain in play, riding up the south field's rise, or the alpha-to-coverage edges on a display without antialiasing.

## The hill through the south gate (TODO 3)

- Agreed with Jamie before building: the south gate is swung open and tied back against the hedge for good, an ordinary gateway for quads, the girls' routes and the runaway tractor. Tipping comes from a rider-weight toppling torque that acts only through gravity (slopes), not from a higher centre of mass, so cornering on the flat is unchanged; a girl on the back adds to it. The girls' own drives stay on the farm; following you or in convoy they come down the lane and through the ford, then pull up at the foot of the pasture and wait, with a couple of lines each; on foot they walk the lane and the bottom of the pasture; on the back they go anywhere, with a couple of lines each on a steep slope.
- Shape (`layout.hill`, `src/world/hill.js`, pure, tests in `tests/hill.test.js`): the lane runs straight south from the gate (x = -30) to z = 134 between hedges 4.6 m either side of its middle, 3.3 m tall (the farm's are 2.4). Its bed falls steadily 2.5 m to the valley floor and runs 1 m deeper again along its middle, so the banks are about 1.7 to 2 m high halfway down. A first try sank it a fixed depth and eased that out at the end, which left a hump where it came out of the cutting; now the land beside it drops down the valley side to meet the bed instead. The stream wanders 2.5 m either side of z = 140, 3.2 m across, 0.75 m deep with 0.32 m of water; the ford is 6.8 m across, 0.42 m deep with 0.18 m of water and a gravel bed, muddy (0.8) for 6 m either side. The pasture is x -96 to 34, z 134 to 203 (the detailed terrain stops at 210). Beyond the stream it climbs in six banks measured from the stream's far edge, 18.2 m between them and 19.3 m from the ford to the top with the farm's own rolling; the two big ones rise 5 and 5.5 m over 11 and 12 m, so the steepest ground is 0.74 to 0.79 (36 to 38 degrees) over a metre, against 0.39 at most anywhere on the farm (a puddle's or the track's edge). Towards the east end every bank spreads out to 2.4 times as wide, the gentle way up at 0.47 at its steepest (25 degrees); 18 m in under 60 m can't be much gentler. A little tussocky rough on top (9 cm). The hill's shape fades into the neighbouring farmland over 40 m beyond its hedges, so it rolls away into the country on the outer ring. A test checks every height on the farm is exactly as it was.
- Fields: the lane and the pasture are areas (`lane`, `hill`) with their own hedges, so `route` goes grass, gate, lane, pasture. The south gate's gap is no longer `shut` (the road's big gate is), so the runaway tractor reads it as open and can roll on down the lane. Hedge colliders are now cut short wherever the ground under them climbs more than half a metre, so they follow the hill. The neighbours' patchwork (ground texture, outer ring, distant trees) is measured from the farm and the hill together (`fields.outside`).
- Props (`src/world/hillProps.js`): the stream's water is a ribbon along it, level across. Gorse (`hill.gorseAt`: on ground steeper than about 0.3 to 0.55, in clumps) is 260 instanced clumps of seven dark lumps with the odd yellow face, each with a short cylinder collider. Bracken (`hill.brackenAt`, patches over the rough above the foot) is 4,500 plants of curved fronds, a quarter turning russet, no collider. 16,000 tussocks over the pasture and the lane's banks and middle strip. The wood is 34 oaks from the farm's models with colliders and 60 bushes under them, plus five hedgerow oaks. The gate's leaf is swung back into the field and tied to a stake with orange baler twine, with its own collider; the jerry can spot "by the field gate" moved 2.4 m west to clear it. A first look showed the water as a grey band like concrete and the gorse as yellow-green boulders (yellow vertices bled across whole faces); the water is now darker and browner, the gorse coloured face by face, darker, with 7% of faces in flower.
- Tipping (`src/vehicle/topple.js`, tested; `riderTopple` 90 kg m and `backTopple` 0.8 in the vehicle params): a torque of `load` times g times the chassis's up vector crossed with down, applied each physics step while someone's at the controls or on the back. Static tip angles for a 300 kg quad with its centre of mass 0.28 m up: 60 degrees side-on with nobody aboard, 38 ridden, 31 two up; backwards, 46 ridden. Measured headless on the steepest bank (36.5 degrees), the red quad placed side-on and left 3.5 s: on its own it stayed up (leaning 38 degrees on its springs); ridden, it rolled onto its roof; nose-up and ridden, it stayed. The kids' quad has 40 (a grown-up on it would tip it at 17 degrees with 90). The mini moto and the ATC have 0: their tuning already counts the rider, and TODO 23 is their proper model. The tractor, the UTV and the Land Rover keep 90, which hardly moves them at their weights. Cornering on the flat is unchanged; the farm's slopes (6 degrees at most) give under a fifth of the tipping push.
- The girls (`followWay` in `companionDriving.js`, `followTarget` in `herDriving.js`, tests in `tests/follow.test.js`): following you, she now heads for the next gateway on the way to you (passing it within 3 m), which also helps following into the east fields. Once you're past the foot of the hill (7 m beyond the stream's far edge, `hill.pastFoot`) she goes to a spot just through the ford and waits, says so once (`hillWait`) and follows again when you come back down. Wandering on foot in the pasture keeps to a strip just past the water by the lane. Her own drives were already kept inside the farm. Walking to you, she will still walk up the hill: stopping her short would leave her never arriving for whatever she was walking over for.
- Lines (`src/npc/hillLines.js`, recorded): `hillWait`, three each, a shout with a 60 s cooldown; `hillSteep`, three each, on the back when the quad is more than about 28 degrees off upright and moving (`voiceSteepUpY` 0.88), 20 s cooldown.
- Ground wear stops at the farm's hedges (the wear grid covers the farm only), so the hill doesn't churn up. Not done: start spots or jerry cans on the hill.
- Checked: unit tests; one headless load with the overlay hidden (no errors or warnings) with views through the gate, down the lane, at the ford, up the hill and back over the farm from the top, and the toppling above; two views again after the colour fixes. Not checked: riding it with real controls (how the climb, the wheelspin and a roll feel), a girl actually following you down the lane and waiting at the ford in play, the lines by ear, the frame rate with the extra instances, and the girls walking up the hill on foot.

## Rolling back on the hill (TODO 3, follow-up)

- Jamie: stopped on the hill with the brake off, the quad didn't roll back. Since the handbrake item (TODO 16), a quad with someone at the controls, stopped and off the throttle, held itself on any slope (the `parkingBrake` drag and the physics' parked hold). That was there to stop the raycast wheels creeping down the farm's gentle slopes. Now (`holdsItself` in `src/vehicle/handbrake.js`, tested) it only does so where its nose is up or down less than `selfHoldSlope` 0.1 (about 6 degrees, a sine). Every slope on the farm is gentler (the yard is 6 to 8%), and there the tyres' rolling resistance would hold a real quad anyway. On the hill's banks an automatic's clutch is out at idle, so it rolls back unless you hold `S` or set the parking brake. The tractor and the manual quads, sat in gear, still hold on any slope.
- Checked headless, you on the red quad facing up a 34% bank: off the throttle it rolled back 11 m in 3 s and stopped on the gentler ground below; holding `S` it slid under half a metre.
- The ford drowning the engine is as the drowning model has it (`src/engine/water.js`): the ford's water counts as deep as a puddle's middle, and it's about 7 m across. Under 4 m/s nothing gets in; at about 5 m/s it splutters; from about 6.5 m/s (15 mph) a crossing takes in enough to stall it. Left as it is.

## The bell by the barn doors (TODO 5)

- Where: on a timber post 0.7 m out from the barn's north-east corner, just past where the north door leaf is slid back (`layout.bell`, x -58.1, z 65.5), with its arm out towards the yard, so it's on the way out of the barn and in sight from the yard. The bell is about 0.32 m across, hung 2 m up on a yoke with a lever and a rope down to 1.1 m. Only the post is a collider.
- Ringing: look at it and press `E` (any time, hands full or not). It's a damped pendulum (`BellSwing` in `src/world/bell.js`, pure, tests in `tests/bell.test.js`): a pull adds `kick` 0.5 rad of swing in the direction it's going, up to `maxSwing` 0.75; it dies away over `swingDecay` 1.6 s; the clapper strikes at each end of the swing until it's under `strikeBelow` 0.1 rad. One pull gives five or six strikes, each softer, over about 2.5 s. The swing period is 0.9 s, so about two strikes a second.
- Sound: made in the game, no recording (`bellStrike`). Nine partials of a cast bell (hum an octave under the 560 Hz prime, the minor-third tierce, quint, nominal and the high clang), each a pair of sines a fraction of a hertz apart so it beats, high ones dying first, plus a 4 ms tick of the clapper. Made once and played per strike at 35 to 100 per cent by strength, through a panner at the bell (`refDistance` 8 m). No barn echo, since it's outside.
- The girls: ringing starts the same recall `N` did (`recallGirls`). They hear it anywhere on the farm, as the TODO said; nothing about distance. Rung again while they're on their way, it doesn't restart the recall (which would have them say `recalled` again); it gives them the full two minutes again, and a girl not there yet may say a `bellAgain` line (0.6 chance, with her usual cooldown). One bell line each added to `recalled`, three `bellAgain` each; both banks re-recorded.
- `N` is gone from the keys and the help. "Call the girls back to the barn" stays in the tuning panel's Girls folder, calling the recall directly with no bell. Tunables under "Bell".
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless: the bell shows on its post beside the door leaf, looking at it from 1.5 m gives "Ring the bell to call the girls back", `E` starts the recall and the bell swings (the rope swings out with it) and stops after about 3 s. Not checked: how it sounds (first guesses at pitch and loudness, all tunable), the girls arriving after a bell, and whether the bell is easy to spot from across the yard (it's small from 10 m).

The barn find shows as "Under the tarp" in grey until the tarp is off, so the map doesn't give away which quad it is. Off the map, a marker is pinned to the edge with a dotted ring. The hose and the tow chain aren't shown: one lives on its reel at the tank, the other on the tractor.

Nothing is drawn while it's shut. The map sits under the prompt, the toasts and the captions, so you can still read what the girls say. Tunables under "Map": `size` (0.88 of the shorter side of the screen), `opacity` (0.96) and `markerScale` (1).
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless load with the overlay hidden, pressing `M`: the map opens with 20 markers and shuts again on a second press, with no errors. In the screenshot the "Barn" and "Yard" names were under the yard's quads, "Farm road" sat on the hedge line and "Field shed" on the shed; all moved. Not checked: how readable it is on a small or very wide screen, and whether the yard's quads, close together at about 2 px a metre, are easy enough to tell apart (`markerScale` makes them bigger but they then overlap more).

## The cattle grid (TODO 1)

- The grid fills the grass field's east gateway (`layout.road.grid`, `src/world/cattleGrid.js`, pure, tests in `tests/cattleGrid.test.js`): 3.2 m along the road, tubes every 0.16 m (20 of them), 0.3 m of pit beyond the tarmac each side so it is 5.2 m across inside a 10.2 m gap. Post-and-rail wings with colliders close the rest of the gap, so the grid is the way through for a quad; the girls' routes and the hedge gap are unchanged and they cross it like any other ground. The ground under it stays solid and level (the wheels ride it flat, the tubes stand 6 cm proud), so there is no new physics: it is all feel and sound.
- Feel (`VibrationModel.gridJolts`, params `gridGain` 2.5, `gridFullSpeed` 8, `gridMaxRate` 28 in the Vibration folder): a run of small knocks into the same springs as the engine's shake, at the rate a wheel meets bars (speed over pitch) but never above 28 a second, since the springs can't tell faster ones apart; the size rises with speed and with the share of wheels on the bars. Unlike the ground jolts, which only show from outside, it shakes the camera and bars when you are riding. Every vehicle gets it.
- Sound (`RideAudio`, `gridVolume` 1.2 in the audio settings): a high short knock for each knock-rate tick, the odd rattle, and the road roll louder and brighter while any wheel is on the bars.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how it looks (the pit, kerbs and wings), or how the shake and the knocks feel and sound at different speeds; the numbers are guesses to tune by ear.

## The girls on the tarmac (TODO 2)

- Pure logic in `src/npc/tarmac.js` (tests in `tests/tarmac.test.js`), lines in `src/npc/tarmacLines.js`, tunables in the Tarmac folder. The tarmac is the road's carriageway only (`layout.road`, `halfWidth` either side of `z`, between `fromX` and `toX`), not the verge.
- Pace: a girl driving on it settles (0.6 a second, so no jump) to 1.7 times her cruising speed, and the speed that frightens her rises 1.6 times, since a smooth straight road is easy. Off the road it eases back. Not applied to races (already flat out), parking, following you (she matches your pace) or the tractor. She remarks as she joins the road (`roadOn`, 60% of the time, both girls).
- Jess egging you on (`roadEgg`, at most every 40 s): she is driving on the road within 45 m of you while you are on it, or she is on your back and you are doing over 7 m/s.
- Amy telling you off (`roadScold`, at most every 30 s): your tyres squeal on the tarmac (the same `squealLevel` the ride sound uses, over 0.35) for half a second. She says it from your back, or from within 25 m if she is stood about and not driving. Only Amy scolds; Jess only encourages.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how the faster pace looks and feels, or whether 1.7 times is right across confidence levels; the numbers are guesses to tune.

## Dust off the stubble (TODO 3)

- Pure logic in `src/world/dust.js` (tests in `tests/dust.test.js`), the cloud itself in `src/effects/dustCloud.js`, tunables in the Dust folder. A quad's `dust` (0..1) is the stubble under it, times how dry the ground is (full at wetness 0, none from `maxWet` 0.25 up), times its speed (nothing under 3 m/s, full at 10). Only stubble raises it, so grass, mud and the road do not. Rain stops it by itself, which is why there is no separate weather rule.
- The cloud (`inCloud`) is a strip straight behind the quad, 16 m long and 1.4 m wide at the quad, widening 0.12 m a metre. It reuses the smoke plume's geometry (`inPlume`) with no wind: dust is dragged along in the quad's wake, so it sits behind it whichever way the air blows. A quad's `dustIn` is the thickest cloud it is in from another quad; the game sets it each frame (`updateDustClouds`).
- Air filter: `dustRate` 0.012 a second at a full cloud, five times `mudRate`, so about 80 s from clean to choked, against seven minutes in full mud. A quad takes the larger of its own dust and the cloud it is in, so riding fast on dry stubble clogs it anyway and following closely is no worse than leading. The usual rules hold: engine running, over 1.5 m/s.
- The girls (`followTarget` in `herDriving.js`): behind a lead with dust over `followDustLevel` 0.25 her following gap is multiplied by `followDustGap`: Amy 2.2 (about 17.6 m, just outside the 16 m cloud), Jess 0.6 (4.8 m, deep in it). It stacks with the two-stroke smoke gap. Each says `followDust` once when she first notices (again for the next cloud), and inside `dustCoughWithin` 12 m she gets `dustCough` every frame she is in, which the voice's cooldown spaces out (30 s for Amy). Amy's cough line is there for when she is stuck close; she rarely is.
- Look: up to 90 tan puffs a second at a full cloud, 1.1 m across growing to 3.5 m over about 3 s while they thin, lifted off the ground behind the quad and carrying a quarter of its speed. 260 puffs a quad. Cosmetic only.
- Left out: the girls' own dust on their way to a field (they ride the same, so it clogs their filters, but nothing reads their filters), dust on the quad's paint, and dust in the camera's view while you follow a girl in a cloud.
- Checked: unit tests and a production build. Not checked in the running game: how the cloud looks (colour, density, size against the stubble), whether 80 s to choke feels right, or how Amy's 17.6 m gap copes with a fast ride. The numbers are guesses to tune.

## Falling out (TODO 4)

Decided with you: friction from a bad race, a quad taken off its owner and the digs they trade; four to six minutes; they won't share a quad, convoy or stand close, each tells you her side, the banter turns sharp; getting them together, a nudge or taking a side changes how long; taking a side moves your favour; no HUD sign.

- `src/npc/fallingOut.js` (pure, tested). One friction number for the pair. Race +3.5, a borrowed quad +5 (the existing `upset` event in `ownership.js`, when the one on it is the other girl), each bicker where they trade digs +1.5 (idle, favoured, rivals, watching, sharing, riding, corrected, swapped, stuck). It drains at 0.02 a second. At 8 they fall out, but never in the first 120 s of a game or within 180 s of making up. Friction does nothing while they are already fallen out. The numbers are a first guess: one race plus one borrowed quad does it, digs alone take a long session.
- It lasts 240 to 360 s. Stood about together (both on foot, within 10 m of each other and 14 m of you) it runs down four times faster. "Make it up" on a girl takes 35% off what is left; both girls within 40 s and they make up at once. "You're right" (only once she has told you her side) adds 60 s and moves 2 favour from her friend to her.
- The one at fault says sorry first: the race's loser, the girl who took the quad, or a random one for digs.
- What changes: `Companion.avoidsFriendOn` makes `canRidePillion`, `canRideRackOn` and `canDrive` false for a quad her friend has claimed, so no pillion, rack or driving with her friend on it. Convoys don't start. Called back to the barn they stand 7 m apart instead of 2.8 m. Closer than 4 m, one walks off to about 7 m (at most every 8 s). The friendly `idle`, `favoured` and `rivals` banter stops and a new `fallenOut` kind replaces it. A quad they were already sharing when the row started is not broken up.
- Lines (`fallingOutLines.js`): a bicker for each cause as it starts (`fellOut`), six sharp ones (`fallenOut`), four apologies (`madeUp`), and for each girl `sideRace`, `sideBorrow`, `sideDigs`, `sideThanks` and `nudged`.
- Left out: no pause on a race or drag already running when the row starts; a row doesn't stop the girls following you or riding separately.
- Checked: unit tests only (friction, timings, siding, nudging, line coverage, the ride block). Not checked in the running game: the pace of friction in a normal session, the walk-apart by eye, the wheel options, or the lines in the recorded voices.

## Round the back of the barn (TODO 6)

Decided with you: she asks when she likes you a lot (an absolute score as well as the favoured standing); behind the barn is only implied, a fade to black, her line and then more of her; the quad scene after two visits is the same with a longer fade; the lock-in means her favour never drops; only one girl can be locked in, and the other stops asking; the bad thing is throwing her off.

- `src/npc/romance.js` (pure, tested). She asks only when the game is over 300 s old, you are on foot with nothing else on, she is stood about within 9 m, not fallen out, her favour score is 12 or more and she is the favoured one. One invitation open at a time, 420 s between asks, 600 s after you say not now, and an open one lapses after 75 s (135 s for the quad, she has to walk there). First two asks are the barn, the third is her quad. Her favourite quad has to be parked, upright and free, or she doesn't ask (and tries again after 60 s).
- Taking it up is a wheel option, not a key: "Go round the back of the barn" / "Not now", and for the quad "Stand next to her on the quad" once she is sat on the back and you are within 3.5 m on foot. She says `inviteBarn` or `inviteQuad` and a toast tells you to talk to her. Accepting stops the invitation lapsing.
- The scene (`Tryst`): fade out 1 s, you are placed 3.2 m behind the barn's west wall facing her (the quad one stays where you are), fade in 1.2 s, 4 s of scene with her `trystBarn` or `trystQuad` line, fade out 1.5 s, 3 s of black, fade in with her `afterBarn`. The quad's: fade out 2.5 s, 6 s of black, fade in 2.5 s. You can't move or look during it. The fade is a black div (`hud.setFade`). Nothing else is shown.
- Locking in: `Favour.lock` keeps her score at 15 or above, ignores anything negative and always reports her as favoured. Her friend's standing against her goes to neglected on its own, so she sulks (`sulkLockedOut`), refuses a little and the old neglected behaviour applies. The first visit or two earn the other girl a `teaseRomance` line 60% of the time. After the quad scene she gets off.
- The bad thing: tipping a quad over while you drive it with her aboard, in any seat (a few seconds' grace, as a tip throws you off first). She says `betrayed`, her favour goes to -8, the lock goes and the visits reset; she won't ask again for 1500 s. I first planned a second one, going round the back of the barn with her friend, but with only one girl able to be locked in the friend has stopped asking by then, so it cannot happen.
- Lines (`romanceLines.js`): 11 situations each for both girls, recorded with the rest.
- Not persisted between games, like the rest.
- Checked: unit tests only (invitation rules, lapsing and cooldowns, visit count to the quad, lock and betrayal, the scene's timeline, favour locking, line coverage). Not checked in the running game: the wheel options, the placement behind the barn (that nothing stands there), the fade by eye, the girl sitting on the quad and stepping off afterwards, the tip detection, or how soon a score of 12 turns up in an ordinary session (a first guess).

## A steadier four-cylinder shake

- Request: the Land Rover's shake was too random for a four-cylinder, too like the quads.
- Each firing kicked the body, bars and head with a random size and a random left or right sign, which suits a lumpy single and not an even four. A new vibration param, `steadiness` (0 to 1, default 0 so the quads and the others are unchanged), pulls the kicks towards a fixed pattern: every kick the same size, the side-to-side parts rocking left then right in turn. The Land Rover has 0.85, so at idle it shudders in a regular rock at the firing rate (about 16 a second at 490 rpm) rather than jittering.
- The engine side was already calm (`pulseStrengthJitter` 0.1, `cycleJitter` 0.04), so only the vibration needed changing. A test shows the spread of the body's roll is under 60% of the random default's.
- Not changed: the tractor and UTV, which are also multi-cylinder; say if they want it too. Not checked in the running game, only in unit tests.
- The UTV (a three-cylinder) has `steadiness` 0.6, a tad rougher than the Land Rover's 0.85, at Jamie's request. The tractor is still random.

## Girls staying full size

- Report: after getting in the Land Rover the girls sometimes ended up too big, and more so each time.
- Cause (found by reading, not reproduced): climbing off uses `Object3D.attach`, which keeps the world transform, so any stretch in the vehicle's chain is baked into the girl's own scale. The vehicle's rotation comes straight from Rapier as a quaternion that is never renormalised, and a slightly long one stretches its matrix; the girl then carries that stretch and takes it into the next vehicle too, so it multiplies.
- Fix: `attachKeepingSize` (`src/npc/attachKeepingSize.js`, tested) attaches and puts her scale back to one, used for every attach of a girl (getting off, sliding off a trailer, shuffling on a quad). The chassis quaternion is also normalised when recorded (`recordTransform`). I didn't confirm the drift in the running game, so if it comes back the cause is somewhere else.

## The green's stutter at high revs (TODO 4)

Jamie: the green's sound isn't right at higher revs; asked what was wrong, choppy and stuttery. Three causes, measured from renders (`node tools/render_engine_sound.mjs green`) and the engine model's firings, each fixed.

- **Its firings were uneven flat out.** Roughness never faded with revs, and the green's (0.55 to 0.7 built in, plus 0.25 to 0.55 of wear in a game) clamps at 1, so flat out each firing's length strayed up to 18%, its strength up to 45%, and about one in six misfired. Fresh in a render: firing period spread 1.46 ms, against the red's 0.18. New engine params `roughRevFade` and `roughRevSmoothRpm` (default 0, so every other vehicle is unchanged): the part of the roughness that is its build, wear and scar evens out as it revs, easing in from idle, by that share at that rpm. The faults (cold, lean, fouled, water, pinking and the rest) still stutter at any revs, and the full roughness still drives the shake, the hunting and whatever reads it. The green uses 0.8 by 3,200 rpm: fresh, its spread flat out is now 0.28 ms; at a typical game's wear about 0.7 ms, with the erratic stumbles that say it wants a service left as they were.
- **Most of its rev recording was thrown away.** Only 234 of the Honda ATV's 730 firings passed the slicer's checks: above about 4,000 rpm the exhaust never goes quiet before the next firing, so "next thump in tail" failed most of them, and the period bounds on the third stretch (19 to 25 ms) didn't cover its start (28). The peaks themselves tracked well; the cuts didn't, wandering about within the gap. New bank option `revTrack`: peaks re-placed within 12% of the median of the nine periods around them (`follow_track`), cut a steady distance before each (`steady_cuts`), and no tail check (`revTail` None). Now 725 of 728 are usable, and firings more than 15% longer or shorter than the one before went from 63% to 10%.
- **Each firing came from somewhere else.** The pulse sound's rev layer picked one of the 8 firings nearest the revs at random every time, from anywhere in 20 s of recording. New audio param `pulseRevFollow` (log rpm ratio, default 0): while the firing after the last one is within it, play that, so the recording reads through as one engine revving and only jumps when the revs leave it behind. The green uses 0.08, as `recFollow` does for the recorded sound. The yellow is unchanged.
- **Measured** (rhythm regularity is the envelope's autocorrelation peak at the firing period, 1 perfectly even): flat out 0.19 before, 0.43 after; half throttle 0.21 and 0.37, where the firing rate now reads about 27 a second (3,300 rpm, as the engine is) and before couldn't be found. The source recording measures 0.67 to 0.85 at those revs. Level unchanged (flat out -16.2 dB, idle -25.8). Half throttle measures darker (spectral centroid 1,000 Hz to 750); I take it the random joins' clicks have gone, as flat out moved the other way, towards the source.
- Renders for listening: `.scratch/green-revs/now.wav` (before) and `after.wav`.
- By ear: Jamie compared the two renders and the after one works better. Not checked: how it sounds in the running game, or whether 0.8 leaves enough of the green's character just off idle. Tests: `tests/engine.test.js` (firings even out flat out, not at tickover; faults kept), `tests/engineBanks.test.js` (the green's revs cut evenly), `tests/engineWorkletSlices.test.js` (reading on, and not without it).

## Frost starts and braking stalls (TODO 3)

- **Frost: the choke couldn't make enough mixture.** A stone-cold engine in a hard frost needs 1.5 times its cold enrichment (`chillChoke`), but full choke only ever supplied `chokeEnrichment` (1.0 for most engines), so the green quad (needs 1.15 x 1.5) could never be started stone cold below about 0 °C, and the red and the Land Rover almost never. New engine param `chillChokeFlow` 0.9: the choke's supply grows by that share of the chill's extra need, so full choke still works but the frost is left needing nearly full choke for longer. Measured at full chill, stone cold, full choke, 20 seeds: green 0 to 20 starts, red 3 to 20, Land Rover 1 to 20 (green takes up to about 11 s of cranking; the UTV was never affected, it runs on glow plugs).
- **UTV: any hard stop stalled it, warm.** Its drivetrain gave the quads' `loadDragRpm` 4000, which pulled a 970 rpm idle below its 450 stall point in about a tenth of a second once the clutch had locked (20 of 20 seeds, every speed and temperature). Now 1,500: warm it never stalls on a full brake; stone cold (temperature 0.02) it stalls about half the time, as the quads do.
- **Land Rover: stalled in every gear, at any speed.** Braking with the clutch out loaded the engine whenever the car was slower than 70% of the gear's top speed (`lugSpeed` 0.7), with a drag (6,000) that killed a 490 rpm idle in a moment. Now the load only comes on below 15% of the gear's top speed, which is where the revs would be near idle anyway, and the drag is 2,200. Warm, braking to a stop with the clutch out: first and second hold on, at gentle or hard braking; third and fourth still stall (you want the clutch there), 20 of 20 seeds either way.
- Tests: `tests/frostAndBraking.test.js`. Not checked: in the running game, or cold Land Rover braking (it needs its choke easing off as it warms, which my sim doesn't do).

## Better bales, bench and ramp (2026-10-08)

You answered "yes, propose others" and "procedural" to the two questions. All three are still procedural, with no new assets and no extra draw calls beyond a few small instanced meshes.

- Round bales (`roundBaleGeometry` in `props.js`, test in `tests/roundBale.test.js`): the plain cylinder is now a 32 by 14 segment body with a 2.5% belly and a rim that eases in over the last 0.2 m to 12% narrower at the ends. Length and radius are unchanged, so the cylinder colliders still match. Straw bales gain loops of baler twine round the middle; silage bales get a wrap texture with overlapping film bands instead of flat black. The Dutch barn's stack shares the same geometry, so it changed too.

The vice has a moving jaw and a handle. A red oil can, two screw jars and a rag sit on top, and the pegboard has a grid of holes (one instanced mesh). The collider is unchanged.

Jamie asked for the vice, Amy's notebook, the hayloft bales and straw, the hayloft edge flicker, the trailer bales and the jerry cans to be done properly.

Loose straw is clumps of stalks (also an instanced mesh), thick beside the stacks, and Jess's falling straw uses the same clumps.
