## Getting on and off, and standing about (TODO 7)

Jamie agreed: poses written in code rather than CC0 clips (Quaternius's library uses a different skeleton, so retargeting would be fiddly); three moves used everywhere; longer moves are fine; a few ways of standing about for each girl.

- **Poses in code** (`src/npc/poseMoves.js`, pure, tested): a pose is bone turns about her own axes plus hands placed by the arm IK that already held the bars (`armIK.js`), optionally with the hand turned (folded arms need the fingers tucked round the other arm, or they stick out past the elbows). A child bone turns about its parent's turned axes, so "bend the knee" means the same however the hip is turned. `CharacterRig.applyPose` layers it on after the clips and before the older offsets and posers, so fidgets, the pillion hold and the grips still sit on top.
- **Moves**: leg over (any quad, the ATC, the mini moto, the front seat), hop on (side-saddle and the front rack: back to the seat, hands behind, a hop, a swivel), step in (the UTV's and Land Rover's bench: foot on the sill, ducking, swung round), climb up (the bale trailer, the tractor's arches and seat, the UTV's bed). Each is keys over 0 to 1 giving the hips' path (`over`, `along`, `lift`), the blend from standing to sitting (`sit`), the turn, and a pose. Authored from the seat's left and mirrored for the right; getting off plays the same keys backwards.
- **The path is the hips', not the root's** (`src/npc/companionMoves.js`): the root goes wherever puts her hips on the path, given how far the Idle and SitIdle clips are blended (`CharacterRig.mix`). That keeps her bottom on the seat at the end however the blend goes, and makes the move land exactly on the seated position, so there's no snap into the seat.
- **Footboard first**: `along` (the fore-and-aft share) lags `over` on a quad, so she stays over the footboard while her leg goes over and only then shifts back onto the pillion. Getting on and stepping off are now from the middle of the footboard (z 0, 0.1 for the front seat) instead of beside the back wheel (z -0.5), where her foot went through the arch.
- **Called off part-way** (asked to get down while still climbing on): getting off runs back from where she'd got to (`from`), so there's no jump.
- Side-saddle, her legs now go over the side she got on from, rather than a side picked at random.
- Timings: 1.8 s on and 1.4 s off a quad (were 1.2 and 1.0), 1.8 s climbing (0.9), 2 s stepping into a bench. Lifts: 0.3 m onto a footrest, 0.32 m onto a sill, the seat's own (0.45 to 0.7 m) for climbing. All in the "Getting on and off" and "Hay ride seat" tuning folders.
- **Sat**: astride a quad the thighs drop 0.3 rad and the knees bend a further 0.25, so the feet sit back on the footrests; a touch more relaxed on a bench; knees together side-saddle.
- **Standing about** (`StandingIdles`): a gap of 2 to 7 s, eased in over 0.7 s, held 4 to 10 s (a stretch 2.2 s), eased out; never the same one twice running; dropped quickly when she does anything else. Amy: arms folded, hands clasped, weight on one foot. Jess: hands on hips, rocking on her heels, scuffing a toe, a stretch, a hand in her back pocket. Lists in `characters.js`. The older standing fidgets (a lean, a look round) still happen on top.
- Checked: unit tests (the moves' data, mirroring, sampling, the idle chooser, and the runner landing on the seat, at the step-off spot and with no jump when called off), and headless screenshots with the game stepped by hand: the leg over on and off from three sides, side-saddle, Jess onto the front, the UTV and Land Rover benches, the UTV's bed on and off, and every standing idle close up. Not checked: the bale trailer, the tractor's arches and seat, the rack, the mini moto and ATC, and a slide off side-saddle at speed. Bodies still pass through the Land Rover's doors (they don't open).

## Sitting a while (after TODO 7)

Jamie liked the idea of seated idles, from the end of TODO 7.

- Same chooser as standing (`StandingIdles`, now given a library), same timings (the "Standing about" folder), with `SEATED_IDLES` in `poseMoves.js` and a `seatedIdles` list per girl in `characters.js`. Amy: arms folded, hands on knees, ankles crossed, leant back on her hands, elbow out. Jess: a jiggling knee, hands behind her head, legs swinging out of step, slouched, leant back, elbow out.
- Where: a bench (the UTV's and Land Rover's seats) or a perch with her legs hanging (the bales, the tip trailer's tub, the UTV's tailgate, the tractor's arches, a quad's front rack). `seatedIdlesFor` keeps each to the seats it suits: leaning back on her hands and swinging her legs only on a perch (a bench has a back and a floor), slouching only on a bench, an elbow out only at the window seat (the left one, by the door). Elbow out never mirrors, so it's always on the door side.
- Not on the pillion or the front seat, where the holding-on and grip poses own her arms; not while getting on or off, lying back on the bales, flailing, or when the ride is jolting hard (`jolt` over 0.6).
- Waves can now be a list with phases, so her legs swing out of step.
- Checked: unit tests, and headless shots of each in the UTV's window seat and on its tailgate, and the elbow on the Land Rover's door. Hands behind the head first sat on top of it, and the elbow first put her hand by her face; both lowered. Not checked: the bales, the tub, the arches and the rack.

## The bucket list (TODO 6)

Decided with you: shown as a list you open with a key (not `H`) that works like a bucket list, with unticked ones showing what to do; nothing persists between games; about 50 or more; each worth 1 to 3 points with a total; a toast and a chime, and no spoken reactions from the girls.

- `src/achievements/achievements.js` (pure, tested). An achievement is a test over the stats, so a new one is one line in `ACHIEVEMENTS`. About 60 in groups: Ride, Tricks, Mud, Company, Places, Jobs. `src/achievements/snapshot.js` reads the running game into a small snapshot each frame, and `Achievements.update` works out the rest itself (riding, distance, top speed, ramp jumps and landings, doughnuts, bogs and how they ended, rolling and righting, time with a girl on the back). One-off events (the bell, the dipstick, a race won) are `achievements.note(...)` calls at the spot in `game.js` where they happen.
- `Tab` opens the list (it was already stopped from moving focus). The panel is in `hud.js`; it refreshes while open when something unlocks. The chime is `AudioHub.chime()`: two soft sine notes, silent when muted.
- The line-up changes each game, so an achievement can name a vehicle it `needs`. If that vehicle isn't on the farm, it is left off the list and out of the total (so "of y" is always achievable). "Full set" means every vehicle on the farm that game, not counting the barn find.
- Jumps: any air time of half a second or more that passes within 30 m of the ramp's lip is a jump; a clean landing is staying upright for 1.2 s after it. A doughnut is turning a full circle (6.28 rad) in one direction at over 2 m/s without the turn stopping. The numbers are in the tuning panel's "Achievements" folder.
- Winning a girl over means being clearly favoured by her at some point (favour.js), so "Everyone's friend" is the two in turn. Romance is not an achievement.
- Not checked: the speed targets (14 and 22 m/s were picked without measuring what each vehicle reaches, so flat out may be out of reach for the slower ones), the doughnut and landing detection by hand on a quad, and how the list looks on a small window. Checked once headless that the game loads and the panel builds with the right count (61 on seed 3), with no page errors, without clicking past the overlay.

**Notebook layout (follow-up).** The first version was a scrolling two-column panel that didn't fit the window. It is now a notebook spread: ruled paper, a red margin, a handwriting-style font stack (Bradley Hand, Segoe Print and the like: no font file, so it falls back to a system cursive), boxes that get a green tick and a strike-through when done (a done item loses its hint line). `src/achievements/pages.js` (pure, tested) packs the list into pages of at most 9 lines, keeping a group on one page where it fits and otherwise running it on as "(cont.)"; two pages make a spread, turned with the arrow keys, and the page is sized in `em` so it scales with the window height (11 to 15 px type). It opens on the first spread each time the game starts and then stays where you left it. Checked headless at 1280x800: the first two spreads fit with room to spare. Not checked: small windows, or a machine without any of the cursive fonts.


## A younger voice for Jess (TODO 1)

Feedback: Jess sounded too old. Her reference was Ruth Golding reading in RP for LibriVox, and Chatterbox copied a voice centred around 170 to 200 Hz.

- Decided with you: audition candidates by ear rather than pick on numbers; Kokoro's British women as the main source; try northern voices too.
- Candidates, each saying the same six Jess lines through Chatterbox at her settings (`.scratch/jess-voices/`): the four Kokoro British women (`bf_alice`, `bf_emma`, `bf_isabella`, `bf_lily`, all southern) and seven young northern women from Edinburgh's VCTK corpus (Manchester, Yorkshire, Newcastle, the North East, Stockton; CC BY 4.0). LibriVox's two accent collections have no northern woman: their Lancashire and Mid-England readers are men. You picked Lily.
- More emotion: a sweep of eight versions of the same lines. Exaggeration also raises her pitch (median 230 Hz at Jess's old lift of 0.1, 294 Hz at 0.6, 311 Hz at 0.9) and Whisper starts mishearing near the top. You picked the livelier reference clip (Lily saying "Oh my god, did you see that? Ha! That was brilliant!…") with a lift of 0.6 and cfg weight 0.25 on every line: median 257 Hz, ranging 181 to 360. In the generator that's `CHARACTER_LIFT` 0.6 and the new `CHARACTER_CFG`; the cap on exaggeration is 2.0 now (it was 1.0), so her scared lines go to 1.5.
- Recording: Jess has 2,213 lines now, so a whole new voice is a long run, resumable from the cache.
  - Memory: one recorder grew past 13 GB. The model is about 4 GB on the GPU (float32: the speech-token model 2.1 GB, the decoder 1.1 GB, Whisper 0.3 GB); the rest was PyTorch keeping each line's GPU scratch memory for reuse, which piles up because lines differ in length (7.9, 8.6, 10.2 GB after three lines). Emptying the cache after each line keeps the GPU side at 3.9 GB and the whole process at 7 to 9 GB, with no loss of speed.
  - Speed: one recorder does a line in about 3.8 s before Whisper's check. Two side by side (`--workers 2`, each taking every other line into the cache) did 24 lines in 53 s, about 1.7 times as fast. Four at once, before the memory fix, pushed other apps into 26 GB of swap and slowed to a line every 10 s.
  - Runaway takes: Chatterbox let every take run to 1000 speech tokens (40 s) whatever the line, and decoding a short line that ran on that far took one worker past 40 GB and twenty minutes. Takes are now capped at the line's longest reasonable length plus a second (`max_seconds`, 25 tokens a second), so a runaway stops early and is thrown out as before.
  - Even so, a worker grows from about 8 GB to 16 GB over a few dozen lines, a leak not found in short tests (Whisper alone and Chatterbox alone each held steady). With `--workers`, each copy now records a batch of 25 lines, exits, and a fresh copy carries on from the cache (`--batch`), so two stay under about 26 GB together. Later batches load the models offline, as a dropped connection to Hugging Face killed one restart.
  - The Mac's idle sleep stalled one run for twenty minutes; long runs go under `caffeinate -i`.
  - The full run: 2,197 of 2,213 lines recorded, about 16 lines a minute with two workers once settled. 16 short shouts came out as babble every time ("Finally!", "Rude.", "Thief!", "Wheeeee!" and the like) and are left to the browser's voice; 33 more are near misses by Whisper's reckoning, listed in the cache's `jess-unclear.txt`. The bank is 44 MB, about the size it was.

## A better tractor model (TODO 4)

Settled with Jamie: it stays an old Fergie (red and grey, no cab, since the sound, dash, gearbox and the girls' climbing are all built round one); better silhouette, better running gear and wear, all three; and front wheels that visibly steer.

- **Silhouette** (`tractorModel.js`): the bonnet is a four-station loft tapering to the nose with a centre seam, side louvres, a radiator cap and an oil bath air cleaner on a pipe; the grille is a chrome frame with black slats and a brass badge; the headlamps sit on stalks; the tank is a separate domed loft behind the bonnet with a filler cap; the gearbox has a clutch bell at the engine end, a hydraulic lift housing behind the seat, a PTO stub, lift arms with rods to the lower links and a top link; the seat is a pressed pan with a turned lip, drain holes, a thin cushion and a coil under it.
- **Running gear:** every wheel is plain pressed steel like an old Fergie's: rim flanges (the outer one rusty), a dished disc with a ring of punched holes (6 at the back, 5 at the front), a cast hub and plain bolts. It first had cast spokes and chrome nuts, which Jamie said looked too posh. The mudguards first floated on one short rod that reached neither the shell nor the body; each now has a bracket on top of the final drive housing, a stay up to a plate under the shell and a brace each way. The front axle has radius rods and a track rod. The front wheels already steered (`Quad` yaws each wheel group); what was missing was anything to see turning, so each front wheel group now carries a yoke and a steering arm on its inside face, which swings with the wheel.
- **Wear:** red oxide primer and rust patches on the bonnet's side, the grille surround, the crown of the bonnet and the outer mudguard lips; oil on the gearbox, the sump and a drip down the grille; mud on the gearbox, the front pedestal and in the drum between the rear lugs (the drum is its own mesh, brown, under the black lugs). All fixed geometry in materials of their own, so it applies whatever body colour the tractor has.
- **Unchanged on purpose:** the physics layout, the anchors, the dash and control positions, and every named part `Quad`, the mounts and the girls look up (`tests/tractorModel.test.js` checks the names, the anchors and that the width and height are still the old ones, since the parking spaces were sized for them).
- **Checked:** unit tests, and one headless look from the front and rear three-quarter views showing the shape, the wheels, the stays and the rolled lips. **Not checked:** the wear patches and oil at close range (they are small and subtle from where I looked), the front steering arms while turning, or the cab-side view from the seat.

## The girls no longer shove the quads (TODO 5)

- Cause: a girl is a kinematic body set to her position each frame, so when she walks, stands or steps up against a quad she overlaps it and Rapier pushes the quad out of the way (kinematic bodies are immovable). The player doesn't, because the character controller stops short of things.
- Fix: her collider has solver groups `0xffff0000` (the same as the player's while stumbling): it makes no contact forces, so it can't move anything. The player's controller still treats her as solid, and her ray probes still see the quads, so her routing is unchanged. The cost is that a quad driven into her passes through rather than stopping.
- `tests/companionNoShove.test.js` shows in Rapier itself that a hand-moved kinematic capsule shoves a dynamic box by default and doesn't with these groups.
- Not done: no extra avoidance logic. The existing probes already stop her walking into quads; the shoving was the contact response when she ended up touching one. **Not checked:** in the running game.

## Pods on the tractor (TODO 6)

- Both pods now fit the tractor. Its model has a `podMount` on a short steel post on the scuttle, to the right of the binnacle so the dials stay clear, leaning back 20 degrees to you. The pod reads the tractor's state like any quad's, so it shows the hours and the odometer the tractor already starts with (the odometer was kept in the background for this).
- Like the rusty quad, the tractor is added after the pods are dealt, so it never starts with one (the seeded placement is unchanged); `PodRack.allow` lets you fit one at the bench. The toolbox shows the pod buttons whenever the vehicle has a mount (`canPod`), so the tractor has them and the UTV still doesn't.
- Not checked: unit tests only. I haven't looked at where the pod sits against the dash and the steering wheel in the running game, or its fit with the lean-in key.

## A real two-wheeler model (TODO 7)

Jamie's calls: the mini moto only for now, with the lean machinery in a pure module the ATC can reuse; balance by torque on two real wheels; power and brake mid-corner decide slides and tucks; a fall throws you off with the engine dead and you right it; a foot goes down at walking pace; the girls ride it with a gentle lean and never fall. This replaces the hidden outriggers and the show-only lean in "The mini moto (TODO 10, part one)".

- **Physics**: `MOTO_LAYOUT` is now two wheels in a line. `BikeLean` (`src/vehicle/bikeLean.js`, pure) takes the chassis's lean and rate (`QuadPhysics.lean()`), the bars, speed, throttle, brake and the tyres' grip, and returns a roll torque, the front wheel's angle and a grip factor per tyre. `Quad.balanceBike` runs it each step and `QuadPhysics.rollTorque` applies it about the chassis's forward axis, so the roll angle is Rapier's own.
- **Lean drives the turn**: the bars ask for a lean (full bars is 40 degrees at 9 m/s or more, less when slower, 0.6 of that for a girl). The front wheel is steered to what that lean turns at, `atan(wheelbase g tan(lean) / v squared)`. From 2.5 m/s up to 5 m/s the steering hands over from the bars to the lean; below 2.5 m/s (foot down) and reversing it is the bars alone and it cannot fall.
- **Grip**: a tyre holds a lean of `tan = gripMu (0.4 + 0.6 grip)`, `grip` being the mean of the touching wheels over dry grass (a rider feels the ground and rides to it, so mud counts for less than raw friction). Full throttle at a full lean uses 55% of the rear's; a full brake uses 65% of the front's. Over the limit a slide builds (about 0.2 to 1.5 a second, by how far over) and takes grip off that tyre, so the rear really steps out in the physics; easing off settles it. A front slide also pulls the lean further over (a tuck). At a full slide, or a lean past 62 degrees, it falls.
- **Falling**: it is pushed over to the side it was leaning until it lies at about 88 degrees, the engine is switched off, `isTipped` is true (so the usual "Right the mini moto" works, with `T` or `E`), and you are thrown clear (`Game.throwOff`, like jumping off, to the low side if there is room). The nearest girl within 40 m says something (`youCameOff`, both banks; **needs re-recording**). Nobody gets hurt and nothing breaks.
- **Who can fall**: only you. A girl riding asks for 0.6 of the lean and cannot slide or tuck; an unattended bike (parked, or carrying on after you jumped off) holds itself upright. A falling girl is a later idea.
- **View**: the camera is held 45% level against the lean (`bikeCameraLevel` in the player params), so a 40 degree lean tips the view about 22 degrees.
- **Tunables**: `DEFAULT_BIKE_PARAMS` and `BIKE_PARAM_RANGES`, in the tuning panel as "Two-wheeler lean". The ATC will want its own geometry (three wheels, tipping rather than leaning) but can use the grip and fall bookkeeping.
- **Measured** (`tests/bikeLeanSim.test.js`, the real Rapier chassis on flat ground, no Three.js): it stands parked and rolls straight with under 0.08 rad of lean; a left bar gives a left lean and a left turn (right likewise); a hard brake in a corner puts it down; the same corner holds on grass and drops it at a quarter of the grip; with `safe` it never falls.
- **Not checked**: the feel on the real farm. I loaded the game headless and saw the moto standing upright with no errors, but I haven't ridden it, so the lean numbers (40 degrees, 55%, 65%, the 62 degree fall) are the first guesses from the simulation. The camera tip, the fall tumble and the girl's lines are unseen and unheard. The tyre gauge now lists two wheels for the moto.

### The quad invitation starts the quad first (romance)

- Request: when she asks you over to her favourite quad, it has to be running; if it isn't she starts it first.
- `Game.romanceGetOnQuad`: if the quad isn't running she walks to it and has a go at starting it (the same attempt as the "Start it" wheel option), trying again up to three times if it won't catch, then gets on the back. She doesn't mind the choke until it's fully warm: at `settleAt` 0.75 engine warmth she takes the choke off and gets on (`Romance` params, in the panel). The invitation's extra time went from 60 to 200 s, because a cold start and warm-up takes most of a minute or more. If you get on it, it goes over or the invitation lapses meanwhile, she stops and the ask is cancelled (a minute's cooldown).
- `Companion.requestStart` takes `then` (called with whether it's running) and `settleAt`; `StartingAttempt` has `settleAt` in its params (unset: she tends it until warm, as before).

## The console (TODO 9)

Request: a console separate from the debug window to set and get any value, put anything anywhere, reset vehicles and girls, set the weather, flight, and turn the girls' autonomy and speech on and off. Lots of commands, Tab completion that goes through what is possible, values named like `vehicle.red.temperature`.

- Opens with backslash. Slash was the first choice but Firefox takes it for quick find. Backslash (and the UK `#` key, which reports as `Backslash`) are unused by the game. Typed keys never reach the game (the input stops them), the mouse stays locked while it is open (typing works under a lock) and backslash shuts it with no click needed. Esc is the browser's own key for releasing the mouse and a re-lock straight after it is refused, so Esc shuts the console and leaves the overlay's small hint (the debug panel's `tuning` look) rather than "Ready to play". Not persisted.
- `src/console/shell.js` is the pure engine: a tree of folders and values (`group`, `value`), the line parser, completion and Tab cycling. `commands.js` is every command, written against a small `host`. `gameTree.js` builds the tree over the live game and `gameHost.js` is the host (positions, verbs, places, weather). `gameConsole.js` is the DOM. Only the last three touch the game; the first two are tested against a stand-in (`tests/consoleShell.test.js`).
- Paths are `vehicle.<id>.*`, `girl.<id>.*`, `weather.*`, `player.*`, `girls.*`, `audio.*` and `tune.<group>.<key>`. A tuning group's name is lower-cased with dashes for spaces and a slash makes a folder (`tune.red-quad.engine.idleRpmWarm`, also under `vehicle.red.tune.engine`). Going outside a slider's range is allowed, with a note, because the point is to test extremes.
- Positions: x east, z south, one heading in degrees for you, vehicles, girls and items (0 faces +z). `tp` takes coordinates, a named place from the layout (barn, yard, ramp, dragstrip, ford...), `to <thing>` or `here`. A girl has to be stood about to be moved, and a vehicle with someone on it will not move: both say so rather than leaving someone floating. Items are the three cans, toolbox, oil bottle and leads.
- The girls' on-demand actions moved from `girlsTab.js` to `src/debug/girlActions.js`, so the panel's buttons and `girl amy drive red` are the same code. The vehicle start/stop likewise moved into `vehicleDebug.js` (`startWarm`, `switchOff`).
- New to the game: `Weather.setKind` (the sky, rain and seat frost follow at once), `Game.setGirlsAuto`, and free-fly (`src/player/flight.js`, tested: WASD along where you look, Space up, C down, Shift fast, through everything).
- Autonomy off sets each girl's `ownMind` off (no boredom projects) and stops the game starting new things: requests, resting spots, pranks, rev-offs, head starts, tractor go, nicking, tea, jumpers, the diesel mistake, rock prank, falling out, romance, races, drag races and follow the leader. What is already under way carries on, so switch it off while they are stood about. Races, follow the leader, tea and the diesel mistake still start from their debug buttons (`forced`). Horns, doughnut and jump reactions and banter stay on; `speech off` silences them.
- Checked headless once: about sixty commands ran against a real game with no errors (reads, writes, tp of you, a vehicle, a girl and a can, weather, resets, girl actions, flight on and off), and Tab completed a path in the real input. Not checked: flying about by eye, or every girl action's scene.

## The girls drive the UTV and the Land Rover (TODO 10)

Settled with Jamie before building: one go for all three vehicles (Jess picks a free one, Amy's single day unlocks all three); the Land Rover's starting handle on a flat battery, Jess kicked back, Amy never; crash gears: Jess grinds and Amy double-declutches; the other girl may join on the bench; UTV 2 to 6 m/s, Land Rover 2 to 8, 40 to 70 s a go, Jess never above second. - **The scheduler stays `TractorGo`** (`npc/tractorGo.js`) and now gets the list of free vehicles; the event says which (`vehicle`). `game.goVehicle`, `goKindOf`, `goFree`, `startGo`, `askDriveVehicle`, `canAskStart` are the game's side; `startGo` keeps her own quad in `homeQuad` as for the tractor. - **Starting.** Anything with `compressionIgnition` (tractor, UTV) uses `TractorStartAttempt`, in the `starting` state too, so "Start it" works for them standing beside. The Land Rover on a flat battery (`needsHandle`) sends her to the front first (`cranking` state, `npc/crankAttempt.js`, pure, tested): key on, choke from cold, hold the handle 0.4 to 0.8 s, let go to swing, up to 10 swings, 1.2 s between; Jess is sloppy a quarter of the time. It drives `helperPull` and `helperGrip` on the quad, and a kick-back from her sets `pullByHer` so the player's "Ow" message and the nearest girl's `landyKick` stay for the player's own kicks. Measured in one headless run each: Jess didn't catch it in six swings (she was kicked once and sloppy twice, hence the limit going from 6 to 10); Amy caught it on the eighth.
- **Gears.** `NoviceShifter.grinds` plus `grindsGear()`: a change into a crash gear above `crashSpeed` while moving counts a `crunch` that the game turns into lines; the gear still engages. Amy's `AutoShifter` never does. The Land Rover also gets Jess's novice shifter (kangaroo hops, second at most, stalls as the tractor's).
- **The other girl** joins with `joinChance` 0.5 (under "Tractor go") when the driver starts mounting, after 0.5 to 2 s, through the existing bench slots; the driver waits for her as on the tractor (`friendOnBack` already counts anyone on the mount). She is "asked" so the UTV/Land Rover rider rules leave her be, and climbs out when the go ends.
- **Lines** (`vehicleGoLines.js`): ask, set off, done and watch for each vehicle, refusals for Amy, and the handle: swing, kick, kick watch, no luck; the crunch pair. Stalls and hops use the tractor's and the usual drive lines.
- **Debug and console.** "UTV go drive", "Land Rover go drive" (same ranges as the tractor's) and "Land Rover go handle" in Tune; the Girls tab's and the console's `drive` verb now go through `startGo` for these three, and there is a new `start` verb.
- **Checked:** unit tests (scheduler, crank attempt, crunch rule, door geometry, handle grip); headless: Jess drove the UTV from the yard (key, glow, crank, 3 to 5 m/s for about a minute and a half, parked, got out), Amy started the UTV by "Start it", Amy swung the Land Rover's handle on a flat battery, mounted and drove it, and the game loads without errors. **Not checked:** how the step-in and sitting at the wheel look from close up, Jess's go at the Land Rover with a charged battery, a crunch actually happening, the other girl joining, reverse on the UTV, her avoiding things in a vehicle this wide, how often goes happen in real play, the lines by ear (re-recording started).


## New models for the girls, a viewer test (parked, 2026-10-08)

Jamie asked whether better models would fix the girls' clothes, which never came out right; the test answered yes, and the work is parked until there's a free body of the right build.

- **Why the clothes kept going wrong.** Both girls are the one Quaternius "Animated Woman" (1,908 triangles, flat shaded, one palette texture). The polo is colour painted on the body, every garment is her mesh pushed out, and a single triangle runs from her shoulder nearly to her jaw, so no neckline or collar has anything to sit on. `outfit.js` (816 lines, 18 commits) mostly works round that.
- **What was tried.** Quaternius's Universal Base Characters (CC0), free tier, in a side-by-side viewer with the game's own Amy (`.scratch/girls/viewer.html`, untracked; screenshots by `.scratch/pp/girlview.mjs`, own dev server on 5181). The female body is about 12,800 triangles with separate eyes, eyebrows and hair, and a 65-joint UE5-style skeleton with full fingers. The free Universal Animation Library (CC0, 43 clips) is on the same skeleton, so nothing needs retargeting: Idle_Loop, Walk_Loop, Jog_Fwd_Loop, Sitting_Enter/Idle_Loop/Exit, Driving_Loop, PickUp_Table, Fixing_Kneeling, Push_Loop, Idle_Talking_Loop and Interact cover nearly everything the game plays.
- **What it showed.** A rough polo and shorts (her own triangles chosen by skin weight and bind-pose height, pushed out along smoothed normals, skinned to the same skeleton) gave a clean neckline at the base of her neck first time, and a smooth fit over the bust and shoulders. Hair and eyebrows bound to the body's bones by name and followed the clips.
- **Why it's parked.** The free tier has only the "Superhero" build (broad muscular shoulders, abs, fists clenched at idle), wrong for Amy and Jess, and reshaping it in code would be fighting the base model again. The "Regular" female is in the $19.99 Source tier, which Jamie doesn't want to buy yet. Alternatives: MakeHuman through MPFB2 (CC0, realistic, separate faces and clothes, but needs Blender, which would be driven by script), or another CC0 body if one turns up. Ruled out: Reallusion's free bases (not CC0), AI-generated meshes (poor topology and rigs).
- **Gotchas for whoever picks it up.** The pack's COLOR_0 vertex colours are shader masks, so turn `vertexColors` off or she renders dark; the "Godot - UE" folder's normal maps are DirectX convention, so use the "Normals Unity - Godot" ones; the hair textures are grey, meant to be tinted; the skin texture is tanned, and a material colour can only darken it; a smooth body needs the sun's shadow bias (the game's sky already has it) or it shows fine stripes of shadow acne. The pack zips are in `.scratch/girls/`.
- **If it goes ahead.** Swap behind `?girls=new`: a bone-name map (our Mixamo-style names to `upperarm_l` and the rest), the library's clips under the names the game plays, garments as skinned shells with drape, then delete the old outfit workarounds.

- **Afterwards, from Jamie:** the Land Rover turned slightly on the spot with nobody in it, and the debug panel had no choke for it. The first was two things: a girl getting out didn't always set the handbrake (she does now on the UTV and the Land Rover), so it crept on its slope; and a parked quad's drift was only cancelled in a straight line, so the wheels-locked chassis jittered in yaw (a parked quad turning under 0.6 rad/s is now stopped; measured ±0.4 rad/s before and 0 after on the Land Rover). The choke row was hidden for anything with no fuel tap, and the Land Rover has a choke and no tap; it shows, and can be set, in the Vehicles tab and the console now.

## Riding beside her in the UTV and the Land Rover (TODO 11)

Nothing needed asking: the item settled it. Built as the quads' pillion on a bench, so the flow you already know carries over.

- **Getting in.** Looking at the UTV or Land Rover while a girl is at the wheel (`herAtTheFront`), the prompt is "Get in beside Jess (she's driving)" and `E` puts you in the seat by the door (`left`), or the middle if the other girl has taken the window (`vehicle/benchSeat.js`, pure, tested). `Player.seat` is 'pillion' with a new `benchSeat`, so `boardUnit` (sound, shake, HUD) and the passenger menu work unchanged; `riddenUnit` stays null, so none of the driver's keys are yours. Afterwards, from Jamie: you should be able to sit parked on your own as on the quads, so `B` on a parked one with nobody at the wheel does that (`canSitOnBack` allows a bench with a free seat; the key shows on the prompt). Still no ride offers from the girls while you sit there. Not offered on a moving one (the quads' "she pulls up and moves for you" is left off these).
- **Camera.** `cameraLeft` and `cameraMid` anchors on both models, at the driver's eye height over each seat (0.7 m on the UTV, 0.72 m on the Land Rover), found as `quad.benchCameras`; `quad.hasBench` is true when they exist. The camera shake and look-back are the pillion's.
- **The girls' seats.** Your seat is marked in the mount's slot (`slot.girl` holds a marker while you're in it) and passed to `UtvRide` as `playerSeat`, so the other girl who joins takes the other seat, and none boards a seat you are in. While you are aboard the UTV or Land Rover doesn't count as parked or free, so nobody climbs in by herself and no new go starts with you sat in it.
- **Menu.** As on the quads' back seat, except "Let me drive" is missing: swapping needs the driver's door, and she's out of the way when she parks anyway. When she has got out, `E` just gets you off.
- **Getting off.** Once stopped, you step down on the left, by the passenger door (`benchStand`, from `UTV_STAND.left` and `LANDY_STAND.left`), trying further out and then fore and aft if it's blocked (`benchDismountSpots`).
- **No new tunables, so nothing new in the panel**: the girls' go and ride tuning are as before. No new lines: she says her `passengerOn` line as you get in.
- **Checked:** `npm test` (seat choice, dismount spots, your seat counted as taken by `UtvRide`). Not run in the game: how the camera sits at each seat (height, how much of the door and window show), the step down on a slope, and the other girl and you sharing the bench in a go.

## Reverse is always S, and the prompt shows only what works from the ground (2026-10-08)

From Jamie: some vehicles backed up on S and some on W, which was confusing, and the prompt over a vehicle listed keys that only work from the seat.

- **Reverse.** The automatics already backed up on S after the reverse button. The manuals (red, green, yellow, tractor, Land Rover) used W as the throttle in reverse gear. Now in reverse gear on a manual S is the throttle and W the brake (`pedals()` in `quad.js`, tested), so S means backwards everywhere. W stays forwards; on an automatic in reverse W still brakes, then drops out of reverse. Chosen because S was already the answer on two vehicles and it is what most games do. The girls drive through the controls object, not keys, so nothing changes for them.
- **Prompt.** Looking at a vehicle from the ground no longer lists the clutch and gears, the Land Rover's reverse, 4WD and diff lock: only keys that work from beside it (ignition, start, choke, fuel tap, dipstick, pull-start, hand throttle, push, sit on the back, winch and chain). The help text in `hud.js` and the README were reworded for reverse.
- Checked: unit tests only; not driven in the game.

## A more Gator-like UTV (TODO 12)

Jamie wanted item 12 done before the full-detail item 108: an interim model that looks much more like a mid-2000s Gator, with a digital cluster and a centre console. Settled with Jamie before building: `B` stays the lever (it moves on the console, F / N / R); `G` and `U` as on the other switched vehicles; `P` for the light switch; lights only from the switch (key on with it off is dark); `T` still tips the bed and the console lever shows it; a seat belt lamp that flashes; the horn gets a button.

- **Body** (`utvModel.js`): the hood has a raised centre, louvres and the maker's name in yellow along each flank, the front has a slatted grille, a badge and chrome-bezelled lamps with amber side-lights, mudflaps behind the rear wheels, a sun peak on the roof, and "STAG 4x4" moulded into the tailgate and each side of the bed. The maker ("DEERFIELD") and model are made up; nothing is a real maker's name or logo. Seats, camera, hands, steering column and the bed are where they were, so the girls' seats and the climbing moves are untouched. Labels are canvas decals (`decal`, `stick` in the model file), so they're the one thing `mergeStatic` leaves alone.
- **Cluster** (`dash.js`, `utv`): a grey-green LCD with the speed in big seven-segment digits, a boxed gear letter (`forwardGearShown`: F, N, R), fuel and water bars, hours, the clock and 4WD and LOCK tags, over a row of lamps: GLOW, CHARGE, OIL, PARK, BELT, LIGHTS (green) and MAIN (blue). The OIL lamp now also lights for a low sump while running, as the red's does, since the UTV has a sump.
- **Console**: a raised pod between you and the glovebox with the F / N / R lever in its gate, a 2WD / 4WD rocker, an orange diff lock button, the light rocker and the bed's tip lever, and a printed legend. The rocker and the button are named `driveSwitchRocker` and `diffLockButton`, so Quad poses them as it does the red's and blue's. The cup holders moved along to make room.
- **4WD and diff lock**: the spec has `driveSwitch` and `diffLock` and `fourWheelDrive` 0, as the Land Rover's. It now starts every game in 2WD, as the other switched vehicles do, and the girls' remember-or-get-stuck logic (`updateFourWd`) already covered any switched vehicle, so they use it with no new code. I haven't measured how often that now leaves a girl stuck in the UTV.
- **Lights** (`lightSwitch.js`, tested): `LightSwitch` cycles off, low, high on `P`. `lightLook` gives the level and colour (dipped 0.7 and warm, main beam 1 and whiter) and how much to stretch the faked beam: main beam is 2.2 times as long, 1.5 times as wide, 1.9 times as bright and has 1.6 times the bloom. They need power (key on, battery up), so a flat battery is dark. The tail lamps now share the headlight supply (they were always lit dull red before) and brighten when you brake. `P` is the quads' pull-start, so the UTV, which has no cord, ignores it for that. Under "UTV lights" in the tuning panel; the switch is under Running on the Vehicles tab and as `lights` in the console (`drive` too, for any switched vehicle).
- **Seat belt lamp**: first built as a 6 second flash after the key went on; replaced by the seat belts below.
- **Horn**: `L` as before. The wheel's pad sinks while any horn sounds, yours or a girl's, through `quad.hornHeld`.
- **Left out**: the girls don't use the light switch (it stays as left), no indicators or hazards (item 19), no roof or doors beyond what was there.
- **Checked**: `npm test` (the switch, the look, the lamps, the belt lamp, the spec; the beam test now covers the main beam), and headless shots of the UTV from the front, side and rear, with the lights on dipped and on main beam, the view from the seat, and close-ups of the console and the cluster, with no page errors. **Not checked**: the lever, rocker and tip lever moving in play, the horn pad sinking, how the UTV drives in 2WD, the girls and the lights, and the tail lamps at dusk.

## The start screen and the FAQ page (2026-10-09)

Jamie asked for a better start screen with a logo and "by Jamie Hurst", and a link to a how-to and FAQ page with far more in it that keeps itself up to date. Settled with him: a painted farm sign drawn in code; the FAQ as its own page opening in a new tab; generated sections plus hand-written answers, with tests; spoilers folded away.

- **The logo** (`src/start/logo.js`, pure, returns an SVG string): "QUAD BIKE FARM" in cream on a weathered green plank board on two posts, a red quad side-on kicking up mud, and "by Jamie Hurst" on a small plank nailed across the posts. The paint wobble is an `feDisplacementMap` over fractal noise; the wear on the big lettering and the border is a second noise thresholded into an alpha mask (`-16 R + 11.6`), which knocks out a few per cent. The first go removed far too much and made the byline unreadable, so the byline has no wear at all. Ids are prefixed so the start screen and the FAQ page can each have one. Font stack starts at Rockwell (on every Mac) and falls back to slab serifs, then Georgia.
- **The start screen**: the overlay card grows to 560 px with the logo, the loading status, the old hint, a tip, and the FAQ link. `src/start/startScreen.js` is its own module script ahead of `main.js`, so the sign is up while the game is still loading. Tips come from the FAQ's answers (`tip` on an entry, never a spoiler), shuffled and changed every 9 s while the overlay shows. A click on the FAQ link stops there, so it doesn't also lock the mouse. The tuning and console views of the overlay (`.tuning`) hide the sign, the tip and the link as they did the title.
- **The FAQ page** (`faq.html`, `src/faq/faq.js`, `faq.css`), a second page in the Vite build (`build.rollupOptions.input`). Generated, so it follows the game: the controls (the help text moved out of `hud.js` into `src/hud/controls.js`, which both read), a card per vehicle with facts read off its spec by `src/faq/facts.js` (engine, gearbox, reverse, starting, tank, 4WD, seats), the girls from `characters.js`, the bucket list from `ACHIEVEMENTS` (65, 86 points, folded as a spoiler), and "Recently changed": `vite.config.js` puts `git log` (date and subject, 300 commits) into the build as `__GIT_LOG__`, and `src/faq/changes.js` groups it by day and drops housekeeping (TODO, DECISIONS, README, debug panel and the like), 40 shown. Hand-written in `src/faq/faqContent.js`: 77 questions in 11 sections (7 of them spoilers), a note and tips for each of the 12 vehicles and both girls, and 24 tips. Questions are folded `details` with a link each (`#its-slug` opens it), a search box filters questions and cards and opens the matches (never the spoilers), the section bar sticks (one sideways-scrolling row on a phone), and it has light and dark colours.
- **Keeping it current**: `tests/faq.test.js` fails if a vehicle or girl has no note (or a note outlives it), if a section or question is empty or repeated, if any of it has an em dash, if there are fewer than 15 tips or one is long or a spoiler, and checks the facts, the changes parser and the logo. CLAUDE.md now has "The FAQ and the start screen", and "Finishing an item" says to add or fix answers with every change; the `/todo` command says so too.
- **Staging**: an earlier commit from another session (960b28b) swept up this work half-done, so CLAUDE.md now says to stage only your own files by name.
- Checked: unit tests; a production build (both pages, the commit list in the FAQ bundle); headless screenshots of the start screen over the loaded game, the FAQ at desktop width in dark and light, at phone width (no sideways scroll), a search, and a link straight to a spoiler opening it below the bar. Not checked: how the sign looks on Windows without Rockwell, or on a very small window.

## Sound in the loading screen, and pausing (2026-10-09)

Jamie: sometimes the game started without its sound, and the game should pause while the start screen is up, including after Esc.

- **Why it started silent.** Every sound (the engine worklet, ten engine banks, the ride sounds, both girls' voice banks) only began loading on the first click, from `AudioHub.unlock`, so you were in the game while they downloaded and decoded. A browser lets a page make an audio context before any click; it starts suspended but decodes all the same. `AudioHub.prepare` now makes it and loads the worklet and banks during `Game.load` (`loadSounds`), with the girls' voices (`CompanionSounds.ensure`) and the ride sounds (`rideBuffers(hub).loading`), and the loading screen counts them ("Loading sounds… 6/13"). The click's `unlock` only resumes it. Measured: "Ready" at about 12.4 s on the warm local dev server with every bank, both voices and the ride sounds in (a first figure of 7.6 s was a cached run); see "Loading faster" below.
- **Pausing.** `overlayPauses` (`src/core/pause.js`, tested) says the farm stands still while the overlay card shows: before the first click and after Esc. Not under the small hint the tuning panel and console leave (`.tuning`), so you can still watch the game live while tuning, nor the toolbox (`.workshop`), nor an overlay a headless script has hidden by hand. While paused, `Game.frame` draws the scene and returns before anything is updated, the audio context is suspended (`AudioHub.setPaused`, re-applied every frame because the click resumes it from inside the gesture, as Safari wants) and the browser's speech is paused. The frame time is capped as before, so nothing jumps on the way out.
- Esc is in the help's "Other" line and the FAQ has "Does the game pause?". Checked headless: the counter running to 13/13 before "Ready", no time passing for the girls in 2 s behind the card, the context suspended there, running after the card is hidden, paused again after it's shown, and running under the tuning panel; the usual load check has no errors. Not checked: a real click in Safari and Firefox.

### Follow-up: changes after Jamie tried it, and seat belts

- **Nose.** The lamps stuck out past the hood because it narrowed to 0.94 m at the front. It is now 1.08 m with squarer corners (`exponent` 4.4), the lamps moved in to 0.37 m and the grille narrowed, so they sit in the body.
- **Indicators** are orange (`MAT.indicator`), not yellow: a lens under each headlamp, and under each tail lamp's red lens a second, orange one, unlit, as the place for the indicators when item 19 gets to them.
- **Console** is now a pod angled up to the driver like the cluster (the same 0.95 rad tilt), with the controls standing out of its face, a legend printed on it, and the lever, rockers and button posed in its own frame. Its names changed (`utvFourWd`, `utvLockButton`, so Quad's own posing of the quads' rocker doesn't move them along the face). The F / N / R lever is a stalk swung up for forward and down for reverse; the tip lever is the same, up to raise the bed. The parking brake handle by the driver's door was taken out by mistake (Jamie's "gear lever on the driver's side" turned out to be it) and is back as it was.
- **Grab handle** now goes down into the dash with a plate over each foot (it hovered 2 cm above it).
- **Seats** have a grey cloth weave (`clothTexture`, tiled, the material's colour multiplies it so the seat frost still works).
- **Cluster** is plainer, for an older machine: a flat LCD, five fuel and four water blocks, F / N / R down the side, the speed, hours and a 4WD note, no clock or boxes; the lamps stay under it.
- **Seat belts** (`seatBelts.js`, tested): `SeatBelts` holds who sits in each seat (driver, mid, left) and whether it is buckled. A girl is buckled `girlSeconds` (2) after she sits and unbuckled when she leaves, in any seat she takes, driving included. You press `;`. The driver's shoulder anchor is on the right of the seat and the others' on the left, with the buckles on the other side: two between the driver and the middle seat, one between the middle and the left, each a steel stalk with a red button. A belt is drawn stowed down the backrest or worn (a sash and a lap strap across whoever is there). The BELT lamp flashes (`flashRate` 2) whenever anyone is seated unbelted with the key on, so it flashes briefly for each girl too; and once you have sat unbelted with the key on for `nagDelay` (1.5 s) a quiet ping sounds every `bingEvery` (4 s) until you buckle, switch off or get out (`beltBingVolume` 0.05 in the sound settings). Tuning is under "Seat belts". Not done: the belt stopping you getting out (it lets go as you do), the girls in the bed having one (nobody does), the Land Rover's belts.
- **The lamp not flashing**: it was set to flash only for six seconds after the key went on, which is easy to miss. Headless, with the key on, it was lit in 39 of 85 dash updates, so it did flash; now it flashes for as long as there is a reason to.
- **Checked**: `npm test` (belt logic, bings, lamp, where the belts and buckles are), and headless shots of the nose and indicators, the pod and cluster, the girls belted in the middle and left, and the empty driver's seat. **Not checked**: the sash against every sitting pose (a girl who slouches or leans back may poke through it a little), the driver's own belt drawn from outside, the ping by ear, and `;` from the left and middle seats.

## Loading faster (2026-10-09)

Jamie asked to start optimising. Measured first (`.scratch/pp/loadtime.mjs` times each loading step and lists the big downloads; `loadprof.mjs` profiles the load by function): a warm load on the local dev server took 12.4 s to "Ready": 0.2 s of models, 6.3 s building the world and 5.6 s of sounds. Agreed with him: fit the clothes once, overlap the sounds with the world, and split the voice banks; download size later (TODO 111).

- **The girls' clothes, 4 s.** The world itself builds in under a second; the rest was fitting the wellies, whose rays were cast at the skinned body, and three's `SkinnedMesh.raycast` re-poses every vertex of every triangle on every ray. Setting each girl's shirt colour (`reshirt`) then refits every garment, so it ran twice per girl. `withPosedBody` in `outfit.js` poses a plain copy of the body once (positions posed, every other attribute and the material shared, same transform) and all the fitting's rays hit that. Checked: every garment's vertices hash the same before and after, both girls (`.scratch/pp/outfithash.mjs`). The first go left out the uv, which `shirtFrontTop` reads the shirt colour by, and moved the placket; the hash caught it.
- **The voice banks, 5.6 s and about 2.3 GB.** The two banks hold 5,587 s and 6,228 s of speech (58 kbps MP3, 40 and 45 MB), which decoded whole at the context's rate is about a gigabyte each. The voice tool now writes each bank as files of about a minute (`CHUNK_SECONDS` 60, each starting with 0.1 s of quiet so the MP3 encoder's start-up never touches a line), 92 for Amy and 102 for Jess, with the json saying which file each line is in; `--bank-only` rebuilds the banks from the cache without recording anything (about 12 s a girl), which is how these were made, with the same 2,361 and 2,241 lines at the same lengths. The game still downloads every file while it loads, but `VoiceChunks` (`src/audio/voiceChunks.js`, tested) decodes a file only when one of its lines is first said, keeps the six used most recently (about 11 MB each) and drops the rest. A line from a file not yet decoded starts when it is: measured 30 to 60 ms late (190 ms for the very first in a game), then instantly. Old one-file banks still load.
- **Overlapping the sounds with the world: not done.** After the two changes above the sounds take about 0.2 s of loading, and the world build is one long run on the main thread that would hold up the decodes' callbacks anyway, so it wasn't worth the extra plumbing.
- **The floor.** The pause (the section above) skipped every frame behind the start screen, including the first, which is the one that puts the camera at your eyes; so the farm was drawn from the camera's starting place, in the ground. The first frame now always runs (a tenth of a second of game time at most, the frame cap), then the pause takes over.
- **Result**: "Ready" at about 1.7 s warm on the local dev server, from 12.4 s. Keep it under about 2.5 s: run `node .scratch/pp/loadtime.mjs` (dev server on 5181) after anything that adds to loading.

- **Second round of tweaks (UTV):** the stowed belts lie on the backrest instead of standing off it; the headlamps and their indicators sit 3 cm lower in the nose; the F / N / R letters are much smaller (the pod's legend and the cluster's side column); `;` no longer shows a "Seat belt on" message; a worn belt now ends at its buckle, with the tongue pushed into it, instead of mid-seat; and the parking brake handle pivots at its bracket (`parkBrakeLever`), up and back when the brake is on and let down forward when it is off, eased. Checked by headless screenshots of the nose and the seat; the buckle end is mostly hidden behind a seated girl, so look at it with the driver's belt on.

- **Switch bank and the bong (UTV):** the 4WD, diff lock, lights and bed controls are now four matching rockers in a row (the diff lock was a round orange button and the bed a lever), each with a symbol on its cap drawn on a small canvas and redrawn only when its state changes: a four-wheel-drive layout (front axle and shaft light up for 4WD), a differential with lock bars (amber when locked), a headlamp with beams (slanting for dipped, level for main beam; green, then blue) and a tipping bed (amber when raised). The selected half goes in: the lower half for on; the lights' top for off, middle for dipped, lower for main beam. The dash has one LIGHTS lamp, green for dipped and blue for main beam, instead of LIGHTS and MAIN. The seat belt reminder is a "bong" now (523 Hz with an octave over it, ringing 0.7 s, `beltBingVolume` 0.16, from 0.05), sounding every two seconds on the edge of the lamp lighting, so it is in time with the flash (`SeatBelts.update` takes the dash clock). Checked in screenshots of the bank with the lights off, dipped and on main beam; the bong by ear not at all.

## No crash of vehicles landing at the start (2026-10-09)

Jamie: the vehicles all dropping into place made a deafening noise when the game first loaded. Every vehicle spawns a little above the ground; before the sounds loaded during the loading screen, they had landed by the time the sound was switched on, so nobody heard it. Now the sound is ready first. `Game.settleVehicles` runs `SETTLE_SECONDS` (2) of physics at the end of loading ("Settling…"), so they're on their wheels before anything can be heard, and throws away the landing thuds and knocks that queued meanwhile. Measured (`.scratch/pp/dropcheck.mjs`, seed 12, 11 vehicles): without it, 19 knocks (3 loud) in the first three seconds and vehicles still moving at 1 m/s; with it, none, and nothing faster than 0.06 m/s. It adds about a quarter of a second to loading (about 1.9 s to "Ready").

- **Belts across the girls, a smaller cluster (UTV):** the belt path was drawn behind the girls' bodies. Measured from the skinned meshes of both girls sitting in the middle and left seats (`.scratch/pp/measure.mjs`): chest front at about z 0.04, waist 0, shoulders -0.02, in the model's ground frame. The sash now goes over the shoulder at z -0.2, across the chest at 0.06 to 0.08 and the lap at about 0.03, then back to the buckle; the lap strap lies across her lap at 0.03. A girl only counts as sitting (so only starts the two seconds before her belt goes on) once she is in the seat (`onTrailer`, or `driving`), not while she walks over or climbs in: headless, the belts came on about six seconds after the request, once she had sat for two. The cluster is scaled 0.95 instead of 1.2, and the F / N / R letters are bigger again (0.014 on the pod; on the cluster the unlit ones are now plain rather than ghosted). Checked in screenshots of the girls belted in and the view from the seat.

- **A smaller console pod and symbols on the dash (UTV):** the pod was mostly empty space, so it is now 0.215 by 0.09 m (from 0.28 by 0.13) with the rockers a little smaller (0.027 by 0.032) and closer (0.036 apart), the gear slot at the near end with F, N and R beside it, and the names printed small underneath. The cluster's warning bulbs are now symbols (`warnSymbol` in `dash.js`): a coil for the glow plugs, a battery, an oil can, a P in a circle, a seated figure with a belt across it, and a headlamp with beams. Out, each is dull grey; lit, each is in its colour with a soft glow behind it, and the belt figure flashes with the dash clock as the lamp did. Checked in screenshots from above; not at your eye level in a game.

- **Badge on the switch pod (UTV):** the pod is 0.1 m tall now, with the maker's name (made up) and the model in a band across the top between two antlers, a yellow line under it, and the switches and gear slot moved down below it. Checked in a screenshot from above.

## Things in the UTV's bed (2026-10-09)

TODO 13. It copies the little tipping trailer's tub rather than inventing anything new.

- **Who can go in:** the same four things the trailer takes (the three jerry cans, the toolbox, the jump leads, the oil bottle; `heldLoad()`). The hose is left out: it is a hose, not a thing you stand. Bales are not pick-up items, so they wait.
- **How:** `CargoHold` (`vehicle/cargoHold.js`, tested) parents the item's model to a spot on the bed, so it tips with it, and unparents it to send it flying out when the bed is up past `slideAt`. Picking one up again just takes it off its spot; nothing has to be told. The UTV's own bed group carries six anchors and an invisible `bedProxy` (`utvModel.js`: `BED_CARGO_SPOTS`, `BED_PROXY`). I did not move the little trailer onto `CargoHold`; it has its own copy of the same code and a test, and can be moved over later.
- **Spots:** two rows of three in the clear between the wheel wells (0.9 m between them), cans and the toolbox turned along the bed. The test checks every item fits every spot. The jump leads' coil is the widest and may overhang a well by a couple of centimetres at the outside spots.
- **Looking:** the bed's box has priority 1 and is only there while your hands hold a load, so it beats the UTV's own box (which would try to fill its tank from a can). Empty-handed you look at the thing in the bed and take it.
- **Tipping out:** items land 1.6 m behind plus up to half a metre, a little to one side, in 0.6 s (`UTV load` in the Tune tab).
- **Left out:** the load adding to the UTV's weight (no effect on how it drives), and the girls putting things in it.
- Not checked in the running game: the look of the items in the bed, the prompt wording, and the tip-out. Unit tests only.

## The girls see the trailers (TODO 14)

- Cause: the trailers follow the hitch and have no physics body, so the girls' ray probes (on foot in `Companion.blocked`, driving in `driveProbe` and `driveClear`) went straight through them.
- Fix: each trailer gets a kinematic box (`src/vehicle/trailerObstacle.js`), moved with it every frame: the bed or tub footprint, 0.2 to 1.0 m above the axle's ground so a ray at knee height hits it between the wheels. Like the girls' own colliders it has solver groups `0xffff0000`, so it makes no contact forces: it can't shove a quad or the tractor towing it, and nothing is stopped by it. Only rays see it (and the player's character controller, so you can't walk through one).
- A girl driving ignores the trailer she is towing (`towedBy`), otherwise her reverse probe would hit it and she would back off for ever.
- Not done: the drawbar and swing of a trailer behind her (she sees only the bed, not the arc it sweeps on a tight turn), and a quad you drive into a trailer still passes through. Walking-round-vehicles avoidance is unchanged beyond the trailers; no new logic for the quads, which her probes already saw.
- Tests: `tests/trailerObstacle.test.js` (box placement, which trailer is ignored, and in Rapier that a ray hits it and an overlapping quad isn't moved). **Not checked:** in the running game.

### Walking round vehicles, a better way (follow-up to TODO 14)

- Cause of the bumps: on foot she looked along one ray down her middle for 1.4 m, so a shoulder clipped any corner the ray missed, and the turn back toward her target began the moment the line looked clear.
- `Companion.blocked` now casts three rays (her middle and 0.4 m out either side, `probeWidth`) when looking more than a metre; close to a destination it is the middle only, so she can still walk up beside a quad to get on. `probeDistance` went from 1.4 to 2.2 m, and the angles tried either side are finer (0.4 rad steps, out to 2.5).
- Once she finds a way round she keeps that heading for `avoidHold` (2 s) while it stays clear, rather than turning back in at the first gap. Both are in the "Moving about" tuning.
- Not tested in unit tests (it needs a whole companion in Rapier); **not checked in the running game**, so the numbers are first guesses.
