## A better Land Rover model (TODO 16)

The Series III already had its chassis, leaf springs, grille, dash and tub, but its body panels were flat sheets with no wheel arches: the front wings' outer sides and the tub's sides sat over the tyres and hid them from the side.

- **Wheel arches** (`archPanel` in `landRoverModel.js`, tested): the wings' outer sides and the tub's sides are now extruded shapes with a round arch (0.42 m, round a 0.38 m tyre) cut from the bottom edge, plus a rolled flare (`archFlare`) standing proud of the panel. The tyre shows through. The wing tops and inner sides are 0.05 m longer to meet the new panel. A test checks the panel's size and that no vertex falls inside the tyre.
- **Small things**: mudflaps behind the rear wheels, front and rear number plates (white and yellow), turned-back bumper ends, rain gutters along the roof edges, and the bonnet's hinges at the screen end.
- **Second pass (Jamie's list).**
  - *Tub*: the floor was at 0.78 m with a box over each arch, so the tyres (tops at 0.96 m) poked through it. The floor is now at 1.02 m (`TUB_FLOOR`, tested against the wheel tops), with a low hump over each arch, the sides 0.1 m taller, the tailgate raised to match and a rear body panel under it carrying the lamps and the plate.
  - *Cab joints*: the doors ran 0.05 m short of the bulkhead and 0.1 m from the cab side, the window frames stopped at 1.66 m and the roof's underside is 1.72 m. The doors now fill their openings, the window frames and the screen's top rail run up into the roof, and the door glass is cut to a trapezoid to follow the screen.
  - *Windscreen*: raked back 0.2 rad (about 11 degrees), its frame in a rotated group; the doors' front uprights are the screen's side posts, so the two meet exactly. The wipers, a rear-view mirror and the dash's shelf were moved to suit.
  - *Badges and plates*: canvas labels (`label` in `landRoverModel.js`): a green oval "LAND ROVER" on the grille panel and the tailgate, FRM 88L on a white plate at the front and a yellow one at the back.
  - *Trim*: black pebbled, stitched vinyl on the seats (the driver's split, foam showing, taped), a grained dash top and a ribbed rubber floor, all drawn in canvases when the Land Rover is built, so the model can no longer be built without a canvas (its test stubs one).
  - *Belts*: `LANDY_BELTS` and `addBelt`, named as the UTV's so `poseUtvBelts` poses them. The driver and the left seat have a diagonal and lap belt from the B pillars, the middle one a lap belt between two stalks. In the game the same `SeatBelts` runs as the UTV's (`updateLandRover`): `;` for yours, the girls buckle themselves, the BELT lamp flashes and the bong sounds while you sit unbelted with the key on. A real Series III had neither; they are there because the UTV's are.
  - *Handbrake*: the thin tube is now a ratchet lever with a grip and a release button, up when the parking brake is on (`Space`, eased), by the driver's seat. The BRAKE lamp was already wired.
  - *Choke and hand throttle*: the knob's shaft was 0.03 m long and the knob pulled out 0.05 m, so it floated clear of the dash. The shaft now runs 0.16 m back into the dash and the travel is 0.1 m, and a test checks the shaft stays inside the dash fully out.
  - *Dash*: the binnacle is squarer and 1.5 times the size it was (about 0.3 m wide), with a strip of lamps along its top (ignition, oil, handbrake and belts as symbols, then CHOKE, 4WD and DIFF) above the speedometer and the combined gauge, and the key at the end. Oil is red now, not amber. I could not find a good reference for the exact Series III layout, so this is from memory: worth a look against your own photos. No main beam or indicator lamps yet: the Land Rover has no light switch, and indicators are TODO 19.
  - *Also*: a rear-view mirror, and hinges and a catch on the tailgate.
- Not changed: the colliders, seats, controls and the sound. Not verified in the running game or by screenshot (the model needs a canvas for its dash, so only the arch geometry is unit tested); worth a look for z-fighting at the flares.

## Land Rover, third pass, and the UTV's empty seat

- **Gaps**: the B pillar was 0.12 m deep and the cab's rear panel starts 0.06 m behind it, leaving a slot you could see through; it is 0.22 m now. The wings' outer sides, tops and inner sides start at the bulkhead (z 0.6) instead of 0.7, and a hinge pillar and a sill under each door close the rest.
- **Badges and plates**: label canvases are drawn in pixels now (the first pass drew text at a fraction of a pixel, which the browser spaced badly, so characters overlapped); the registration is sized to fill the plate. The tailgate badge is on two lines, LAND over ROVER. A small badge is on the dash and in the middle of the steering wheel.
- **Dash**: four lamps, ignition, oil, handbrake and choke (with a choke symbol, drawn like the others). No belt lamp (a Series III had none), and no 4WD or diff lock lamps. The Land Rover's belts still work and the girls buckle up, but there is no bong either: `updateLandRover` ignores the nag.
- **Knobs**: the hand throttle is on the left of the choke, both over the edge of the gearbox tunnel at the dash's face, raised to 1.095 m, with light heads (grey and red) so they show on the black dash.
- **Gear lever** (`gearLeverPose`, tested): a normal H, first top-left (forward, away from you), second below it, third top-right, fourth below that, and reverse top-left of first. Before, the two columns were the wrong way round and first and third were back towards you.
- **Exhaust**: the pipe ran through the rear wheel. The silencer is 0.34 m from the middle and the pipe runs straight back inside the wheel to come out under the tailgate, where it shows.
- **UTV belt nag**: with the key on and nobody in the driver's seat the lamp flashes and the bong sounds after the usual 1.5 s (`SeatBelts.emptyFor`), whoever is nearby, since someone is expected to be sitting there. A girl who has just sat down is left her two seconds. The Land Rover's `SeatBelts` has the same logic but nothing reads it.

## The 200TDi (a second Land Rover)

Decided with Jamie: a 90 County rather than a Discovery for now; a faded pastel green; both Land Rovers can be on the farm together; the sound is the UTV's for now, with its own idle and top end; the newer dash and centre controls; a square back with the spare wheel on the rear door; back seats later.

- **A variant, not a copy** (`variant: 'defender'` in `buildLandRoverModel`, `DEFENDER_SPEC` in `quadSpecs.js`, `landRover: true` and `defender: true`). They share the chassis, wheels, arches, belts, handbrake, steering wheel, tow hitch and exhaust, and the spec flags every Land Rover path in the game already looked at. The Series spec is renamed "Series III" (its id is still `landrover`); the new one is `landrover200`, "200TDi".
- **Body**: the roof runs the length of the car in the body colour with black gutters; the cab's rear panel and the tub are replaced by side bodywork with a window band and the arches cut in it, a rear door with a window and a carpeted load floor over the wheel humps. The spare is on the door, 0.1 m proud (the model reaches 2.18 m behind its middle; the physics box is unchanged). No bonnet spare, a raised ridge on the bonnet instead, black wheel flares, silver wheels, a front plate and badge as the Series. Nothing rides in the back yet.
- **Interior**: grey cloth seats with headrests, a lighter grey dash moulding and carpet, a moulded console with the gear, transfer and diff lock levers coming up through rubber gaiters (the transfer lever black and the diff lock yellow, the Series' being yellow and red), a gaiter on the handbrake, a black four-spoke wheel. No choke or hand throttle: the knobs exist hidden because `poseLandyControls` looks them up by name.
- **Dash** (`buildDefender` in `dash.js`, style `defender`): a rev counter with the red zone from the engine's redline, the speedometer to 100 mph with a six-digit odometer, the fuel and water gauge, a lamp strip with glow, charge, oil, handbrake, belt and diff lock, and the key. It reads the same dash state as the others.
- **Drive and engine**: a clutch and the same four gears as the Series, synchro on all (`crashGears` 0), 1,750 kg and 8,400 N of drive (against the Series' 1,350 and 7,000), about 62 mph. The engine is the UTV's diesel (glow plugs, the key stops it, petrol soaks it) retuned: idle 800 (770 to 830 a game), maxRpm 4,400, redline 4,300, `loadDragRpm` 2,000 so a manual diesel stalls if you dump the clutch high. Rattly: roughness 0.10 to 0.16 (the Series' 0.04 to 0.10), `cycleJitter` 0.05, `pulseStrengthJitter` 0.16 and a vibration `steadiness` of 0.35 against the Series' 0.85. Its sound is the UTV's Gator bank with `pulseRefRpm` 800: the pace follows the revs, so it ticks faster and rattles more than the UTV; a recording of its own is a TODO item.
- **In the game** (`game.js`): `landys.landy` and `landys.defender` each hold `{ spec, unit, mount, ride }`; the old `landyUnit`, `landyMount` and `landyRide` are gone. `GO_KINDS` is the list of vehicles the girls take for a go ('tractor', 'utv', 'landy', 'defender'), `mountOfKind` and `rideOfKind` find the seats, and the 200TDi shares the Series' lines for the girls (so Amy still says "it is a Series": TODO). Each has its own riders group in the Tune tab ("Land Rover riders", "200TDi riders"), its own seed for wear, quirks and faults, and the diesel has no carb to go wrong. The belt bong plays on the 200TDi only, and fades with distance (`beltBing(at)`).
- **Checked**: unit tests (the spec, both models building with a stubbed canvas, the 200TDi's named parts); headless: both models from outside and from the seat, the 200TDi starting on its glow plugs and idling at 787 rpm, and Jess walking to it, driving it and bringing it back (seed 9). Seen: a girl asked to take a vehicle that is out in a crop field (the wheat or barley field) walks towards it and stops about 55 to 75 m short; I haven't checked whether the tractor and the UTV do the same out there, but it will make Jess's go on a vehicle in a crop field fail. Not checked: a girl taking the 200TDi with a flat battery (it has no handle, so she will say it won't go), riding in its bench, and the Series and the 200TDi both on the farm at once beyond loading.

## The 200TDi, second pass

Jamie's list after trying it.

- **Five-speed.** `gears: 5` on its gearbox, with `topSpeed5`, `torque5` and `launchLoad5` (new gearbox params, ranges added, `GEARS` and the `5` key). Tops 5.0, 8.6, 13.6, 21 and 32 m/s (11, 19, 30, 47 and 72 mph), each step about 1.55 times the last; `maxSpeed` 32 (the first go was a four-speed with 28, about 62 mph). The Series still has four. The gear lever's H for five (`gearLeverPose(gear, gears)`, tested): first and second on the left, third and fourth in the middle, fifth top right, reverse below it.
- **Lights.** Both Land Rovers now have the UTV's three-way light switch (`lightSwitch: true`; `P` from the seat: off, dipped, main beam), so their lights are off until you switch them on and need the key. The Series' starting handle is also on `P`, but only from the front on foot, so the handle takes precedence there (`pullDown` in `quad.js` checks the crank first). Tell-tales: the Series has only the blue main beam lamp (`beam`, a headlamp with four straight rays); the 200TDi a green lights lamp and the blue main beam one; the UTV keeps its one lamp that changes colour. The lights' brightness and reach are the UTV's "UTV lights" group in the Tune tab, shared.
- **Dash** in a different style: charcoal moulding with a squarer corner, white figures and orange needles, the rev counter and speedometer larger and the fuel and water gauges separate and stacked at the right, the lamps in a row along the bottom (glow, charge, oil, handbrake, belts, lights, main beam). The diff lock lamp is gone.
- **Steering wheel**: a thicker, softer rim on two spokes with a padded hub and the badge (the Series keeps its thin four-spoke one). **Plate**: H471 DKP, front and back (the Series is FRM 88L). **Badge**: a small black "200Tdi" plate on the left of the rear door, opposite the maker's. **Rear lamps**: a tall black housing on each corner with the red stop and tail lens over the amber indicator over a clear reverse lens (the Series has two small lamps). **Headrests** are on two steel posts into the backrest, on the driver's and the left-hand seat only.
- **Less bouncy**: the first go had `suspensionStiffness` 36, compression and relaxation damping at the Series' 3 and 2.6, `wheelShake` 0.9 and vibration gains of 1.9, 0.2 and 0.26 (pulse, heave, pitch). It has 32, 4.4 and 3.8, travel 0.18, `wheelShake` 0.55, and 1.4, 0.12 and 0.16 with the heave damping at 0.45. Not tried on a long run: all of these are in the 200TDi's Vehicle and Vibration groups if it still looks wrong.
- **Revs** (for the record): idle 800 warm, dealt 770 to 830 each game; full throttle 4,400, dealt 4,350 to 4,450; redline 4,300; stalls below 350; the starter turns it at 230 and it catches at 1,000; it rises at 3.8 and falls at 3.0 per second. All are in the "200TDi/Engine" group in the Tune tab (the sliders for `maxRpm`, `redlineRpm` and `idleRpmWarm` now go down to 2,000, 1,800 and 300 so a diesel's values are on them), and the per-game spread is `idleRange` and `topRpmRange` in the spec.
- **The girls' wheel** gets a slice for each Land Rover on the farm, "Series III" and "200TDi" (get in, drive it, start it, and get out when they are in), checked in a run: Amy got into the 200TDi and out again, and the wheel offered the right thing.

## The clutch vehicles' revs follow the road, and the Land Rovers stay put

- **Revs tied to the road.** Every manual (the red, green and yellow quads, the old three-wheeler, the tractor and both Land Rovers) had its revs worked out like a CVT quad's: the throttle's target, sagged by the load, with only the closed-throttle overrun reading the wheels. So at full throttle in first they leapt to 3,600 rpm at 5 mph. Now `Quad.engineControls` sends `gearRpm` (road speed over the gear's top speed times `maxRpm`) and `gearLock` (the clutch's bite), and the engine model pulls its target to `gearRpm` by `gearLock`, once the road speed is more than idle's worth (a smoothstep over 250 rpm above idle, so pulling away from rest is unchanged and the stall and lugging logic still has the last word). Measured on the 200TDi in first at full throttle: before 3,583 rpm at 4.8 mph and 4,103 at 8.7 (the top of first is 11 mph); after 2,082 at 4.9 and 2,963 at 8.7, easing up to 4,224 at 10.9, and lifting off lets it fall with the speed (4,311 to 3,620 as it slows from 11 to 8.8 mph) instead of dropping to idle at once. Tested in `tests/gearLock.test.js`. A two-stroke's powerband reads these revs, so the yellow now has to be kept in its band by choosing the gear: I haven't tried that by hand.
- **Spawning and sliding.** Two things made a Land Rover move at the start. (1) The tractor, UTV and both Land Rovers appeared 13 to 16 cm above their springs and sank in front of you; each layout now has a measured `spawnSag` (0.155, 0.14 and 0.128) and the body starts already sunk, so the first-second drop is under 1 cm. (2) The 200TDi, heavier than the Series (1,750 against 1,350 kg) with the same parking brake, slid down any slope over about 2 degrees at 0.3 m/s with the handbrake on: parked bodies are only held still below `parkedHoldSpeed` (0.15 m/s), and a slide that started at the spawn settle stayed above that. Both Land Rovers now have `parkedHoldSpeed` 0.4 and the 200TDi a `parkingBrake` of 30 (the Series' 22 scaled by weight). Measured on five seeds with the 200TDi on 2 to 5 degree slopes: drift in 8 s from 2.7 m to 0.02 m.


## Girls stay full size in the Land Rover

- **The cause.** Both Land Rovers (and the UTV) are drawn at 1.15 times, and the model's scale passes down to anything hung under it. The quads already cancelled that on their seat anchors (`1 / scale`), but the bench seats (`seatLeft`, `seatMid`) and the UTV's `bedSeat` were left out, so a girl in the back or on the bench came out 15% taller than on the ground.
- **The fix.** `src/vehicle/unscaleAnchors.js` holds one list of every seat anchor and undoes the scale on each; `quad.js` calls it. A new seat anchor goes on that list. The model's scale stays, since it sizes the vehicle to the girls.

## Better instrument pods (TODO 18)

Settled with Jamie: all five (the red and blue dashes, the tractor's, and both accessory pods); looks and behaviour only, no new readouts; words on lamps and gauges become symbols; "HONDA" comes off the red's dash; the pods keep their mounts.

- **Symbols instead of words.** `warnSymbol` moved from `dash.js` to `dashParts.js` (it was the UTV's) and gained four symbols: a fuel pump, a thermometer in water, two axles with a shaft for 4WD and the same with a locked ring for the diff lock. It takes a `scale` for dial faces and `glow: false` for the LCDs. Oil, temperature and fuel lamps on the red, blue, loan, tractor and both pods, the tractor's WATER and FUEL dial labels, the Land Rover's combined gauge, and the 4WD notes on the loan quad's and the UTV's LCDs now use them. N and R stay as letters (`letterLamp` for the pod). Units stay as words (MPH, ODO, TRIP, HRS), as on a real dial.
- **Analogue pod.** One row of four: the speedo and rev counter large at each end, fuel and temperature small between them, each in its own chrome bezel, with the lamps along the top (N, R, temperature, fuel, oil, then 4WD and diff lock on a quad that has them, centred). The housing is wider (210 mm against 176) and a little shorter. Fixed positions are `DIAL_Y`, `BIG_X`, `MINI_X` at the top of `dashPod.js`.
- **Digital pod.** Its OIL, FUEL, TEMP and 4WD words are symbols too.
- Not done, as agreed: backlighting (item 33's dusk), new readouts. The analogue pod's needles now lag the real speed and revs (0.4 and 0.3 of the gap per frame, like the stock dashes) instead of snapping; a key-on sweep is not done.
- Checked: unit tests, and a headless run (clicking through the overlay) of the red quad with each pod, the tractor with the analogue pod and the tractor's and loan quad's stock dashes. Not checked: the blue's own dash by eye (it wasn't dealt that game), the Land Rover's combined gauge, or the pods while moving.

### Better instrument pods, second pass (Jamie's feedback)

- **Analogue pod:** the big dials are larger (35 mm, was 28) so the pod is only as tall as they are, and the lamps are small and sit in a row over the two small dials between them. The centre text (MPH, ×1000 r/min) moved up and shrank, the odometer, trip and hour drums sit lower, and the ODO / TRIP / HRS words beside the drums are gone (they collided with the 60 and 0). All seven lamps are always on the face (N, R, temperature, fuel, oil, 4WD, diff lock); on a quad with no drive switch the last two just stay out.
- **Digital pod:** the 4WD and diff lock symbols are always drawn, dim when out or when the quad has no switch.
- **N and R** are lit-up letters (`glyphLetter`, no lamp behind them) on the red, blue, loan and green dashes and the analogue pod, to match the symbols. The `letterLamp` helper from the first pass went.
- **Tractor dash:** the rev counter is the big dial on the left, water temperature and fuel side by side on the right, and the GLOW, CHARGE and OIL symbols and the key in a line under them. The dial symbols are gone (an old tractor wouldn't have them; C / H and E / F are enough). It looks old: a worn dark housing, dull pitted bezels (`agedSteel`), brown-black faces with faded cream figures, and yellowed glass with a haze, scratches and specks over each dial (`agedGlass`, fixed so nothing shimmers).
- **Debug panel:** the repeated `reading 'mode'` page error was the Vehicles tab's `lights` and `drive` controls reading `quad.lights` and `quad.drive` on a picked vehicle that has neither; both now read through `?.`.
- Checked: unit tests and a headless run (past the overlay) of both pods on the red quad and the tractor's stock dash. Not checked: the blue's dash, or the analogue pod on the tractor after this pass.

## Starters of their own (TODO 19)

The item was clear (the UTV and both Land Rovers wanted better starter sounds), so I picked the recordings without asking.

- **What was wrong.** The UTV and the 200TDi (which plays the UTV's bank) cranked on the old Fergie's starter, and the Series on a quad's. Worse, every bank's crank loop played at its recorded pace when the engine turned at 320 rpm, but these three crank at 170 to 260, so the loops always played slowed: the Series' at the 0.6 floor.
- **Finding recordings.** Freesound's search page now needs JavaScript, so the old scraper (`.scratch/shop/fs_search.py`) finds nothing; a new search would need the API and a key. The earlier shopping had already downloaded about a hundred CC0 recordings, and five had a usable crank (found by plotting the first seconds' loudness, then pulse timing and spectrograms, `.scratch/shop/crank*.py`). The Land Rover clip from the earlier brief (craigsmith 675226) turned out to be someone talking until the engine is already running.
- **Picks.** UTV: a small tractor's quick diesel crank (radwoc, 256808), compressions about 7 a second, a three-cylinder at the UTV's 260 rpm; the loop is two compressions (0.415 to 0.614 s), cut in the quiet between them. 200TDi: a Citroën Jumpy van's four-cylinder diesel (chlund, 500193), about 7.5 a second at 230 rpm, three compressions a loop (1.273 to 1.716 s); the old Ford Transit cold start the brief had picked (622829) turns over only twice a second, which would sound like a dying battery every time. Series: an old Ford stake bed truck (D.jones, 521857), a heavy, uneven crank about five compressions a second for its slow 170 rpm, six or so compressions a loop (1.247 to 2.388 s).
- **How.** The slicer's one-shots can carry fields: a crank loop's `crankRpm` (the vehicle's own cranking speed, so the loop plays at its recorded pace on a good battery and slows as the battery goes; banks without it still use the bank's 320), and a `gain` from `oneshotLevel`, which scales a starter so that its own engine's idle in the same recording would sit at the bank's idle level. Before this, one-shots had no gain of their own (the `fixedGain` on borrowed starters only ever scaled engine pulses). The UTV's bank holds two starters; `recStarterSet` ('Tdi', a dropdown in the Audio tuning) picks the 200TDi's, so it didn't need a 3.7 MB bank of its own. Rebuilt banks have the same engine grains as before, checked one by one.
- **Loudness**, measured on offline renders (`render_engine_sound.mjs`, which now has `--start` and `--no-catch`; WAVs in `.scratch/starters/`, old and new): with `recStarterVolume` set to each vehicle's `recVolume`, so the recording's own balance holds, the UTV cranks 8.9 dB under its idle and the Series 0.2 dB under. The Jumpy's quiet modern idle put its crank 5 dB over the 200TDi's rattly one, so its starter volume is 0.39, 1 dB under. Before: UTV -10.3 dB, Series -3.2, 200TDi -10.5, on the wrong starters.
- **Tests.** `tests/starterSounds.test.js`: every vehicle's bank has the starter its spec asks for, and these three loop at their own cranking speed. The FAQ's vehicle list (`src/faq/facts.js`) now comes from every spec `quadSpecs.js` exports, so a new vehicle can't be left off the page or its note.
- Not checked: listening (Jamie's ears; the renders are there), and the starters in the running game.

## Better frost (TODO 20)

On a freezing day (`Weather.frost`, see "The day's temperature") the frost is now on the ground as well as the seats, and looks like frost.

- Ground: the terrain shader (`terrain.js`, `addDetailLayer`) whitens the grass by `uFrost`, patchy over the field, with a glitter of crystals from the detail texture and a little less gloss. It lies half as thick on the road. Churned ground (soft mud) has none.
- Thaw: `GroundWear` keeps a `thaw` grid next to wear, set from the same tyre passes (`thawAt` param, 0.006 of wear, so about two metres of one tyre melts a cell) and sent to the shader in the wear texture's blue. Unlike wear it never recovers: frost does not regrow in a day that does not move on. Footprints do not thaw it (the girls do not trample the grid).
- Seats: the tint is kept, with a speckled bump map (`frostTexture.js`, shared) and a faint cold glow so it reads in the shade. The tractor's seat now has its own material, so it frosts too (it was left out before). Sitting on any of them still clears it over 4 seconds.
- Not measured: the look in the running game, and what the extra shader work costs the frame rate (a few texture reads on the terrain). The sun does not melt the frost, as the day does not advance; that waits for item 33.

## Indicators and a reverse light (TODO 21)

- Keys: `,` left, `.` right, `=` hazards, on the UTV and both Land Rovers (the quads have no indicators). Pressing the key for the mode already on switches it off. They need the key on and a charged battery, like the dash.
- Logic in `indicators.js` (pure, tested): 1.5 flashes a second with the lamp lit for 55% of each, which sits inside the legal 60 to 120 a minute. A left or right indicator cancels itself once the bars have gone beyond `cancelTurn` (0.35 of lock) the way it points and come back inside `cancelStraight` (0.12). Hazards never cancel. Tunables are in the Tune tab under "Indicators".
- Lamps: `addSignalLamps` (`quadModel.js`) gives each vehicle its own left, right and reverse lens materials, sorting the model's existing orange lenses by which side of the middle they are on (front and back of a side flash together), so no model needed its lamps rebuilt. `setSignalLamps` lights them. The Series III and the UTV gained a clear reverse lens by the tail lamps (the 200TDi already had one, unlit until now). The reverse lamp lights when reverse is selected with the key on, from `quad.reverse`.
- Dash: a green pair of arrows on the UTV's, the Series' and the 200TDi's (the 200TDi's lamps were respaced from 28 to 25 to fit it), flashing with the lamps. The quads' pods and the tractor have no indicators, so none.
- Sound: a dry relay click, higher on than off (`indicatorClick`, volume `indicatorVolume`), fading with distance like the belt bong. It plays for any vehicle left flashing, not only yours.
- Debug: Vehicles tab has an `indicators` selector (off, left, right, hazard) and the console has `indicators` on the same vehicles. It is not a fault, so "Put it right" leaves it alone.
- **Second round**: the lamps did not light on the Land Rovers because the mud coat clones every body material for its own shader and had cloned the new lenses; it now skips them (as it does the headlamp and tail lenses). The dash arrows were pointing inward; the Series' one lamp is now "<- ->" and lights for either side, while the UTV's and the 200TDi's have a left and a right arrow that flash on their own side (the 200TDi's lamps were respaced again to 21.5). An indicator stalk sits on the left of each steering column (down for left, up for right, eased, centred for hazards) and a hazard switch, red with a white triangle, on the Series' and the 200TDi's dash top and under the UTV's lights rocker, which glows with the hazards only. The keys are `,` `.` and `=` (slash and apostrophe both clashed with Firefox). The Series III now has a single round reverse lamp on the driver's side so it differs from the 200TDi's pair of square ones.
- Not checked: how the stalk and the hazard button look and sit (placed from the model's numbers, not seen).
- Left out: the girls do not use the indicators when they drive, and nothing flashes the lamps on the rear view. No light cast on the ground from the reverse lamp (the lens glows only).

- **Third round**: the Series' reverse lamp moved to the left (nearside) as the driver sits on the right. The stalk now swings about an axis worked out from the column's rake (`addSignalStalk`), so down is towards the floor and up towards the roof; it is in the column's own material, has the "< >" symbol on its end facing the driver, and is a thin round one with a button on the Series and a flat moulded one with a wide end on the UTV and 200TDi. The hazard switch is built facing +z with the triangle's point up and then turned to face the driver (`faceAlong`): on a black shroud on top of the Series' column, in the UTV's console pod (made 7 cm wider, other switches kept where they were; red cap in the same surround as the rockers, no plate) and, on the 200TDi, drawn on its cluster above the key. Its red cap now lights because the mud coat skips it (it had copied it, as with the lenses). The pod's legend text was dropped by some browsers because the decal drew fonts a fraction of a pixel high under a big scale; `text` in `utvModel.js` now draws at the real pixel size. "Pointed down" for the UTV switch I took as the same point-up triangle with its flat edge at the bottom.

## The 200TDi's own sound (TODO 115)

The item was clear (a 2.5 litre turbo-diesel four, the clatter of an old direct-injection engine and the turbo spooling, matched to its idle and revs), so I picked the recordings and built the turbo without asking.

- **Recordings**, from the CC0 ones already downloaded (a fresh search would need a Freesound API key, see TODO 19's section): the idle is a Ford Transit 2.5 DI ticking over (Mullumbimby, 622829), the same old clattery kind of four, about 960 rpm with its beats in groups of four, cut into whole cycles from 8 to 26 s and read in order (a belt screech at 6 to 7.8 s is left out). The revs are a Land Rover Safari diesel's pull-up and fall-back (D.jones, 521892, 10 to 16.2 s, about 1,300 to 3,000 rpm). The starter (moved over from the UTV's bank) and the run-down are the Citroen Jumpy van's (chlund, 500193). Looked at and left: the "LandRover idle belt" clip (707216) fires at 59 a second, more like a petrol V8 than a diesel four, and the onboard game drive (504674) has road noise all through. `engine-bank-tdi` is 2.3 MB.
- **Settings**: `pulseRefRpm` 960 (the Transit's tickover), blending into the Land Rover's own firings from 1,300 to 2,000 rpm (`pulseRevLo`, `pulseRevHi`, reading on through them, `pulseRevFollow` 0.08). Levels matched to the sound it had on the UTV's bank, from offline renders: `recVolume` 0.72 puts the idle at -23.3 dB (was -22.9), `pulseRevGain` 1.25 full throttle at -12.9 (was -13.3); `recStarterVolume` 0.4 puts the crank just under the idle.
- **The turbo** is made in the worklet (`turboSample`, added to every engine sound mode like the radiator fan), since none of the recordings has one to hear: its boost heads for a share set by the revs (`turboFromRpm` 1,500 to `turboFullRpm` 3,400) and, more, the throttle, reaching it after a lag (`turboSpoolUp` 0.9 s) and falling slower than it rises would (`turboSpoolDown` 0.6 s); a whistle rising from `turboMinHz` 2,200 to `turboMaxHz` 6,800 Hz with the boost, over a band of air at its pitch (`turboHiss`), both growing with the square of the boost; and, shutting the throttle on boost with no blow-off valve, the air chattering back through the compressor about 14 times a second (`turboFlutter`). `turboVolume` is 0 on everything but the 200TDi, whose 0.14 puts the whistle about 18 dB under the engine flat out. All of it is in each vehicle's Audio tuning. Tested in `tests/turbo.test.js` (silent without one, no boost at tickover, the lag, louder with boost, running down, only the 200TDi).
- **Undone from TODO 19**: the UTV's bank no longer holds the 200TDi's starter, and `recStarterSet` is gone, as nothing needs a second starter in one bank now.
- **Guards**: `tests/engineBanks.test.js` now checks every exported spec (it had a hand-written list without the 200TDi) and that its bank is in the hub's `BANK_NAMES`, the list that loads while the game loads.
- Renders in `.scratch/tdi/` for Jamie: `old.wav` (as it was on the UTV's bank) and `new.wav` through the revving timeline, `turbo-only.wav`, and `start-new.wav` and `crank-new.wav`. Not checked: listening, or the sound in the running game; the whistle is a clean tone and may want more air or less level by ear.

## Sheltering in a cab (TODO 23)

- `src/npc/cabShelter.js` (pure, tested) decides; `game.js` (`updateCabShelter`, `handleCabEvent`) acts, using the existing bench riding (`boardTrailer`, `UtvRide.ask`) and `requestStart`. No new girl state: she is "going" (walking over, or starting it first) and then "in" (aboard on the bench), and the existing hay-ride states do the climbing.
- Rain: when the rain sets in, each girl rolls once for the spell (`rainChance` 0.5) whether she'll use a cab instead of the barn, with a delay of 2 to 8 s (the barn's starts at 4, so the cab is picked first; a girl who is already heading for a cab is busy to the barn logic). The nearest open cab within `cabRange` 45 m with a free bench seat: the UTV (roof and cage) or either Land Rover. She starts a Land Rover that is not running 35% of the time (`rainStartChance`); the UTV has no heater, so she never starts it.
- Cold: on a dry day with warmth under `coldBelow` 0.5 (overcast, fog; the same scale as warming their hands), every 3 s a free girl near a Land Rover has `coldChance` 0.06 to go (doubled if it is already running). She starts it if it is not running (the starting handle on a flat battery, as when asked), and gives up after `goTimeout` 90 s if she cannot get in. Land Rovers only.
- Out: a cold-day girl stays 60 to 170 s and then gets out while it is still cold; a rain girl stays at least 40 to 120 s and then gets out 6 to 22 s after the rain has dropped under `dryBelow` 0.08. She also goes if the ride's own bored timer (120 to 300 s) gets her first, or if you take the vehicle. An engine she started is switched off when none of the girls who came for it is left, unless you are on it. Cooldown 150 s before she does it again.
- She has to be stood about and not resting, warming her hands, on a favour or a diesel job, so it does not fight the other stand-about behaviours. A cab with a girl driving, mounting or cranking it, or with you in it, or the UTV with its bed up, is not open.
- Debug: "Shelter in a cab" in Tune (Girls: moving about); each girl's folder has "gets into the nearest cab" and "starts a Land Rover and sits in it" (console `girl <name> shelter` and `warm-up`, both through `game.sendToCab`, which skips the weather and the dice).
- Lines: `cabRainGo`, `cabColdGo`, `cabIn`, `cabOut` for each girl (`cabShelterLines.js`).
- Not checked in the running game: how the walk, step-in and sitting look in the rain, how often it happens against the barn in a real shower, or the lines by ear. Unit tests cover the decisions, seat counts, give-up, switch-off and the lines.

## Bump-starting (TODO 24)

Decided with Jamie: all three ways (rolling it, the girls pushing, the tractor towing); the wheels turn the engine at the road's pace in the gear, on any manual with a clutch; the girl pushes and you drop the clutch yourself.

- `src/engine/bumpStart.js` (pure, tested): `rollTurn` gives how fast the wheels turn the engine as a fraction of a starter's speed, from the revs the road speed makes in this gear (`gearRpm`, already worked out for a manual) over `maxRpm`: nothing under `rollMinFraction` 0.08, a starter's speed at `rollFullFraction` 0.35 (about 1.7 m/s in second on the quads), and scaled by how far the clutch is out (nothing under `rollClutchMin` 0.5). In `EngineModel.updateStarter` this puts it in the cranking state with no starter whirr, needs the key on and not a seizure, and does not touch the battery. The catch chance is the starter's, times `rollCatchTwoStroke` 1 or `rollCatchFourStroke` 0.55, so choke, cold, flooding and an empty tank still count and a cold four-stroke wants a longer roll. Tunables are in the engine params.
- Clutch in again stops the turning; it can also restart a stalled engine that is still rolling above the minimum, as a real one does. `engine.caughtBy` says whether the starter, the cord or the wheels fired it; the bucket list's "Bump start" (2 points) uses it.
- The tractor towing is the existing chain: the towed vehicle's wheels turn, so a manual with the clutch in and then out fires. Nothing extra was built for it.
- The girls pushing (`src/npc/pushStart.js`, pure, tested; a `pushing` state in `companionDriving.js`): you at the controls of a stopped manual, the girl idle, the wheel's "Push start it". She walks to a point 0.8 m behind the back wheels, turns the key on if you haven't, and after 0.6 s pushes with `force` 520 N (Amy 0.8 of it), an acceleration between 0.45 and 2.2 m/s2, up to 3.4 m/s, applied as an impulse on the vehicle's body. At 2.2 m/s she shouts `pushNow` and again every 3.5 s until you do; she says `pushStart` going in, `pushCaught` when it fires and `pushTired` after 16 s (Amy 10) or 45 m, then stands. She stops if you get off. Her pose is a forward lean with both hands IK'd to the back of the vehicle. Lines in `pushLines.js` (three each, recorded). Tuning: "Girl pushing a start". Debug: a "push-start" button on each girl's folder (and `girl <name> push-start`); the Running folder and `rolled` in the console show the wheel turn.
- Left alone: the engine does not drag the vehicle back while it is rolling on a dead engine in gear (so it rolls rather freely); the girls do not drop the clutch for you, and neither girl will push a vehicle you are not in.
- Not checked in the running game: the walk behind, the pose and the hands on the back, whether 3.4 m/s is enough for a Land Rover in second by hand, how the fire sounds with no starter, the new lines by ear (not yet recorded when this was written), or the wheel option's wording. Unit tests cover the roll maths, a two-stroke and a four-stroke catching, the clutch in and out, the key off and a seizure, and the push (shouts, tiring, giving up, abandoning, the key).

## A game without the girls

Asked for by Jamie: a URL switch that leaves the girls out, with a name that means nothing so it can sit in the address bar without saying what it does. It is `?vx=2` (exactly that value; `src/npc/leftOut.js`, tested), and it is kept out of the README and the FAQ on purpose.

- Nobody is built rather than built and hidden: `game.companions` is empty, so everything that loops over the girls has nothing to do, and autonomy is switched off so nothing that waits on a girl starts. The per-girl ledgers (favour, skills, falling out, romance) are still made for both, untouched, so code that reads them by name still works. Sitting on the back waiting for a ride (`updateRideOffer`) was the one place that needed the first girl and now just returns.
- Checked headless on three seeds (one cold and wet): no girls, no errors, riding every vehicle there, sitting on a back, and every debug tab. Not checked: the console's `girl` commands, or every wheel and scripted moment by hand.

## The throttle and brake on the keyboard

Asked for by Jamie: a dab on `S` braked fully and the girls called it hard braking. Decided with Jamie (all as recommended, then made slower to build, since half a second of holding a key turned out not to be long):

- The brake squeezes in on the keyboard (`src/vehicle/pedalKeys.js`, pure, tested): the first instant of a press is `start` 0.3 of full brake, rising to full over `rise` 1 s while held, off at once when let go. Every vehicle you drive goes through it (they are all a `Quad`), including the tractor's brake pedal and `W` braking in reverse; the girls' own driving sets part-brakes itself and is untouched. The parking brake and the reverse button only ask whether the brake is held, so they are unaffected. Tuning: "Brake key" (Driving and towing).
- Then the throttle, asked for straight after: `W` (and `S` backing up in reverse) opens it the same way, `start` 0.25 rising to full over `rise` 1.2 s, and it snaps shut on release as a sprung twist-grip does. It is applied before the thumb and hand throttles are added, so those still hold where they were set. Two things counted your blips on the old snap-open throttle and had to change: the rev-off's `blipAt` is 0.3 (was 0.5), and the drag challenge, which wanted the throttle to jump from under 0.15 to over 0.6 between two frames and so could never be answered with a squeezed throttle, now counts a rise from under `blipFrom` 0.15 past `blipSnap` 0.3 within `blipRise` 0.6 s. A blip of about a tenth of a second counts for both. The automatics' gentler reverse throttle is opened the same way up to its own top. Tuning: "Throttle key".
- What she calls hard braking (`src/npc/hardBrake.js`, pure, tested) was a smoothed deceleration over 6 m/s² above 4 m/s, hardcoded, so a quarter-second dab at speed crossed it. Now the slowing has to stay over `voiceHardBrakeDecel` for `voiceHardBrakeTime` and shed `voiceHardBrakeShed` m/s, from over `voiceHardBrakeMinSpeed`, once per stop. Defaults 6 m/s², 0.4 s, 2.5 m/s, 4 m/s; Amy 5.5 and 0.3 s (she notices sooner), Jess 6.5 and 3 m/s. In each girl's tuning.
- Not checked in the running game: how a tap and a held stop or a held throttle feel on each vehicle, and whether these times are right by hand. Her lurching forward on the back follows the quad's real deceleration, so it should shrink on a tap without any change, but I didn't watch it.

## Engine mounts (TODO 118)

Nothing was undecided in the item, so it was built as written, with these calls.

- `src/vibration/engineMounts.js` (pure, tested). Each firing used to kick the frame, bars and head. Now `mountGain` of it (0 to 1) goes into a separate engine oscillator pair (`mountRoll`, `mountPitch` in the vibration model) at its own frequency (`mountFreq`), and only `mountPass` of that reaches the frame and `mountCamera` of that your head. The rest still kicks the frame. 0 is the old behaviour exactly (a test checks), so the quads' singles stay as they were: they thump the whole frame. The Land Rover has 0.7 at 9.5 Hz, the tractor 0.6 at 8.5 Hz, the UTV 0.55 at 10.5 Hz.
- The lurch: the engine stopping or catching kicks the engine oscillators (`mountStopKick`, scaled by how mounted it is, half as much on catching). Nothing happens on a bolted engine with sound mounts.
- Worn mounts are a fifth fault, `mounts`, in `faults.js`, so they get the panel entries, the console path, "Put it right", the girls' noticing and the history for free from `FAULT_KINDS`. They start while the engine runs (`mountsEvery` 5,400 s, slower than the rest since rubber perishes) and take `mountsSeconds` 1,500 s to go from sound to shot. Wear adds to the share the engine takes (`mountWornShare`) and to the pass-through (`mountWornPass`), so a worn single shakes more through the seat too, and `MountClonk` clonks the engine when the throttle moves by `mountClonkStep` within `mountClonkWindow`, only with worn mounts and scaled by wear. A slow roll of the throttle does not.
- Fixing: `M` in the toolbox is a rough fix like the others (it must not clash with the map key, which `game.js` now ignores while the toolbox is open); the workbench has a ninth job, "Renew the engine mounts", last in the list so the existing job numbers do not move. A botched one leaves the usual `botchedLevel`.
- The girls: `faultMounts` (Amy knows what it is; Jess says it has character) and `histMounts` (a fault it already had). Both are in `faultLines.js` and `historyLines.js`, so they are recorded with the rest.
- Not checked in the running game: how any of it feels or looks. The numbers were picked, not tuned by hand; in a test the worn mounts shake a mounted engine's frame and camera at least 1.5 times as much as sound ones. Left alone: the walk-round (the girls do not find worn mounts on a walk round the quad), a clonk sound, and a mounts gauge in Running on the Vehicles tab.

## Keeping a sick one alive (TODO 119)

Decided with Jamie (all as recommended, with two changes): she mostly does not switch it off when she gives up, and "sick" covers more than a hard start, so heavy wear and a creeping choke count too.

- `src/npc/keepAlive.js` (pure, tested). `sickness` scores a quad from what the girls can tell: the goes the last start took (`quad.startTrouble`, set when a girl's start finishes and cleared once it stops), plug fouling, a weak battery, heavy wear, the choke-creeps quirk and erratic running. Each cause is worth a score; `sickFrom` 0.5, so a hard start, wear over 0.5 or a creeping choke is enough alone, and fouling (0.35) or a weak battery (0.35) need a second cause.
- `KeepAlive` is the hand on the throttle. While it is running, sick and unattended she gives it `holdBase` 0.1 plus whatever gets it to `fastIdle` 1.5 times its idle, and a full handful for `catchHold` 0.4 s when the revs sag under `falterBelow` 0.85 of idle (not more often than every `catchGap` 3 s). She goes through the quad's `helperThrottle`, the way the revving and prank features do, so the engine, smoke and shake follow. Those features now leave a quad alone while she holds it.
- Who and when: the girl who last had the quad (the left-running record) walks over and sits on the back when it is sick, running and unattended and she is free within `goRange` 14 m (`goChance` 0.5 a second). She stays until you come or `giveUpAfter` 180 s pass, then gets off and does not do it again for `cooldown` 240 s. When she gives up she switches it off only `keyOffChance` 0.15 of the time; otherwise she leaves it running to its luck.
- Letting go: if you take the quad (or she gets off) after holding it for `letGoAfter` 4 s, a sick one dies with chance `letGoStall` 0.6 times its score, and she says so. If it dies under her she says so too.
- Her reasons: `keepSit` is the practical one. `keepTruth` is the buzz: Jess says it once after `truthAfterJess` 40 s; Amy only after `truthAfterAmy` 90 s with you within `truthRange` 4 m. The extra shaking on the back is item 124's, not built here; the girls' buzz tally is untouched.
- Panel and console: Running shows how sick it is and why; Problems has "hard start (goes it took)" (cleared by "Put it right", with a test); `hardStart` under `engineHealth` in the console; the Girls tab and `girl <name> keep-alive` send a girl to the picked vehicle (it makes the quad count as hard to start first). Tuning folder "Keeping it alive".
- Checked headless once: a forced go had Jess walk over, sit on the red quad and hold it at about 1,680 rpm with a throttle of about 0.11 for 24 s, with no page errors. Not checked: how it looks (the hand on the throttle has no pose of its own, so she just sits as on any back seat), the lines in play, or whether the numbers feel right over a real game.

## Charging that follows the revs (TODO 120)

Nothing was undecided; item 119's hold-the-revs routine is reused for the girls.

- `src/engine/charging.js` (pure, tested). The alternator gives `batteryChargeIdle` 0.3 of the full rate at idle and all of it by `batteryChargeFullAt` 0.3 of the way up the rev range. The running ignition draws `batteryRunDraw` 0.0001 a second and lamps on the battery's supply `batteryLightDraw` 0.0004. Net: a quad at idle with those lamps lit holds about even (it was a flat +0.0017 before), idling with nothing lit gains 0.0004 a second (twenty minutes or more from flat), and revved it gains about 0.0012 to 0.0016 (ten to fourteen minutes from flat, measured in the running game at 0.0012 a second with the girl holding about 2.3 times idle).
- Which lamps load it: only those run off the battery, where the light follows `steadyLights` (the loan quad, the tractor) or the UTV and Land Rover light switch when it is on (`engine.lightLoad`, set by the vehicle). The old quads' lights are on the magneto, as they always were, and draw nothing from the battery.
- Dimming: those lamps also dim when the battery is weak and the revs are low (`lampSupply`, up to 65% down at a nearly flat battery at idle), and come back with the revs or a charge.
- Dying: under `batteryRunBelow` 0.4 a petrol engine with a starter can't keep its spark going below `batteryRunRevs` 1.15 times idle, and dies with a chance of up to `batteryStallRate` 0.25 a second (worse the flatter). A jump start takes the battery to about 0.3 to 0.4 in seconds (the leads give 0.06 a second), so it dies at its first stop unless the revs are held. Diesels (compression ignition) and the engines with no starter motor (magneto ignition) are exempt. It takes no draw from the random stream when it is well, so seeded runs don't shift.
- The girls: a battery under 0.45 now counts as sick on its own in `keepAlive.js` (it was a light cause), and she goes on holding the revs once it is no longer that weak, until it reaches `chargeDone` 0.85. For charging she holds `chargeRevs` 2 times idle against 1.5 for a sick engine. Amy sits there up to `chargeHoldAmy` 720 s and says it needs ten minutes (`keepCharge`), and `keepCharged` when it is full. Jess is bored after `chargeBoredJess` 60 s and gets off, leaving it to idle, so the battery goes flat again later (she does not switch it off or let a stall be rolled; the battery does the rest). Any free girl near a running weak-battery quad with nobody on it may go: Amy readily, Jess at `chargeGoJess` 0.3 of the rate.
- The panel and console show the charge a minute at the current revs and lamps ("charging"); the new tunables are in each vehicle's Engine group.
- Checked headless once: a running red quad at 0.3 battery had Amy walk over unasked, sit on it and hold it, with the battery rising from 0.30 to 0.335 in 28 s and no page errors. Not checked: how the dimming looks, the lines in play, or the balance of the numbers over a real game.

The cab is closed to everyone else while she is in it (`isSulkCab`), so the other girl can't climb in; you can still ride it, but looking at it gives the knock prompt and not "Ride the Land Rover".

Keeps a little choke and 0.15 throttle on while the engine is below half temperature, which a headless run showed it needed (without it the engine died at 298 rpm as soon as she stopped tending it). Jess: revs `jessRevs` 3 times (`sulkAnswer` shouted over a blip of the throttle for `revSeconds` 0.9 s), then relents on the 4th. A knock that does not do it takes `knockCut` 40 s off the sulk. `pushMax` 3 knocks in a row, each within `knockGap` 2.5 s of the last, is pushing it: `sulkPushed` and `pushExtra` 120 s on. Talked round, she says `sulkRound` and gets out; out by time she says `sulkOut`.

## Ready and waiting (TODO 123)

Decided with Jamie (all as recommended): a new option on her wheel, and she sits on the back (always, in practice: only a quad with a free pillion seat will do).

- `src/npc/readyWaiting.js` (pure, tested) times it; `game.js` (`askToWait`, `readyQuadFor`, `updateReady`) acts. The option "I'll be a minute" is on the wheel whenever she is stood about and not already doing it. The quad is the one you ride most (`headStart.usual()`), else her favourite, else the nearest with a free back seat she may ride (`readyQuadFor`). She starts it with the ordinary routine if it is off (so she can flood it or give up), then mounts the pillion seat and stays (`seatedByChoice` off, so she is not bored off it).
- Waiting: she revs it every `revMin`/`revMax` 18 to 38 s (the quad's `helperThrottle` for `revSeconds` 0.7 s). The horn starts at `tootFromJess` 40 s and `tootFromAmy` 75 s, then every `tootGap` 28 s, shortening by `tootRamp` 0.82 each time to a floor of `tootGapMin` 9 s; short toots (`tootLength`, the horns' own 0.22 s) and from the `tootsLong` 3rd on long ones of 0.7 s; she says `readyToot` on the first and `readyImpatient` on the `impatientAt` 2nd. `Horns.toot` is new and shares the answering beeps' machinery, so the quad's horn, the wheel pad and the sound all follow.
- You come back (within `doneRange` 2.5 m, or on that quad): `readyHere`, and she is a normal pillion. Too long (`giveUpJess` 150 s, `giveUpAmy` 210 s): `readyGiveUp`, switching it off `keyOffChance` 0.5 of the time, and she gets off. If it dies under her, or she never gets going in `goTimeout` 150 s, it just ends.
- While she waits nothing else revs her quad or switches it off: `holdingIdle` on the companion (the same flag item 119's hold uses) is also true while `readyHolding` is set, and the rev-off, prank, drown-out and key-off checks already stand aside for it.
- A cold engine: she keeps a little choke and 0.15 throttle on until it is half warm, once she has stopped starting it and while she walks over and sits (without it the engine died at 298 rpm before she got there, in a headless run). She stops tending it at 0.3 temperature, lower than the cab shelter's 0.5.
- Debug: "Ready and waiting" in Tune (Girls: how they feel about you); each girl's folder has "gets a quad going and waits on the back for you" (console `girl <name> wait-ready`). Lines: `readyGo`, `readyToot`, `readyImpatient`, `readyGiveUp`, `readyHere` for each girl (`readyLines.js`).
- Checked headless once, with the toot times shortened: Jess started the red quad, sat on the back with it running at a held throttle, and the horn toots counted up, with no page errors. Not checked: how it looks and sounds (the revs, the horn, her on the seat), the lines by ear, or the real-time pacing (the stretch to the first toot, the give-up times) in a real game.

## Seats that ride differently (TODO 124)

Decided with Jamie (all as recommended): you feel it too, the girls react with lines only (no new pose), and a hard enough bounce is a slam.

- `src/vehicle/seatRide.js` (pure, tested). Each seat is a damped spring driven by the frame's vertical acceleration (`UpAccel`, from the model's height plus the vibration heave, clamped at `accelMax` 60), so a bump pushes it the other way and it rings. The kinds and their starting numbers (gain, Hz, damping, extra pitch share, travel, slam point): pillion 0.9, 3.2, 0.35, 0.6, 5 cm, 0.9; rack 0.7, 4, 0.4, 0.4, 4 cm; front (the cab seats) 0.5, 3, 0.5, 0.2, 4 cm; rear (the 200TDi's back bench) 1.1, 2.1, 0.12, 0.7, 9 cm, 0.8; bed 1.2, 5, 0.2, 0.7, 12 cm, 0.75. The back bench has the lowest, least damped spring and the biggest travel, so it bounces and rings; the bed is short and sharp.
- Pitch: the seat's distance behind the middle times the vibration body's pitch, times the kind's share, added to its height (rear is +z in the model), so the back rides the pitch harder than the nose. Not the physics pitch, which the anchors already follow as part of the model.
- Slam: the seat has run into its stop (`slam` of its travel) faster than `slamSpeed` 0.25 m/s, at most every `slamGap` 0.7 s per seat. The travel is a hard stop (it bottoms out or tops out). In the bed, an upward slam is her being thrown up.
- `Quad.updateSeats` moves the seat's anchor and its matching camera anchor (the pillion's, the bench's `cameraLeft`/`cameraMid`, the rear seats'), so a person sitting there, you included, moves with it. The driver's seat is left alone, so driving is as it was. Offsets are divided by the model's scale. `quad.seatSlams` lists the step's slams.
- Reactions: `Game.updateSeatSlams` finds the girl in that seat (on the pillion or rack, or in a bench slot of that vehicle) and says `seatPillion`, `seatRack`, `seatRear`, `seatBed` or (thrown up) `seatBedAir` with chance 0.6 when the slam is over 0.3 strength; the voice's own cooldowns space them. Lines in `seatLines.js`. There is no reaction for the front seats, and no new pose (no grab handle).
- Panel: each vehicle has a "Seats" folder next to its Vibration (the vehicle's own tuning on the Vehicles tab).
- Checked headless once: every vehicle in the seed built its seats (pillion and rack on the quads, the two front seats and the bed on the UTV, the front seats on the Series III), shaking the frame by hand moved the anchors a few millimetres, and there were no page errors. Not checked: how it looks and feels in a real ride (the numbers are physically reasoned but not tuned by hand), the lines in play, the cattle grid throwing a girl up in the bed, or the 200TDi's back bench in that seed (it was not in it).

## Buzz-seeking requests (TODO 127)

Asked as the others are: you on foot, close, free girl, after the cooldown.

The quads near her with a free back and wear over that are the options, the roughest first. She starts it first if it is off, then sits on the back (`requestBuzzSit`) and stays a while like the other sit-on-it choices. Jess says `selfBuzz` openly ("This one shakes properly now. Mine."); Amy's `selfBuzz` is neutral ("I think I will sit on this one for a bit"), since she never admits it. Item 126's favourite-follows-the-buzz already makes the rough one her favourite, and now she goes to it.

## Numb (TODO 128)

Nothing was undecided. The item said the lines were already there; they were not (nothing in the voice files mentioned it), so they are new.

- `src/npc/numbness.js` (pure, tested). Numbness builds while she sits on a seat that shakes at least `shakeFrom` 0.4 on the same body-shake level the buzz counts (a warm idle is about 0.6, a cold one about 1.2), at `level / shakeFull` (capped at `shakeCap` 2) over `fullAfter` 150 s: about two and a half minutes at level 1, a bit over four at a warm idle's 0.6, 75 s at the cap. It tingles at `tingleAt` 0.65 of the way, and is numb at 1, then resets. It drains at `drainPerSecond` 0.05 while she is not sitting on it, so a short sit leaves nothing.
- Which sits count (`companionNumb.js`): on the back of a quad of her own choice (`seatedByChoice`: the bored sit and the buzz-seeking sit of item 127), parked with the engine running and nobody riding. Not holding it for you (`holdingIdle`: item 119's hold, waiting for you, warming it up), and not while you ride with her. Those have their own reasons to stay put.
- When numb she gets off (the ordinary dismount), walks `walkDistance` 5 m away, stretches (the existing stretch fidget, `walkAfterMin`/`Max` 3 to 7 s) and gets back on with the same sort of sit if the quad is still running and her seat is free; if not she just carries on standing about. She says `numbTingle`, `numbGo`, `numbStretch` and `numbBack`. The voice now keeps `level` (the shake it already measured) for this.

When both girls are free and within `together` 7 m, the game has been going `startAfter` 240 s, each has felt at least `minTotal` 60 shake-seconds across the quads, nobody else is talking and `cooldown` 300 s has passed, they have `chance` 0.015 a second to compare. `ranking` orders the quads by a tally, leaving out any she has felt nothing on, and only the quads in this game.

A girl who cannot (a seat taken, a quad she may not ride) just stays, and the verdicts are said anyway.
