## The girls' attention (TODO 130)

What a girl looks at is now decided in one place, `src/npc/attention.js` (pure, tested), instead of a timer that glanced at you and a fidget that turned her head at random.

- Each frame she is handed what's around her (you, the other girl, moving vehicles: `game.watchNotices` builds `attentionWorld`), her focus (from her state's `look`, or a sub-activity's `attend()`), her intent (where she's about to walk) and any notices (things that just happened). Each is scored: how much that sort of thing catches the eye, times how close (1 near, 0.3 at the edge of range), times whether she can see it (full within 1.1 rad of ahead, down to 0.1 behind her; sounds are heard from anywhere), times her interest (her `attentionEngines`, `attentionVehicles`, and for you `attentionYou` times 1 + 0.8 of how much she likes you). She looks at the best. She holds a look for 0.8 s before something only a little better (30%) can take it, though anything startling (a backfire, a bang, a horn, a tip) takes it at once. She tires of a thing: interest halves after 3 s of looking and the tiredness fades over 10 s once she looks away, so she glances between you, her friend and gazing about (`attentionIdle` 0.3, a point out in front, new each time) rather than staring. Talking to her, she looks at you and doesn't tire.
- Notices: `game.notice(kind, at, { key, strength })` is the one way the world gets her attention, and `NOTICE_KINDS` the list of what can happen (engine start, stall, backfire, pop, spit back, boil-over, rev, horn, tipped, bang), each with how long it stays interesting, how far it carries and whether it is heard or has to be seen; how much each catches the eye is the shared "What catches the eye" tuning. `src/npc/noticeWatch.js` (pure, tested) turns every vehicle's engine events, horn and tipping into notices, and counts a stood engine as revved (how it decides was changed by TODO 136, below), at most every 1.2 s.
- Every state says what she looks at (`look` in `STATES`): driving, the road 10 m ahead, strongly (weight 2, so she glances away only now and then); starting, cranking and pushing, the vehicle; getting on, the seat; getting off, where she steps; pranking, what she's meddling with; on the rack, ahead of the quad; walking, where she's going; idle, resting and the hay ride, nothing in particular. On the back behind you the pillion pose keeps her head (`'own'`) but turns it to whatever has caught her eye instead of a random glance, leaving you to its look-back. Warming her hands is the first sub-activity to use `attend()`.
- Walking from standing (idle or resting) she looks at where she's going for `lookLead` 0.6 s before setting off (the walking clip starts after it); `hurry: true` in the walk's data skips it. `StateMachine` now remembers the previous state for this.
- Held over her shoulder (past 1.1 rad) for 1.5 s, stood about and `freeToTurn()`, she turns to face it, and back to her own facing after.
- Her eyes sweep to the next thing (`lookSweep` 7/s) rather than jumping. The head and neck turn up to 1.3 rad (was 1.2). The chest doesn't turn with the look, because the hands on the bars are placed before the look is applied and would come off them.
- Removed: the girls' `glanceEvery*`, `glanceHold*` and `glanceRange` params and the `lookAround` fidget (the pillion pose's own glance params stay, for when nothing has caught her eye). The Girls tab shows "looking at" for each girl, with a "Make a bang behind her" button (`girl <name> bang`), and the console has `girl.<name>.lookingAt`.
- The pattern for anything new is written into CLAUDE.md ("The girls' attention"), and `tests/companionLooks.test.js` fails if a state has no `look` or the game sends a notice kind attention doesn't know.
- While this was being built, another session's commit (825f79c, the girls comparing notes) took in this item's first `game.js` changes, which needed files not yet committed; the core of this item was committed and pushed straight after (84c60b7) so main was never left broken.
- Checked: tests, and headless on seed 3 with both girls stood in the yard: they glanced between you, each other and looking about every few seconds, Amy held on Jess while Jess talked, a bang 8 m behind turned both heads within half a second, and Amy turned round to face it after 1.5 s; no errors. Not checked: how it looks close up (the sweep speed, the head limits), the girls driving, on the back and on the hay ride, and whether revving near them and a quad going past feel right in play.

## How the girls react (TODO 131)

Something sudden near a girl now shows in her body as well as in what she says, built on the notices from TODO 130 so nothing new has to be wired twice.

- `src/npc/reactions.js` (pure, tested): six reactions, each with a length and what it needs: flinch (0.9 s, anywhere: hunched, shoulders up, head down, hands up in front of her), step back (1.1 s, on her feet: the old boil-over jump back, 3.2 m/s easing off over 0.5 s, plus the hunch), cover ears (2.2 s, hands free), gasp (2.4 s, hands to her mouth), cheer (2.2 s: arms up and pumping, or a clap when her `reactCheerArms` is under 0.5) and turn away (3 s, on her feet: turns her back on it, looks away and waves it off). `NOTICE_REACTIONS` maps notices to them: backfire and bang within 20 m, spit back within 4, a pop within 3 (30%), a horn within 4 (60%) make her flinch; a lurch within 3.5 m or a boil-over within 4 makes her step back; a rev within 5 m at strength 0.8 or more makes her cover her ears (`reactEarsChance`: Amy 0.85, Jess 0.15); a tip within 40 m makes her gasp; a jump within 45 m makes her cheer (`reactCheerChance`: Amy 0.45 with a clap, Jess 0.95 with arms up). Nothing about her own vehicle except a flinch at its backfire. A stronger reaction takes over from a weaker one (step back, then flinch, then ears and gasp, then cheer and turning away); otherwise one has to be easing off before the next.
- New notices: a lurch (a vehicle speeding up by more than 3.5 m/s per second from under 4 m/s, at most once a second) and a jump (landing after 0.35 s or more in the air). Revving was redefined after watching it in play: every pull-away from a standstill counted, because the revs climb before a quad moves. Now it's the revs climbing faster than 2,500 rpm/s and coming back down while the vehicle never goes faster than 0.5 m/s, counted at the top of the blip.
- `src/npc/companionReacting.js` poses them as the last rig poser, so a reaction shows over whatever else her arms are doing; `mayReact` keeps hands reactions to a girl with her hands free (on foot, sat resting, on the hay trailer or the rack; not driving, holding on behind you, carrying, warming her hands or starting a quad). Turning away overrides her facing while it lasts and attends to a point away from the smoke. The two old direct calls now go through `react()`: the spit back under her (`startle`, removed) and the boil-over jump back; the two-stroke smoke and the haze turn her away when there's a wind to say where it comes from, and just wave it off otherwise.
- The hand poses were set from headless screenshots from in front of each girl (`.scratch/pp/reactshot.mjs`): the IK targets are wrists and the fingers reach past them, so the first flinch and clap crossed her arms and the first covered ears were at the back of her head; the targets now sit off her middle and beside her head.
- Debug: a button for each reaction in each girl's folder (`girl <name> flinch`, `cover-ears` and so on), and "reacting" under what she's doing (`girl.<name>.reacting` in the console). Tuning: the four `react*` params in each girl's own group, and the lurch, jump and rev numbers in "What catches the eye".
- Checked: tests; headless screenshots of every reaction on both girls; and two rides past the girls on seeds 3 and 11, which gave genuine flinches at a backfire, ears covered at a quad revved flat out in neutral, a step back from a hard pull-away beside Amy, and both gasping at the kids' quad going over, with no errors. Not checked: the cheer at a real jump in play, and how the reactions look while seated on the rack or the hay trailer.

## Whether a girl is free (TODO 132)

Every feature now asks one question, `companion.availability()` (`src/npc/availability.js`, pure, tested), instead of `fsm.is('idle')` plus its own pick of flags.

- Before, 31 checks in `game.js` used twelve different combinations of: no menu open, not warming her hands, not off to sit somewhere, no diesel job, not off with your quad, not fallen off the hay, not resting, not carrying. So a girl warming her hands could be picked by one feature while another thought she was busy. Now: she's free when she's stood about (idle), has none of the jobs (`JOBS`: off with your quad, catching up the hay trailer, carrying something, going to sit somewhere, tidying the quads, walking off a numb bum, warming her hands), no feature holds her (`hold`/`release`, new), and nothing holds both girls (`worldHold`: a menu open, the red diesel job). It says why when she isn't.
- Three kinds of asking. A feature giving her something to do uses `free`. Noticing and remarks (two-stroke smoke, the haze, barn fumes, talking about the quads, and her eight remarks in `companionVoice.js`) use `settled`/`canRemark` (stood about or sat resting), so a girl warming her hands by a two-stroke still coughs, and one sat on the bales still remarks. You asking her (the debug panel, the console) uses `asked`, where the soft jobs (tidying, the numb walk, warming her hands) give way and the farm's holds don't count, and the toast says what has her.
- A feature checking its own girl mid-job passes `mine`: the hay ride was the one that needed it (a girl who fell off counted as free to catch it up; her own `hayFell` would otherwise have stopped her ever getting back on). Stricter in places, on purpose: a girl tidying the quads, walking off a numb bum or carrying something is now left alone by every feature, where before only some features knew.
- The request methods' own `fsm.is('idle')` guards stayed: they say what the state machine can do from where she is, and some are called while the feature's own job flag is set (the places feature holds her spot, then sends her).
- `STATE_WHY` names every state for a person; `tests/girlsMinds.test.js` (renamed from the attention test) fails if a state has no entry, or if game.js decides she's free from her state and flags again. The Girls tab shows "free" (and what has her), as does `girl.<name>.free` in the console.
- Checked: tests; headless, the girls left to themselves for 90 s and with boredom forced short for 60 s, against a worktree of the code before this change on the same seed: the same kinds of choices (resting, starting, driving, getting on), with the run-to-run spread the game's own randomness gives, and no errors. Not checked: every feature by hand.

## Needs and a chooser (TODO 133): the plan

Written before any code, as the item asks.

**What's wrong now.** Each thing a girl does on her own has its own clock and its own dice: boredom rolls among eleven choices when its timer runs out; warming her hands rolls 20% every 2 s while it's cold and a quad idles near her; a cab in the rain is decided once a spell by its own coin, racing the barn doorway's own delay; tea rolls every frame; asking you a favour rolls 15% every 5 s once it's due. Nothing joins one choice to the next, so she reads as random.

**The shape.**
- `src/npc/needs.js` (pure): five needs per girl, 0 (met) to 1 (pressing): warmth (rises with the day's chill outside, faster wet; met by warming her hands, a cab, the barn, a ride), company (rises with nobody near; met by being near you or her friend, riding together), excitement (creeps up all the time; met by driving, riding, the shake, jumps, doughnuts), rest (rises while she's on the go; met by sitting), and purpose (rises while she stands about with nothing to do, at a rate set from the old boredom wait so the cadence and its spread stay as they were; met by starting anything she chose). Rates are her params, so Jess's excitement climbs faster and Amy feels the cold sooner.
- `src/npc/chooser.js` (pure): offers are what she could do now, each `{ key, satisfies: { need: amount }, weight }`, from `OFFER_KINDS` (which says what each kind satisfies and which of her params weights it: the old chance params become weights, so her personality carries over). Every couple of seconds while she's free, each offer scores the needs it meets times its weight times a whim (the old `boredWhim`), and she takes the best if it clears a threshold; the scores are kept for the debug panel.
- Features stop deciding when. Each moved feature's pure module keeps everything about whether she could (`offers(ctx)`: weather, distance, cooldowns, room) and everything after she's started (staying, leaving, complaining), and gets `take(offer)` for what its dice roll used to start. Its own start roll goes. Details inside a start (whether she starts the engine of the cab she gets into) keep their dice: that's how, not whether.
- In game.js, `updateMinds` runs the needs for each girl, gathers the offers from the moved features and from her boredom choices, and lets the chooser pick. With autonomy off nothing new starts (warming her hands included, which never used to check). Shelter offers can take a girl who is sat resting, so the rain still gets her off the bales.
- TODO 134 comes with it: the Girls tab shows her needs (settable), what she chose and what for, what she nearly chose with the scores, and when she next thinks; the console the same.

**Stage one (this item).** Boredom's choices (wander, get on a quad, start one, drive, sit somewhere, hop on the back, sit on a rough one, tidy the quads, show off, jumps, doughnuts), warming her hands, sheltering (the barn doorway and a cab in the rain, a Land Rover's heater on a cold day), tea on the rack, and asking you a favour. **Later stages**, left on their own dice until they move, each to be an offer the same way: the rides together (follow the leader, races, drag), pranks, rev-offs, the tractor and other vehicles taken for a go, the head start, nicking your quad, the jumpers, the red diesel mistake, the rock prank, falling out, the romance, the L plates, quad nicknames and talk, comparing notes, sulking, waiting ready, keeping a sick one alive.

**Checks.** Each moved feature's tests move from its dice to its offers and take; needs and the chooser get their own. Headless: the girls left alone on the old code (a worktree) and the new, same seeds, comparing what they get up to, on a fine day, a cold day and in rain.

## Needs and a chooser (TODO 133, with 134): what was built

Built as planned above, with these changes and numbers.

- Needs (`src/npc/needs.js`): the day's chill for a person is the old warming rule (a dull or wet day, weather warmth under 0.6) plus the temperature's chill, 0 under the barn roof or in a cab. Rates per second: cold 0.025 at full chill (Amy 0.032) and 0.035 more when it's raining on her, warming up 0.06 (a third of that on a mild day anyway); lonely 0.004 with nobody within 15 m, company 0.03 with someone within 6 m or riding with her; restless 0.002 all the time (Jess 0.003), going fast or in the air meets it at 0.06; tired 0.003 on the go, rested 0.03 sitting; something to do builds at 1 over her boredom wait while she's free and fades at 0.05 while she's busy. Taking something meets a quarter of what it satisfies, and all of her want of something to do.
- Chooser (`src/npc/chooser.js`): eighteen kinds of offer in `OFFER_KINDS`, each with what it satisfies. Scores are needs met times weight times a whim (log-normal, 0.6 wide, the old `boredWhim`); she takes the best if it reaches 0.5, thinking every 2 s give or take half. The plan had a second gate (some need pressing at least 0.45) as well; on a cold day her warmth need sat at 1 with nothing near to meet it, which held the gate open all day and would have made both girls busier, so the gate went: a score is already the needs it meets, so a girl whose pressing needs nothing on offer meets is content to stand. Starting a quad now meets a little warmth too (an engine to warm her hands by). Boredom's odds became weights divided by 0.25, so a typical one is about 1.
- Moved: boredom's eleven choices (`boredOffers` on the companion: the timer and `pickBoredChoice` went, `boredOdds` keeps the weights), warming her hands (`warmChance`, `joinChance` and `checkEvery` became `warmWeight` 1 and `joinWeight` 1.3), the cab (the once-a-spell coin and its delay became `rainWeight` 1.1 against the barn doorway's `shelterWeight` 1; the cold-day roll became `coldWeight` 0.6, doubled for one already running), the barn doorway (its random delay went: the rain builds her need until shelter wins), tea (`chance` became `weight` 0.7; the debug panel's forced one still starts in `update`) and favours (`chance` and `checkEvery` became `weight` 0.8). Each module's `offers(ctx)` and `take(offer)` replaced its start, and game.js's handling of each start moved into `handleWarmingEvent`, `handleCabEvent`, `handlePlaceAction`, `handleTeaEvent` and `handleRequestEvent`, which a chosen offer and the update loop both call.
- Shelter is `urgent`: a girl who isn't free but is only doing something idle (sat resting, or a soft job such as warming her hands: `interruptible()`) weighs only urgent offers, so the rain still gets her off the bales and away from an engine.
- Debug (TODO 134, and item 83 with it): each girl's folder has "Why": her five needs as sliders, what she chose and for which need (the one that made up most of its score), what she nearly chose with the scores, when she next thinks or what's stopping her, and "Think now". The console has `girl.<name>.needs.<need>` (settable), `chose`, `nearlyChose` and `thinking`. Tuning: the `need*`, `choose*` and `thinkEvery` params in each girl's own group, and each moved feature's weights in its own.
- Checked: tests (the moved features' tests now check their offers and that they never start on their own; needs and the chooser have their own; the old boredom picker's tests in five files now go through the odds and the chooser). Headless against a worktree of the code before, same seeds: on a fine day the girls take something up about four times in two minutes between them, against two or three before (livelier, and within the spread from run to run); in the rain Amy left a quad for the barn doorway and Jess got into a cab within a minute, where before both were still starting quads; on a cold day with a quad left idling near them Jess went and warmed her hands on it, as before; no errors. The Why folder and console values checked headless. Not checked: a long game end to end, and how the sliders feel in play.
- Still on their own dice (later stages, as the plan lists): the rides together, pranks, rev-offs, vehicles taken for a go, the head start, nicking your quad, the jumpers, the red diesel mistake, the rock prank, falling out, the romance, the L plates, nicknames and quad talk, comparing notes, sulking, waiting ready, keeping a sick one alive, and getting bored on the back of a quad.

## Going about it (TODO 135)

What a girl does once the chooser has picked, in `src/npc/intent.js` (pure, tested).

- Saying so: most of what she chooses already had a line as she set off (the boredom choices' self lines at 80%, warming her hands, the cab, the barn doorway), so nothing was moved; tea, the one that went quietly until the mug was down, got `teaGo` (70%). Three lines each for `teaGo`, `interrupted` and `resumed` (`src/npc/mindLines.js`), recorded.
- Looking first is TODO 130's look ahead before she walks off from standing, which every chosen walk now gets.
- Pace: `OFFER_KINDS` says a choice's pace. Hurrying (shelter, hopping on the back of her friend's) is 1.25 times her walk and she may jog; briskly (a drive, the jump, doughnuts, showing off) 1.1; dawdling (a look round, a sit down) 0.7, never jogging. Rain makes any walk but a dawdle a hurry. Tired at most, she walks at 0.85 of it, keyed up at most 1.1. It lasts the walk to the thing she chose and is cleared when the walk ends. Measured headless: a tired Amy going to sit down dawdled at 0.65 of her walk; in the rain, a walk to a quad was 1.13 with jogging allowed.
- Interrupted: what she chose is "on her way" until her state moves on from the walk to it (she's got there). Anything you ask her through the wheel (every option, wrapped when the wheel opens for her), and the bell's recall, interrupts: if she was on her way, she says `interrupted` (85%) and it's on her mind for 120 s at 1.8 times the weight; choosing it again she says `resumed` (80%). Checked headless: Amy on her way to sit down, asked to follow you, said so, and sitting down was kept at 1.8.
- Debug: in "Why", what she's set on and still keen on, her pace, and "Stop her on her way"; `girl.<name>.setOn` in the console. Tuning: the `pace*` and `resume*` params in each girl's group.
- Not checked: the hurry and the dawdle by eye, and an interrupted choice being picked up again in play (the tests cover it).

## Needs and a chooser (TODO 133): the rest moved

The features left on their own dice at the end of stage one, sorted into two kinds.

- Choices, now offers like the rest (each module's start roll became `offers(ctx)` and `take(offer)`, its chance a weight, its start handling a game.js method shared with the update loop): jumpers (`jumper`, weight 1, still only after she's been cold 25 s), the red diesel mistake (`refuel`, 0.6), pranks (`prank`, 0.6, doubled for a neglected girl and never by a favoured one), the rock prank (`rockPrank`: Jess's `rockPrankWeight` 0.9, Amy 0), the head start (`headStart` 2.5 for the favoured girl and `snub` 1.5 for the neglected one, still once per approach while you walk at your usual quad), nicking your quad (`nick`, 1.5 times her nickChance, while the moment after you get off lasts), Jess's go on the big vehicles (`bigGo`, 1.3), the L plates (`lPlates` 1 for Jess, `lPlatesTractor` 1.5 for a confident Amy), follow the leader (`convoy` 1), races (`race` 1.1, and `raceDare` 1.5 for Jess daring you at the line), the drag challenge (`dragDare`, 1.5), comparing notes (`compare`, 1), the romance invitation (`invite`, 2; it had no dice, she asked the moment she qualified) and getting off a quad she chose to sit on (`getOff`, 1). The joint ones are offered to both girls; whichever takes it starts it, and who leads or challenges is decided as before.
- Sat about on a quad of her own choosing (parked, nobody else on it) now counts as idle for her want of something to do, and her only choice there is to get off (`fromSeat`). Put on the back by you, her patience running out is still her own clock: that's a reaction to being left.
- Left as reactions, on purpose (they answer something that just happened rather than being something she decides to do with free time): answering your rev and drowning Amy out, keeping a sick one alive, Amy peeling the L plates off, Amy's day on the tractor (a story beat), falling out and sulking after it, gossip, nicknames and quad talk (remarks), waiting ready (you asked her), and the other girl climbing in beside a driver or hopping on the back as part of a ride already chosen.
- Debug-panel forces still start each feature directly. Tests: each moved module's tests now take what it offers (as a chooser that always says yes) and check it never starts on its own; `tests/girlsMinds.test.js` checks every offer kind the code makes is in `OFFER_KINDS`.
- Checked: tests, and headless with each module's clock moved past its start delay: Amy (favoured) was offered tea, a favour, a race, a ride together and the barn invitation as well as her boredom choices, Jess the jumps, doughnuts, a go on the tractor and the L plates, and with both free a while Amy chose a race within a second; no errors. Not checked: every one of them through to the end in play.

## The two as a pair (TODO 136): the plan

Written before any code. The item became TODO 138 when two fixes were put in front of it; see "The two as a pair: as built" for what changed.

- A pure `src/npc/pair.js` (tested): when both girls are free (`availability().free`), the one with the higher company need offers to go and stand with the other (`pairUp` in `OFFER_KINDS`: company 0.9, purpose 0.3, `pace: 'normal'`), walking to a point at talking distance (about 1.4 m) facing her friend; the other turns to face her (`attend` plus a `facing`). Not while they've fallen out (`fallingOut.active`): then a `pairApart` check keeps a free girl from choosing anything that ends within 10 m of the other.
- Walking somewhere together: when one takes a walking offer (wander, rest, tea) and the other is free and paired (within 4 m), a `tagAlong` offer for the other follows her, 1.2 m behind and to one side, until she arrives (`followTarget` on foot; the companion already follows quads).
- Opening the circle: you walking up within 5 m of a pair makes each turn a little (about 30 degrees) towards you, so the three of you face the middle (attention already looks; this is the body turn in idle, via `freeToTurn`).
- Needs: being paired meets company (`together` is already true within 6 m), so the pull fades once they're together, and builds again apart.
- Debug: "pair up now" and "follow her" buttons per girl in `girlActions.js`; "paired with" in the Girls tab and console. FAQ and README for the player; CLAUDE.md under "The girls' minds".

## Revs, not idling (TODO 136)

The girls' heads were on a swivel round idling quads. Revs were counted from how fast the revs climbed (2,500 rpm/s between frames), and a lumpy single, a hunting choke or a rough engine's idle climbs that fast all the time with nobody near the throttle.

- A rev now needs someone asking for it: the throttle open past 0.2 (`revThrottle`). An idle never counts, however much it wanders.
- And the revs have to pull a fifth of the way up that engine's range above where it was idling (`revAbove`, of idle to top revs), so a whiff of throttle isn't a rev. That suits every engine without per-vehicle numbers: the Land Rover's diesel and the yellow two-stroke are measured on their own ranges.
- "Where it was idling" follows the revs over 1.5 s while the throttle is shut (`revBaseSeconds`), so a choke's fast idle is the new floor, never below the warm idle. Starting with the choke out and then blipping it is still a rev, from the fast idle.
- One notice a blip, at its top (the revs stop climbing or the throttle shuts), as strong as how far up the range it got (0.5 up to 1.5), so a flat-out blip still makes Amy cover her ears and a half one only turns heads.
- The three new numbers replace `revRate` in "What catches the eye".

Each girl's folder on the Girls tab opened with about thirty action buttons, so with both folders open her details were off the bottom of the screen. The buttons now sit in their own "Make her do" folder below "Why", closed, and the folder opens on what she is doing and why. Actions still live with the girl they act on (the panel's rule), only folded. The two buttons inside "Why" (stop her on her way, think now) stay there, as they are for watching the chooser.

## The two as a pair: as built (TODO 138)

Built to the plan above, with these differences:

- `src/npc/pair.js` offers `pairUp` (company 0.9, something to do 0.4, no pace: an ordinary walk) and `tagAlong` (company 0.8, something to do 0.5, brisk, so she keeps up with a dawdling friend). Keenness is per girl: `pairWeight` (Jess 1.3, she can't stand about alone) and `tagWeight` (Amy 1.2, she keeps an eye on Jess).
- She only goes over to a friend who is stood about (`canRemark` and idle), not sat on the bales: the spot at talking distance from a girl on the bales can be inside them. Her walk aims at her friend as she is now each frame (`standWith`), so a friend who shifts a step doesn't leave her stood 4 m off, which the first headless check showed with a fixed spot.
- No `pairApart`: falling out already moves one off when they're within 4 m (`apartRange` in fallingOut.js), so pair.js only stops offering anything while they're fallen out.
- Tagging along walks to a point 1.2 m behind and 0.7 m to the side she was already on (so she doesn't cross behind), waits there if she catches up, and once her friend stops (or after 90 s) stands by her. Only wander, rest and tea are tagged (`TAG_KINDS`): a drive or a race has its own way of including the other.
- Facing: stood within 4 m of each other, both free and nothing turning them (`freeToTurn`), each faces the other (`pairFacing` in the idle state, after a turn to look at something). You within 5 m of the middle of them, each turns towards the middle of the three of you, by at most 0.6 rad (about 35 degrees), which for you stood at talking distance comes out near the plan's 30. Not while you're riding.
- Lines: `pairGo` and `tagGo` in mindLines.js, said sometimes (0.4 and 0.6).
- Debug: "goes and stands with her friend" and "her friend wanders off and she tags along" in each girl's "Make her do"; "with her friend" on the Girls tab and `girl <name> pairedWith` in the console; "The two as a pair" tuning under "Girls: moving about".
- Checked once headless: going over ends 1.5 m apart facing each other; tagging along follows and stands together at the end. Opening the circle to you is checked by the unit tests only.

## Waiting that fits where she is (TODO 139)

Stood about, a girl's ways of standing were picked at random from her own list. Now what's around her adds to the list, and the poses that fit it are likelier.

- The item said the poses were already there; only hand-warming was, and that is a job (hands over a running engine), not a way of standing. So six are new in `STANDING_IDLES`: kicking a tyre, wiping a seat, rubbing her hands, shading her eyes, looking up at the sky and checking her phone. Rubbing and wiping move the hands to and fro (a `move` on the idle, metres added to where each hand is held), which the bone-only `wave` couldn't.
- `src/npc/waiting.js` (pure, tested) says which suit: a wheel within 1.2 m, a free seat within about 1 m, the day's chill where she is over 0.3, the sun over 0.65 of a sunny day's and not raining, or the rain heading up (a shower coming on: `target` over `rain` by 0.05). Each that suits is 2.5 times as likely as one of her habits; the phone, which suits anywhere, 0.6. Re-weighed once a second while she's stood about (`StandingIdles.setChoices`, now weighted).
- Personality is each girl's `waitTastes` in characters.js: Amy wipes the seat (2) and seldom looks at her phone (0.4); Jess kicks the tyres (1.6), barely wipes a seat (0.3) and is on her phone (2).
- Kicking a tyre and wiping a seat turn her to it (`waitTurn` in the idle state) and pin her (`freeToTurn` says no while they last), so she doesn't turn round mid-kick. Each says what she looks at with `attend`: the tyre, the seat, her phone, out ahead under her hand, up at the sky.
- Her phone is a procedural box with a lit screen, put each frame along her right hand's fingers with the screen to her eyes, and only shown while she checks it (and not while reacting). While she has it, or a hand on a seat, `mayReact` says no to hands-up reactions. Item 52 (Amy's phone ringing) can reuse it; that's the only overlap with the later phone item.
- Debug: a button per way in each girl's "Make her do" (a tyre or seat has to be within reach), "waiting about" on the Girls tab, `girl <name> waiting` and `girl <name> wait-kick-tyre` (and the rest) in the console, and "Waiting about" tuning under "Girls: moving about".
- Checked in one headless run from beside her: she turns to the wheel and the kick reaches 0.44 m forward (0.29 m at first, so the swing was doubled); shading her eyes, rubbing her hands, wiping and the phone in her hand read as meant. Looking up was not looked at.

## The day running on into dusk (TODO 33): what Jamie settled

Asked before building, 11 October 2026:

- The game starts at a different time each game, morning or afternoon, and the day runs on from there through dusk into night, which then stays. A clock and a little weather station (a thermometer and a barometer) on the barn wall show the time and how it is.
- Night is dark enough that headlights matter, with the farm still faintly there under a blue night light. Stars and a moon stay with item 94.
- The vehicles throw real light, not only the faked beams.
- The temperature falls from the afternoon into the night by a random amount (about 6 degrees), and frost forms on the ground and the seats if it gets cold enough; a morning start is colder and thaws.
- As it gets dark the girls say they want to go in, and once it is dark the barn doorway and the cabs pull at them, through the chooser; you can still ask them out. Nothing ends the game.
- Item 121 (lights to work by), next: she brings a vehicle round to light what you are working at, and most of the time moves over to the passenger side once it's there. Ask her from her wheel, or Amy does it unasked, through the chooser like everything else she decides.

## The day running on into dusk (TODO 33): as built

- `src/world/daylight.js` (pure, tested): the time of day starts between 08:00 and 15:30 (from the seed; `?time=` sets it) and runs at 12 times real time, so a morning start reaches dusk in about 40 minutes and a late one in about 10. An October day: sunrise 07:15, sunset 17:15, the sun 29 degrees up at noon in the south (north is -z, so east +x), crossing from east to west, so the shadows move round. Dark (the sun 7 degrees under) at about 17:55. After that the sun stays down: no second morning, however long you play.
- The sky (`skyAt`, applied in world.js on top of the weather's): golden as the sun gets low (less on a cloudy day), then dark blue. At night the sun's light is replaced by a faint blue light from high up (0.35) and the sky's fill drops to 0.14 of the day's; the baked reflections dim with it. The first night looked too dark to see Amy at 0.07 and 0.12, so both went up.
- The temperature: the weather's picked temperature is now the day's warmest, at 14:30; it is coldest at dawn and falls by 4 to 8 degrees (from the seed) over the nine hours after the peak, most of it early. So morning starts are colder than before, and frosty days colder still at night. `?temp=` and the console set the temperature as it is now (the peak moves to match). The sun's warmth (`Weather.warmth`, which the engines' heat and the girls' cold read) goes with the sun, so the night feels cold to them as well.
- Frost both ways: the ground frost already followed `Weather.frost`; seats now creep towards it, forming over 240 s on a seat nobody is on and thawing over 150 s (`frostAfter` with the day's frost). Tyre tracks through ground frost still never refreeze.
- Real light: a fixed pool of six spotlights (`effects/vehicleLights.js`), given each frame to the lit vehicles that matter (the one you're on, then the one lighting the way for you, item 121, then the nearest), each from between that vehicle's lamps along its beam, as bright and coloured as its lamps (`headlightLevel`, so revs, flicker, a weak battery and the UTV's main beam all show). A fixed count, because changing the number of lights recompiles every shader; six because more costs every pixel. 12 per cent strength by day. No shadows from them. Loading still takes 1.67 s to Ready (measured).
- The barn's bulb stays on after dark, so the barn glows from outside.
- Smoke, steam, dust, mud spray, the yard haze and the rain are drawn with their own fixed light, so they're dimmed with the daylight; at night they glowed white.
- The trees sparkled at night: the leaves' soft cut-out edges let a little of what's behind the canvas through (Chrome on Metal, though the canvas is opaque), and behind it was the page's light grey. The canvas's background now follows the sky's horizon colour, which is what an edge should blend to anyway.
- The weather station (`world/weatherStation.js`): a board on the north wall past the door end of the bench, at eye height, with a clock, a thermometer (-20 to 40) and a barometer (Stormy to Very dry, from the weather, falling ahead of rain), drawn on canvases redrawn only when they move on. The dashes' clocks read the same time; their flashing and cycling still go by real seconds.
- The girls: `gettingDark` once as the light goes, `darkGoIn` now and then once it's dark and they're out in it (not in the barn or a cab), and going in is an offer (`goIn` in places.js: warmth 0.7, rest 0.5) to the barn doorway once it's 60 per cent dark; once in, they stay in while it's dark. The cold at night raises their warmth need on its own, so the Land Rover with the heater on (`warmCab`) competes. You can still ask them out.
- Debug: "time of day" slider and "the clock" on the Girls tab's "Both girls and the farm"; `weather.time`, `weather.clock` and `weather.darkness` in the console; "Time of day" tuning under world and "Vehicle lights" under driving.
- Checked headless: dusk at 17:12 (golden sky), night at 19:00 (dark, Amy just visible), a running red quad's real light pool on the grass at night, the weather station, and 25 s after dark with the girls free and no errors. Not checked: how it feels to ride the hill in the dark, the frame rate with all six lights lit, the morning frost thawing in the running game, or the girls actually going in (they chose a start and a drive in the 25 s).

## Wet and binding brakes (TODO 1)

Nothing needed settling; the numbers were chosen here.

- `src/vehicle/brakeState.js` (pure, tested in `tests/brakeState.test.js`): `BrakeState` per quad, with `wet` (0 to 1) and `binding`. Wet rises while moving above `soakSpeed` 0.8 m/s with water deeper than `soakFrom` 0.25 under a wheel (the same `puddleAt` the air filter uses, which includes the stream and ford), at `soakRate` 0.8 a second at full depth, so the ford soaks them in about a second and a half. It falls at `dryRate` 0.03 a second driving, plus `dryBraking` 0.35 a second at full brake pressure, so dabbing dries them in a few seconds and plain driving in about half a minute. Stood still they stay wet.
- Wet scales only the lever's brake by `1 - wetLoss (0.8) * wet`. The parking hold, the handbrake and the rolling resistance are unchanged, so a wet quad still stays put on a slope.
- Binding is a drag, not a brake: `bindDrag` 1.1 m/s2 times mass is added to the physics `hold` (the same path as the bog's drag), because the brake path cuts the drive and a binding brake shouldn't. It frees after `bindMetres` 6 of riding (each quad's own is 0.6 to 1.5 times that, from the seed) with a knock from `rideAudio` and no drag after. Each quad starts binding with `bindChance` 0.2, from the game's seed, so `?seed=` repeats it. A binding brake does not come back in a game; the quad stood all game is the start.
- Debug: "wet brakes" and "brake binding" in Problems, wetness in Running, `brakes` in the console tree, both in `problemSummary` and cleared by "Put it right"; "Brakes" tuning under Driving.
- Not done: the girls don't comment on it, and the barn find's seized brake bench job is separate and unchanged. Not checked in play: how the 0.8 loss and 1.1 m/s2 drag feel, or the clunk's sound. Unit tests only.


## Water in the fuel (TODO 2)

Nothing needed settling; the numbers were chosen here.

- `src/vehicle/fuelWater.js` (pure, tested in `tests/fuelWater.test.js`). Water is a fraction of the fuel, like diesel and oil: `FuelSystem.water` (tank), `bowlWater` (the carb's float bowl, which is what the engine is fed) and `sediment` (0 to 1, how full the sediment bowl on the tap is). Cans have `water` too, and `quad.fuel.add` and `fuelText` carry it.
- The bowl is the delay that makes it readable. Fuel flowing from the tank past the tap leaves `bowlCatch` 0.92 of its water in the bowl while there is room; the bowl holds `bowlLitres` 0.03 L of water (a tank at 8% water fills it in about four minutes of riding, a can at the 12% ceiling in about two and a half). Full, everything goes on to the carb. The engine is fed `fuelWaterLevel(bowlWater)`, which is the carb's water fraction over `fullAt` 0.08.
- Unlike red diesel there is no soak: the engine's `fuelWater` follows what is in the carb right now, so it clears when clean fuel arrives. At its worst it adds roughness 0.6, loses 50% power, adds 0.45 to the misfire chance a firing, makes a start harder from half (down to 5% of its usual catch chance) and may cut out above 0.45 (8% a firing, stall reason `fuelWater`).
- Cans collect water when not held, not carried and not under cover (`world.covered`, so the barn and the lean-to protect them), in rain above `canRainFrom` 0.25, at `canRainRate` 0.0004 a second at full rain, to at most `canMaxWater` 0.12. Refilling a can from the tank dilutes it. The barn find's old tank has water 50% of games (`waterChance`, `tankWater` 0.06), drawn last in `addRusty` so seeded layouts don't shift. The bench's "drain the varnished fuel" job clears it with the rest of the fuel.
- The cure: `G` in the toolbox panel ("Drain the water at the tap", greyed out when there is none, hidden on the diesels, which have no tap or bowl). It runs the water out of the bowl, the tank's bottom and the carb at once and costs `drainLoss` 0.15 L of petrol. I took "drain it at the tap" to mean at the panel, as red diesel's pump is, rather than a new on-foot interaction with the tap.
- Seen on the quad: a small glass bowl under the tap body (`buildSedimentBowl` in `quadModel.js`, also on the ATC), with a brown water column that rises with `sediment`.
- The girls (`waterLines.js`, 4 situations, recorded): Amy says it is water when a quad coughs or cuts out, tells you to stop cranking and look at the bowl, and notices a full bowl on a parked quad; Jess blames the quad. Their `crankHint` gives `waterNoStart` above 0.5.
- Debug: Running has "water reaching the carb"; Problems has "water in the fuel (fraction)" and "tap bowl full of water", both in `problemSummary`, `fuelWater` and `tapBowl` in the console tree, and cleared by "Put it right" (`FuelSystem.clearWater`). "Water in the fuel" tuning under Engines, fuel and faults.
- Not done: the girls don't drain it themselves, and the diesels are not affected. Not checked in play: how the bowl looks, or how long the water takes to show on a real ride. Unit tests only.


## Top-end rebuild (TODO 3)

Nothing needed settling; the numbers were chosen here.

- `src/engine/topEnd.js` (pure, tested in `tests/topEnd.test.js`): `canRebuildTopEnd` (a two-stroke that is seized or scuffed past `topEndScuffFrom` 0.05), `readScoring` (light, deep from `topEndDeepScuff` 0.5, or seized) and `rebuildTopEnd`. The bench job is `topEnd` in `benchJobs.js`: head nuts (hold), barrel up (hold), look at the piston (the inspect step, a toast), new piston and rings from the shelf (press), barrel down (hold), head nuts with a window. It is the tenth job, so the number keys went to `0` for it.
- A seized engine is no longer freed at the toolbox: `F` toasts that it has to go to the bench, and the panel says so. `F` still cleans a nipped-up (scuffed, not seized) engine as before, since that is a quick clean rather than a rebuild. `EngineModel.freeSeized` is unchanged.
- The rebuild costs less than the strip down it replaces: a seizure leaves `topEndSeizeWear` 0.15 (was 0.3) and the new piston and rings take `topEndWearRemoved` 0.2 off, so a seized engine ends up a little fresher than it was. The price is the bench time and the running in: it calls `startRunningIn`, so the existing rules apply unchanged (ten minutes under about 60% revs, or several times the wear and a scar).
- A botched rebuild (head nuts uneven) leaves `topEndBotchedScuff` 0.3 of scuffing, under the 0.55 where it nips up, so it runs but gets there sooner than it should. All five are in "Workshop bench" tuning.
- The shelf is only a step in the job for now; the spares shelf (TODO 34) would make the piston and rings run out.
- Amy writes "top end rebuilt, new piston and rings" in the logbook. No new girl lines, so nothing to record; they react to the job through the bench's usual start, step and done reactions.
- Not checked in play: how the bench list looks with ten rows, or how the piston toast reads. Unit tests only.

## Tyre pump and gauge (TODO 4)

- The toolbox panel gets a pump under the rows: a gauge reading and a − / + button per real wheel (`Tyres.realWheels`, so a bike has two and the outriggers none). A click moves one tyre by `pumpStep` 0.5 psi (`TyreSet.pump`), between `ventMinPsi` 0.5 and `maxPsi` 7. It sits beside the existing Tyres row, which still levels all four to one setting, and `T` and the bench still pump to that. A punctured wheel's reading falls with its puncture, as the gauge always did.
- Too low: below `rimPsi` 2.2 a tyre starts to roll off the rim. `rimLevel` is 0 at 2.2 and 1 at 0 psi; the bars are pulled towards it (`rimPull` 0.06 rad at worst on a front wheel, half for a rear, left and right cancelling) and the drive loses up to `rimDrag` 25% for the worst tyre. Both ride alongside the puncture's own pull and drag in `quad.js`. Dealt pressures still stop at `minPsi` 1.5, so no quad starts the game pulling from this.
- Grip and ride from pressure were already per wheel, so nothing else changed. The pump's range tuning is under "Tyres" in the Tune tab; the Vehicles tab's pressures go down to 0.5 and show each wheel's off-the-rim level, and the console's psi values likewise.
- Not checked in play: how the pump rows look in the panel, how the pull and drag feel. Unit tests only. No new girl lines.

## The old three-wheeler leans in (TODO 5)

Jamie's calls: the rider's weight shift is automatic from the steering (no new key, Z is already the choke off), the girls ride it gently without using the lean, and the model's tilt is the chassis rolling as it is pushed. The item's premise was a little off: the ATC never rode on the two-wheeler model (`bikeLean.js`, the mini moto's), it is the three-wheel raycast vehicle, so this is its own small module.

- `src/vehicle/riderLean.js` (pure, tested; tunables in "Three-wheeler rider lean"): the rider's weight goes off the middle by `maxShift` 0.25 m at full bars and `fullLeanSpeed` 6 m/s (less below), eased at `shiftRate` 1.5 a second so it follows the bars a moment late. Its roll torque (`riderMass` 75 kg times g times the offset, about 184 N m at most) goes through `QuadPhysics.rollTorque`, leaning the chassis to the inside against the corner's outward roll. A girl driving manages `girlShift` 0.75 of it; nobody aboard, reversing and standing still give none. Switched on by `userData.riderLean` on the model.
- The ATC's `centreOfMassY` went from -0.06 to 0 so that sitting still tips it sooner. Real Rapier on flat ground (`tests/riderLeanSim.test.js`, bars ramped in over 0.3 s, speed held): without the lean it rolls over from 8 m/s, which is the old 13 m/s pulled back to about 8; with the lean it is up at 11 and over at 14. Between 12 and 13 m/s is the edge.
- Nothing is drawn for the rider's shift; the visible lean is the chassis rolling under the torque, which the model already follows. The girls' poses don't move her body. Not checked in play: how it feels, or a girl riding it round the yard.

## Loading a quad onto the bale trailer (TODO 6)

Settled with Jamie before building: the bale trailer (not the little one); the bales move to the front so a quad fits behind them; `T` folds the ramps; both a push and a winch; an `E` strap; every quad but the tractor, UTV and Land Rovers; the girls stay out of the loading itself but say a line or two.

- **Not a physics job.** A loaded quad is moved by hand along the ramp, never driven or rolled by Rapier. `vehicle/quadLoad.js` (pure, tested) is a small machine in the trailer's own frame: `approach` (the winch drags it across the ground to the foot, turning it nose first), `ramp` (up or down, held by the winch cable or just pushed), `aboard` (on the bed). `HayTrailer` works out a world pose from it every frame (`poseLoad`) and sets `quad.pinned`; `Quad.fixedUpdate` then only holds its body there (`QuadPhysics.pin`) and runs nothing else, so there is no fighting the suspension, brakes or engine on a moving trailer. `quad.loadedOn` is the other half, and `Companion.mayRide` says no to a loaded quad.
- **Ramps.** Two aluminium planks hinged at the back edge, stowed under the bed, 2.4 m long (an 18.9 degree slope up to the 0.78 m bed). `T` toggles (on foot within 5 m of the foot, or on the tractor hitched and under 0.8 m/s); they won't fold up while a quad is on them or being fetched. `rampSeconds` 1.4.
- **Winch.** Hold `X` with the ramps down, within 9 m of the foot, not looking at the green or the tractor (their `X` is a tap and keeps working): the nearest quad within `winchReach` (7 m) that `mayLoad` allows is hooked. It is dragged at 1.1 m/s, then wound up at 0.7 m/s; let go and the cable holds it where it is. `Shift` + `X` pays it out, down to the ground, where it is let go. The cable is a plain line from a drum against the back of the bales to the quad's nose.
- **Push.** The existing `V` push. A quad pushed straight on to within 1.5 m of the foot (0.4 rad and 0.7 m tolerance, either end first) takes the ramp, and climbs at 0.55 m/s only while pushed; let go and it runs back down (slope times gravity, at most 3 m/s) and is released at the foot. Pushing is `quad.pushDir` times which end leads.
- **Dead quads only** (`mayLoad`): not running, nobody on it or heading for it, not tipped, not on the winch or chain, not the tractor, UTV, Land Rovers or the barn find under its tarp. The mini moto and the three-wheeler go on like the rest.
- **Strap.** `E` on a quad that is aboard and still toggles it; two orange ratchet straps appear over it. Unstrapped it keeps going when the trailer speeds up or brakes: past 2.2 m/s squared it slides (0.8 of the excess), slows at 2.5 m/s squared, stops against the winch drum at the front ("thump") and, with the ramps down, goes out of the back and down them. With the ramps up it stops at the back. Strapped nothing moves. So gentle driving never matters and a hard stop does.
- **Weight.** A loaded quad's mass goes through `towEffect` for the tractor (it feels 15% of it, as with the tipping trailer: about 7% of its drive for a 290 kg quad). The empty bale trailer still takes nothing, as before.
- **The bales and the seats.** The bed was two layers of bales at the back and the front with a well between. Now the front holds two rows of two across, the back row only one layer high; the girls' seats are the back edge of that low row, so the seat anchors are turned round and they sit facing back over the clear bed, leaning on the high bales when they lie back. All their climbing and bumping works from the anchor's own frame so it is unchanged. The clear bed is 2.3 m; a full-length quad has about 0.4 m of play on it between the back of the bed and the winch drum.
- **Lines** (`npc/loadLines.js`, merged into the main sets): `loadAboard`, `loadStrap`, `loadSlid`, three each, one girl within 25 m picked from the seed.
- **Debug.** Tune: "Loading ramps" (every number above, ramps re-pose on change). Vehicles: "on the bale trailer" under Running, and "Winch it onto the bale trailer" / "Pay it back off" actions; console `vehicle.<id>.trailer`, and `load` / `unload` verbs. They use the real winch and pin (the winch input is held by the game until it is aboard).
- **Checked:** unit tests (`tests/quadLoad.test.js`: geometry, lining up, winch up and down, push and roll back, strapped and unstrapped on the bed, ramps folding), and headless: a switched-off red quad winched across the grass, up the ramps and strapped, with the cable, straps, ramps and bales all drawn as meant, then paid out down the ramps and released. **Not checked:** pushing it up with real keys, towing it behind the tractor with real driving (and so how much it slides), the girls sitting facing back on the new seats (the code is unchanged but I did not see it), how it looks with a smaller quad, the mini moto or the three-wheeler on the ramps, and the three girls' lines by ear (recorded in the background).

## The girls on the mini moto (TODO 7)

The item settled the shape, so I chose the details without asking.

- `src/npc/motoGo.js` (pure, tests in `tests/motoGo.test.js`, tunables under "Mini moto nerve"). Each girl who rides it has a `MotoNerve`: Jess starts at 0.15, gains 0.012 a second while moving over 2 m/s and 0.25 for coming off, so it carries between goes. Her top speed on it is 0.45 of her usual at no nerve, rising to all of it at full nerve. Her bars wobble by up to 0.45 of full lock at no nerve (two slow sines), fading to nothing at full nerve; she squeals about it now and then.
- The first go always ends in a fall, 15 to 35 s of riding in. Later goes fall with chance 0.5 times (1 minus nerve), 20 to 60 s in, so a steady Jess rarely comes off. The fall waits until she's on the grass (not the tarmac) and moving.
- Coming off: `Quad.fallOver` → `BikeLean.forceFall` puts the bike on its side the way your own fall does (engine dead, left lying; you right it with T) and she goes to a new `thrown` state (`companionThrown.js`, tunables under "Thrown off the moto"): an arc to 1.7 m beside the bike, about two seconds lying, up, idle. She says `motoCameOff` on landing and Amy, if within 40 m, `motoFellOther`. Modelled on the hay trailer's slide off, so the same stiff pose.
- Amy: `refusesMoto` (`amyRefuses` 1; 0 lets her ride it, with careful lines of her own that are recorded in case). `canDrive` is false for her on the moto, so she never picks it; asking from the wheel menu still shows "Drive it", which makes her say `motoWont`. The Girls tab has a `moto` action for each girl, and the console has it as `girl <name> moto`.
- Not done: Jess choosing the moto on her own is whatever her ordinary drive choice gives; there is no separate offer for it. Her nerve isn't shown in the Girls tab or the console tree, only the tuning groups. 
- Not checked in the running game: only unit tests. Not seen: the throw and lying pose, the wobble by eye, or whether the first go's 15 to 35 s feels right.

## Gamepad (TODO 8)

- **A layer under the keys, not new call sites.** `input/gamepad.js` (pure, tested) turns a pad snapshot into keys held, keys tapped and analogue amounts; `Input.poll` feeds them into the same `down`/`pressed` sets the keyboard uses, so every `isDown('KeyE')` works unchanged. Only the places that gain from analogue read it: the walk (`padMove`, speed scaled by lean), the look (the right stick is added to the mouse delta, so the wheel cursor works too), steering (`steerAxis`) and the pedals.
- **Triggers are amounts.** RT and LT also hold KeyW and KeyS, so the reverse rules and the automatics' brake logic keep working, but the quad skips the squeeze-in of `pedalKeys.js` when the pad holds the key: the pedal is where your finger is. Which trigger is throttle follows reverse, as W and S do on the keyboard.
- **Gears.** The manuals' digits are absolute, so LB plus D-pad up or down is worked out from the current gear (down from first is neutral, up from reverse is neutral). Reverse stays `B` on RB.
- **The ring (R3).** Keys with no button left sit on a radial menu (D-pad or right stick to point, A to do it, B to cancel), listed per place (`RADIAL_ITEMS`, on foot or on a vehicle). Items that need holding (crank, choke, pull-start, dipstick, push) hold their key for a set time, and any other press lets go, so cranking can be cut short.
- **Start plays and pauses.** A gamepad button is not a user gesture, so pointer lock is requested but may be refused; `Input.padPlaying` stands in for it and the start card is hidden by `startFromPad`. Esc still works with a mouse. This took Start for pausing, so the bucket list moved to the ring and Select is help.
- **Menus.** The wheel takes A, B and the D-pad as Enter, Escape and arrows, with the sticks moving its cursor. The toolbox uses the D-pad and B (A would be Enter, which closes it). The bench maps A to E and four job picks to X, Y, LB, RB. The other toolbox and bench keys stay on the keyboard.
- **Detection.** Browsers list a pad only after a button press, so at load the console says "none detected, press a button" and prints again on connect and disconnect (`gamepadconnected`); the start card gains "Gamepad found: press Start to play".
- **Not done.** The interaction prompts still show keyboard letters. Rumble is item 99.

## Sheep on the hill pasture (TODO 9)

- Agreed with Jamie before building: only the flock this time. The gate that opens and shuts, escapes onto the lane and the road, the girls herding and the dog are three follow-up items (180 to 182). Sheep are kinematic, with no Rapier bodies: you can't hit or hurt one, they just move off. They look procedural but must look reasonable; Jamie may ask for better ones. Bleats are a CC0 recording, not synthesised.
- The flock (`src/world/flock.js`, pure, tests in `tests/flock.test.js`): nine sheep, each grazing (head down, still), walking to a patch 2 to 7 m off, or running. A graze lasts 5 to 20 s. A walk's goal is pulled back towards the flock's middle the further she is out (`cohesion`, `spread`), and sheep keep 1.3 m apart. Anything (vehicle, girl, the player) closer than `fleeRadius` 9 m plus 1.3 m per m/s of its speed, to at most 28 m, makes her run directly away (with a little pull towards the others) for 3 s after it stops mattering, and a sheep within 6 m of one that bolts bolts too half the time. A step onto ground she may not stand on is refused: a walker stops to graze, a runner turns about.
- Where they may stand (`setUpFlock` in game.js): in the pasture, 3 m in from the hedges, at least 9 m past the stream's far edge (the girls' "foot"), out of the wood, the water and thick gorse, and where the climb's slope is under 0.45 (the steepest banks are 0.74). `findFlockSpot` scores the grid by how much allowed ground is round it and picks one of the best by the game's seed, so each game starts them in a different roomy spot, and `?seed=` repeats it.
- Bodies (`src/world/flockView.js`): three instanced meshes (lumpy low-poly wool, a dark head hung from the neck that dips to graze, legs), laid on the drawn ground and tilted to the slope, rocking and bobbing as she steps. Shadows on. A first look had them too white with long thin legs; the wool is now creamier and greyer and the legs shorter and thicker. Still faceted, with no leg animation.
- Sound (`src/audio/sheepAudio.js`): `sheep-baa.wav` loads the first time a sheep within 130 m bleats, and plays from the sheep through a panner at each sheep's own rate (0.82 to 1.22), duller with distance, louder and a touch higher when frightened. A calm sheep calls every 12 to 40 s, a frightened one every few seconds. Credited in ASSETS.md. Not listened to.
- Debug: the "Sheep" tuning group (World, weather and sound). No actions or console paths beyond that.
- Checked: unit tests and two headless views of the flock on the pasture. Not checked: how it sounds, the frame rate with them, and riding into them.

## Farm jobs: the chalkboard and the muck (TODO 10)

Agreed with Jamie before building: this session does the chalkboard and the muck job only. Round bales to the trough and harrowing are follow-up items (183 and 184). Fork-by-hand, drive-and-spread, a close view of the board in the barn, two jobs a day from the ones that exist, and a line each from the girls.

- **Pure logic** (`src/world/farmJobs.js`, tests in `tests/farmJobs.test.js`): `MuckLoad` holds the tub's load (0..1), the patches laid and whether it is done; `JobBoard` picks the day's jobs (`jobsPerDay`, 2, but only jobs the farm has what they need for) and ticks them off. `FARM_JOBS` is the registry, so the next job is one entry plus its own module.
- **Forking.** Hold E at the trailer, empty-handed, with the tub down and the trailer within 5 m of the heap's edge: 12 s fills it. It is the tipping trailer's existing interaction (`hold`), so no new key, and the heap's own collider is untouched.
- **Spreading.** Tub up past 0.3 (below the slide point of 0.5, so the tub's own cargo tip-out is not triggered) and moving between 0.4 and 2.2 m/s (the UTV bed's auto-lower is at 2 m/s, so the tub lowers itself above that); a patch every 1.8 m, 22 to a full tub, 14 on the stubble (`world.fields.stubbleAt`) to count. Patches off the stubble are wasted, with a toast. Stood still with the tub right up, the whole load goes in one heap and the job has to be started again.
- **Drawing** (`src/world/muckView.js`): a squashed sphere in the tub that grows with the load, and one instanced mesh of 160 flat dark lumps for the ground (a ring, so the oldest goes). A normal lit material, so night dims it without help.
- **The board** (`src/world/jobBoard.js`): drawn on a canvas and hung on the barn's sliding door leaf nearer the first wall segment, redrawn when a job ticks. A close view is a card like the logbook's (E or G to leave); the gamepad treats it as the notebook menu.
- **Girls.** `src/npc/farmJobLines.js`: muckStart (nearest girl), muckFull (the farthest, so they don't talk over each other), muckDone (nearest). Amy is keen and tidy; Jess minds the smell. They need recording.
- **Debug.** Tune: "Muck job", "Job board". Girls tab "Both girls and the farm": what's in the tub, the board, and fill / finish / reset buttons calling the same game methods. Console: `farm.muck`, `farm.laid`, `farm.muckDone`, `farm.board`.
- **Weight.** A full tub counts as 3 things in the tow load (`towWeight`).
- **Not checked in the running game:** only unit tests (and a load check, see the summary). Not seen: the board on the door by eye, the mound in the tub, whether 12 s of forking or 1.8 m patches feel right.

- **Follow-up: the tilt (after Jamie tried it).** `T` used to tip the tub all the way, which dumped the lot in a heap. With muck in the tub `T` now tilts it to `spreadTipTo` (0.4, between the spreading threshold and the slide) and holds there (`UtvBed.cap`); `T` again lowers it and `Shift`+`T` tips it right up. Also the chalkboard hung inside the door's white battens, which stand 0.11 m proud: it now hangs 0.16 m out on a slate backing.

## Snow (TODO 11)

A weather kind, `snow` (weight 0.06, so about one game in sixteen; the temperature is rolled only from the frosty and cold bands, 60/40). Jamie settled the scope: the roll, falling snow, ground and seat cover, tracks that stay, grip and the cold; snowballs are left for a follow-up.

- **Weather** (`weather.js`): `snow` is how hard it falls now (flurries, eased towards a target re-rolled every spell, like rain) and `cover` is how far the ground is under snow. A snowy game starts at `SNOW_AT_START` 0.45; cover grows at `snow * 0.9 / 150` a second, so about three minutes of heavy snow covers it. It melts above 3 degrees (full cover goes in about 400 s at 8 degrees) and with rain (`rain / 300` a second). Nothing melts it on a frosty day, which is the point. `seatFrost` is the larger of the frost and 0.85 of the cover; the game feeds that to the quads' seats instead of `frost`, so the existing seat frost (sat on to clear, forming again) does the seats.
- **Ground** (`terrain.js`): one more block in the same shader as the frost, `uSnow`. Coverage is patchy at low cover and joins up as it grows. It is thinned by the wear map's blue (the tyre lines, the same as frost) and mostly removed where the ground is churned, so tracks show as the grass and mud beneath. Not done: hedges, roofs, trees, props and the outer ring stay bare, so a snowy world is a white field with a green surround. A material-wide treatment can come later.
- **Falling snow** (`effects/snow.js`): 1800 points in a cylinder round the camera, swaying and wind-slanted, none under roofs; tinted down at night in `updateWeather`.
- **Grip** (`surfaces.js`): `snowGrip` 0.62 and `snowRoll` 1.25 at full cover, scaled by cover, on top of tarmac and stubble (not a new surface type, since cover varies and sits on all of them). In the Tune tab's surface group.
- **Girls:** no new lines (they would need recording). A snowy day reads as frosty or cold to them, so they warm their hands and remark on the cold as before.
- **Debug:** console `weather.snow`, `weather.snowCover`, `weather.kind snow`; `?weather=snow`. Barometer reads 0.4 for it.
- **Not checked in the running game:** only unit tests. Not seen: how the white ground and the flakes look, whether 0.62 grip feels right, and the shader cost.

## Muddy girls (TODO 12)

- **State** (`npc/muddiness.js`, pure, tested): per girl `mud`, `dust` and `wet` (0..1). On a quad she takes mud at `mudRate` times how much her quad's wheels and bodywork carry times its speed (to 12 m/s), the pillion at full share and the rider or driver at `riderShare` 0.45 (further from the back wheel), plus a puddle splash. Dust comes from the quad's own cloud or one it is in (`quad.dust`, `quad.dustIn`), `dustRate` 0.04 a second in a full cloud. A full coat from a very muddy quad takes about 30 s on the back.
- **Coming off:** wet mud stays; as it dries (`dryTime` 240 s) it flakes off at `flakeRate`, and at `brushRate` more while she is walking. Dust rubs off out of the cloud. The hose takes `washRate` 0.4 a second of mud (more of the dust) and leaves her soaked. Aimed the same way the old "sprayed her" reaction was (within 7 m, in front of you), so she still shouts.
- **Look** (`effects/girlMud.js`): a shader patch on her body material, from the bind-pose position (so it stays on her as she moves): mud from the boots up, to the knee at light and the hips at heavy, flecks on the face past about 0.35; dust pale and patchy, more on head and shoulders. Up is taken as the longest side of the body's box, in case the glTF is not y-up. Her wellies and shorts are separate garments and are not patched, so they stay clean. Dark mud when wet, lighter when dry, like the quads'.
- **Words:** `muddy` and `dusty` for both (`muddyLines.js`), once each time she gets dirty (at `noticeAt` 0.3 mud or `dustNoticeAt` 0.4 dust) and not again until she has been below `forgetAt` 0.12, and no more often than `remarkGap` 150 s. It waits its turn rather than cutting in. Not a chooser offer: it is a reaction to her own state, like the reaction to being left on a quad.
- **Left alone:** the girls do nothing to clean themselves (no wash offer), and she does not ask you to hose her. Both are easy to add as an offer if wanted. Nothing saved between games.
- **Not checked in the running game:** unit tests and a headless load with mud set on both girls (no console errors). Not seen: how the mud and dust look on her, and whether the box-based "up" and the height bands sit right on the Quaternius body. Amy's and Jess's new lines are recorded in the background.

- `src/npc/flask.js` (pure, tested): `offers(ctx)` for the chooser (`flask` in `OFFER_KINDS`: warmth 0.4, rest 0.6, company 0.6, purpose 0.4): a free girl within `girlRange` 30 m of the rock, you within `offerRange` 20 m and moving under 2 m/s, dry (`rainAbove` 0.12) and light (`darkAbove` 0.7), after `startAfter` 120 s and a `cooldown` 420 s. `begin(girl, how)` is also what your E press and the debug action use.

You then have to stay within `stayRange` 11 m for `sitSeconds` 40.
