## Hill climb and the records board (TODO 14)

Agreed with Jamie before building: you and the girls both climb; cones mark it like the drag strip; and the chalkboard grows to hold every record (lap, drag, climb and longest jump), not just the climb. The rest as recommended: scoring kept for the game only (it joins the farm that remembers, item 15), per-quad bests, the girls choosing to climb as an offer, falling out not fed from it, real traction and weather left to decide the times.

- **The course** (`layout.hill.climb`, `npc/hillClimb.js`, pure, tested): four gates at `points`, each `gateHalf` 7 m either side of its point and across the way to the next, marked by cones (`buildClimbCones` in `startLine.js`: two a side at the start and finish, one a side between). The points run up the gentler ground in the east of the pasture (steepest 0.44 measured with the terrain function, against 0.7 up the west banks), so the girls can drive it; a test checks the gates are in the pasture, out of the wood and the water, and no step is steeper than 0.6. The start is 4 m clear of the stream's far edge, so the girls' staging point is not in the water.
- **The run** (`ClimbRun`): the clock starts when a step crosses the start gate forward between its cones, then each later gate has to be crossed in order (a step that goes round outside the cones, backwards, or through a later gate first counts for nothing), and the last stops it. Out after `outAfter` 10 s off the quad or stalled (a stall cured sooner is forgiven), or `timeout` 150 s. You have to ask first (`Enter` within `nearStart` 25 m of the start, `armFor` 90 s), so riding past the cones is not a run; asking is in `askForRace` next to the lap and drag cases.
- **The girls** (`HillClimb.offers` and `take`, the chooser's `climb` offer): after `startAfter` 150 s, `cooldown` 360 s, not in the dark (`darkness` above 0.6), not while a race, a drag race or a recall is on, and not while the other is climbing. Jess `weightJess` 1.6, Amy `weightAmy` 0.7. She drives HerDrive's race mode: down the lane and across the ford (the fields' gateways, then a point 9 m past the stream on the ford's line, or she would cut the stream away from the ford), a staging point, a grid point `gridBack` 3 m behind the cones, a wait of `leaveAfter` 2.5 s, then through the gates and 8 m past the last. The same `ClimbRun` times her, from her quad's position, so her time and yours are comparable. She is out if her quad stalls, she stops doing anything about it for 4 s (as in the races), or she never reaches the start in `gatherTimeout` 120 s (called off, no event to the player).
- **What is said** (`hillClimbLines.js`, six lines each): `climbOff` as she sets off, `climbDone`, `climbRecord`, `climbOut`; and for your run `climbYou` and `climbYouRecord`, said by whichever girl is stood near the top (within 40 m, free to remark). When you take the record from the other girl she says `climbYouRecord` too, 2.5 s later. Lines carry no times (they are recorded ahead).
- **Records** (`world/records.js`, pure, tested): `Records.submit(kind, who, value, quad)` keeps the overall best and the best per quad for `lap`, `drag`, `climb` (lower) and `jump` (higher); a tie is not a record. Lap and drag times are the race clock from the go to the finish for any racer, `noteRecord` in game.js feeds them from the existing finish events. The longest jump is `JumpMeasure` (take-off to landing along the ground, at least `jumpMinAir` 0.45 s in the air and `jumpMinDistance` 4 m), for whoever is on any quad, you or a girl driving, so the ramp is not the only way to a record. You get a toast for a jump and for any record you set; the girls' records are only on the board.
- **The board.** The jobs board keeps its leaf; the records board hangs on the other leaf past the doorway (same backing and frame, `hangBoard` in `jobBoard.js`). Looking at either and pressing `E` opens the same card, which now lists the records under the jobs. The boards redraw when a record is set.
- **Bucket list:** King of the hill (a timed climb), On the board (set a record).
- **Debug panel and console:** "Hill climb" in the Tune tab under the girls' things, "Records" under items; each girl has a "% climbs the hill against the clock" action (`girl <name> climb`); the Girls tab can take you to the start and clear the records, and shows the climb's phase and the records; `farm.records` and `farm.climb` in the console.
- **Checked:** unit tests, and a headless run (past the overlay) of Jess sent up the hill: she went down the lane, over the ford, waited on the grid, set off and passed the first gates, the clock running only from the cones. She was slow on the banks (about 1.2 m/s over the first gates). The finish handling and the board card were driven by calling the game's own methods (a record on the board, the card's text), without errors. **Not checked:** a full girls' climb to the finish (the run was cut at about 35 s of climbing), your own run by hand, how the cones and the second board look, whether the girl's route needs help on the gorse in the east, and the new lines in the girls' voices (recorded in the background).

- **Follow-up (Jamie, after trying it):** the cones were hard to read as a course (gates 16 m across, small, and two a side close together at the ends). Now `gateHalf` is 4.5 m, the cones are 1.8 times the lap's size, a checkpoint is one cone either side, and the start and the finish are two such pairs 3 m apart along the course to ride through like a gateway. The crossing test is unchanged (the middle of the gate). And the two chalkboards moved inside the barn, side by side on the back wall (the jobs one 2 m south of the barn's middle, the records one 2 m north, 1.75 m up), and are 1.3 m tall instead of 1 m, because four records did not fit on the old slate. Not looked at in the running game: where they sit against the barn find quad parked at the back, and whether the cones now read.

## A farm that remembers (TODO 15)

Agreed with Jamie before building:

1. The same vehicles are on the farm every game. "New farm" deals a fresh pool. Vehicles arriving or being sold between games is left for a later item.
2. A vehicle keeps its condition and not where it was. Kept: hours, miles, engine wear, any fault left unfixed, the toolbox tuning, tyre pressures and wear, the sump, the fuel and its oil mix, the coolant, its quirks and the barn find's progress. Overnight the battery drains a little (flat if the key was left on), mud dries to about half and wet brakes dry. Dealt fresh each day: where everything is parked, the weather, the temperature, the time of day, and which faults start in play.
3. The girls: favour carries at half strength; a falling-out not made up carries as friction; what you taught them carries in full; romance carries once she is locked in, and so does a betrayal (trips round the back that hadn't got that far start again). Grudges, pranks and moods don't carry.
4. The bucket list keeps its ticks (still showing only what today's fleet makes possible). The records board keeps its bests, with a mark on those set today. The logbook keeps its last 40 entries, by day, and stops inventing "Yesterday" once there is a real yesterday.
5. Each load is the next day ("Day N" on the start screen). It saves to the browser every 30 seconds and when the tab is closed or hidden. A save from an older build fills in what it lacks from the deal rather than being thrown away.
6. `?seed=N` ignores the save and never writes it, so a seeded game repeats exactly. The console's `save` shows the save and `save load <json>` loads one (on the next reload); "Wipe the farm" is in the Girls tab's "Both girls and the farm".
7. The start screen has "Continue" and "New farm" when a save exists (New farm asks first, as it wipes the save); with no save, clicking to play starts a new farm, saved as Day 1.

How it was built:

- **Two seeds.** `this.seed` is today's (random each load, or `?seed=`); `this.farmSeed` is the farm's, saved with it, and is the first day's seed. What the farm *is* comes from the farm seed's streams: the fleet (or the saved list of ids, in order; if a saved id has gone from the game the fleet is dealt again from the farm seed), plates, top revs, the pods' first places, quirks, each quad's fault luck, the oil burner, sticky floats, the coolant leak, the worn gearbox, the girls' quads and their names for them, the barn find, and the tractor's, UTV's and Land Rovers' own streams. On a new farm the two seeds are the same, so a seed deals exactly what it did before. Everything else stays on today's seed. The quads' idles are on the main stream after the spawns, so a remembered quad's idle is still drawn (keeping the stream in step) and the saved one used instead.
- **What a vehicle keeps** (`src/farm/farmState.js`, pure, tested with the real pure parts as a stand-in unit): a named list of engine fields that are condition rather than running state (`ENGINE_KEPT`: wear, fouling, scuffing, seized, silencer, running-in, scar, coolant and its leak, boiled, diesel soak, big bore and its fit, battery), roughness (a service lowers it), hours and miles, the air filter, the fuel (litres, tap, oil, diesel, water, the sediment bowl), the faults' levels, started flags, caps and which tyre, the tyres' psi, brand and tread, the sump, the stiff tap, the mud and the toolbox settings. Restoring only writes fields the save has and that are numbers or flags, so an older save fills in what it lacks from the deal. The settings are put back the way the history's tinkering always was (`captureStock` then `applyTune`, kept as `unit.leftovers` for the toolbox), and `applyHistory` skips its faults, wear and tinkering for a remembered quad so nothing is applied twice; the history the girls know is then given the saved faults.
- **When it saves:** on the first unpaused frame (so loading and leaving without playing doesn't use up a day), every `saveEvery` 30 s, and on `pagehide` and the tab going hidden. Never on a seeded game, and never again after "Wipe the farm" or `save load` (so this game doesn't write over it). Restoring is in two steps: vehicles before the start report (`restoreVehicles`), and the girls, bucket list and records once everything is built (`restoreDay`); the logbook restores itself in `Notebook`.
- **Start screen:** the game behind the card is already the next day, so "Continue" is just a click on the card; "New farm" confirms, wipes and reloads the page. The buttons go once you've started (the card's `started` class).
- **The girls:** `favourKept` 0.5; friction carries at `frictionKept` 0.5, or at `frictionFallenOut` 6 if they were fallen out (8 falls out); skills in full; a lock-in carries (favour locked at `lockScore` again); a betrayal sets `nextInvite` to `betrayedCooldown` the next morning only (`Romance.betray` now marks `betrayed`).
- **Records and the logbook** carry the day they were set or written. The board adds "(today)" from day 2 on. The logbook shows today's entries by time, yesterday's as "Yesterday", older ones as "Day 3", and the new farm's dealt entries (from before day 1) as "Before"; it stops dealing those once there's a save. Entries now keep what they were (`what`), so Amy still knows a fault she wrote down that is still on the quad (the `wontRide` refusal).
- **Checked:** unit tests (`tests/farm.test.js`, the console's `save` in `tests/consoleShell.test.js`), and one headless run past the overlay: day 1 had no buttons; after playing, setting hours, wear, a belt fault, mud, Amy's favour, a climb record and a bucket-list tick and reloading, the card said "Continue: day 2" and every one came back as expected (mud and favour halved, the fleet the same), with no page errors. **Not checked:** the "New farm" confirm and reload by hand, the save on closing the tab, a remembered barn find part-way through, pods moved and then remembered, a long-running farm over many days, and how a saved game from this build reads into a later one.

## Girls stuck at the cattle grid (walking east)

- Found by walking Amy and Jess from x 40 to the hardstanding in a headless run: Amy stood at x 78.9, 1.5 m past the gateway point before the grid, for the whole run, and Jess dithered there for 25 s. Cause: `fields.nextWaypoint` skipped a gateway point only while she was within `reach` (1.5 m) of it. The grid's gateway points are 2.5 m either side of the hedge line, so walking on through, she got more than 1.5 m past the first point while still in the old field, and it became her target again, behind her. She turned back, came within 1.5 m, skipped it, and so on. The same would happen at any gateway she approached head on.
- Fix: a point is also skipped once she is past it along the way to the next point and within `lane` 1.5 m of that line. Alongside the hedge but away from the gap, she still goes to the gap's point first. Test in `tests/fields.test.js`.
- Checked headless: both girls now walk x 40 to the hardstanding and the Dutch barn door in about 52 s with no stop, and back west through the grid, and Jess walked through the road's south gap into the south field. Static colliders along the road corridor were not the problem (the hedges and the grid's two wings only). Not checked: other girls' routes round the east end beyond these, and anything but walking (quads were not affected, they follow their own routes).

## Where the vehicles start, spread out (2026-10-11)

Jamie: too many vehicles started near the barn and caused traffic, and some started on the little tipping trailer. Agreed: at most four in the yard, spread further out; the tractor, UTV and Land Rovers never in the yard and out wider; new spots mostly on the home side.

- **Measured before** (500 deals, `.scratch/spawnstats.mjs`): 4.7 vehicles in the yard on an average farm of 8.4, all seven slots full in 1 game in 7, about one of them a big vehicle; slots 4.4 m apart along two rows. In 1 game in 10 a vehicle started within 4.5 m of the tipping trailer: the "by the paddock" spot (-53, 30) with its 3 m nudge reached it. The straw bales spot (18, 10) could land on a bale too.
- **The yard:** eight slots spread round it, 8 to 12 m apart (a test keeps them over 8), of which `spawn.yardMax` 4 are used; the big vehicles are always away. Gaps between vehicles 4.5 m (was 2.8) and 6 m for a big one (was 4.6).
- **Away spots:** "by the paddock" is gone (no clear ground near it: the paddock, the trailer and the track); "by the straw bales" moved to (28, 14). New: out from the yard (-14, 64), by the south hedge (8, 70), by the east hedge (70, 40), by the north hedge (30, -70), in the far corner (-68, -50), down the west hedge (-72, -22). Twelve in the grass field and five out east. The hill pasture was tried and is too steep (25 to 68% at its foot).
- **Keep-out** (`spawnKeepOut`, `spawnBlocked` in `world/spawns.js`, pure): circles round both trailers, the tank, the bowser, the hose, the bell, the barn and its doorway, the silage, the paddock, every straw bale, the trees behind the barn, the jump and its run-up, the drag strip and the field's bog, plus 3 m off the track's edge. Placing a vehicle tries up to eight nudges that miss them, then uses the spot's middle. A test checks every spot is clear nudged any way, over 3.3 m from a hedge and under 15% slope, and that 200 deals of twelve never start one on anything.
- **Measured after:** 3.4 in the yard on average, none big, nearest neighbour in the yard 13.8 m on average and 5.2 m at the closest, no starts on anything.
- **Not changed:** where a girl leaves a quad when she brings it home (`homeParking.js`, east of the row by the barn), which is play rather than the start. **Not checked** in the running game: how the spread looks, the girls fetching quads from further away, or the big vehicles' new spots by eye.

## Chain drive (TODO 16)

- It is a fault kind, `chain`, in `faults.js`, so it gets the farm's save, the Problems list, "Put it right", the girls' fault lines and the history for free. Only a spec with `chainDrive: true` (yellow, mini moto, ATC) has one: `Faults.hasChain`, set by `Quad`. Other quads never start it and history never deals it to them.
- Two things wear it. Slack (`levels.chain`) starts at 0.15 like any fault, then grows with riding at 900 s from sound to hanging. Oil (`kept.chainLube`, 1 fresh to 0 dry) runs out over 1,500 s of riding; a dry chain starts stretching up to three times as soon and stretches three times as fast (`chainDryWear` 2). Both are in `kept`/`levels` and saved by `farmState.js` (`kept` joins the saved fault fields).
- What slack does: a lash taking up to 12% of the drive at its worst; a slap (the `knock` sound, harder with more slack) when the ground gives a vertical jolt over 4 m/s2 and slack is over 0.3; and from 0.8 slack with the throttle over half open, a chance a second (up to 0.25 at full slack) of jumping the sprocket, after which `driveFactor` is 0 until it is put back. The jolt is the change in vertical speed per second, read in `Quad` before the faults step.
- The fixes are two: the toolbox (`N`, a rough fix like the others: back on, oiled to 0.6, slack left at the field-fix level and capped) and a new bench job. The job (`chain`, eleventh, on the minus key because the digits ran out at ten) has an inspect step that says how slack and dry it is. Adjust, nuts or oil done badly: slack comes back to 0.35 (adjust, nuts) or the chain is left half oiled (oil). Only chain quads can start it (`jobAvailable` takes `hasChain`).
- The girls: Amy and Jess each have a `faultChain` line (a slapping chain, said when riding) and a `faultChainOff` line (said when the engine is running and it has come off), plus a `histChain` line for a chain that was already loose. Same notice rules as the other faults (`faultNoticeFrom`, twice at most).
- Not done: a squeak for a dry chain and a visible droop on the models. The slap is the only sound.

## Valve clearances (TODO 17)

- A fault kind, `valves`, in `faults.js`, so it gets the save, the Problems list, "Put it right", the girls' fault lines and the history for free. Only four-strokes have it (`Faults.hasValves`, set by `Quad` from the engine's strokes; the history pool leaves it out for a two-stroke spec). It starts after about 4,800 s of the engine running and wears wider over 2,400 s.
- Two directions, one level. Worn wide is the natural way: `valveLoose` goes to the engine, which ticks (it shares the `tick` the low sump uses, so the same `coolTick` sound, taking the louder of the two) and loses up to 5% power. Too tight (`kept.valveTight`) only comes from a bad bench job: no noise, up to 30% power lost, and a heat-soaked engine's hot-start richness multiplied by up to three, so it starts hard hot. A tight clearance does not wear any further. The tight flag is kept in the farm save with the rest of `kept`.
- The bench job (twelfth, on the equals key) has a feeler-gauge inspect step that says wide, right or too tight. The `gap` step is a window: too little turn leaves it wide at 0.5, too much leaves it tight at 0.5, a slack locknut leaves it wide. Any proper job, or "Put it right", clears both. There is no toolbox quick fix: a valve adjustment is a bench job.
- The girls: each has a `faultValves` line (said when the engine runs and it ticks) and a `histValves` line. Too-tight valves make no noise so nobody says anything.
- Not done: tight valves burning out over time, and a visible or audible difference between the sump tick and the valve tick.

## The barn calendar (2026-10-11)

- Asked for so you can tell which day you are on once loaded in (the start screen says it only before). It hangs on the barn's back wall at the middle of the boards' height, halfway between the jobs and records boards (`WallCalendar` in `world/wallCalendar.js`; the boards are 2.5 m apart edge to edge and it is 0.9 m wide). Nothing to press: you read it from where you stand.
- A page is `DAYS_PER_PAGE` 30 days, laid out seven across from a Monday, so it is the farm's own calendar rather than the real one. It starts in April and goes round twelve months, then says "year 2". Past days get a red cross, today a circle on a yellow highlight, and the rest are plain. `calendarPage(day)` is pure and tested; the canvas is drawn once at build, since the day cannot change while the game is loaded.
- The picture is "Miss Mudguard", a clothed 1950s garage-calendar pin-up (dungarees, headscarf, a wink and a spanner) in the cheeky style of the ones in every workshop, drawn on the canvas, so no asset to credit. The first version was a sheep in sunglasses; Jamie wanted something more garage. `PICTURES` holds one drawing per page, chosen by page, so more are a new function and a line in the list. April is arbitrary and fixed: the game has no seasons, so nothing ties the month to the weather, and every farm starts there.
- Not done: nothing is written on it (birthdays, a job due), and there is no close view. Not looked at in the game, only unit tested, so the sheep's drawing and the calendar's height on the wall want a glance.

## Asking the girls is a priority (2026-10-11)

- The fault. Since the girls' minds were rebuilt, a race you asked for (Enter) waited for both girls to be "free" in the chooser's sense, which is stood about with nothing on and nothing held. A girl walking somewhere, sat on a quad, resting or driving was not free, so the ask sat there with its flag set and nothing said. The drag race wanted both girls free too, though it needs one; the hill climb Enter only timed you, no girl was ever asked.
- The rule now (`npc/asking.js`, `Game.askGirls`/`updateAsk`): asking is a priority. `availability({ asked: true, pull: true })` takes a girl from walking, sitting, mounting, driving or resting (`PULL_STATES`; she still can't be taken from carrying something, getting off, or a feature's hold). The farm's holds and the soft jobs don't count, as for the wheel. She may take the quad she is already on (the other girl's, the one you are on, a stuck one and a quad belonging to the other girl are never offered). `Game.sendToDrive` then does what the bell's recall does: says she was interrupted, stops a drive or gets her up from rest first, and requests the drive. Used by all three.
- Needs: the lap race both girls and two different quads; the drag run and the climb one girl (Jess or Amy at random, whoever is free). Asked, the climb ignores the dark and the cooldown; it still won't start while a race, a drag run, the bell or a falling-out is on.
- Never silent. An ask lasts `ASK_PATIENCE` 15 s. If nothing has started by then it is called off with a toast saying why ("The lap race: Amy is carrying something; Jess has no quad she can take"), kept as "last ask". `askProblems` (pure, tested) writes the reasons. Girls tab, "Both girls and the farm": a button to ask for each kind, a line per kind saying what is in the way right now, and the last ask; console `farm.ask.race`, `.drag`, `.climb`, `.last`.
- Not done: a girl in the middle of a bench job or a conversation menu is not specially handled (the menu hold is ignored, as for the wheel); the 4 s idle timers that drop a racer (race, drag, climb) now count resting, sitting, starting and cranking as busy, so a girl getting up or starting her quad isn't dropped; not played in the game, only unit tested.

- Amendment (2026-10-11): a quad that is running when the farm is saved does not come back flat. The flat battery is for a key left on with the engine stopped (`keyOn && !running` in `overnight`); a running engine just loses the usual overnight drain. Saved as `running` beside `keyOn`; a save from before this has no `running`, so behaves as before. Jamie refreshes with quads running and does not want to switch them all off first.

## Walking round the barn (2026-10-11)

- The fault. A walking girl steers by ray probes a few metres ahead, trying angles either side and sticking to one side for a moment. That cannot see round a whole building: with the barn between her and where she is going, the straight line ran into the back wall, she tried one side, then the other, held a heading past the point it made sense, and shuffled about at the wall until the five-second stuck timer made her push through. A walk that then got interrupted by something else looked like being lost or distracted.
- The fix (`npc/detour.js`, pure, tested): before steering, `World.detour` asks for a first point to head for. If the straight line crosses a building's footprint (grown by `body` 0.6 m) she aims at one of its corners (grown `corner` 1.5 m): the one that is clear from where she stands, not within `reach` 1.2 m of her (she has passed it), with the shortest way on; the corner is kept until she gets there. From a corner it is usually clear, or the next corner is chosen, so a walk from the back of the barn to the yard goes round the nearer end.
- The barn has a doorway in the +x wall: going in, she lines up on a point `doorOut` 1.8 m in front of it (round the corner first if she is at the back) and then goes straight in; coming out from the inside she goes to a point `doorIn` 1.2 m inside it and then through. Walks that start and end inside (the bales, the loft) are left alone. The field shed is a building without a door. Either end of a walk hard against a wall (a quad parked by it) is left to her own steering unless the way there crosses the building. The Dutch barn's open bays and the sheds in `outbuildings` are not included.
- Tunables: "Walking round buildings" in the Tune tab (Girls: moving about). It applies to every walk using `walkToward`, not just the barn trips.
- Not checked in the game, only unit tested (the shapes of the barn, the door and some walks). The follow-the-quad and driving routes use their own routes and do not use this.

- Amendment to the race (2026-10-11): "The girls are going without you" no longer comes up while you are lined up on the grid. The `youWait` 30 s was counted from the invite whether or not you were on your point, so a girl slower than that (more likely now they are pulled from further away when asked) dropped you for waiting. It now only counts while you are not lined up; once you are, they have `gatherTimeout` 100 s to arrive before it is called off. Drag race already worked this way.

- Amendment to the hill climb course (2026-10-11): the last two gates moved west, from (18, 182) and (6, 197) to (12, 182) and (-2, 196), into the clearing. A map of `hill.gorseAt` showed a band of gorse across the pasture at z 189 to 193 from x 8 eastwards, and gorse thinly over the old finish gate at z 195; west of about x 6 the ground at those heights is clear, and clear at the new finish all the way to the far hedge. The line from the new second checkpoint to the finish stays in the clear ground, and the girls' run-off past the finish (8 m on) now ends at z 201.7, short of the hedge (the old one ended inside it). The first two gates are unchanged. Only checked against the gorse density and the existing course tests, not ridden.

- Amendment (2026-10-11): asking (Enter) only works on the line. `nearLine` for the lap race was 45 m, which also let Jess dare you and the girls invite you in from across the yard; it is now 12 m. The drag strip's `nearStrip` 15 becomes 12 and the climb's `nearStart` 25 becomes 12, so all three starts are the same. The lap race's line is the track's start point, the strip's is its start line, the climb's is the first gate. The distances are tunable ("Racing", "Drag race", "Hill climb"). Not checked on the ground: 12 m is roughly where the grid sits (lanes are 3 m apart), so it may want a metre or two either way.

## Drives that go somewhere (2026-10-11)

- The complaint: the girls mostly drive round in circles. Cause: `HerDrive.plan()` only ever made a loop of 5 to 9 m radius (`planLoop`) round where she started, again and again until she was bored (45 to 85 s). Leading you somewhere (TODO 95) was never built.
- Now (`npc/outings.js`, pure, tested; `HerDrive` option `outing`): each time she plans afresh, with `chance` 0.6 she picks a place instead: from the farm's list (`outingSpots`: the drag strip, the end of the road, the muck heap, the trough, the jump, the foot of the hill, a point in each stubble field and three on the track) that is between `minDistance` 35 m and `maxDistance` 230 m away, weighted to nearer ones (1 over the distance, at least 40 m). The way is `fields.route` (the gateways), and a place is only picked if every leg of it passes `driveClear` (no hedge, collider or bog in the way, no steep bank). She drives it a third quicker than her loops (`speedScale` 1.3, kept under her 5.5 m/s scare speed), brakes to stop at the place for 4 to 9 s, drives back to where she started the same way, and goes back to loops. The drive's time is lengthened to there-and-back at her pace plus the stop plus 25 s, so she is not bored half way. If something clears her route on the way (backing off twice, a friend getting on) the trip is dropped.
- Not for: races, tidying, doughnuts, jumps, showing off, following or leading, the tractor, UTV and Land Rover, or the mini moto. Giving you a ride and the girls' quads that you are on the back of use it like any other drive.
- Debug: "Drives that go somewhere" in the Tune tab (Girls: things they do); each girl's `drive somewhere` readout; a "drives somewhere on the farm and back" action in her folder (and `girl <name> outing`), which makes the next drive go for certain.
- Not done: she says nothing about where she is going or when she gets there, and nobody has a view on the places; a place in the hill pasture past the foot is not on the list; not run in the game, only unit tested with a stand-in quad, so how the legs through the gateways look on the real farm (the hedges, the cattle grid) is not checked.

## Too much engine oil (TODO 18)

- Decided without asking, as the item settled it: the sump (`src/vehicle/sump.js`) holds up to `maxLevel` 1.4 where 1 is the top mark, and the bottle's pour carries on past the mark while `Y` is held (before it stopped there). Nothing stops you but the stick: it says "Over the top mark" from `overFrom` 1.04 and "far too much" from `overFull` 1.25.
- Effects, all from the overfill (`overfill()`, 0..1): blue smoke at any throttle (`overSmoke` 0.5 of full, through the engine's existing `oilBurn`, running only), and oil blown into an air box (`Sump.airbox`) at `airboxRate` 0.012 a second at full overfill scaled by work, about a minute and a half to soak. A soaked air box adds up to `airboxClog` 0.6 to the filter's clog, so the engine does what dirt in the filter already did (rich, darker smoke, power lost at the top end); I reused that rather than add a second sluggishness. It dries at `airboxDry` 0.004 a second once the sump is back at the line (about four minutes), a little even stood. Not saved overnight (the level is); a fresh day starts dry.
- Draining: away from the bottle, holding `Y` on an overfilled quad lets oil out at the drain plug at `drainRate` 0.1 a second, down to the line and no further. The bench's "top up the engine oil" job drains an overfilled sump instead of pouring. Debug panel: the engine oil slider and the console's `oil` now go up to 1.4; "Put it right" resets the level and the air box; "burning oil" in Running now includes this.
- Amy drains an overfilled quad before she rides (`oilOver` line, new, needs recording) and logs it in the notebook as an oil job. The girls never pour, so only you can overfill one. Jess never checks.
- Checked: unit tests (`tests/sump.test.js`). Not checked in the running game: how the smoke and the sluggishness feel, or the status text while pouring. Numbers are first guesses.

## A frozen throttle cable (TODO 19)

Nothing needed settling; the numbers were chosen here.

- `src/vehicle/frozenCable.js` (pure, tested in `tests/frozenCable.test.js`): `FrozenCable` per quad, `quad.iceCable`, with `ice` (0 free to 1 solid) and a `mode`, dealt 50/50 from the seed: `shut` (won't open: the throttle is capped at `1 - openLoss * ice`, so 0.1 at full ice) or `open` (from `holdFrom` 0.3 ice a throttle you open stays where you left it; as it thaws below that it comes back). Below `freezeFrom` 0.15 it is only stiff and does nothing. It sits after the existing sticking-cable fault in `quad.js`, so the two stack; it is a condition of the weather, not a wear fault, so it is not in `Faults`.
- Dealt once the day's weather is known (`deal`): on a frosty day (`weather.frost` above 0), a quad not under a roof starts frozen with `startChance` 0.7, ice `startIce` 0.5 to 1. From a stream of its own (`seed ^ 0xf205`). Not saved between days: it is the morning's weather, not the quad's lasting condition, and each load deals again. The barn find stands in the barn, so is never frozen.
- Freezes again while stood out with the engine off in frost: `freezeRate` 0.0011 a second at full frost (about fifteen minutes). Thaws with the engine running (`thawEngine` 0.015, scaled 0.3 cold to 1 warm: about a minute warm, three cold), in the sun (`thawSun` 0.005 times `weather.warmth`, under a roof none) and per degree above freezing (`thawWarm` 0.002). Read from the weather every frame in `updateQuirks`, as the temperature and frost move over the day.
- Frozen open on a quad someone is driving: she switches it off, as for the stuck cable. A frozen-shut quad she is driving just idles until it thaws; I did not add a girl line for either, so nothing to record.
- Debug: "ice in the throttle cable" in Running; "frozen throttle cable (ice)" and "cable freezes open" in Problems (in `problemSummary`, cleared by "Put it right"); `iceCable` in the console; "Frozen throttle cable" tuning under Driving.
- Not checked in play: how the frozen-shut idle feels, or the real times. Unit tests only.

## Drawing less (frame time) (2026-10-12)

Jamie: the game was working his M4 Max MacBook Pro hard, and more is coming. Measured first, headless at 1728 by 1117 points at Retina 2x (the 16" default), with `--disable-gpu-vsync --disable-frame-rate-limit` so the frame rate shows the real cost (`.scratch/pp/perf.mjs`, `tris.mjs`, `ab2.mjs`; headless fps drifts by a fifth over a run, so each change was alternated with the baseline three times).

- **What it was.** The game's own work was small: about 2 ms a frame for the girls, the vehicles and the physics, and 6.7 ms including handing the frame to the GPU. The GPU was the problem: 7.6 million triangles and 2,100 draw calls a frame at 3200 by 2000 pixels with 4x antialiasing. Uncapped it managed about 80 fps (12 ms), and a ProMotion screen asks for 120, so the laptop ran flat out and still missed. About 6.5 million of the triangles were ground cover and trees: tussocks 2.3M (16,000 of them, 144 triangles each), stubble 1.4M, grass 0.6M (90,000 clumps), bracken 0.3M, hedges and trees 1.2M. Each was one InstancedMesh spanning the farm, which three.js culls as a whole, so every stalk was drawn every frame, behind you or half a kilometre away. Each of these was worth about 2 ms: rendering at 1.5x instead of 2x (2.4), no ground cover (2.0), no instanced trees and hedges (2.0), no shadow pass (1.7). Small vehicle parts not casting shadows saved nothing. The engine sound worklets never sleep, but one renders 27 s of sound in 0.7 s, so a dozen cost little and were left.
- **Tiles.** Everything scattered in numbers is split into square tiles of ground, one InstancedMesh per tile with its own bounding sphere (`src/world/tiles.js`, pure and tested; `tiledInstances.js` builds them). The renderer leaves out tiles out of view, and out of the sun's view in the shadow pass. Ground cover uses 30 m tiles (`COVER_TILE`), hedges, trees, bushes and gorse 60 m (`MODEL_TILE`, through `instanceModel`, so every model scatter gets it). The layout is unchanged: the same random draws in the same order, so a seed lays out the same farm.
- **Draw distance.** Grass and stubble stop at 70 m (`coverDistance`), tussocks and bracken at 90 m (`roughDistance`): at eye height that is just under the horizon line, where the ground's own colour and the haze take over, and a side-by-side screenshot across the grass field with and without it couldn't be told apart. A tile goes when its nearest instance is beyond the distance (`DrawDistance`, updated by `World.update` from the camera).
- **60 fps cap.** `maxFps` 60: the physics already steps at 60 Hz, so 120 fps only added interpolated frames for twice the work. A schedule (`nextFrameAt`), not a gap since the last frame, so it holds 60 on a 120 Hz, 144 Hz or unthrottled screen (a gap with a jitter allowance let 80 through unthrottled). A skipped tick does nothing: no time passes and keys wait for the next frame. 0 lifts it.
- **Render scale 1.5.** `renderScale` caps the pixel ratio (it was the screen's, up to 2). With the antialiasing on, the difference is hard to see on a Retina screen.
- **Paused.** Behind the start screen and the pause card nothing moves, so the farm is drawn when the pause starts or the window changes, and twice a second in case something finished loading, rather than every tick.
- **Result.** Uncapped, about 175 fps (5.4 ms a frame) from about 80 (12 ms); 2.8 million triangles from 7.6 at the spawn view (draw calls about the same, 2,200: more tiles, fewer of them drawn). Capped, 60 frames drawn a second and the main thread about 30% busy, from 42%. "Graphics" in the Tune tab (World) and `tune.graphics` in the console set all four; the console's `perf` gives the frame rate, the game's own time a frame (`FrameStats`) and the draw calls and triangles.
- **Keeping it there.** Anything new scattered in numbers goes through `tiledInstances` or `instanceModel`, never one InstancedMesh for the lot, and small ground cover registers with the draw distance. After adding to the world, run `node .scratch/pp/perf.mjs` (dev server on 5180), which checks the spawn view against a budget. Not done, and measured after the tiles: the whole shadow pass is about 1.5 ms of the 5.3; the vehicles' shadows are 730 of its draw calls (about 110 casting parts each) and 0.5 ms; hiding every hedge saves 0.06 ms, so a simpler hedge model isn't worth it. If the budget gets tight, the vehicles' shadows are next: a few invisible boxes per vehicle that only the sun's shadow camera sees, standing in for the parts, rather than merging the parts (many move, change colour or get fitted and taken off).

## Wind (TODO 20)

- `src/world/wind.js` (pure, tests in `tests/wind.test.js`) keeps the day's wind. The weather kind's `wind` is still how windy the day is (1 on overcast, 0.3 foggy, 1.5 raining); the new part is a heading, dealt from `createRandom(seed ^ 0x817d)` so `?seed=` repeats it, which veers up to `veer` 0.25 rad round the day's over a minute or two, and gusts. A gust comes on over a fifth of `gustSeconds` 5 and dies away slowly, about every `gustEvery` 12 s; strength is the day's wind times `1 + gustiness * (1.5 * gust - 0.3)`, so between gusts it is a touch under the day's and at a peak about 1.7 times it. At strength 1 with no gust the smoke drifts at the old fixed breeze (`drift` 0.76 m/s), so nothing about calm days changed.
- Smoke, dust, rain and snow all read `wind.x`/`wind.z` now (the smoke's `windX`/`windZ` are set from it each frame, so editing them in the panel does nothing, as before). Dust puffs drift at 2.5 times the wind as they age. Haze and barn fumes still use the weather's scalar wind: gusts would only make their levels jitter.
- Hedges and trees lean in the vertex shader (`src/effects/windSway.js`): the shared materials are patched with `onBeforeCompile`, the lean is along the wind in world space whatever way the instance is turned (`inverse(mat3(instanceMatrix))`), grows with height squared and rocks out of step from place to place. Nothing on the CPU. `swayHedge` 0.14 m and `swayTree` 0.6 m at the top in an ordinary wind. Not swayed: the shadows (the depth pass uses its own material), grass, crops and the distant tree clumps.
- The hay door's shutter (`Shutter` in wind.js, mesh in `barnBuild.js`) is hooked back against the outside wall so the loft keeps its view. The wind blowing out of the door (east) past 0.5 lifts it; a spring pulls it back and it bangs on the wall hook (a damped spring with a bounce of 0.35, `bangSpeed` 0.8 rad/s to count). Wind into the door holds it against the wall. A bang is a synthesised slap (`audio/doorAudio.js`, positional at the door) and a `bang` notice at the door, so the girls within 20 m flinch through the existing rule; at most one every 3 s. Only the hay door has a shutter: the big doors slide and stay open.
- Loose hay: a gust of at least `hayWind` 1.25 blowing out of the hay door sends a second `StrawFall` of stalks out and downwind, at most once in `hayEvery` 90 s. Cosmetic.
- Debug: "Wind" in the Tune tab (World, weather and sound); console `weather.windHeading` (degrees, settable), `weather.windNow`, `weather.gust` (set it on for a gust now).
- The girls' minds: no new offer or need. The bang reuses the `bang` notice and its flinch. Not done: the girls remarking on the wind, hair and jumpers moving, the sound of wind in the trees, grass swaying.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless load (no shader errors, the wind, smoke and shutter updating after a forced gust). Not checked: how the sway looks, whether it costs frame time, how the bang sounds, the shutter's look and swing by eye, or the hay.

## Wildlife (TODO 21)

- **Pure logic** (`src/world/wildlife.js`, tests in `tests/wildlife.test.js`): `Rabbits` and `Pheasants`, both fed the same threats each step ([{ x, z, speed, yaw, beam }]: every vehicle, you, the girls) and returning events for the game to sound and pass on. `hedgeSpots` finds the places along the hedges (1.5 m out on the field side, every 6 m, never in a gap) that are both the rabbits' burrows and the pheasants' hides.
- **Rabbits:** sit 3 to 14 s, hop 0.8 to 3 m, and bolt at 7.5 m/s for the nearest burrow when anything comes within 8 m plus 0.9 m per m/s of its speed (to 22 m). They go to ground for 40 to 110 s. Half the bolts thump (sound only). Grass field only for now: the hill and the sheep field are not wired.
- **Headlights:** after `beamDark` 0.4 of darkness, a rabbit within 32 m and 0.3 rad of a lit vehicle's bearing freezes (`caught`), upright, with two eyes drawn in a basic material that only shows while it is caught and dark. It bolts when the vehicle is within 3.5 m plus 0.3 m per m/s. The bearing is the quad's heading, not the exact lamp aim, which is close enough at that range.
- **Pheasants:** three lie hidden in the hedge feet. A vehicle moving over 0.8 m/s flushes one within 5 m plus 0.5 m per m/s (to 13 m); you, a girl or a crawling vehicle within 3.2 m. It flies away from the threat for 3.6 s, climbing to 5 m, then is lost; a replacement hides somewhere else, at least 30 m from you, after 50 to 140 s. Hidden ones also call now and then. The sound is synthesised (`src/audio/wildlifeAudio.js`): two rasping notes and a band of fluttering noise for the wings, a low knock for the rabbit. Not listened to.
- **The girls:** a `pheasant` notice (heard, 40 m, salience 1.6) with a `flinch` rule within 14 m, chanced by a new per-girl `reactStartleChance`: Amy 0.95, Jess 0.4, so Amy jumps. No new spoken lines (they would need recording); a line about the pheasant, and one about a rabbit in the lights, are an easy follow-up through `banter` or a reaction line.
- **Look** (`wildlifeView.js`): instanced and procedural, built to the sheep's level of detail (faceted, lumpy, vertex-coloured, roughly 700 triangles each). A first pass was smooth blobs and looked flat beside the sheep, so both got the sheep's lumpy noise and their markings. Rabbit: agouti-brown back, pale belly and cheeks, long ears with dark tips, eyes, nose, short thick forelegs with pale paws, long hind feet, white scut; sits up or stretches out on a hop; eyeshine is a pair of unlit spheres drawn only while it is caught and dark. Pheasant: copper speckled body and breast, green head with a white collar, red face patches, eye and beak, folded wings, a barred three-plate tail, legs with toes; the open wings are a fan of nine banded flight feathers over a pad of coverts and beat while it flies. Looked at in headless close-ups, not on the running farm at night. Nothing in `drawDistance`: 14 rabbits and 3 pheasants in four meshes.
- **Debug:** Tune "Wildlife"; Girls tab "Both girls and the farm" shows the counts and has "Flush the nearest pheasant"; console `wildlife.rabbits`, `.caught`, `.pheasants`, `.flush`.
- **Checked:** unit tests, and one headless load (no errors, 14 rabbits, 3 pheasants, a flush turning one to flying). Not checked: how the animals look, how the sound sounds, the headlight freeze at night in the running game, or the frame time (not measured with `perf.mjs`).


## Traffic on the lane, visitors and gates that open (TODO 22)

- Jamie, before building: use the farm's own vehicles for the traffic (the post van is the 200TDi in red); the neighbours are on the girls' model, all women; borrowing the trailer is left out (maybe never); the gates have to open and shut, the south one and the big one both; you pull in for a visitor by choice (they wait and hoot, nothing forces you); a quiet road.
- The lane (`layout.publicLane`, x 206.4, 4.6 m of tarmac, drawn as a ribbon over the ground in `buildPublicLane`) is outside the farm's east hedge and inside the 420 m detailed terrain, so it has real ground. It is scenery: no collider, and vehicles on it brake for anything ahead instead (`Drive.inTheWay`). They drive on the left, so the southbound lane is the east one.
- `src/world/traffic.js` is pure (tests in `tests/traffic.test.js`). `Drive` follows waypoints at a turning circle of 4 m. `Traffic` deals a vehicle every `passEvery` 160 s on average by day (three times rarer at full dark, `nightQuiet`), at most three at once; it picks from `LANE_KINDS` (van, Land Rover, tractor, UTV: the existing models, in a few paints each). A visitor sets out about every `visitEvery` 540 s from `firstVisit` 150 s in, never in the dark, one at a time; `postShare` 0.6 of them are the post. `Visit` is a list of steps from `planVisit`: in along the lane, turn across it, stop outside the gate, the driver gets out and opens it (only if it is shut), drive in along the farm road's left side, across the grass field to the barn, stop, the driver walks to the door and waits (`parcelSeconds` 5, or `chatSeconds` 30 for a neighbour), back in, a loop round in front of the barn to face east, out along the other side of the road, open the gate from inside if you shut it meanwhile, stop outside and shut it (or, `leftOpenChance` 0.25, drive on and leave it). Tests run whole visits through a stand-in gate, with the gate shut, open, shut by someone mid-visit and blocked.
- The view (`trafficView.js`): each model is built once per paint and pooled. The body has to be hung from `bodyRoot` at the ground offset and the tyres set to `radius + offset + sag` (as quad.js does), or it floats. A passing driver is a dark torso and head; the visitor's driver is the girls' rig with a plain outfit (a red shirt for the post, a green jumper for the neighbour), sat on the seat with `SitIdle` and walking with the girls' walk clip. At night the lamps glow and the beam cones show, with no real lights (the pool in `vehicleLights.js` is untouched). The parcel at the barn door is a box that stays until the next one. Nothing is shared with the quads' physics, so nothing on the lane or farm road collides.
- Sound (`audio/trafficAudio.js`): a sawtooth drone through a low-pass and a band of tyre hiss per vehicle, through a panner, its pitch shifted by `dopplerFactor`. Synthesised, no assets.
- Gates (`world/gates.js`, pure, `tests/gates.test.js`): a `Gate` swings over `swingSeconds` 1.8; it is solid (collider on, `blockBelow` 0.5) until half open, and its hedge gap's `shut` follows, so `hedgeAhead` (the runaway tractor) and the girls' `route` read it (a shut gateway is skipped, so a girl told to go through a shut south gate heads straight at it: she is not taught to open gates). Both gates are `Gate`s built in `props.js`; the south gate keeps its tied-open look (the stake and twine show only when it is right open) and has a second collider for the leaf swung into the field; the big gate's leaves swing inwards. Shutting is refused (and a toast says so) with a quad, a girl or you within `clearRadius` 3 m; opening always works. A visitor shutting it ignores her own vehicle. Gate state is not saved: each day starts with the south gate open and the big gate shut.
- Girls: a new `traffic` notice (a lane pass at strength 0.5, a visitor arriving or chatting at 1) and a new `wave` reaction (a hand up, swinging, `reactWaveChance`: Jess 0.85, Amy 0.25, 0.4 default), with a button in the Girls tab. The nearest girl who `canRemark` says one line a visitor comes (`postVan`, `neighbourCalls`), a parcel is left (`parcelLeft`), the big gate is left open (`gateLeftOpen`) or you shut it afterwards (`gateShutting`): `npc/trafficLines.js`. No new offer or need: they never go to the visitors themselves yet.
- Debug: "Traffic" and "Gates" in the Tune tab (World); the Girls tab's shared section shows the lane and has buttons for a vehicle, the post van, a neighbour and each gate; the console's `lane` path does the same and sets each gate.
- Not done: borrowing the trailer, the neighbour speaking, sheep getting out of an open gate (item 180), the girls opening or shutting gates themselves, the visitors' driver having hands on the wheel, the drivers of passing traffic being more than a silhouette, and road noise beyond a drone. The gates' look swinging was checked by eye in one headless run (the post van at the gate and at the barn, a wave); not checked: night lights, the sound, a visit with you in the way, riding into a swinging gate, or the lane's look from the road.

## The electric fence (TODO 23)

Built by `world/electricFenceBuild.js` (merged posts, a tape ribbon with a little sag, a canvas sign, the energiser box and its LED), sound in `audio/fenceAudio.js` (synthesised: a click per tick, a falling snap for a zap).

- The girls: a new `zap` notice (every girl within range hears it, and `NOTICE_REACTIONS.zap` makes her flinch unless it is her own quad). The girl on the quad (driving or on the back) yelps (`fenceZap`) and flinches; the nearest free girl nearby says `fenceZapSeen`. Girls on foot are not zapped: they would walk through it with no brain for a fence, and it is more in character that they know better. Lines in `npc/fenceLines.js`.

It only offers with a quad in the bay (not the barn find), the engine off, nobody on it, the toolbox on the bench, the bench closed and no half-done job waiting. `pickJob` chooses plug, carb, tyre or oil by what the quad needs (puncture, then carb, then low pre-mix oil, then a fouled or worn plug, else a plug). The other bench jobs are left to you, since they want kit or a decision.

She nags every `nagEvery` 70 s and gives up after `waitForYou` 240 s, leaving the quad untouched (no partial effect applied: the botch only counts if you finish). Moving the quad out of the bay drops it silently.

## Photos (TODO 26)

Jamie's answers: all as recommended (a camera key, small JPEGs in the save capped at twelve, a corkboard in the barn with random pinning, the girls pose, pull a face or put it away, photo mode left for item 122, two bucket-list entries).

- The key is `5` (`Digit5`), not `P`: every letter is already used by something (P is the quads' lights and pull-start and the bench's drain). The digits are only gears with Shift held, and 5 is above them. It works on foot and riding, but not in a menu, on the ladder or hidden in the hay. No gamepad button yet.
- `src/photos/photos.js` is pure (tests in `tests/photos.test.js`): `inFrame` (a girl within `frameRange` 14 m and `frameHalfAngle` 0.5 rad of where you look), `photoReaction` (her three weights times a mood factor: neglected triples the palm and cuts the pose and face, favoured cuts the palm to 0.3), `isBlank` (the average spread of the colours under `blankSpread` 10 means nothing is in it) and `PhotoWall` (the prints, the cooldown of 2 s, the cap of `maxKept` 12, `deal` which tries eight random spots and takes the one furthest from the others so they scatter, and the save's `restore`, which drops anything that is not a `data:image/` URL).
- `photoHost.js` is the game side. Pressing starts the shutter delay (`shutterDelay` 0.9 s) and the girls in the frame react at once, so the shot shows the pose. The picture is the game's canvas copied centre-cropped to 160 by 100 straight after `renderer.render` in the same frame (the canvas has no `preserveDrawingBuffer`, so it can't be read later), at JPEG quality 0.7: the headless run's eight shots (seven plain gradients and one real view) came to 12.6 KB of save, so twelve real ones should sit well inside localStorage. Who is in the shot is worked out again when the shutter goes.
- The girls: `pose`, `face` and `palm` are new reactions (`REACTIONS`, poses in `companionReacting.js`, buttons in `girlActions.js`), all needing hands, rank 1 so anything startling wins. Her taste is `photoPose`, `photoFace`, `photoPalm` in `characters.js` (Amy 1, 0.12, 0.4; Jess 0.6, 1, 0.08). A new `photo` notice (range 20 m) makes her look at you. Lines in `npc/photoLines.js`: `photoPose`, `photoFace`, `photoPutAway` when the camera comes up, and `photoWall` (half the time) when you open the board with one of them within 8 m.
- The board is `photoBoard.js`: a cork canvas with each print drawn on it (white border, tilt, a coloured pin, "Day n" in pencil), 1.3 by 1 m on the back wall 3.65 m south of the barn's middle. The chalkboards are at 2 m either side and the calendar is between them, so nothing else had room. E opens a DOM card with the prints, newest first, with day, time and who is in each.
- Kept with the farm: `photos` in the save (`captureFarm`, `restoreDay`); a seeded game never writes it. The pinning random stream is `farmSeed` and the day.
- Bucket list: Say cheese (a shot with a girl in it) and Gallery (six pinned at once), both in Jobs.

## A girl's phone rings (TODO 27)

Jamie's answers: none asked. The item settled the shape (rings, she wanders off, comes back with news or a mood, both girls with different calls), so these were chosen as the obvious defaults.

- Pure module `src/npc/phoneCalls.js` (tests in `tests/phoneCalls.test.js`), an offer (`phoneCall` in `OFFER_KINDS`, meeting a little company and excitement) weighed by her `phoneWeight` (Amy 0.9, Jess 1.3). Nothing before 120 s into the game, and 420 s between calls for either girl. Phases: going (the phone rings every 2.4 s as she walks to a spot 9 to 16 m from you, on the side she is on, kept inside her field by `keepToField`, which `wander` now shares), talking (14 to 32 s, a muttered `phoneTalk` line every 9 s) and back (`walkToYou`, then she tells you within 3.5 m). She holds herself with `hold('phoneCall')`, so nothing else starts her on anything. If she cannot reach her spot in 40 s she answers where she is; if she is taken off it, the call ends; if she cannot find you in 60 s she keeps it to herself.
- Whether a call is bad is rolled when it is offered, from her `phoneMoodChance` (Amy 0.35, Jess 0.2). A bad call is not a favour change: the mood is `Favour.sulk(id, seconds)`, which pulls her standing down by `moodDrag` (0.6) while it lasts (Amy `phoneMoodSeconds` 360, Jess 150), so the existing neglected behaviours do the work (sulky lines, refusing, taking a quad off alone) and nothing needs a new code path. It never shortens a mood she is in, never touches a girl whose favour is locked by romance, and is not saved: it is minutes long, not a lasting condition.
- The phone is the existing procedural one from the waiting poses, now also shown for a new `phoneCall` standing idle (phone to her ear, a hand on her hip). It is never in her habits (`forced` in `WAITING_IDLES`); `updateWaiting` keeps it in her list while she is on a call so it is not eased out. `callSpot` on the companion also keeps her from turning round to look at things.
- The ring is procedural (`src/audio/phoneAudio.js`): eight alternating sine blips, heard to 45 m and dimmed with distance. No asset.
- Lines: `phoneRing`, `phoneTalk`, `phoneNews`, `phoneMood`, four to five for each girl, in `phoneLines.js`. The news is flavour only, with no effect on the game.
- Panel: "Phone calls" in the Tune tab (items and workshop); the Girls tab's shared section shows the call and anyone in a mood; the actions `phone-call` and `phone-call-bad` in each girl's "Make her do" (and `girl <name> phone-call`); the console's `phoneCall` path.
- Left out: the ring heard as a notice for the other girl to react to, calls while she is on a quad or at the bench, and news that changes anything (a bucket-list tick, an errand).
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked in the running game: how the phone sits at her ear (the hand targets are guessed from the existing phone and shade-eyes poses), whether she stops where the call spot is, and how the ring sounds.

A `Drive` now also looks along the way to its next waypoint, so a corner no longer swings it into something it had not faced; it waits for anything in its way, and after `patience` 40 s edges past it for 8 s (so a tractor left in the yard cannot hold the visit up for ever).

## A live trace on the Vehicles tab (TODO 29)

- `src/debug/trace.js` (pure, tested in `tests/trace.test.js`): a `Trace` ring of 20 samples a second for 10 seconds. A long frame adds one sample, not a run of copies. `lines()` turns each series into 0 to 1 heights.
- Four lines: revs (scaled to the engine's own top), speed, wheel slip (the driven tyres' `spinSlip`, m/s) and throttle. Speed and slip fit the window with a floor (5 m/s and 2 m/s) so a still quad draws a flat line, not noise.
- It is a canvas added once at the top of the Running folder, so the rebuild of the rows leaves it alone. It samples from the panel's own animation-frame loop, so it only fills while the panel is open on the Vehicles tab, and it empties when the picked vehicle changes. A trace of the minutes before you opened the panel would need the game to sample all the time; not worth it for a tuning aid.
- Not a tunable: the length and rate are constants. Not checked: how it looks in the running game (no headless check, per the testing rule); drawn by reading the code only.

## Crash damage (TODO 30)

- Two fault kinds, `rod` and `rim`, in `faults.js`, so they get the save, the Problems list, "Put it right", the girls' fault lines and the history for free. Unlike the others nothing starts or worsens them with time: a knock does (`Faults.damage`), and `CRASH_KINDS` marks them as bench-only (a rough fix returns false, and there is no toolbox button). The level is how bent: a second knock only raises it, and moves it to the wheel that took the worse. Which wheel is saved (`rodWheel`, `rimWheel`).
- Spotting it is `CrashWatch` in `crashDamage.js` (pure). There is no collision callback in the physics to hang this on, so it watches the velocity: speed knocked off against the way the quad was going, added up over `window` 0.08 s, counts as a hit over `hitMinDv` 3.5 m/s; a fall brought up short, added up the same way (a suspension spreads a landing over several steps), counts as a landing over `landMinDv` 5 m/s. Hard braking tops out near 0.8 m/s in that window, so it cannot trip it. A step over 0.1 s, or nobody aboard (`rider` or `driver`), resets it, so a teleport or stopping a parked quad by hand is never a crash; one crash counts once (`cooldown` 1.5 s).
- What bends (`damageFor`): a hit picks the wheel nearest the obstacle (the way the quad was shoved, taken into its own frame). A front wheel can have its rod bent (`rodChance` 0.7 at the worst, half that if the hit is more sideways than head-on), any wheel its rim (`hitRimChance` 0.6). A landing can only bend a rim, on a random wheel (`landRimChance` 0.45). The lightest hit that counts has 0.4 of those chances and level 0.35; the worst has all of them and level 1.
- Effects at full: a rod pulls the bars 0.07 rad towards its side (a flat tyre's is 0.09) and toes its wheel 0.14 rad, for show; a rim wobbles its wheel 0.09 rad once a turn (a little rolling too) and costs 5% of the drive. Both are shown on the wheel in `Quad.update`'s wheel loop. Wheels that can't be hurt (the mini moto's outriggers) are never picked, and a rod is only on a wheel the layout calls `front`.
- Bench: one job, `crash` (thirteenth, on `[`, since the digits, minus and equals are used), mends whichever is bent. Its look step says which wheel, in words. The `true` step has a window, and a hold too short, too long or nuts left loose leave both at `crashBotched` 0.3. The girl at the bench doesn't do it.
- The girls: `faultRod` and `faultRim` (said when it's being ridden and bad enough to notice, as a flat tyre is) and `histRod` and `histRim` (a quad that starts the day bent). New lines, so the voice banks want re-recording. Nobody reacts at the moment of the hit; that is the existing shout and flinch work, not touched.
- Panel: the two levels in Problems, which wheel, and a "Hit a post head-on" button that calls the real `crash` method. Tuning per quad under "Crash damage". Console: `faults.rod`, `faults.rim`.
- Not done: a sound for the knock, the girls reacting at the instant of the hit, a rim leaking air, and bending anything on the tractor or Land Rover specially (they take the same rules).
- **Not checked** in the running game: the thresholds against real gatepost hits and the ramp landing (picked sensibly, not measured), and how the toe and wobble look on the models. Unit tests only.

## The green quad stalling (2026-10-12)

Jamie: the green stalls a lot, cold and warm. Found with the real engine model run headlessly (no browser), not by changing the stall RPM, which was fine: a stall point of about 270 to 300 against an idle of 800 to 900.

- **Warm:** an engine is erratic (stumbles, surges, now and then dies) once its build roughness plus wear passes `erraticFrom` 0.8. The green was dealt roughness 0.55 to 0.7 plus wear 0.25 to 0.55, so every green was born past the line: 16 of 40 stalled within five minutes of idling at wear 0.25 and 37 of 40 at 0.4 (red and blue, none). The old three-wheeler (0.80 to 1.35) and orange (0.79 to 1.10) were the same. Wear only goes up (0.012 a minute idling), so a green will cross the line again after enough play until it is serviced.
- **Fix:** wear ranges lowered, roughness left alone: green 0.08 to 0.15, three-wheeler 0.12 to 0.20, orange 0.02 to 0.04. `tests/startingCondition.test.js` keeps the green's and three-wheeler's middle roughness plus worst wear under the line. The fleet test's "worn" for the three-wheeler is now "at least as worn as the yellow's low end".
- **Cold:** the green needs 1.15 of enrichment at temperature 0 and full choke gave 1.0, so it was lean on full choke until it had warmed a little and died within seconds. `chokeEnrichment` is 1.25 for the green. It still wants choke for about three minutes and eases off as it warms (with the choke following its need it ran 400 s without a stall); take the choke off early and it dies, as intended.
- **Not changed:** the stall RPM and the spec idle. The yellow, orange and three-wheeler also have a choke a little short of their cold need (1.0 against 1.05 to 1.1); left, as they are two-strokes with their own jet error.
- **Orange:** its roughness alone (0.75 to 0.9) is on the line, so it is still erratic in many games. That is a roughness decision, not wear.
- **A weak battery** (under 40%) kills any petrol engine at idle (the 10 October charging change); I could not see Jamie's save, so that is unchecked as a cause.
- **Not checked** in the running game: the headless runs use the engine alone, with no clutch or braking load.

## A day that changes, and the forecast board (TODO 28)

Jamie settled: a scheduled turn rather than random drift (so it can be forecast), only believable turns, a board that is right most of the time, the girls reacting lightly, nothing for the tarp or the washing line.

- **The turn is dealt at the start** from `this.seed ^ 0x2c9d` (`src/world/forecast.js`, pure, tested): 40% of games turn, between 1 and 5 game hours after the start. The table `SHIFTS` is the believable ones only (fair weather clouds over, grey goes wet, wet clears, fog burns off); snow never turns, because its own cover and melt rules already deal with it, and nothing turns to fog or snow.
- **Easing across, not jumping.** `Weather.beginShift` blends the sky (`baseSky`) and the wind over `shiftHours` (0.75 game hours, about 3 real minutes at the usual clock rate). The rain retargets 40% of the way through so the sky darkens before it falls, and the barometer moves with the sky, a little ahead of the rain. The day's warmest moves by the difference in `KIND_WARMTH` between the kinds (sunny +1.5, rain -1.5 degrees), spread over the blend through `peak`, which `updateDaylight` already reads each frame.
- **The board is right 80% of the time about whether the day turns.** One roll per day: right means it shows the real turn; wrong means no turn when one comes, or a made-up turn when none does. It shows three slots, with the slot's middle hour deciding which kind it shows. It says nothing about wind or temperature. It never changes its mind during the day.
- **Forced or hand-set weather stays put.** `?weather=` makes the forecast `frozen`, and `setWeatherKind` cancels any turn left, so the debug panel and console work as they did.
- **The girls.** Amy reads the board early (`forecastChat` 0.85, Jess 0.2) and says whether it is turning worse or better. As the sky turns (once the blend is 30% across) either may remark, more often when the board said so; when it did not, `forecastMissed` is the fallback. These are reactions to the weather in `watchWeather`, with their own cooldowns, not offers.
- **Not saved.** The day is dealt afresh each load like the weather it came from; the farm's save does not carry it.
- **Not checked in a browser:** the slate's drawing (symbols, text fit) and how the sky blend looks. Unit tests cover the dealing, the board, the blend, the rain timing and the temperature move.

## The girls' cars (TODO 161)

Settled with Jamie before building (2026-10-12):

- Amy's is a Mk1 Fiesta (1976 to 1983) in faded pale blue with a beige interior, tidy and cared for, with L plates on (item 167 has her pass her test in it). Jess's is a 1993 Corsa B 1.2i three-door: fuel injected (no choke, no carb faults), tatty and dressed up, primer on a panel and a loud big-bore exhaust. No real maker's badges on either.
- Built like the Land Rovers: their own spec, procedural model, dash, sound bank, wear, quirks and faults; front-wheel drive, manual boxes, key and starter; dashes and controls correct-ish for the real cars; a stereo in each, for looks only for now.
- Four seats each, two in front and two in the back, with belts, got in and out of as the 200TDi's are: the back reached past a tipped front seat. No opening doors yet (later, with the Land Rovers'), no wipers yet.
- A new hard-standing to park on. Road cars: low, on road tyres, slithering on wet grass, bogging easily and scraping on bumps. The girls take them out only on the yard and the farm road; you can take them anywhere.
- Each girl takes her own car for a go and only ever drives her own. Borrowing: take it without her and she minds when she sees, more if it comes back damaged or bogged; with her in it she's fine but remarks on your driving. Amy minds a lot, Jess less unless you're slow.
- Seats as the 200TDi's.
- Sound from BBC Sound Effects recordings Jamie downloaded: RemArc licence, not CC0, allowed as a named exception for this private game (ASSETS.md says so at the top, and CLAUDE.md points at it), to be replaced if the game is ever made public. Exterior recordings only, as the Land Rovers have no interior sound.
- New TODO items 183 to 188: arriving down the lane in the morning, driving home at the end of the day, Jess too fast down the lane, opening doors, wipers, working radios.

What was built:

- **Specs** (`FIESTA_SPEC`, `CORSA_SPEC` in `quadSpecs.js`, `car: 'fiesta' | 'corsa'` and `owner`), sharing `CAR_VEHICLE`, `CAR_ENGINE` and `CAR_VIBRATION`. Both at `scale` 1.15 like the Land Rovers, so they don't look small beside the girls. Fiesta: 830 kg, 4,000 N, four gears to 8.5, 15, 24 and 37 m/s (19, 34, 54, 83 mph), idle 1,010 to 1,090 (its recording's 1,084 to 1,187), 6,000 rpm, a pull-out choke. Corsa: 930 kg, 4,100 N, five gears to 8, 14, 21, 29 and 40 m/s (89 mph flat out), idle 860 to 900 (its recording's 854 to 909), 6,300 rpm. Both `cylinders` 4. Road tyres are their grip params: `grassFrictionSlip` 1.4 (a quad's 2.2), `mudFrictionSlip` 0.42, `tyreGrip` 0.6, `mudGrip` 0.24, `mudLoad` 0.3; soft springs (`suspensionStiffness` 24) and a low `rollInertia` (170) so they lean. Numbers are first guesses, not driven yet.
- **Front-wheel drive** needed no new physics: the layout's back wheels are `driven: false` and the spec's `fourWheelDrive` is 1, so only the front pair is driven (quadPhysics already honoured both).
- **Injection** is a new engine param, `injected`: the mixture gets exactly what a cold engine needs whatever the choke, no carb boils when hot, the choke keys do nothing on it, its quirk features drop the creeping choke, and its carb fault can't start. `coldIdleRpm` (450 on the Corsa) is the injection's own fast idle, falling away as it warms.
- **Models** (`vehicle/carModel.js`, one builder with a table per car, tested): the body side is one extruded profile with both wheel arches cut up into the sill (a test keeps every point clear of the tyre), with the bonnet and tail deck as skins under the same upper line, a glasshouse leaning in from the waist (A, B and C pillars, glass, a roof with a headlining), square lamps, a black slatted grille and black bumpers with a chrome strip on the Fiesta, oval lamps, body-coloured bumpers, a grey primer wing and a big chrome tailpipe on the Corsa. Inside: cloth trim drawn on canvases (beige check, grey fleck), carpet, door cards, two front seats whose backrests tip (`seatBack_left`, `seatBack_driver`), a back seat for two, four diagonal-and-lap belts named as the UTV's so `poseUtvBelts` poses them, a raked column and wheel (two spokes and three), a floor gear lever in the H of `gearLeverPose`, a handbrake between the seats, pedals, the Fiesta's choke knob, the stereo, and Amy's L plates and road atlas and Jess's air freshener and back-window sticker. Same named anchors as the 200TDi (no `seatMid`), so Quad, the mount and the girls' seat code work unchanged; `UtvMount` skips a seat whose anchor isn't there, `UtvRide` takes the front's seats (`front: ['left']`), and `pickRearSeat` only picks seats a vehicle has.
- **Dashes** (`dash.js`, `fiesta` and `corsa`): the Fiesta's is a big speedometer to 100 mph with its odometer, fuel and water dials beside it and the lamps underneath (charge, oil, choke, handbrake, indicators, main beam), white needles; the Corsa's has a rev counter to 7,000 with the red from its redline, a speedometer to 120 mph, stacked water and fuel gauges and a strip of lamps including a new engine management symbol, red-orange needles. The binnacle stands out of the dash face so it is seen through the top of the wheel, about 30 degrees below your eye line, which the -12 degree pitch you get in with puts on screen (checked by screenshot from the seat).
- **Sound** (`engine-bank-fiesta`, `engine-bank-corsa`, recipes in `tools/slice_engine_audio.py`): every BBC file carries its own description, so all 26 were sorted by what they hold. Neither car has a recording with high revs, so: the Fiesta idles on its exterior idle (62 to 88 s, whole cycles of about 109 ms) and revs on its depart (labelled 2,600 to 4,200) and the CC0 Mini's blips above; the Corsa idles on the Carlton's warm idle before it departs (34 ms between firings, 875 rpm) and revs on the same car pulling away (875 to 1,770 rpm) and the cold engine held at about 2,170 rpm (the hold's firings come every 13.8 ms). Both play a whole cycle every fourth firing (`pulseDivide` 4 with `cylinders` 4). The exterior starts catch in about a fifth of a second, too short to loop, so the starters are the interior recordings' (about half a second of cranking each). The shutdowns are the "approach and stop, switch off" endings. The Corsa has a stall of its own: the worklet plays a bank's `stall` one-shot for a stall where it has one (the cold Carlton dying), else the shutdown as before. Both banks preload with the others (2.3 and 3.0 MB). Rendered offline (`.scratch/cars-sound/renders/`) for Jamie to judge: at idle the Corsa's beat sits at its firing rate (28 Hz at 880 rpm); full throttle is about 11 dB over idle on both.
- **The hard-standing** (`layout.carStanding`, `world/carStanding.js` pure, `carStandingBuild.js`): first tried off the barn's south-west corner, but the land there falls a metre and more below the yard, and a level slab left a step a car couldn't climb. The flattest clear ground near the barn (searched against the spawn keep-out, the track and the slope) is the yard's east side at (-31, 60), 28 m from the barn doors, with 0.3 m of fall across it: a 10 by 6.8 m slab there, levelled with a 2 m shoulder, drawn as weathered concrete in bays with oil under each car. It counts as tarmac for grip (`fields.tarmacAt`), grows no grass, and keeps quads from starting on it; two quad slots moved off it, to (-54, 52) and (-24, 54).
- **In the game** the cars live in `landys` beside the Land Rovers (`car: true`), so their mounts, rides, seat tips, belts, wheel slices, sheltering from the rain and saves come for free; `GO_KINDS` has them, and `CAR_KINDS` keeps them out of the big-vehicle go (Jess never takes Amy's car). Their own go is an offer (`carGo` in `OFFER_KINDS`, `npc/carGo.js`, weighed by `carGoWeight`: Amy 0.8, Jess 1.3), every `every` 480 s per girl from `firstAfter` 200 s, always an outing down the farm road and back (`roadSpots` in `outings.js`: the end of the road or halfway along it; no distance limit), at her own pace (Amy 5 to 11 m/s at 0.6 throttle, Jess 8 to 16 at full) with a driver's gear changes. Only the owner gets "Drive it" on the wheel; asked about the other's car she says so (`carNotMine`).
- **Borrowing** (`Borrowing` in `carGo.js`, tested): taken without her and seen (within `seenRange` 45 m): `favourTaken` -0.5 and a line; brought back with a new fault or bogged: `favourDamaged` -1; taking her along: `favourRidden` +0.3. Sat beside you in her car, Amy says something above 18 m/s, Jess after 20 s under 7 m/s, and either when you stall it.
- **Lines** (`npc/carLines.js`): off, setting off, watching, back, not mine, taken, back, back damaged, ridden, stalled, too fast (Amy), too slow (Jess only).
- **Panel**: "Their cars" (Girls: things they do), "Fiesta riders" and "Corsa riders" (Driving) in the Tune tab, a line in the Girls tab's shared section and the console's `cars`, and `car-go` in each girl's "Make her do" (`girl amy car-go`). Each car's own groups are in the Vehicles tab as any vehicle's.
- **Checked**: unit tests (models, specs, front-wheel drive, arches, controls, the car go and borrowing); one headless run: the game loads without errors, both cars stand on their wheels on the slab, and screenshots from outside, from above and from the driver's seat of each (the dashes read). **Not checked**: driving either (grip, roll, bogging, scraping, top speeds), a girl's go in one (getting in, the route down the road, coming back to the slab), riding in the back, borrowing and the lines in play, the sound in the game by ear, and the spawn sag (guessed at 0.06 m, not measured).
