Black arch liners close off the inside (the brown in the arches in play is the mud coat on them).
- **Shorter boots**: the Fiesta's tail is 8 cm shorter with a more upright hatch, the Corsa's 9 cm.
- **A rounder Corsa**: its upper line is a smooth curve through its points (`round`), the glasshouse leans in more (`tumble` 0.26 against the Fiesta's 0.16), the pillars are fatter, the roof is crowned (more on the Corsa), the nose is a rounded block and the side panels' edges softer. Its bumper sits 7 cm further back, and its front indicators are amber lenses wrapping round the outer ends of the lamps (they were inside the bumper). Both have a repeater on each front wing.
- **The wheel and dash**: the wheel was inside the binnacle's hood. The dash is now shallower (its face 0.2 m behind the screen's foot), the column comes out of the face and the wheel's middle is about 0.4 m ahead of the hips and 0.33 m up; the shroud is small and low so the dials show over it. The Fiesta's hazard switch is on top of the column, as the Series III's; the Corsa's has no surround and is in the middle of the dash over the stereo, which moved there from the console in both. The Fiesta's choke is a pull knob just inside the wheel's rim. The stalk shows below the wheel now the column is clear of the dash. Amy's dash, binnacle and the panel round her dials are brown.
- **The plates** on the Fiesta's front bumper (number and L) stand proud of its chrome strip. **Belts** hang from the B pillars (the front) and the rear quarters (the back), with a mounting at the top. **The handbrake** pivots on top of the tunnel (it was inside it).
- **Jess's max-power bits**: a small sports wheel with a red top mark, a billet alloy knob with a red top, drilled alloy pedals, fluffy dice and the air freshener, a sun strip with JESS on it, a boot spoiler and a bass box in the boot.
- **Starters**: the BBC's starts catch within half a second, the Fiesta's interior crank was barely above the hiss, and the Carlton's fired part way through, so looped they stuttered and seemed to catch. Both cars now crank on a CC0 recording of a car that won't start (jerry.berumen, 511685): seven seconds of even cranking with a compression every 120 to 140 ms, each car's loop four steady compressions cut just ahead of one (the Fiesta about 220 rpm, the Corsa, from its second attempt, about 240), set about 10 dB under the idle (a new `oneshotGain` in the slicer, since one-shots don't take a source's gain). The crank now runs straight into the catch, with no 50 ms dead gap. Offline: cranking at about -33 dB against an idle of -22.
- **Frame time**: only the cars' outsides cast shadows now, and the tyres are lighter. The spawn view: 2,516 draw calls (budget 2,600; the last commit had 2,247), 3.98 M triangles (budget 3.5 M, but the last commit was already at 3.77 M before the cars), the game's own time 6 ms. Loading to Ready: 2.10 s.
- Checked: unit tests; screenshots from outside and from each driver's seat; a headless drive of each (starts, pulls away in first to about 17 mph and into second). Not checked: everything listed under the first pass's "Not checked", and how the hazard switch reads on top of the Fiesta's column (it may sit in the shadow of the wheel).

### The girls' cars, third pass (Jamie's list)

- **Everyone sat too far forward**, in the cars, both Land Rovers and the UTV: the hips were 0.23 to 0.28 m in front of the backrests' faces (a person's are about 0.12), which put the wheel against your chest and the mirror over your head. The Land Rovers' hips moved back 0.12 m (front to z -0.34, the 200TDi's back bench to -1.12, the cushions staying where they were), the UTV's 0.11 m (to -0.31), and the worn belts' chest and lap points with them (`SEAT_BACK` in each model), so the girls' poses and your eyes follow. The Land Rover's column is shorter (the wheel at 0.42 along it, was 0.5). The cars' front hips are about 0.3 m behind the screen's top (Fiesta z -0.27, Corsa -0.26) with the back seats 0.08 m further back, the wheel much closer to the dash (0.2 along the column, was 0.28), and the mirror lower on a stem, so it is ahead of you.
- **The Fiesta's hazard switch** couldn't be seen because its column points almost at your eye and the dials sit behind the wheel: anything on top of the column hid behind the wheel's boss, and "up" off the raked column also leans into the binnacle. It is on a pod standing up off the column just ahead of the wheel, where it shows over the boss, and the Fiesta's lamps moved into two short rows above it. Checked by screenshot from the seat.
- **The Fiesta's dials**: two the same size, the speedometer and a combined fuel and water gauge (as a Mk1 L's), with the lamps between, black rims, pale green-white figures and orange needles, set lower so the hood doesn't clip them.
- **Boots**: shorter again (Fiesta tail at -1.64, Corsa -1.68), the hatch glass coming down nearer the tail, and a shut line across the tail just above the bumper and up each side to the glass, with a lock, so the tailgate reads as one.
- **The Corsa**: its tail lamps are lower, all on the tailgate below the glass; the plate between them; the sun strip lies on the outside of the screen (it was turned edge-on); Jess's seats are tall buckets in a loud zigzag cloth, with side bolsters, the headrest built in and a harness slot (the old headrests floated off the backrests); it shakes less (vibration `steadiness` 0.82, `pulseGain` 0.75, less heave and pitch, half the random stumble, `erraticHunt` 0.02, less wear and roughness) and its springs are damped firmer (3.2 and 3).
- **Belts** have their top mountings inside the glass, where the pillars lean in; the back ones hang from the rear quarters ahead of the hatch (they stuck out of the doors and the back).
- **Mud**: a model's `cleanMaterials` (the seats, dash, carpet, door cards, headlining and belts) are left out of the mud coat, so the insides of the cars and the Land Rovers stay clean.
- **Parking**: the hard-standing is 8.4 m deep (was 6.8) and the cars 5.2 m apart (were 3.4), so a girl can walk between them.
- **The Fiesta's idle** is 830 to 870 warm (Jamie: about 850). Its recording idles at 1,084 to 1,187, so each beat is stretched to fill the longer cycle, to about 0.73 of its speed at the slowest, above the 0.45 where the sound would leave a gap (a test keeps it there).
- **The Fiesta breaking up as it revved** was a bug in every vehicle's sound: the game passed the engine's events to the sound without their strength, so an overrun pop or a spit-back had a loudness of NaN, which silenced the whole engine sound until it reset. The Fiesta pops often on the overrun, so it showed there. `engineAudio.js` and the render tool now pass the strength, and the worklet falls back to 0.5 if one ever comes without. Also, the Mini's blips had given the Fiesta's bank some grains a quarter or half the length of a cycle (13 to 20 ms, labelled up to 9,000 rpm); the slicer's period check now refuses them for both cars. Renders of the Fiesta, the Corsa, the red quad and the Series III: no NaN.
- **Tunable**: yes, as every vehicle; a FAQ answer says where.
- Not checked: the girls' poses in the moved seats (sitting, climbing in and out, the belts across them), the UTV and the Land Rovers from the seat after the move, and the cars on the move.

### The girls' cars, fourth pass (Jamie's list)

- **The windscreen's distance from your eye.** Measured the same way in each (horizontally, at eye height, at the same 1.15 scale): the Land Rovers' glass was 0.95 m ahead with the screen's top 0.92 m ahead; the Fiesta's 0.66 m with its top only 0.34 m ahead, the Corsa's 0.68 m with its top 0.16 m ahead and the mirror 0.09 m in front of your eye, so you couldn't see it. The cars' screens are less raked (the Fiesta 45 degrees from upright, was 54; the Corsa 49, was 60), the seats sit further back (front hips at z -0.4 and -0.34, the back seats at -1.1 and -1.14), with the B pillars and the roof's back edge moved back to match: the glass is now 0.92 and 0.94 m ahead, the screen's top 0.69 and 0.6, the mirror 0.62 and 0.53. `carEyeToGlass` and `landyEyeToGlass` measure it, and a test holds each car within 0.06 (model units) of the Land Rovers and the mirror at least 0.4 ahead of you.
- **The wheel and dash** for a driver sat further back: the dash is deeper (its face 0.32 m behind the screen's foot) and the column longer (each car's `wheelOut`), so the wheel's middle is about 0.5 m ahead of the hips without sticking far out. Jess's small wheel sits lower (`columnY` 0.575 against Amy's 0.71) with a smaller boss, so all her dials and lamps show over it. The pedals, handbrake and tunnel follow the seats. Checked from both seats.

- **Door handles**: the side details (handles, shut lines, rubbing strips, repeaters, mirrors) sat inside the Corsa's rounder side, whose bevel puts its face further out than the Fiesta's. They're placed on each car's own outer face now, and the Corsa's handles are dark grey pulls in a recess.
- **A boomier Corsa**: a new audio param, `pulseDrone` (dB) at `pulseDroneFreq` (Hz), a low boost on the pulse sound that is full at idle and half of it high up the revs. The Corsa has 9 dB at 75 Hz and `recVolume` 1.1: offline its idle is 3.7 dB louder, nearly all of it below 150 Hz, and full throttle half a dB. Renders at 6, 9 and 12 dB are in `.scratch/cars-sound/renders/` (`corsa-revs-drone*.wav`). A first go: more refinement later.


## The Corsa's dash and idle (TODO 54)

- Jamie: the Corsa's warning lights were hidden behind the wheel. A first go raised the binnacle and lowered the column; Jamie then found the column far too low and asked for the Fiesta's way instead: the dials seen through the hoop of the wheel. The column is back up (`columnY` 0.71, was 0.575) and `carDashThroughWheel` in `carModel.js` measures where the sight line from the driver's eye to the binnacle crosses the wheel's plane: the lamp strip at 0.062 above the middle (the boss reaches 0.035) and the top of the dials at 0.136 (the rim's inner edge is 0.15). A test holds both cars to that, so a change to the seat, wheel or dash that hides the dials fails. Not checked by screenshot.
- **Idle too high when warm**: the injection's fast idle (`coldIdleRpm` 450) fell away linearly with temperature, so at half way up the gauge the Corsa still idled about 225 rpm high and took the full 300 s to reach 880. It now falls with the cube of how cold it is (`engineModel.js`; only injected engines use it): about 1,110 rpm at 0.2, about 30 rpm over 880 by 0.6 and 7 by 0.75. Cold starts are as before.

## Plug-saver for the two-strokes (TODO 31)

Settled with Jamie before building (2026-10-12): all six recommendations taken.

- **Where it goes.** A bottle by the oil bottle treats the pre-mix can (hold `E` with the can in your hands); the treated share carries into the tank on a fill. Unlike the oil bottle it is a fixed prop, not carried: nothing needs to take it anywhere else, and carried items cost plumbing (hands, trailer, bench spots).
- **How it lasts.** Not a timer: a `additive` share (0..1) of the fuel, mixed with `mixOil` like the oil, diesel and water fractions. It thins as untreated fuel goes in and is gone when the fuel is. `treatRate` is 2 litres a second, so a full 10 L can takes five seconds.
- **What it does.** `additiveFoulCut` (0.85) takes that share off the idle fouling (`idleFoulRate`) and the excess-oil fouling (`oilFoulRate`) at full strength. Rich fouling from the choke or a clogged filter is untouched, so mixture mistakes still matter. Diesels have no plug and ignore it.
- **The girls.** One remark each (`plugSaver` in `npc/additiveLines.js`), said by whoever is nearest and free to remark when a can first goes over half treated. Amy adding it to the can on her own would be a new offer and a pose; left out, noted as an idea.
- **Kept.** `additive` is in `FUEL_KEPT`, so a tank's treated share survives a reload. The cans are not saved, as before.
- **Panel.** Running on the Vehicles tab shows and sets it for a two-stroke, "Plug-saver" in Tune (Items and workshop) has the pour rate, and the console has `plugSaver`. It is not a fault, so "Put it right" leaves it.
- **The Vehicles tab's columns (same session).** lil-gui gives a row's label `min-width: 45%` and `white-space: pre`, so a label longer than that pushed its value to the right and the columns drifted from row to row. The label is now a fixed width that wraps (`src/style.css`). Not checked in a browser.
- **Not checked in a browser:** the bottle's look and place, and the column fix. Unit tests cover the mixing, the engine's fouling cut, the save and the text.

## A gate on the cattle grid (TODO 32)

- Jamie: a gate across the whole gateway, opened with `E` on foot, shut at the start of a game, opened and shut by the girls and the post van, and no consequence for leaving it open.
- The gate is the big gate's steel pair (`buildSteelGate` in `props.js`, now shared), `road.grid.gateOffset` 2.4 m east of the grid's middle and spanning the corridor between the road's hedges (9.3 m between the posts), so there is no way round. It is east of the grid because the hedge line at the grid has open field either side of the wings, while the corridor is closed by hedges. It is a `Gate` with id `grid`, no hedge gap (the girls' routes ignore it), and its leaves swing out east along the verges. Cross the grid from the field and the gate is the next thing you meet.
- The visit (`planVisit`): stop east of the gate, the driver opens it, drives through to just west of the grid, stops and shuts it; on the way out the same in reverse (opens it if it was shut meanwhile, shuts it again from the east). A `gate` step with `gate: 'grid'` finds its gate in `ctx.gates`, and is skipped where there is none, so older stand-in worlds are unchanged.
- The girls (`world/gateMinding.js`, pure, `tests/gateMinding.test.js`): a girl walking or driving (not riding pillion) at 0.6 m/s or more within 10 m of a shut grid gate opens it. If she opened it, she shuts it once nobody (you, a quad, a girl) has been within 14 m for 8 s. A gate you opened, or set from the panel or console, is left alone (`taken()`). She says nothing: no new lines. Tuning: "Girls and the grid gate" in the Tune tab (World). Girls' route planning still treats the grid as open ground, so she never stops for it; she opens it on the way.
- Debug: a "Swing the cattle grid gate" button in the Girls tab's shared section, and `lane.gridGate` in the console.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how it looks (the leaves against the hedges, the posts against the grid's kerb), whether a girl driving at speed gets the gate open in time (10 m and 1.8 s is a guess), or a girl opening it needlessly while stood about near it.

## Backing out of the hedge (TODO 34)

- Jamie: the girls keep getting stuck in the bottom hedge by the end of the road and can't reverse round. Found by reading `herDriving.js`: backing off steered with `avoidSide`, a coin flip that had nothing to do with where the hedge was, and only for 1.5 to 2.5 s, which is too short to bring the nose round. And in reverse the nose swings the opposite way to forward driving, so a lock chosen for going forward winds her further in.
- Now `chooseReverseSteer` looks 6 m (`backOffLook`) to either side of the nose and swings it towards the side with more room (bars the other way, as reversing needs). Backing off the same thing again she tries the other lock unless that side is clearly worse, and reverses half as long again each time (`backOffLonger`), up to the existing give-up at the fifth go.
- Tested as pure logic. Not checked in the running game: the bottom hedge itself, or how it looks.

## Hour meters in game time (TODO 35)

- **Hours are game hours.** `advanceUsage` (`src/vehicle/dashState.js`) multiplies the frame's seconds by a `timeScale`, which `Quad.updateDash` takes from `dashClock().rate()`; the game sets that to the day's `clockRate` (12 game seconds a real second by default), so five real minutes of running adds one hour, the same hour the dash clock shows. It follows the Tune tab's clock rate live.
- **Odometer and trip stay as they were.** They are distance, and the farm's metres are already the game's metres, so nothing scales them.
- **Starting readings and saves are unchanged.** The ranges in the specs were already meant as hours of use, so they now sit in proportion to how much a game adds. Wear and faults are tied to the readings at the start only, not to hours added during play.
- **Not checked:** the feel of the numbers over a long run in the browser; unit test only.

## The girls' wheel when she's busy (TODO 36)

- **Decided with Jamie:** a small set of general requests that always make sense, not just un-gating the old idle-only ones. Asked with `availability({ asked: true, pull: true })`, so soft jobs and the farm's holds give way and a girl who is walking, driving or sat on a vehicle can be broken into. Her lines are the existing acknowledgements (`ackStand`), so nothing new is recorded.
- **What shows (`src/npc/generalRequests.js`, pure, tested):** "Come here" (idle, walking, driving, sat; not when she is already within 2.5 m), "Stop there" (walking only), "I'll be a minute" (idle, walking, driving). "Push start it" is open to the same states. Each calls `companion.interrupt()` first. "Come here" gets her off a vehicle first (`requestComeHere`, using the usual stop-driving and dismount hand-offs) then `walkToYou`.
- **When she's held** (carrying something, the nick, the hay trailer): the wheel doesn't open, and the toast says what has her rather than "She's busy".
- **Not checked:** in the running game; unit tests only. "I'll be a minute" from a walking or driving girl leans on `requestMount` and `requestStart`'s existing hand-offs. Not added to the debug panel or console (they are wheel requests, and the Girls tab's existing actions are the same game methods).

## Carb icing (TODO 38)

- **What:** `src/vehicle/carbIcing.js` (pure, tested). Ice builds in a petrol engine's carb when the throttle is steady between 12% and 75% open, the revs are above idle, and the weather suits it: `icingWeather` is worst at 2 to 8 degrees C and fades to nothing at -4 and 14, and needs damp (the greater of the rain and 0.8 of the ground's wetness, at least 0.25). About half a minute from clear to choked in the worst weather; a warm engine builds it half as fast.
- **Clearing:** a throttle opened faster than 3.5 a second (a blip) breaks off 0.5 of the ice; closed throttle melts it at 0.06 a second, and an engine that's off at 0.03. Working the throttle at all stops it building.
- **Effect:** the engine takes `ice` as a control and loses `carbIcePowerLoss` (0.55) of its power at full ice, and runs a little uneven. It is not saved between loads: it melts within a minute of stopping. Diesels (no carb) have it disabled; the petrol Land Rover has a carb, so it can ice.
- **The girls:** `carbIcing` lines (`iceLines.js`): Amy names it and says to blip it, Jess thinks it's broken. Said at most twice a spell, only while the quad is moving, and again after it has cleared. The lines need recording.
- **Panel and console:** "ice in the carb" in Running, a settable "iced carb" in Problems (cleared by Put it right), `carbIce.ice` in the console, and the "Carb icing" tuning group (Engines, fuel and faults).
- **Not checked:** in the running game; unit tests only. Whether the temperature and damp window turns up often enough on the farm's weather is a guess.

## Fuel starvation on the banks (TODO 37)

- **What:** below `starveFraction` (0.3) of the tank, the fuel slops away from the tap's pick-up when the nose is up a steep enough bank, and nothing reaches the float bowl (`FuelSystem.coversTap(climb)`, `uncovered`). The bowl is 12 ml, so a running engine stumbles and dies in a few seconds, and picks up when it levels out and the bowl refills. The slope that does it falls with the tank: `starveClimb` (sine 0.3, about 17 degrees) just under the threshold down to `starveMinClimb` (0.1, about 6 degrees) nearly dry.
- **Only quads with a tap:** the `noTap` vehicles (cars, tractor) have bigger tanks and are untouched. Tap OFF is already dry.
- **The girls:** the stall goes through the existing `stalled` notice, so they react as to any stall. I added no new line saying whose quad it was.
- **Panel and console:** "fuel slopped away from the tap" shows in Running and as `tapUncovered` in the console; the three params are in the vehicle's Fuel tuning.
- **Not checked:** in the running game; unit tests only. Whether 17 degrees is often met on the farm's banks is a guess.

## A quad towing a quad (TODO 39)

Settled with Jamie before building: a rope that stretches (not the chain), taken and hooked as the chain is, either girl steers the dead quad when asked, her braking a simple controller with a per-girl difference, and no snapping in this first version.

- **The rope** (`src/items/towRope.js`, `ropeForce`, `ropeLengthFor` and `isSnatch` pure and tested). `X` on foot looking at a red or blue quad takes it off the rack (the green's `X` is its winch; the tractor and the cars have their own ways), `E` on a dead quad hooks it, `E` again unhooks, `X` puts it back. It is hooked with 0.3 m of slack, kept between 2.5 and 5 m long. Once tight it is a spring and damper: 5,000 N/m plus 900 N s/m while it is lengthening, at most 5,000 N. The damping is what makes a snatch: pulling off hard takes up the slack at speed and the dead quad is jerked. A tow quad of about 290 kg feels the whole pull back (`reaction` 1), unlike the chain, which drags a 2,000 kg tractor a quarter. The hooked quad is `towed` (brakes off), as with the chain.
- **A snatch** is the rope going tight at 1.8 m/s of lengthening or more (`snatchRate`). The game passes it on as a drive event to the girl steering, who says `towSnatch`.
- **Steering it** (`src/npc/towSteer.js`, tested). `TowSteer` has the shape of `HerDrive` (`controls`, `update`, `stop`, `done`, `result`) so the driving state runs it in place of her own driving; no engine is started and nothing is chosen. The bars aim at the towing quad's hitch. The brake is from time to contact: the room between the hitches (less 0.4 m for the quads' ends) divided by how fast it is closing, against her horizon (`towHorizon`: Amy 2.6 s, Jess 1.2 s, 1.8 default). There is a touch of brake on a slack rope she is closing on, and a hold while the towing quad is stopped and she is close. Jess looks away now and then (`towDistractChance` 0.04 a second against Amy's 0.004, for 1.6 s): no steering, no braking, which is how she runs up into your back. A real collision does the rest, as the dead quad has a collider. Running into it at 1.2 m/s or more gets `towBump`. She gets off by herself when the rope comes off (`towDone`), or after 25 s with nobody on the towing quad. She does not drive the dead quad home afterwards (`towing` is checked in `endDrive`).
- **Asking her** is on her wheel: "Steer the towed quad" when the rope is on a quad nobody is on, it is still, and she could sit on it; "Get off the towed quad" while she is on it. Debug: "Hook the tow rope on it / take it off" on the Vehicles tab (from the nearest utility quad within 8 m) and a "tow rope" line in Running; the Girls tab's "steer-tow"; the console's `vehicle.<id>.towRope` and `towRope`; two Tune groups, "Tow rope" and "Girl steering a tow".
- **Left alone:** snapping, a girl driving the towing quad while the player steers the dead one, towing uphill specially, and the rope on the UTV or the Land Rovers. The trailer and the rope can't share a quad's hitch (each says so).
- **Checked:** unit tests (rope force and hooking with stand-in quads, the controller and the driver). **Not checked:** how the rope looks, how the dead quad behaves with real physics on the end of it (the stiffness and damping are sensible guesses and unmeasured), a girl walking to and sitting on a quad that is being pulled, and no headless run was done.

## The spares shelf (TODO 40)

- Jamie, before building: the toolbox's quick fixes draw from the shelf as well as the bench jobs (so a belt swapped in the yard costs a belt); all five parts are counted (plugs, inner tubes, belts, jets, pistons and rings); a job with no part is refused; ordering is at a rack in the barn and the post van brings it; the girls get a line each for running low.
- `src/workshop/spares.js` (pure, `tests/spares.test.js`): `Spares` holds the counts, what is on order and what is in the parcel. Start counts are plugs 3, tubes 2, belts 2, jets 2, pistons 1; a pack is 4 plugs, 2 tubes, 2 belts, 2 jets, 1 piston and rings; `maxOnShelf` 8 counts the shelf, the order and the parcel together. All of it is in "Spares shelf" in the Tune tab.
- Where a part is used: a bench step with `part` (plug `fit`, tyre `patch`, top-end `piston`) takes it when it is pressed, so a job abandoned before that step costs nothing. `Bench.canBegin` also refuses a job whose part is missing, which is what greys it out and what the girls' `benchWorkPlan` reads, so a girl never starts a job she cannot finish; if the shelf is emptied under her mid-job she stops. In the toolbox `repair` checks the shelf only when there is something to fix (nothing is used up, or complained about, if the tyre or belt is fine), and takes the part when the fix works. A bodge patch became a spare tube and "turned the belt round" a spare belt, so the toasts say what it cost.
- The jet: changing to a size other than stock takes one (`jetShelfChange`), going back to stock (a click, or "Back to stock") gives it back, and one size to another is free (one out, one in). Total jets out of the box never goes above one per quad that is off stock.
- The toolbox service (`V`) takes a plug too (Jamie), and says so if there is none. Not counted: the big-bore kit, which has its own piston.
- Ordering: the rack (`sparesShelf.js`, on the back wall beside the bench, `sparesModel.js`: a back board, two shelves, tags; plugs in a yellow box, jets in a red organiser with its lid propped, pistons with rings in a blue box, tubes stacked in a card tray, belts on pegs, each unit hidden as it is used up) opens a panel with the number keys. An order waits `orderDelay` 270 s (plus or minus `orderJitter` 30, so five or six minutes with the drive in; Jamie wanted it long enough to be a real wait), then asks the traffic for the post van the moment no one else is visiting and it is light (`Spares.update`). If a random post visit leaves its parcel first, the order is in that one. The parcel is the box the post already leaves; `E` on it opens it onto the rack (it only prompts when the order is in it). The farm keeps counts, order and parcel (`captureFarm`/`restoreDay`); an order restarts its wait each morning.
- Girls: `sparesLow` (a part dropped to its last one) and `sparesOut` (to none), in `trafficLines.js` for both girls, said by the nearest free girl via the traffic host's `remark`, with a three minute cooldown. Recorded.
- Debug: Tune "Spares shelf"; the Girls tab's shared section shows the shelf and fills, empties, orders and opens the parcel; the console's `spares` path.
- Not checked: how the rack looks (position at the north wall, clear of the bench and the barn-find), the panel in play, or a whole order through a real post visit. Unit tests only.

## The trickle charger and carried batteries (TODO 41)

Jamie, before building and again part way: the jump leads must not charge a dead battery instantly; the charger must look like the other bench and workshop pieces, not basic; a battery has to be a thing that moves from a vehicle to the bench, with its place tracked, for every vehicle that has one, and carriable in the UTV's bed.

- `src/items/battery.js`: `BatteryItem`, one per vehicle, made the first time its battery comes out (`game.batteryOf`). `where` is `in`, `held`, `loose` or `charger`, and `sync()` sets the engine's `batteryOut`, which is the one flag the rest reads. Same pattern as the oil bottle and the leads: `heldLoad`, `handsFull`, `G` to put down, the status line, the map markers, and the UTV bed's `CargoHold` (it tips out with the rest). A battery belongs to its vehicle, so the wrong one gives a prompt rather than fitting. Small for a quad, big for the cars, tractor, UTV and any diesel (`batterySizeFor`); `hasBattery` is false for a pull-start-only machine and for the barn find, which has its own battery job.
- `EngineModel.batteryOut`: no dash (`hasPower`), no starter and no click, no drain or charge (`updateBattery` returns early), so the number is frozen while it is out. A cord or a magneto still fires it. `jumpTarget` returns null if either end is out. In `ENGINE_KEPT`, so which vehicle is dead is saved; `overnight` does not drain a battery that is out.
- Taking it out: two bench jobs sharing the last row of the list (`chargeOn` "Take the battery out", `chargeOff` "Fit the battery you are carrying", chosen by `batteryOut`; a fourteenth key, `]`, was added), and `H` in the toolbox panel for the yard (the battery stands at your feet because the toolbox has your hands; `H` again fits one lying within `fitRange` 4 m). Fitting also works by `E` on the vehicle with the battery in hand, switched off. The girls' bench plan only picks from its own list, so they never take a battery out.
- `src/workshop/charger.js` (pure, tested): `rate` 0.0036 a second (flat to the green lamp in about 4.5 to 5 minutes), easing to 30% of that from 85%, `fullAt` 0.98. One battery at a time; it charges the engine's own number wherever the vehicle is. `src/workshop/chargerModel.js` is the charger: a steel case with vents on the sides and top, carry handle, a dial with a real face and a needle that climbs, red (charging) and green (full) lamps, a rotary switch, a mat for the battery with the two leads and clips lying on it, rubber feet, a mains lead off the back. Placed by `layout.benchCharger`, at the west end of the bench, which is now 4.2 m long (its east end, vice and logbook stay where they were; the barn's bench things that were placed from the west end or the middle were re-anchored so they did not move, and the parts rack, placed from the west end, is a metre further west). `E` with a battery in hand stands it on the mat; `E` with empty hands takes it off.
- Kept overnight: `captureFarm` records which batteries are out and whether on the charger. In the morning the one on the charger is still on it, full; any other loose battery is left on the floor by the bench (`restoreBatteries`), since where it was left in the field is not saved.
- Jump leads: `chargeRate` 0.06 down to 0.012 a second, so a flat battery wants a minute or two joined to a running quad to reach starting (0.25), against 4 seconds before.
- Left out: the tipping trailer does not take a battery (only the UTV's bed does); the girls neither carry nor fit batteries; a girl who tries to start a quad whose battery is out cranks to nothing and, with no click, has no line for it.
- Tuning "Battery charger"; the Vehicles tab says where a vehicle's battery is and can take it out or put it back ("Put it right" does too); the Girls tab's shared section shows the charger and clears it.
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless: the game loads with no errors, a battery goes out, into the hands, onto the charger (20% to 63% in 120 s), into the save and back in, and a close screenshot of the bench showed the charger, mat and battery sitting clear of the vice, logbook and leads. Not checked: the dial's tick marks at normal distance, whether the bed spots take the big battery without clipping, the bench job and `H` feel by hand, a battery carried across a day boundary, and the other vehicles' batteries (the tractor, Land Rover and UTV) in the yard. Unit tests only.

Jamie, before building: yes to the barn bulb and the charger both depending on it, a diesel set outside the barn door started with a pull cord, off every morning, the girls starting it, and a real recording rather than a synthesised sound.

The jump leads, the bench tools and everything else are as they were. "Coupling other things to it" is still for later: anything new reads `game.generator.powered`.
- `src/items/generator.js` (pure, tested): a 5 L tank, `burn` 0.004 L a second (about twenty minutes from full) plus `loadBurn` 0.0025 while the charger has a battery on, `catchChance` 0.45 on the first pull and 0.2 more for each that fails (to 0.95), `pullSeconds` 1.1 between pulls. An empty tank only wheezes. It takes red diesel only (`DIESEL_ENOUGH` 0.5 of a can) and nothing while running. I first said petrol; the recording is a diesel set, so the set is diesel and the existing dark green can serves. Running dry stalls it; `overnight()` leaves it off, keeping the fuel (`captureFarm`'s `generator`). No choke, because it is a diesel.

It has a collider. `src/items/generatorModel.js` is the red cage, engine cover, alternator drum, recoil housing and handle that stretches when pulled, tank with sight gauge, silencer and stack, and a panel with a fuel dial, lamp, switch and socket. It shakes a little while running.
- Sound: Freesound 516759 (CC0, credited in ASSETS.md), cut by script into `public/audio/generator.wav`: the start (3.7 s; the engine catches about 1.6 s in), a 28 s run cross-faded at its seam so the loop does not click, and the stop (6.9 s). 32 kHz mono, 2.4 MB, decoded only when you are within 120 m and it is wanted. `src/audio/generatorAudio.js` plays it through a panner at the set, duller with distance and a lot duller and quieter when you are in the barn. A failed pull plays the cranking part; running dry plays the stop a little faster. No exhaust smoke (the smoke effects belong to vehicles).
- Notices: it catching is an `engineStart` and a stall a `stall`, so the girls turn to look; no new notice kind.
- The girls (`src/npc/generatorStart.js`, pure, tested): an offer `generatorStart` in the chooser, weighed by `generatorWeight` (Amy 1.1, Jess 0.6). It is offered when the set is off with diesel in it and the barn is dark (over 0.3) or a battery is waiting on the charger, not in the first two minutes and not for five minutes after a go. She walks to `layout.generator.stand` and pulls up to four times, 1.9 s apart, saying a line when it coughs and when it catches. They never fill it. Lines in `generatorLines.js`: `generatorGo`, `generatorCough`, `generatorCaught`, and `generatorOut` (said by a girl near enough when it runs dry).
- Debug: Tune "Generator" and "Starting the generator"; the Girls tab's shared section shows the set and pulls, switches off, fills and empties; each girl's "Make her do" has "goes and starts the generator"; the console's `generator` path (`now`, `fuel`, `running`).

Not checked: how it sounds (the clip was cut by numbers, not listened to), the pull animation, the girl's whole walk and pull, the diesel pour by hand, and the set's look from close up or at night.

## The stream after rain (TODO 43)

- `src/world/streamLevel.js` (pure, tested in `tests/streamLevel.test.js`): a level from 0 to 1. Rain over `rainFrom` 0.12 raises it by rain / `riseSeconds` 360 a second (steady heavy rain, about 0.85, takes about seven minutes); dry weather lowers it over `fallSeconds` 1100. It lags the shower on purpose, as the pasture's water takes a while to arrive. A game starts at wet ground x `startWet` 0.7, since weather isn't saved and a rainy start should look rainy. The level is not saved for the same reason.
- What it changes: `hill.setRise` lifts the water's surface by `level x floodRise` (0.22 m at most, under the ford channel's 0.42 m, so it never goes over the banks). `waterAt` and `puddleAt` read it live, so the water is wider across and wheels and the girls see it. The drawn ribbon (`hillProps.js`) is lifted by the same amount, browns with the level and drifts a streak texture faster (`flow`, 1 to 3.6 times). Engine drowning is multiplied by `gulp` (1 to 2.2) for a wheel in the stream (`World.gulpBoostAt`, used in `Quad.waterGulp`), because the ford's water already counted as full depth in its middle, so more depth alone changed little. There is no current pushing a quad downstream; flow is visual and the gulp is the cost.
- The girls: `streamUp` when it crosses `upAt` 0.5, `streamDown` when it falls back under `downAt` 0.2, said by a girl stood about within 40 m of you (`updateWeather`). Lines in `streamLines.js`.
- Debug: Tune "Stream", the Girls tab's shared section (level slider) and the console's `weather.stream`.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked in the running game: how the rise looks at the ford, the streak texture, how much the extra gulp changes a crossing by hand, or the lines by ear.

## The bale spike (TODO 44)

Settled with Jamie before building: only the bales standing on their end (the ten wrapped silage bales beside the barn) can be moved, not the ones on their sides that the girls sit on or the Dutch barn's stack; a rigid carry with no bale physics; stacking up to two high; keys `]` and `[` (free on the tractor); the girls only react; positions kept between loads; and a farm job to move bales to the Dutch barn. He also asked for a hand lever on the left of the seat that shows the arms' position.

- Because the movable bales stand on their end, the spike goes into the curved side at the bale's middle and the bale hangs upright on it: there is no tipping a bale onto its side, which was Jamie's question on 2. Reversing the point into one is the whole of spearing (no key), so the tractor can't bump the bale first: the spike reaches the middle before the body reaches the skin.
- `src/items/baleSpike.js` (pure, tested in `tests/baleSpike.test.js`): `SpikeLift` (0 to 1, 5 s for the full travel, only with the engine running) and `BaleYard` (settle onto the ground or the bale below, `spearable`, `spear`, `carry`, `release`, `countIn`, `toJSON` and `restore`). A spear is the point within 0.45 m of a bale's middle along the ground and within 0.4 m of its height, with nothing stacked on it. The bale slides up to hang 1.15 m behind the plate over 0.35 s. Lowered onto the ground or a bale it rests there; it is left once the point is 0.9 m from its middle (drive forward), and lifted again if the arms rise first.
- `src/items/baleSpikeRig.js` joins that to the game: a bale on the spike has no Rapier collider (removed), and a bale put down gets one again where it lies. The silage meshes and colliders come from `props.js`, which now returns them as `silage`.
- Model (`tractorModel.js`): two red rails braced back to the lift housing, a carriage that runs up them with a plate and a tapered spike, the lever and its quadrant on the left of the seat (forward is down). `poseBaleSpike` poses the carriage and the lever; the whole mast hides while a trailer is on the drawbar (the spike would sit in the hitch ring), and `J` refuses with a bale on it. The height test for the tractor leaves the mast out; it stands about 0.2 m above the exhaust.
- The job: `silage` in `FARM_JOBS`, "Three silage bales into the Dutch barn", on offer only with the tractor in the game and fewer than `balesNeeded` (3) bales already in the open bay (`dutchBarnShape().open`), so a later day does not tick off by itself. It is checked each time a bale is left. Bucket list: Bale lifter.
- Kept: `captureFarm` writes `bales` (id, x, z, yaw, y), restored before the job board is dealt, so a farm that moved bales yesterday offers the job only for what is left to move. A bale on the spike when saved is put down where it hung. Nothing stops a bale being left in a gateway or on the track, or the tractor being dealt on top of one next morning.
- The girls: `baleSpiked` and `silageDone` lines for each (`farmJobLines.js`), said by whoever is nearest and stood about. Nothing else.
- Left out: moving the other bales, bales scattered to random places each day (Jamie's idea for later; the saved positions are the start of it), a front loader, the weight swinging, anything stopping a bale clipping through a hedge or another bale as you carry it.
- Debug: Tune "Bale spike"; the Girls tab's shared section has the arms slider, what is on the spike, "Silage job done now", "Put the silage bales back where they started" and "Put the bale on the spike down"; the console has `farm.spikeLift` and `farm.silageDone`.
- Checked: unit tests; a production build; and headless, the game loads with no errors and, with a bale put at the point, it is speared, loses its collider and hangs 1.15 m behind the plate, and the job shows on the board. Not checked by eye: how the mast, spike and lever look, how the carry feels on a slope, or driving up to a bale by hand.

## A bonfire at dusk (TODO 45)

Settled with Jamie before building (all the recommendations): a fixed site on the grass field's south side; hedge cuttings as bundles carried like the cans (`E`, `G`) and put on a pile by hand, three to a fire; lit with `E`; a point light made at the start and only dimmed; the girls sit round it as an offer, with new lines; rain and wind put it out and a vehicle too near is warned.

- Site (`layout.bonfire` at -6, 71, `layout.brushwood`): 19 m from the track, 9 m from the south hedge, 10 m from the nearest "left here" spot; a keep-out circle of 6 m (`spawnKeepOut`) so nothing starts on it. Six bundles lie along the hedge at z 77, so one fire uses half of them and a second can be built the same day.
- `src/world/bonfire.js` (pure, tested): `Bonfire` with states none, built, lit and ash. Lighting needs the full pile (3) and rain under `rainOut` 0.4. It grows over `growSeconds` 25, burns `burnSeconds` 540 (counted from lighting), then dies over `dieSeconds` 70. Rain over `rainDamp` 0.06 makes it smoky and burns it `dampBurn` 2.5 times faster; rain over `rainOut` puts it out; wind over `windOut` 5 m/s blows out a fire that has not yet reached 0.7. `warms(distance)`: within 4.5 m of a fire at 0.4 or more.
- `src/items/brushwood.js`: `BrushwoodHeap`, a tied bundle of sticks with a few leaves, carried like the oil bottle (counts in `handsFull`, `G` puts it down, shown on the map). Used ones vanish.
- `src/world/bonfireView.js`: a ring of 12 stones, up to 18 sticks shown a bundle at a time (black once burnt, with an ash disc), three crossed additive flame planes that flicker and lean with the wind, a ground glow, 36 sparks, a woodsmoke emitter (its own `ExhaustSmoke`, dimmed with the daylight in `updateWeather` like the vehicles' smoke), the flickering `PointLight` (`lightCandela` 90, a fifth of that by day), and four logs lying round it. Flames and sparks are additive and fog-free, so they glow at night and wash out by day without needing dimming. No colliders: you can ride through it, and "Mind the fire!" shows within about 2.3 m of a lit fire.
- Girls (`src/npc/fireSit.js`, pure, tested): an offer `fire` in `OFFER_KINDS` (warmth 0.9, rest 0.6, company 0.5, purpose 0.3), weight `weight` 0.9 times 1 + `duskBoost` 1.2 times how dark it is, only while the fire is blazing, a log is free, rain is under 0.12 and she is within 90 m. Each girl has her own session and log; she holds herself with `hold('fire')`, goes by `goRest` to a rest spot of kind `log` (hips 0.46 m up), sits `sitSeconds` 110, and says `fireSit`, `fireWarm`, `fireRomance` (only if favoured and you are within 6 m), then `fireDone`, or `fireOut` if the fire dies. Cooldown 240 s a girl. Favour 1.5 (+1 if favoured) at the end. Sitting near a blaze counts as warming in `needsContext`, so her warmth need falls. E at the fire asks the nearest free girl, as the flask rock does.
- Lines: `src/npc/fireLines.js` (`fireLit` when you light it, said by the nearest girl stood about, `fireOffer`, `fireYes`, and the five above), recorded for both girls.
- Not kept overnight: the fire and the bundles are dealt fresh each load. Not done: sharing the flask at the fire (the flask's set is tied to the rock), anything on the fire but brushwood, the girls adding fuel, the fire spreading, marshmallows.
- Debug: Tune "Bonfire" and "Sitting by the bonfire"; the Girls tab's shared section shows the fire's state and builds and lights it, puts it out, clears it, and takes you there; each girl's "Make her do" has "fire"; the console has `farm.bonfire` and `farm.bonfireLit`.
- Checked: unit tests, and headless: the game loads with no errors, the prompts go from "Put the brushwood on the pile (1 of 3)" to "Light the bonfire", a lit fire reaches a full blaze with the flames shown and the light on, and a girl sent to it starts a session. Not checked: how any of it looks, the smoke and sparks in the wind, a girl actually sitting on a log (the seat height 0.46 m is a guess), or the frame-time cost of the extra light and smoke.

## A frame-time readout (TODO 46)

Nothing needed asking: the item said what and where.

- `src/core/frameBreakdown.js` (pure, tested in `tests/frameBreakdown.test.js`): `FrameBreakdown.begin(now)`, `lap(name, now)` and `end()`. A lap is the time since the one before, so `frame()` in game.js only needs a `fb.lap('stage', performance.now())` at each boundary (nine `performance.now()` calls a frame, well under 0.01 ms). Each stage's figure is smoothed (0.9 of the old kept) and its worst over the last 120 frames kept. `FRAME_STAGES` names the ten stages in order, and a test checks the game laps exactly those.
- The stages are cut where the frame already falls: input and prompts; physics (the fixed steps); vehicles (`unit.update`); farm features (every `update*` up to `updateMinds`); girls (minds, bodies, voices); player and items; audio; world, weather and effects (daylight, `world.update`, `updateWeather`); HUD; drawing (the renderer call). A frame paused behind a card is not counted.
- Shown as the Tune tab's first folder, "Frame time" (`src/debug/frameTimeFolder.js`), closed and read-only, and as a line in the console's `perf`. It sits on Tune because that tab is the one for the game as a whole rather than a vehicle or a girl.
- Measured once headless on seed 2 (a software-ish GPU, so the drawing figure means little): vehicles 2.0 ms, farm features 0.9, girls 0.4, world and weather 0.3, the rest under 0.2. "Vehicles" is the biggest of the game's own stages, which is expected with twelve engine models running.
- Not done: a per-feature split inside "farm features" (the console's `trace` is the place for that), and a graph over time.

## Mud on the lens (TODO 47)

Asked and settled: `X` wipes (a ring item on the pad); open vehicles only; the sources are the quad ahead on the mud, puddles and the ford at speed, and hard rain; a screen overlay; goggles are item 48, so this has a `guard` value they can set; no new girl lines for now.

- `src/effects/lensMud.js` (pure, tested in `tests/lensMud.test.js`): a list of splats (x and y over the screen, size, thickness). `leaderAhead` finds the nearest moving, muddy vehicle within `followRange` and inside a cone ahead; `splatRates` turns that, a puddle under any wheel and the rain into splats a second, scaled by `1 - guard`; `updateLens` rolls them and dries the old ones (mud over `dryTime`, drops over `dropDryTime`); `wipeLens` makes the first wipe a wider, thinner smear (`smearGrow`, `wipeSmear`) and the second take it off (`wipeClear`).
- Open or not is by spec id (`isOpenVehicle`): the UTV, both Land Rovers, the Fiesta and the Corsa are closed. The rain only counts when the quad is not under a roof (`world.covered`).
- `src/effects/lensMudView.js` draws it as a 2D canvas under the HUD, about ten times a second and only while something is stuck, so a clean lens costs nothing. Its colours are dimmed with `daylight.darkness` so it does not glow at night.
- The lens is yours, not the vehicle's: it dries while you are off, and is hidden on foot or in a cab.
- Not saved between games. Not measured for frame time: a small canvas redrawn at 10 Hz.
- Not done: girl lines about it, and goggles (item 48).

## Helmets and goggles (TODO 48)

Asked and settled: your helmet and goggles are two separate things you take from the hooks with `E` and keep until you hang them up (kept in the save); a helmet changes no physics, only what Amy thinks; the girls fetch theirs from the hooks and take them off often, leaving them on a seat or the ground; models are procedural and may need adjusting. Not done: taking yours off on entering the barn (the hooks are the only way off).

- `src/npc/headgear.js` (pure, tested in `tests/headgear.test.js`): a helmet is on the hooks (`rack`), on her head, on a vehicle's seat or on the ground. `update` watches each girl's open-vehicle ride (from the game: sat on or driving a quad, mini moto, three-wheeler or tractor) and turns the edges into events: getting on picks up a helmet lying within `pickUpRange` 30 m (or the one on that very seat); getting off takes it off with her `helmetLeave` chance, onto the seat or, with `helmetGround`, the ground; wearing one on foot for `footMax` 90 s drops it; a helmet on a seat falls off over `dropSpeed` 2.5 m/s. Fetching from the hooks is an offer (`helmet` in `OFFER_KINDS`, purpose 0.5) weighed by `weight` times her `helmetWeight` (Amy 2.2, Jess 0.5), so it is chosen like everything else she does on her own.
- Amy's minding is `watch`: riding open, moving over `nagSpeed`, no helmet for `nagAfter` 6 s, her within `nagRange` 30 m and able to remark (or on a vehicle herself) gives a nag, then not again for `nagEvery` 140 s. The first time she sees yours on, one thank-you and `praiseFavour` 1 favour. Only girls with `helmetNags` nag (Amy).
- Goggles set the lens mud's `guard` to `goggleGuard` 0.85 while on (the lens code is item 47's). `src/hud/gogglesOverlay.js` is a plain element under the mud canvas: a dark frame round the edges for goggles, a peak along the top for a helmet.
- Girls' helmets (`src/npc/helmetModel.js`, `helmetView.js`): one shell per rider, a stripe, a peak, a rolled rim and chin straps, goggles in a frame on an elastic, down over the eyes (Amy) or pushed up on the front (Jess). Each is fitted from her own head: `measureHead` reads the vertices her Head bone moves most, takes the crown and width from them and the skull's axis from the bone (the hair pulls a middle-of-the-vertices guess backwards), then the helmet is attached to the bone and scaled by `helmetFit` 1.35 (her hair stands out from her head) times her `helmetSize` (Jess 1.2, over her big hair), raised by `helmetLift` plus her `helmetRaise`. Jess on foot tips hers back. Loose ones sit on the seat (just behind the rider anchor) or lie on the grass.
- The hooks (`src/world/helmetHooks.js`, `layout.helmetHooks`): a plank with four pegs inside the south wall at x -64.5, hung with Amy's, Jess's and your helmets and your goggles; a girl goes in by the doorway, stands in front and takes hers.
- Lines: `src/npc/headgearLines.js`: `helmetOn`, `helmetFell` and `gogglesOn` for both girls, `helmetNag` and `helmetPraise` for Amy. Said when she takes hers down, when a left helmet falls off, when you put goggles on near her, and the nag and thanks. Recorded for both girls.
- Debug: Tune "Helmets and goggles"; the Girls tab shows each girl's helmet and has a button for yours; "Make her do" has a fetch-or-hang-up (`girl amy helmet`); the console's `kit.helmet` and `kit.goggles`.
- Checked: unit tests, and headless screenshots at full size of each helmet from the front, side and back, the hooks, and one on a quad seat. Amy's looked right; Jess's needed the size and lift above. Not checked: the ground helmet's look, the nag in play, the goggles overlay, or a girl actually walking to the hooks.

## An air leak at the carb boot (TODO 49)

Settled without asking, as the item was clear.

- A ninth use-driven fault, `leak`, in `faults.js` like the carb and mounts: starts while the engine runs (about every 6000 s of running, scaled by the quad's own proneness), from level 0.15, takes about 1200 s to open right up. Not on a diesel (`hasBoot`, set in `quad.js` from `compressionIgnition`; a diesel has no carb or throttle body). Quads can start a game with it (history), and the girls mention it (`faultLeak`, `histLeak`) like the others.
- What it does is in `EngineModel` (control `airLeak`): leans the mixture (`leakLean`, strongest at idle where the vacuum is, less with the throttle open), a slow idle hunt (`leakHunt`, 0.45 Hz, plus a little noise, mostly gone with the throttle open) and extra overrun-pop propensity (`popLeak`, added in `popPropensity`). Leaning also costs a little power and makes the lean-driven run-on a touch likelier, as a real leak would. The noise term throws no dice while the boot is sound, so every existing seeded test and every healthy engine is unchanged.
- Bench only (`BENCH_ONLY_KINDS`): a rough fix returns false, so no toolbox key. The new bench job "Renew the intake boot" (the fifteenth key, semicolon, as the overflow keys ran out) has an inspect step that reads `bootLook`; a clip not nipped up right leaves a faint leak at the usual botched level. No spare part is used.
- The Problems folder and the console's faults path list it automatically, and "Put it right" clears it, because they walk `FAULT_KINDS`.
- Tests in `tests/airLeak.test.js`. Not checked in the running game: the hunt amplitude (22% of idle at its worst) and pop rate are sensible numbers, not tuned by ear.

## The polo as a garment (spike, behind `?polo=shell`)

Jamie asked whether the polo could be built on top of her body like the jumper instead of painted on. Painted, it can only change colour where the body's triangles allow, and one triangle runs from her shoulder nearly to her jaw. A shell of her own triangles (as the jumper is) has the same limit: it either keeps that triangle (a roll neck) or drops it (a scoop on her shoulders). The way through is to cut the shell's triangles along a smooth line, adding vertices of its own, so its edges are where they should be however coarse the body is.

Settled with Jamie before building:

1. Behind a flag, `?polo=shell`, compared side by side with the painted polo before the painted one goes.
2. Collarless first and get that right, then a collar. Fairly detailed: a placket at least as long as now, better buttons.
3. The hem tucked into the shorts.
4. Short sleeves.

What was found first, and matters for every garment: **her body mesh is stored in a different pose from the one she stands in.** The positions in the mesh (which `skinnedGarment`'s `wear`, `offset` and `shape` get, and which `cutNeckline` measures) are a T-pose turned a quarter round: her front is +x and her left is -z, about 2 cm higher. Her bones are measured as she stands: front +z, left +x, arms down. Every rule that mixes the two (an angle round her neck, "in front of her", distance to her hand) is a quarter turn out. The painted neckline's dip and the jumper's scoop neck are on her right side, not her front, and the jumper's fall from the bust is worked out across her side. With the painted polo this hid itself, because what showed through the jumper's misplaced scoop was shirt-coloured body. It likely explains a good part of the collar's troubles.

- **The pipeline** (`src/npc/garmentMesh.js`, pure, tested): each body vertex carries both positions (`bind` as stored, `rest` as she stands) and `skin`, the 3x4 map from one to the other (her skinning matrices at rest; checked against the posed positions to 4 micrometres). Decisions are made on `rest`; the garment is built on `bind`, which is what gets skinned. A shape worked out as she stands (the fall from the bust, the placket) is carried back through the inverse of the skin. Steps: weld the flat-shaded corners into one vertex with an averaged normal; subdivide once, the new points pushed out over a surface through the corners' normals (Phong tessellation) so the cloth is rounder than her body; cut along fields (below 0 is inside), splitting triangles exactly on the line with new vertices whose weights are blended (strongest four, summing to 1); draw band lines in with both sides kept and their own copies of the points, so a colour changes sharply; smooth normals by position so the copies shade as one surface.
- **The tops** (`src/npc/polo.js`): one builder, `buildTop`, with `POLO` and `JUMPER` as its settings. The neck is a line round her neck (`neckBack`, `neckSide`, `neckFront` above the neck bone at `neckRing` out, lower by `neckSlope` per metre further out) capped at `neckCap` 4 cm above the neck bone. Without the cap, her jaw (wide, and far from the middle of her neck) counted as inside and grew a patch of shirt under her chin, which looked like shirt-coloured lips. Her neck base is a flat ring (the top of the model's old roll neck), so the line is set by how far out it sits: about 5 cm at the sides and back, 6 cm at the front where the placket starts. Sleeves are cut square across the arm, blended by how much that part of the arm carries the vertex, so her side by the armpit is never cut. The hem is a height, measured as the shorts measure theirs.
- **The polo**: 7 mm off her body, 5 mm on the sleeves flaring to 8 mm at the hem, 66% of the way from shoulder to elbow (her upper arm has only two rings of vertices, and the painted sleeve stops at 56%, so the cut runs through one long band of triangles and covers it). A 1.1 cm neck band and 1.6 cm cuffs 16% darker, turned in so the edges have thickness. The hem is 3.5 cm below the top of the shorts, drawn in to 2.5 mm below their waistband (not rounded out there, since the shorts are her flat faces pushed out) and blousing 5 mm over it. Below the bust the front falls back at 0.32 m per m instead of following her in. Built in about 20 ms, about 1,800 triangles.
- **The placket**: found by rays at the finished shirt as she stands, 13.5 cm long (12 before) and 2.4 cm wide, 2.2 mm proud with its long edges turned down, flat across (it bridges any dip between her breasts). A stitch texture (tonal thread down both edges and a box across the bottom, drawn once). Three buttons, each a turned profile with a raised rim and a dished face, and four holes in the shirt's colour, darkened. The fabric matches the shirt: the texture's ground is divided out in linear colour (dividing by its sRGB value left it darker than the shirt).
- **The body underneath** stays shirt-coloured, so anything poking through in an odd pose is shirt on shirt. Its neckline paint (`cutNeckline`, given the garment's line) sits 6 mm inside the garment's edge, so no shirt colour shows above it.
- **The jumper** goes over it on the same pipeline when the flag is on: 2.6 cm off, to 86% of the forearm, ribbed cuffs drawn in to 9 mm, a hem 9 cm below the shorts' top (11 cm caught the tops of her thighs, whose triangles belong to her legs) with a 3 cm rib, neck band 2.2 cm, everything 24% darker at the bands, falling straight from the bust at 0.18 m per m. The old jumper over the new polo let the polo through at her right shoulder (its scoop) and under the bust; this one covers it, with its neck showing just the polo's band. With the flag off, the old jumper and painted polo are untouched.
- **Measured**: load time with and without the flag, alternated, 2.10 and 2.80 s against 2.15 and 3.06 s (no difference beyond noise). `npm test` passes; `tests/garmentMesh.test.js` covers the skin maps, welding, subdivision, cuts, rays, and the polo and jumper built on a stand-in body stored turned a quarter round, so a mix-up between the two frames fails.
- **Checked headless** at full size, both girls in a mustard shirt and Amy in her seed's dark green: front, close on the neck, three-quarter, side, back, shoulder, full length; Amy sitting on a quad from the side, front and behind, and driving one with her arms forward on the bars; both in jumpers; and the same shots with the flag off for comparison. Not checked: the shirt in motion (walking, mounting), Jess sitting or driving.
- **Not done yet**: the collar (next, built from the neck's edge loop, which the cut leaves as an ordered ring of vertices with known weights); the shorts' waistband, which is still whole triangles and dips in a V at her back; tunables in the debug panel (the settings are plain objects for now, to register once the painted polo goes); fixing the old jumper's quarter turn with the flag off (it goes when the garment version replaces it).

### Follow-up: more buttons, Jess's open neck, tucked in, the jumper's neck

Jamie: more buttons and a slightly deeper placket with the top one nearer the neck; try Jess with her top button open, before the collar; the polo tucked fully into the waist; the jumper's neck very slightly deeper.

- **Placket**: 15.5 cm with five buttons 3.2 cm apart, the top one 1 cm below the neckline (`buttons` are now distances down it). At 17.5 cm the last button sat on the crease under the bust. Buttons sit on the placket's own face, which bridges the dip between her breasts; on the shirt under it they sank in and showed as crescents.
- **Jess's open neck** (`poloOpen: 1` in her outfit, `open` in the settings): the neck cut gets a V at her front, from the round neckline down to 9 mm above her second button, 1.7 cm either side at the top (`openNeck`, measured on the rounded shirt before it's cut; its field is only on her front, so her back is never cut). Above the V's point the placket is two half-strips lying along its edges, each with its own turned-down sides; the undone button is on her left half and a buttonhole slit on her right. Inside the V is her skin: the body's paint can't follow a V across its coarse triangles (a triangle spanning the V is painted shirt all across), so a patch of her own flat-shaded triangles cut to the V and lifted 0.8 mm covers it, in her skin colour (`buildInset`). Lifting each face along its own normal opened cracks at the creases that showed the shirt paint; each corner is lifted along the average of its faces, still drawn flat.
- **Tucked in**: the shorts were her whole triangles, so their top dipped in a V at her back and the polo showed down into it. With the flag on they are cut the new way too (`src/npc/shorts.js`): level at the waist, square across each thigh at 56% to the knee, a 3 cm waistband a little darker, turned-in edges, 6 mm off at the waist flaring 12 mm towards the hem. The waist and the polo's hem are level as she stands, not in the stored pose (which dipped 1 to 2 cm at her back), at the same height as the old shorts' top. The polo is drawn in to 2.5 mm from 2.5 cm above the waist down, so it is inside the shorts all the way to their top (it reached its tuck only below the waistband before, standing level with them right at the top). A test checks every polo point below the waist is nearer her than every point of the shorts.
- **The shorts aren't rounded**: rounded like the polo, they tore open across her lap when she sat on a quad. A point between two of her vertices, given their weights blended, doesn't stay between them once skinned at a joint bent as far as her hips are sitting; at the inside of the bend it sinks and her body comes through. Her own vertices bend exactly as she does. (The polo's rounding hasn't shown this at the shoulders or waist in the poses looked at.)
- **The jumper's neck** is 6 mm lower at the front and 3 mm at the sides and back: it shows Amy's top button and the top of Jess's V.
- Checked headless at full size: both girls front, neck, three-quarter, side, back and full length; Jess driving from the front and side; Amy on the back of a quad from behind; both in jumpers. Not checked: the shirt in motion, Amy driving since this change.

### Follow-up: Jess further open, the seat of the shorts, tonal buttons, and the collar

Jamie: open Jess's shirt more, 3 or 4 buttons, with a tiny visible cleavage, and keep 1, 2 and 3 open as designs to adjust or make random; the hips blend into the shorts badly (a visible bottom, then straight in); buttons a shade darker than the shirt, as in real life; then the collars, straight away.

- **Buttons undone**, 0 to 4, each a V of its own (`veeWidths`: half-widths at the neckline 1.7, 2.2, 2.7 and 3.1 cm, down to 9 mm above the first button done up). Jess has 4: the V reaches the top of her bust and shows a little of it. It's per girl (`poloOpen` in her outfit) and settable live: "polo buttons undone" in her folder on the Girls tab, `girls.jess.poloOpen` in the console (`Companion.setPoloOpen`, which refits her clothes). Not random yet.
- **Cutting a V exactly.** The V's point is narrower than the shirt's triangles, so a triangle straddling her middle couldn't be cut along both sides of it, and the shirt and the skin inside covered the lower part of the V. Both are split down her middle first (`cut` with `keep: 'split'`: the line drawn in, nothing thrown away, the points shared); either side of it the V's edge is a straight line, which a cut follows exactly. The skin inside is also split flat (each triangle into four in its own plane, so it still matches her facets) and picked generously, since the V's point can lie inside one of her triangles without touching a corner. The turned-in edge is left off down the V: the placket halves cover that edge, and turned in it filled the opening.
- **The seat.** The shorts clung to her bottom and went straight in under it. Below the fullest part of her bottom (its furthest-back point in each 2 cm across, the widest of five so it bridges between the two sides) they now fall towards the hem at 0.12 m forward per m down, up to 6 cm off her, fading out towards her sides and her front. 0.22 and 0.3 hardly showed: her bottom's fullest point is high, so at those slopes the fall had come back in by the hem. The shorts are still her own coarse triangles (rounded, they tore across her lap when sitting), so the seat has some facets.
- **Buttons** are the shirt's colour at 72% (`buttonShade`), their holes and the buttonholes darker still.
- **The collar** (`src/npc/collar.js`, pure, tested). The earlier attempts were fitted to her body, which has no neckline to sit on; this one stands on the garment polo's cut neck edge, a smooth ring of points, interpolated round her neck by angle. A stand 2.1 cm high at the back and 1.6 at the front ends, leaning in 3 mm; the fold rolls out 9 mm over its top; the fall comes out 2.6 cm and down to 1 cm below the neckline, more at the points (2.2 cm further out and 3.8 cm further down, turned towards her front, over the last 0.9 rad at each end), the corners rounded. Done up, its two ends meet over the placket (Jamie asked for a collar buttoned up and closed at the front before); on an open neck they're at the top corners of the V, so the points splay. The fall is laid on whatever is outermost by rays from a point inside her chest (the shirt, or the jumper when she has one on, so the collar lies out over the jumper's neck), 2.5 mm off it, and its skin blends from the neck edge's to whatever it lies on, so it moves with her shoulders. Laid point by point, neighbours lifted by different amounts folded a triangle over and showed its dark back; three passes evening the fall out, each followed by lifting anything that went under back out, fixed that. It's a slab 1.6 mm thick with every edge closed, thinned to a third over the fold (at full thickness the inside showed through the sharp bend); the outer edge 10% darker and the inside 28%.
- **Order.** With the flag on, the polo is built, then the jumper, then the collar over both. About 1,300 points and 2,600 triangles, built in about 70 ms a girl. Every refit (her shirt colour at the start, a jumper on or off) rebuilds the lot, about 0.2 s a girl.
- **Measured**: load time with and without the flag, alternated, 2.30 and 2.31 s against 2.26 and 2.06 s, so up to about a quarter of a second more, inside the 2.5 s budget.
- Checked headless at full size: both girls' collars close up from the front, three-quarter and back; Jess with 3 and 4 undone; both in jumpers with the collar over; Amy driving; Jess's hips from the side, back and three-quarter back before and after the seat. Not checked: the clothes in motion, Jess driving since the collar.
- Not mine: the console shows "THREE.Object3D.add: object not an instance of THREE.Object3D" twice on loading, from `src/world/assets.js`, with or without the flag.

The wings (the fall) start 2 mm beyond the placket's edges, so the placket shows between them, and at each end turn out towards her shoulders (`pointSpread` 0.75), so each wing's front edge runs down and outward as a polo's does; the points come 2 cm further out and 3 cm further down than the back, their corners rounded only slightly (`tipRound` 0.05 rad). Open, both pieces start at the top corners of the V.
- **Over the jumper**: the jumper's neck is a narrow ridge, and the fall, laid at six points from fold to edge, let it through between them. It's laid at eleven, each resting on the highest of what's under it there and 4 mm either side down the fall (`feel`), 3 mm off it.
- **All five undone** (`setPoloOpen` and the panel and console go to 5): the V runs to the bottom of the placket, where the shirt is sewn shut, the placket in two halves its whole length with every button on her left half and a buttonhole on her right. The V's sides curve out the further it's undone (`veeBows`, 1 for one or two buttons down to 0.5 for all five; at the neckline 3.3 cm either side for four, 4 cm for five), so it opens round her bust.
- **Cleavage, drawn on.** Her body is a few flat faces on her chest, so nothing in the V showed her bust. The skin in the V is shaded: a soft shadow down her middle (half as bright at the centre, gone 7 mm either side, fading in over 4 cm from 9 cm above the fullest part of her bust), and a lighter one along the V's edges where the shirt's fronts stand over her skin. Split either side of her middle so the line stays narrow. Jess has 4 undone.
- **The placket's top**: the shirt's top at her middle is found to a tenth of a millimetre (it was walked down in millimetre steps, then started 2 mm below), and the placket starts there. A ray at that very top, or at a V's point, can just miss the shirt, so each looks up to 12 mm lower for what's under it (only for its skin; the point stays where it is). Without that the placket vanished on Jess.
- **Tucked in, again**: just above the waist the polo stood out about as far as the shorts, so the outline ran straight on and the shorts looked like the shirt coloured blue. The waistband stands 11 mm off her now (the shorts are 6 mm), stepping out at its bottom edge too, a shade darker (88%), and the shirt above it blouses 4 mm rather than 5: it reads as a band the shirt goes into.
- **Shorter shorts**: the hem 40% of the way from hip to knee (was 56%).
- **Buttons** stay at 72% of the shirt's colour.
- Checked headless at full size: Amy's collar close up from the front; Jess's with 4 and 5 undone straight on from 30 cm (`.scratch/pp/veeshot.mjs`) and from further off; both in jumpers with the collar over them; both girls' waists from the front and three-quarter back; Amy sitting on the back of a quad; Jess standing in her own red shirt. Not checked since these changes: driving, and the clothes in motion.
