### The garments made the default (2026-10-13)

Jamie: the orange polo was more of a red; take the flag away and make the garments the default, deleting the old code.

- **Colour.** The painted polo's colour was its hex's linear value written into the texture as if it were sRGB, which comes out darker and redder (that's why Amy's navy was once lightened to #3a5690). The garments carried that over to match the paint under them. They now use the colour as it is, the quad's own, so the orange quad's girl wears orange; the shirt band of the body's paint under them is written the same way (`recolourTexture`, the shirt band only: hair and shoes keep their old reading, which their colours were chosen for). The logo's thread turns dark on the lighter shirts this makes.
- **Deleted**: the flag (`?polo=shell`), the painted polo's placket and buttons (`addPoloFront`, `chestPoint`, `shirtFrontTop`), the old jumper (`addJumper`) and shorts (`addShorts`), `skinnedGarment` (only they used it), the old neckline (`necklineAt` and its constants, and `cutNeckline`'s own line: it now always takes the polo's), `shirtVertices` and `shirtColour`. The outfits' `collar` key is now `polo`. Kept: the wellies (`fittedShell`, rays at a posed copy of the body), the palette recolour and the neckline strip under the polo. The lane's visitors use the plain original outfit, and the neighbour's jumper is the garment jumper.
- **Fitted once.** Each girl's garments were built when she was made and again straight after, when her shirt colour and buttons are dealt. They're now only fitted then (`applyOutfit`'s `garments: false`, then `setShirt`, or `Companion.dress` for a girl with no favourite quad). Load to "Ready": 2.38 to 2.54 s (2.52 and 2.69 before, building twice), at about the 2.5 s budget.
- **Mud.** The mud and dust drawn on a girl (`girlMud`) are on her body's material, so under her polo they don't show now, as they never did under her shorts or wellies; her face and arms still show them. Mud on the clothes themselves is left for later.
- The FAQ's notes on the girls say their polos are the colours of their favourite quads (they said navy and burgundy, true only before the shirts matched the quads), with a new answer on why the shirts and buttons change between farms; the README likewise.
- Checked headless: both girls full length in their own colours (Amy green, Jess red on seed 3), both in the orange quad's orange, Jess's open neck in yellow, the game loading with no errors of its own.

### Follow-up: a crease over the shoulder when driving

Jamie: an artefact poking out of the rear shoulder on a quad, e.g. Jess with 2 or 3 undone. It was a crease in the polo over her left shoulder blade with her arms forward on the bars, for both girls and with any number undone; her body alone (the polo hidden) showed none. Ruled out first: a gap in the collar's neck ring (it has a 0.6 rad gap with nothing undone too), and the rounding (unrounded, a thinner crease stayed). The body's weights change abruptly there, between her back, shoulder and upper arm; with no thickness it doesn't matter, but the polo stands 5 to 7 mm off, and skinning carries that offset in quite different directions at neighbouring points once the shoulder bends, which makes a valley.

- `smoothSkinning` (`garmentMesh.js`, tested): each point's bone weights, and its skin map with them (it's linear in the weights, so rest positions stay right), move halfway towards its neighbours' average, four passes (`skinSmoothing`), right after the polo is rounded, so the placket, logo and collar, built on it afterwards, take the evened-out skinning too. Points at one spot are kept together.
- Restricting it (her back only, or not the sleeves, or not the sleeves' fronts) left the crease, the edge of the smoothed part making its own; smoothed everywhere it's gone. Counting triangles that turn over when driving: 2 before; 37 smoothed everywhere, at the front of her shoulders and the inside of her upper arms, where the arms coming forward squeeze the cloth. None of those showed in close shots of both armpits from the front, the sides and front three-quarter.
- Checked headless: both girls driving, from behind either side and straight behind, the front and both sides; Jess with 2 and 3 undone; standing from the front, three-quarter, back and shoulder, and Jess's open neck straight on. A shadow on Amy's upper back standing is her hair's (it's there before the change too). The jumper is built by the same code with the polo's settings under its own, so it's evened out too. Not checked: the jumper while driving.







## A worn clutch on the manual quads (TODO 50)

Settled without asking, as the item was clear.

- A tenth fault, `clutch`, in `faults.js`, on the four manual quads only (`hasClutch`, set in `quad.js` from having a gearbox and not being the tractor, a Land Rover, a car or the UTV; history only deals it to the same). Unlike the others it starts and worsens from how the clutch is used, not from running time: `clutchSlipping` (pure, tested) gives 0 to 1 for the pedal at the bite (clutch 0.05 to 0.85), in gear with the engine running, and either loaded (throttle 0.3 or more, or a climb of sine 0.05 or more) or creeping below 2 m/s in first or second. It is strongest in the middle of the bite. Half-holding the pedal while rolling fast on the flat with no throttle counts for nothing.
- Starts after about 400 s of full slipping (`clutchEvery`, scaled by the quad's own luck like the rest), then takes about 500 s more of slipping to go from 0.15 to worn out (`clutchSeconds`); it does not get worse unless slipped.
- Effect (`clutchSlipUnderPower`): nothing until wear passes 0.2, then slip grows with wear and with throttle. In `quad.js` that slip takes up to 75% off the drive (`clutchDriveLoss`), is fed to the engine's existing `slip` control (the belt's flare, so the revs run away and the load stops holding them down) and loosens the engine's tie to the road (`gearLock` times one minus the slip). So it is the belt's symptom by another cause.
- Bench only (`BENCH_ONLY_KINDS`): "Fit a new clutch plate", the sixteenth key (the quote), with an inspect step (`clutchLook`). Done badly it leaves a fault at the usual botched level. No spare part. The girls mention it (`faultClutch`, `histClutch`, which says it smells hot) while the quad is ridden. Tests in `tests/clutchWear.test.js`.
- Left out: a real smell or smoke from the hot clutch (only the girls' words), the clutch plate on the tractor, Land Rovers and cars, and a clutch cooling down. Not checked in the running game: how quickly it wears in play (the numbers are first guesses) and how the flare feels.

## A thunderstorm (TODO 51)

Jamie settled: rarer than snow, flashes and thunder only (nothing is struck), the girls as recommended, nothing blocked, and the debug and console controls.

- **A weather kind** `storm` (`weather.js`): weight 0.03 (snow is 0.06), a near-black sky, rain 0.95, wind 1.9, cold to warm. `Weather.storm` is 1 in a storm and eases with the turn into or out of it. It is not a separate layer on top of rain, so the existing rain, wet ground, barn doorway shelter and cab shelter all apply as they do in heavy rain.
- **It always passes** (`forecast.js`): `SHIFTS.storm` goes to rain, drizzle or overcast, always, between `stormEarliest` (0.4) and `stormLatest` (1.5) game hours after the start, which is about two to eight minutes of play. A storm that lasted the whole game would keep the girls in the barn all day. The board shows it with the rain picture; the barometer reads 0.08.
- **Lightning and thunder** (`thunder.js`, pure, tested): a strike every 7 to 35 s at the height of a storm, 20% of them close (90 to 500 m). A strike is 1 to 3 flickers, the thunder comes `distance / 343` s later. Far strikes are dimmer and only rumble; near ones are bright and crack. The flash is the whole sky at once, so `updateStorm` in `game.js` adds to the hemisphere and sun light, the tone mapping exposure and the fog and sky colours for that frame (all reset by the sky's own update next frame), and less indoors. Tunables under "Thunderstorm".
- **Sound** (`audio/thunderAudio.js`): filtered brown noise with a rolling envelope, plus a bright burst for a crack. Procedural, so no asset to credit. Not heard by ear yet.
- **The girls**: thunder is a notice (`thunder`, heard, placed where you are since it comes from the whole sky, with the strike's nearness as its strength). A reaction rule makes her flinch only for close ones (strength 0.9 or more, `reactStartleChance`). One girl who is free to remark says a thunder line after each clap (`thunderClose` or `thunderFar`). Amy has `stormWont` 1, so `refusesStorm()` (`companionDriving.js`) stops her riding pillion, on the rack or driving, from her own choices and from the wheel, where she answers with `stormWont` instead. A seated Amy on a quad stood still gets off once (`stormGetOff`). Jess has no refusal, and her thunder lines are glad. Lines in `stormLines.js` (recorded with the rest).
- **Left out**: a girl already driving when the storm starts carries on (she isn't stopped mid-drive); Jess does not actively seek a ride out in it; no strike marks, no puddles gulping or fords rising faster, no rain on the camera. Not checked in the running game: how the flash looks (the amounts are first guesses), how the thunder sounds, and the lines in play.

## The barn gets a second span, and the bench takes any vehicle (TODO 52)

Jamie: the tractor, UTV, Land Rovers and cars can be bent like the quads but don't fit the bay. First plan was a repair pad in the yard; Jamie preferred a wider barn with a second bay inside, a second door, a second light, the same loft, and better jack and stand models.

- **Shape.** `layout.barn` is still the first span (loft, hay door, boards, bench all hang off it, so none of that moved). `barn.annex` adds a second span of the same size on its south side (z 64.6 to 73.8), so the barn is 18.4 m across. Each span has its own gable roof at the same height and pitch (a valley between, not one tall roof), and the wall between them is left out: three posts (`dividerPosts`, with colliders) carry a beam and a steel gutter. The second door is in the same +x wall, 4 m wide and 3.2 m high, centred on the span. `barnShape` gives `outerAll`/`innerAll` for both spans; `insideBarn`, `underRoof`, `indoorLevel`, `floorBlend` and `wallBoxes` use them (the east wall is cut in pieces round both doors), while the loft and the first door keep using the first span.
- **The valley.** The two roofs' lower edges at the valley end at the wall line with no overhang (with the overhang they crossed each other and flickered at the front). The loft's top rail is drawn only within 3.7 m of the first span's middle; the roof comes down below its height nearer the valley and it stuck through the tin once the valley could be seen from above (the collider keeps the whole rail).
- **Bays.** `layout.bay` is the quad bay, as it was. `layout.bay2` (6 by 3.8 m, long way to the door) is the big one, with a trolley jack and two axle stands (`workshop/jackModel.js`, about 30 primitives each: chassis channels, wheels and castors, pump housing, ram, lift arm with a rubber saddle and handle; stands with a three-legged base, a sleeve, a ratcheted column, a pawl and a saddle). `Bench.unitAtBench()` is the quad in the small bay (or the barn find), else anything in the big bay; the girls only use the small bay (`unitInBay`, unchanged), so none of her bench work changed.
- **Bench not moved.** I had said I would move it to the back wall between the bays. It turned out that nothing needs it: the bench acts on whichever vehicle is in a bay and the toolbox only has to be near the bench. Turning it would also have touched the rack, the notebook, the charger, the leads and the oil's places, the girls' work spot and the tests. If the 13 m between the bench and the big bay feels wrong in play, that is the next thing to change.
- **Jobs for everything.** Per Jamie, the jobs apply to any vehicle and grey out when they don't fit: `plug` needs a spark ignition (no diesel), `carb` and `boot` need neither a diesel nor `injected` (the Corsa). The rest already went by the engine's flags.
- **Moved to make room:** the generator (to z 75.7, outside the new south wall), the helmet hooks and the jumper rail (to the new south wall, with the girls' door waypoints now through the second door), the bell (north of the first door's north leaf), the silage (to the west wall, `layout.silage`), a jerry can's start spot, and one yard quad slot (-53, 72, gone: the door's keep-out circle took it and nothing else fitted). `spawnKeepOut` has circles for the extension and its doorway. The yard grass no longer grows under the roof of either span (it grew inside the second span's floor).
- **A second bulb** (`light2`, made at the start and dimmed with the first) hangs over the big bay.
- **Not done:** the fumes haze is still drawn only over the first span (an engine run in the second counts as indoors for coughing and the echo, but its fumes show less), the girls' pathing to the second door beyond the waypoints, and a second interior detail pass (shelves, tools) in the new span.
- **Checked** with unit tests and headless screenshots (outside, front roof from above, the valley close up, inside the big bay, the jack and stand at full size). The game logged `THREE.Object3D.add: object not an instance` twice from `instanceModel` in `assets.js` on load (an empty tile list); it comes from the hedges, bushes or trees, not this change, but I did not run it on the commit before to prove it. **Not checked:** driving the Land Rover or tractor through the new door and into the bay by hand, how it feels at night under the two bulbs, and the girls walking to the hooks and rail by the new route.

Below her hips her body's triangles are her legs', so the coat carries on with a **skirt** of its own (`addSkirt`): 10 cm straight down from the hem, flaring 1.2 cm, each column skinned like the hem point above it blended 45% towards her thigh at the bottom, so sitting on a quad it rides up over her lap instead of her thighs coming through. ### Follow-up: slimmer, a hood that joins the coat, a front you can see

Jamie: the coat made the girls look dumpy and round; the hood didn't seem to join the coat at the sides; the coat needs a zip or button line down the front.

- **Slimmer.** 3.4 cm off her (was 4.2), sleeves 2.6 cm, and hip length, a Bedale rather than a Beaufort: the skirt is 5 cm with no flare (was 10 cm, flaring). Rounded as the jumper is (0.75): rounded less (0.35 was tried), its faces lay flatter between her corners than the jumper's, and sitting on a quad the jumper came through at her chest. Below the bust it falls at the jumper's slope; any steeper and the jumper would come through under it.
- **The back hem** had a notch in the middle: like the jumper, the coat left out triangles her legs carry, and the bottom of her backside is her thighs'. It keeps the tops of her thighs now (`bare` leaves out only her lower legs, feet and hands), and the hem cuts them level.
- **The shorts under it.** Their seat falls up to 6 cm off her bottom, which came through below the coat's back; under a coat it falls at most 1.8 cm.
- **The hood joins the coat.** Up, it comes down over the collar on to her shoulders, just beyond the collar's fall, instead of stopping inside the collar (which from the side looked like it ended at her jaw). It's skinned now, not stuck to her head bone: her head carries it round her head and her chest carries its bottom edge (`chestShare`), so it stays joined to the coat when she turns her head. Down, it's built as if gathered inside the collar (the wide one turned over stood up behind her head) and skinned to her chest.
- **The front.** The storm flap is 62% of the coat's colour, so its line shows, with bigger (8.5 mm) brighter brass studs.
- Checked headless: Jess in the coat front and back; Amy with the hood up from the front, side and back; Amy sat on a quad from the front and side; the rack. Not checked: the hood with her head turned well round.

Odds per girl in `characters.js` (`outfitOdds`, `poppedChance`): Jess 50/30/15/5 with a popped collar 30% of the time; Amy never wears the crop-top (its share is her polo's, so 55/30/15) and pops hers 10% of the time. Under it the body's shirt paint is the plaid's ground.
- **Open over the polo** (`PLAID_OPEN`): every button undone, and the V carried on to the hem with the fronts at least 5.5 cm either side of her middle (`veeFloor`), so it hangs open; 1.8 cm off her over the polo. Its fronts are laid along the edge as found on the finished shirt (`veeMeasured`, as the coat's), and an all-undone placket has no closed strip. The polo's collar lies over the plaid and the plaid's collar over that, so both show at the front.
- **The check** is drawn by the material, not a texture on the garment (which has no UVs): a few lines of shader on `MeshStandardMaterial` take the cloth's stored position, in which her arms are straight out, and blend three flat projections by which way the cloth faces, so the check runs straight across her and along and round her arms, about 9 cm a repeat. Four flannels: red and black, green and navy, blue and white, brown and cream, each a ground, a check and a thin line.
- **The double polo** (`OVER_POLO`): her own polo done up, and over it a white or black one 1.35 cm off her (hers is 0.7), sleeves to 58% (hers 66%) and its neck 6 mm lower, with a smaller collar (`OVER_COLLAR`), so her polo's cuffs, neck band and collar edge show; the over one has her buttons undone, showing her polo's placket in its V. Both tucked in, the over one just outside hers.
- **A popped collar** (`popped` in `collar.js`): the fall turned up from the fold, standing round her neck and flaring out, higher towards the points; laid on nothing. In a double polo both are popped.
- **The crop-top** (`src/npc/crop.js`, pure, tested): a band round her bust, from 5.5 cm below its fullest point to 4 cm above at the front, narrowing to a bra's strap round her back, and two string straps 1 cm wide over her shoulders, 7 cm either side of her middle. The straps are narrower than her triangles, so their edges are drawn into the mesh first, then the whole is cut along the band and the straps together. Under it the body's shirt band is painted her skin. Not checked: the notch where the band's bottom edge crosses between her breasts (her body has a crease there).
- **Faster collars.** With a collar laid over two or three layers of cloth, its rays tried every triangle of all of them, about 175 ms a collar, and the first day's outfits on seed 3 took load to 2.9 to 3.1 s. The cloth is now sorted by which way round her neck it lies and each ray tries only its own direction: 45 ms a collar. Load to "Ready", alternated: 2.29 and 2.19 s with both in plain polos, 2.39 and 2.30 s as dealt (before the speed-up 2.59 and 2.28 against 3.08 and 2.91).
- **Layers**: the polo, then the plaid or the over-polo, then the jumper, then the collars from the inside out, each lying on everything outside its own top and on the collar under it; the coat over all. The open plaid's rolled cuffs came through the jumper's sleeves at first; they stand 1.7 cm off her now, inside the jumper's 2.1.
- **Debug panel**: each girl's folder has an "outfit" picker, "plaid worn open" and "collar popped", and "wearing" says what she has on; the console has `girls.<name>.outfit`, `plaidOpen` and `popped`.
- Checked headless at full size: both girls in each outfit (plaid buttoned, plaid open, two polos white and black, popped, crop-top) from the front and three-quarter, and Jess in a jumper over the open plaid. Not checked: any of them sitting on a quad or driving, in a wax jacket, or the plaid check close up on the sleeves.
